# Decision log

> **IDs are slugs, not numbers.** Entries are identified by a content-derived slug
> (`SD_vaadin_first_binding`) with an explicit `<a id="…"></a>` anchor, so numbering never has to be
> allocated — and never clashes across worktrees. Old numeric IDs map at
> [DECISION-ID-MAP.md](../DECISION-ID-MAP.md).

> **Licence.** This log is Apache-2.0, like the module it documents
> ([`PROVENANCE.md` § Lanes](../PROVENANCE.md#lanes)). JDK source, javadoc and Oracle notices
> quoted here remain under their own terms; quoting them relicenses nothing.


ADR-lite, same shape as [../emulators/decisions.md](../emulators/decisions.md): stable IDs that never renumber or reuse, content edited in place as understanding evolves (fully-superseded entries shrink to a one-line pointer at the superseding ID), each entry captures **Decision**, **Rationale**, **Alternatives**, and **Accepted limitation**.

Surrogate decision IDs use the `SD*` prefix (`SD_mixin_mechanism`, `SD_naming`, …) to disambiguate from `:emulators`' `D*` entries — when "D_never_fail_on_gaps" appears unlinked in a commit message or conversation it always means [emulators D_never_fail_on_gaps](../emulators/decisions.md#D_never_fail_on_gaps), never the surrogate version.

The overarching split — why `:surrogates` exists alongside `:emulators` and how the two interact — is captured in [D_emulator_surrogate_split over there](../emulators/decisions.md#D_emulator_surrogate_split).

---

<a id="SD_mixin_mechanism"></a>
## SD_mixin_mechanism. Mixin mechanism: interface + default methods + `ComponentUtil.setData` state

**Decision.** Each Swing class surface (`java.awt.Component`, `java.awt.Container`, `javax.swing.JComponent`, …) is exposed to surrogates as a Java interface with default methods (`ComponentMixin`, `ContainerMixin`, `JComponentMixin`, …). Concrete surrogates extend a Vaadin component and `implements` the mixin(s) they want. Stateful concerns (listener lists, mapped-property storage, layout manager slot) live in dedicated holder classes under `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.internal`, attached to the surrogate via `ComponentUtil.setData(this, Holder.class, …)` and looked up lazily through a static `Holder.of(this)` accessor.

**Rationale.**
- A shared base class is impossible: surrogates extend different Vaadin classes (`Slider`, `IntegerField`, `Button`, …) and Java has no multiple inheritance. Mixin interfaces compose without that constraint.
- Default methods cover stateless delegation cleanly (`setEnabled` → `HasEnabled.setEnabled`, etc.). For the few methods Vaadin doesn't already provide (`setName`, `addPropertyChangeListener`, …) the default supplies the implementation.
- State via `ComponentUtil.setData` removes per-surrogate boilerplate. A surrogate writes `class SButton extends Button implements ComponentMixin {}` — no helper field, no constructor wiring, no manual storage initialisation. Same pattern `:emulators` already uses for the peer↔emulator map (D_peer_emulator_mapping).
- Lookup overhead is one map read per call; trivial for the access patterns Swing components see in practice (listener registration is rare, listener firing is in-list iteration after the lookup).

**Alternatives.**
- **Shared base class** (`abstract class SurrogateBase`) — impossible: can't extend both `SurrogateBase` and a Vaadin component class. Would require fragmenting into per-Vaadin-class bases (`SButtonBase extends Button`, `SJSliderBase extends Slider`, …), which is the boilerplate the mixin approach is designed to avoid.
- **Helper field per surrogate** (`private final SurrogateSupport support = new SurrogateSupport(this);`) — works but adds 2–4 lines of boilerplate per surrogate plus the obligation to remember it. `ComponentUtil.setData` is invisible at the surrogate site.
- **One large mixin interface covering the entire AWT/Swing surface** — works but loses the opt-in clarity of `implements ComponentMixin, ContainerMixin`. Future container-only or button-only mixins would have to be a no-op for non-applicable surrogates, hiding what each surrogate actually exposes.

**Accepted limitation.** Each holder access costs a `ComponentUtil.getData` map lookup. Negligible for normal use; would matter only for hot-path code that calls into the mixin millions of times per second, which Swing API isn't.

---

<a id="SD_naming"></a>
## SD_naming. Naming: `S*` / `SJ*` for surrogates tracking the Swing/AWT source; `*Mixin` for mixin interfaces; JDK types reused unchanged

**Decision.** Four naming conventions:

- **Concrete surrogates** name the Swing/AWT class they target, with a marker prefix that mirrors the JDK's own `J`-for-Swing convention:
  - `S` + Swing/AWT short name for an AWT source (no `J` in the original): `SButton` for `java.awt.Button`, `SFrame` for `java.awt.Frame`, `SWindow` for `java.awt.Window`.
  - `SJ` + Swing/AWT short name for a Swing source (has `J` in the original): `SJButton` for `javax.swing.JButton`, `SJFrame` for `javax.swing.JFrame`, `SJSlider` for `javax.swing.JSlider`, `SJSpinner` for `javax.swing.JSpinner`, `SJRootPane` for `javax.swing.JRootPane`.
  - The Vaadin class each extends is a peer choice made independently of the name — `SJFrame extends SFrame extends Dialog`, `SJSlider extends Slider`, `SJSpinner extends CustomField`, `SJRootPane extends Div`.
  - Surrogate-local Vaadin helpers without a Swing/AWT source keep their natural name unprefixed (e.g. `LongField`, a long-typed sibling of Vaadin's `IntegerField`).
- **Mixin interfaces** suffix `Mixin` to the Swing source class they expose: `ComponentMixin` for `java.awt.Component`, `ContainerMixin` for `java.awt.Container`, `JComponentMixin` for `javax.swing.JComponent`.
- **Ported events / listeners** prefix `S` to the JDK name: `SComponentEvent`, `SComponentListener`, `SComponentAdapter`, `SContainerEvent`, … (which are the events whose source is a `java.awt.Component` and therefore can't be reused — see [SD_event_port_stance](#SD_event_port_stance)). All event ports today target AWT classes, so the prefix is plain `S`; a future port of a Swing-package event (e.g. `SJInternalFrameEvent`) would take the `SJ` form.
- **JDK-reusable events / listeners** keep their original names — `java.awt.event.ActionListener`, `javax.swing.event.ChangeListener`, `java.beans.PropertyChangeListener`, etc. Their sources are typed as `Object` so a Vaadin Component is a valid source; no port needed, no rename needed.

**Rationale.**
- The `S` / `SJ` prefix at call sites visually marks the Swing-emulation surface. Inside `SJButton`, three APIs co-exist: `addClickListener(ComponentEventListener<ClickEvent>)` is Vaadin-native, `addActionListener(ActionListener)` is JDK-reused (no port needed since `ActionEvent.source` is `Object`), `addComponentListener(SComponentListener)` is surrogate-origin Swing API. The prefix lets a reader tell origin from the parameter type alone.
- Naming by Swing/AWT source rather than by Vaadin peer keeps `SButton` (`java.awt.Button` surrogate) and `SJButton` (`javax.swing.JButton` surrogate) distinct once both get backfilled — both would otherwise collapse to `SButton extends Button` under a peer-keyed rule, even though they emulate different JDK classes with different APIs. The JDK itself already encodes this distinction with the `J` prefix (AWT `Button` vs Swing `JButton`); mirroring it in surrogate names preserves the 1:1 mapping a migrator expects.
- Two distinct name-spaces (concretes vs. mixins) need distinct conventions to avoid confusion. `S<SwingName>` / `SJ<SwingName>` for concretes and `<SwingName>Mixin` for mixins are unambiguous: a class with an `S` or `SJ` prefix is a concrete surrogate; an interface with a `Mixin` suffix is a mixin.
- Mixins and ported events keep their original Swing-source package position: `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.awt.ComponentMixin` mirrors `java.awt.Component`, `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.awt.event.SComponentEvent` mirrors `java.awt.event.ComponentEvent`, future `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.swing.JComponentMixin` will mirror `javax.swing.JComponent`. A migrator searching for the analog of `ComponentEvent` finds `SComponentEvent` under the matching package by reflex; same for the mixin equivalents. The `S` / `SJ` (or `Mixin`) marks "this is the surrogate version"; the package position marks "yes, this is the AWT/Swing-lineage type you remembered." Concrete surrogates stay flat at `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.*` — the prefix already encodes AWT-vs-Swing origin, and the count is small enough that a flat namespace is the simplest organisation.
- `S` prefix matches existing Vaadin community convention for surrogate / replacement classes (e.g. `SLF4J`-style "S for surrogate" naming); `SJ` extends that by keeping the `S` marker while adding the `J` that's part of the Swing class name being emulated.

**Alternatives.**
- **No prefix** (`Button extends com.vaadin.flow.component.button.Button`) — name collisions with the supertype, confusing imports, no visual marker for Swing-vs-Vaadin-vs-JDK at call sites.
- **Plain `J` prefix** (matching `JButton`) — collides with the Swing class names users came from; reading `JButton button = new JButton()` in a surrogate codebase would be ambiguous about whether it's a real `javax.swing.JButton` or our surrogate. The chosen `SJ` combines the visual `S` marker (call-site origin signal) with the `J` (AWT-vs-Swing distinction), avoiding the collision while keeping the JDK mapping explicit.
- **Prefix `S` for everything regardless of source** (`SButton` for both `java.awt.Button` and `javax.swing.JButton`) — forces the two to share a class name even though they emulate different JDK types; once both are in scope the name becomes a point of ambiguity the JDK itself avoids.
- **Prefix on mixin too** (`SComponentMixin`) — redundant. `Mixin` suffix already disambiguates; the `S` would be noise.
- **Suffix on concretes** (`ButtonSurrogate`, `JButtonSurrogate`) — verbose at every use site; the prefix wins for readability.
- **Ported events without `S` prefix** (`ComponentEvent` in `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.awt.event`) — works but loses the visual call-site marker. A migrator reading `addComponentListener(ComponentListener)` would have to inspect the import to know which `ComponentListener` it is.

**Accepted limitation.** A migrator who blindly carries over `import java.awt.event.ComponentListener` gets the JDK type, not the surrogate's, and the `addComponentListener` call won't compile (the surrogate takes `SComponentListener`). The compiler error is loud and the fix is mechanical (rewrite the import) — the same trade-off [D_import_swap_target](../emulators/decisions.md#D_import_swap_target) makes for emulators.

<a id="SD_shelper_statics"></a>
## SD_shelper_statics. Surrogate-local `SHelper.onUnimplemented`, not shared with `:emulators`

**Decision.** `:surrogates` ships its own `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SHelper.onUnimplemented(Object self, String method, Object... args)` rather than reusing `vaadinx.EHelper.onUnimplemented` from `:emulators`. Same WARN-via-slf4j contract, same vararg-formatting rules, same never-throw behaviour.

**Rationale.**
- Module direction is `:emulators → :surrogates`, not the reverse. `:surrogates` has no compile-time visibility of `vaadinx.EHelper`; importing it would invert the dependency and couple surrogates to emulators (against the one-way direction stated in [architecture.md](./architecture.md) §"Handoff contract with `:emulators`").
- The first arg is the receiver instance instead of a class-name string (`vaadinx.EHelper` takes `String className`) so the log captures the runtime class via `self.getClass().getSimpleName()`. Surrogates extending different Vaadin types each show their actual class in the WARN, which would be lost if we standardised on a class-name string. (In `:emulators` the runtime class is unavailable because `Component` is regenerable and the helper signature is fixed by the generator — see [D_ehelper_statics](../emulators/decisions.md#D_ehelper_statics) over there.)
- Keeping the helper local lets `:surrogates` evolve its formatting / logging / dedup independently of `:emulators` if the two ever need to diverge (e.g. surrogates may want to surface the Vaadin parent component in the log message; emulators don't have a useful parent at the helper-call site).

**Alternatives.**
- **Cross-module dependency** (`:surrogates → :emulators`) — forbidden by the architecture; would couple the two modules and prevent the surrogate-extracts-cleanly-into-its-own-jar story.
- **Promote `SHelper` to a shared third module** (`:common` or similar) — possible but premature. Two helpers with the same shape is less infrastructure than a third module. Revisit if a third or fourth helper-shape consumer emerges.
- **Inline the WARN at every call site** — loses the central seam for future enhancements (per-class dedup, usage tracking, coverage reports).

**Accepted limitation.** Small duplication of the formatting logic (arg rendering rules) between `vaadinx.EHelper` and `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SHelper`. If they drift, the two modules will produce subtly different log output for the same situation. Acceptable — both are dev-aid logs, not user-facing contracts.

---

<a id="SD_dropin_stance"></a>
## SD_dropin_stance. Drop-in stance: hierarchy navigation excluded; everything else unsupported is a no-op stub

> **Partially superseded by SD_api_three_buckets (three-bucket partition) and SD_vaadin_first_binding (Vaadin-first rewrite).** The hierarchy-exclusion + never-fail-on-unsupported principles stand. Specific category membership (which listeners WARN vs. fire vs. store) has shifted; the mixin class javadoc and SD_api_three_buckets carry the current split.

**Decision.** Surrogates expose three categories of API:

1. **Excluded entirely** — not on the mixin, doesn't compile if user code references it. Used only when the JDK signature fundamentally clashes with Vaadin's. The set: hierarchy navigation/mutation (`getParent`, `add(Component)`, `remove(Component)`, `getComponent(int)`, `getComponents()`, `getComponentCount()`, `isAncestorOf(Component)`).
2. **No-op stubs via `SHelper.onUnimplemented`** — present on the mixin, log WARN, return a sensible default. The set: pixel bounds (`setBounds`, `setSize`, `setLocation`, …), paint pipeline (`repaint`, `revalidate`, `paint`, …), AWT peer lifecycle (`addNotify`, `removeNotify`), AWT input listeners whose event types aren't yet ported (`addFocusListener`, `addKeyListener`, `addMouseListener`, …), programmatic event firing without a target (`dispatchEvent`, `processXxxEvent`).
3. **Stored but inert** — present on the mixin, state round-trips, side effects deferred until later. The set: `setLayout`/`getLayout` on the Container mixin (kept for future container surrogates); `addComponentListener`/`addContainerListener` (stored, fired when peer wiring lands).

Mapped-property setters (`setEnabled`, `setVisible`, `setName`, `setToolTipText`, `setForeground`, `setBackground`, `setFont`) initially **do not** fire `PropertyChangeEvent` automatically. Migrated code that calls `firePropertyChange` directly works; code that expects setters to fire silently doesn't get notified.

**Rationale.**
- The drop-in goal trumps API minimalism. A migrated app that calls `comp.addMouseListener(...)` or `comp.repaint()` should compile and run, not require a source rewrite at every call site. Logging WARN tells the migrator what's quietly broken without crashing the app — same contract as [R_match_swing_errors](../CLAUDE.md) / [emulators D_never_fail_on_gaps](../emulators/decisions.md#D_never_fail_on_gaps).
- Hierarchy navigation is the one place the trade-off doesn't work: `getParent()` returning `Optional<Component>` (Vaadin) and `Container` (Swing) cannot coexist in one method signature. Vaadin's wins because the surrogate **is** a Vaadin component; users have Vaadin's `getParent()` available and that's enough. Same logic for the rest of the hierarchy-mutation family.
- `setLayout`/`getLayout` is kept as inert stored state because future container surrogates may genuinely need layout management; storing now (cheap) means container surrogates can wire dispatch later without changing the mixin signature.
- Skipping auto-PCE on mapped setters is a simplification, not a stance. Auto-PCE matches Swing's contract; the cost is a property-name string per setter and a `PceSupport.fire` call. Worth doing — but worth doing as its own slice with a test surface that catches every mapped property.

**Alternatives.**
- **Omit unsupported APIs from the mixin entirely** — forces source edits at every call site in migrated code. Worse drop-in feel; migrators would have to discover and remove every `addMouseListener` call before the surrogate will compile. WARN-and-no-op preserves the behaviour migrators expect from a half-supported component.
- **Throw `UnsupportedOperationException` on unsupported APIs** — too aggressive. Migrated apps wouldn't boot. Discussed and rejected for the same reason emulators rejected the same stance in [D_never_fail_on_gaps](../emulators/decisions.md#D_never_fail_on_gaps).
- **Implement everything now** — premature; the job is to stand up the mechanism, not to chase coverage.
- **Auto-fire PCE on mapped setters now** — viable but expands scope. Pulled out as its own follow-up so we can test the mechanism without dragging the property-name table along.
- **Keep `setLayout`/`getLayout` excluded for now** — possible since no concrete container surrogate ships yet, but reopening the mixin signature later is more churn than storing 8 bytes now.

**Accepted limitation.** Migrated apps that depend on the no-op'd APIs running for real (custom paint, mouse/key event handling, addNotify-driven setup) silently misbehave; the WARN log is the only surface. Apps that depend on Swing's auto-PCE on `setEnabled` etc. won't see those events fire. Both gaps are documented; both are scheduled to close as later slices port the underlying mechanisms.

---

<a id="SD_event_port_stance"></a>
## SD_event_port_stance. Event port stance: eager `SComponentEvent` + `SContainerEvent`, lazy thereafter

> **Accepted-limitation list superseded by SD_vaadin_first_binding.** SFocusEvent and SHierarchy{,Bounds}Event have since been ported and wired; addFocusListener / addHierarchyListener now fire. The core stance (port eagerly when a slice wires firing) still holds.

**Decision.** Eagerly port `java.awt.event.ComponentEvent` + `ComponentListener` + `ComponentAdapter` and `java.awt.event.ContainerEvent` + `ContainerListener` + `ContainerAdapter` to `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.awt.event.SComponentEvent` etc. — full types with the JDK's constant set, source retyped from `java.awt.Component` to `com.vaadin.flow.component.Component`. The remaining Component-sourced events (`FocusEvent`, `KeyEvent`, `MouseEvent`, `MouseWheelEvent`, `WindowEvent`, `HierarchyEvent`, `InputMethodEvent`) stay unported; their `add*Listener` methods are no-op stubs per [SD_dropin_stance](#SD_dropin_stance) until a slice needs them.

**Every ported event's constant surface is generated and delegated, never transcribed — this holds for the whole `S*Event` family, not just the two ported here.** A constant is written as `public static final int VK_A = java.awt.event.KeyEvent.VK_A;`, so no constant *value* appears anywhere in `:surrogates` source, and the block is emitted into a fenced do-not-hand-edit region by `DelegatedConstantsTest` reflecting over the JDK counterpart's public runtime API. Ten files are on it today (`SKeyEvent`, `SInputEvent`, `SComponentEvent`, `SContainerEvent`, `SFocusEvent`, `SHierarchyEvent`, `SMouseEvent`, `SWindowEvent`, `SAncestorEvent`, `SInternalFrameEvent`); **a new port adds a `Target` row and regenerates** rather than copying a table across. The reason is provenance, not tidiness: a table transcribed in the JDK's own declaration order and its own inconsistent hex casing is carried OpenJDK *expression* and pulls the file into `HDR_jdk_derived`, while a generated delegating table is arrangement of our own with the values staying AWT's by construction — `PROVENANCE.md` § "The exceptions" carries the full argument and `LicenseHeaderTest` is the other half of the gate. **Since [D_licence_lanes](../emulators/decisions.md#D_licence_lanes) this decides the module's licence, not just a header**: `:surrogates` is Apache-2.0 because it carries no JDK expression, and the boundary a consumer sees is the jar — so one transcribed table coming back would put GPL code inside a permissive artifact, and `LicenseHeaderTest` rules 2 and 6 fail on it from both sides. Constants are still the JDK's *set*: each target is verified to declare exactly its counterpart's, so emitting the counterpart's widens no public API, and a constant AWT adds arrives by regeneration. **The emulator side is deliberately the opposite** — `vaadinx.awt.event.KeyEvent` keeps its own transcribed table under [D_event_port_policy](../emulators/decisions.md#D_event_port_policy), where GPLv2+CE makes the derivation a non-issue; a sweep that "fixes" both layers has gone wrong.

Listener firing for the eagerly-ported events is **not yet wired**. Storage works (`addComponentListener` stores via `SComponentListenerSupport`), the types exist for callers to construct, but no Vaadin event currently translates into an `SComponentEvent` fire. Wiring lands when a downstream slice (resize handling, attach/detach surfacing) actually wants them.

**Rationale.**
- `ComponentEvent` is the supertype of the rest of the AWT event hierarchy (`FocusEvent extends ComponentEvent`, `KeyEvent extends InputEvent extends ComponentEvent`, …). Porting it eagerly establishes the foundation any later port will extend; doing it later means refactoring whichever event ported first.
- `ContainerEvent` extends `ComponentEvent` directly and is small (one constructor, one extra getter). Porting alongside is essentially free, and `ContainerMixin` is in scope, so the event-storage path is already there.
- The deferred-port stance for the rest is justified differently than `:emulators`'s `:emulators` D_event_port_policy wholesale-up-front: the mixin/event mechanism is **already validated** in `:emulators`. The judgment-call tax that D_event_port_policy was avoiding (rederiving "should we port this listener type yet?" every slice) doesn't apply here because the answer is mechanical — we port when a slice wires firing.
- Storing listeners now without firing is foresight that costs almost nothing (one `ComponentUtil.setData` per first-registration) and avoids retroactive call-site changes when wiring lands.

**Alternatives.**
- **Wholesale port up front** (D_event_port_policy-style for the whole AWT event family) — premature given that the mechanism is validated. Each ported event is dead code until firing wires up; eight ported event types waiting for consumers is more carrying cost than the foundation needs.
- **Defer `SComponentEvent` too** — possible but means the next slice that needs `SFocusEvent` has to port both `SFocusEvent` and its `SComponentEvent` supertype simultaneously. Pre-laying the supertype is cleaner.
- **Stub `addComponentListener` instead of porting `SComponentEvent`** — saves the port but means the mixin signature changes when wiring lands (`addComponentListener(SComponentListener)` is a different signature than the stub's). Mixin signatures are the part that's hard to change once consumed; lock the right one now.

**Accepted limitation.**
- `addFocusListener`, `addKeyListener`, `addMouseListener`, `addMouseMotionListener`, `addMouseWheelListener`, `addInputMethodListener`, `addHierarchyListener` stay as `onUnimplemented` stubs — registrations log WARN, listeners are not stored. When those event types port, the corresponding `add*Listener` methods upgrade from stub to real storage; user-side code doesn't change.
- `addComponentListener` / `addContainerListener` accept and store registrations but no event currently fires. Migrated code that expected resize / show / hide notifications doesn't get them. The WARN appears at the listener-registration site (intentionally, so the migrator sees the gap immediately) but not on each missed fire.
- `instanceof java.awt.event.ComponentEvent` returns `false` on `SComponentEvent`. User code walking the AWT class hierarchy via `instanceof` must rewrite those checks against `SComponentEvent` — same trade-off as `:emulators` D_event_port_policy.

---

<a id="SD_api_three_buckets"></a>
## SD_api_three_buckets. Three-bucket partition of mixin API: implement / `onNoop` / `onUnimplemented`

**Decision.** Every `ComponentMixin` / `ContainerMixin` method is sorted into one of three buckets, replacing the uniform `onUnimplemented` default:

1. **Bucket A — Implement.** Methods where Vaadin has a close-enough analog that's worth routing through. Today: `setSize`/`getSize`/`setBounds`/`getBounds`/`setPreferredSize`/`setMinimumSize`/`setMaximumSize` (all via `HasSize`); `setCursor`/`getCursor` (via CSS, see SD_size_cursor_from_vaadin); `isShowing` (via `_self().getUI().isPresent()`); Path 1 listener storage for Focus/Key/Hierarchy/HierarchyBounds (see SD_awt_listener_storage).
2. **Bucket B — Deliberate no-op.** Methods that are redundant by design in a Vaadin-rendered world *or* have no Vaadin counterpart and no upstream-blocked one waiting; calling them logs at DEBUG via `SHelper.onNoop(Object self, String method)` but does *not* emit to `warnHook`. Today: `repaint` (all overloads), `revalidate`, `invalidate`, `validate`, `doLayout`, `paint`, `update`, `paintAll`, `print`, `printAll`, `getGraphics`, `addNotify`, `removeNotify`, `isDisplayable`, `paintComponents` / `printComponents` on the container mixin, location (`setLocation` / `getLocation` / `getX` / `getY` — reclassified from C, see *Accepted limitation* below), and on `JComponentMixin` paint hints (`setAutoscrolls`, `setInheritsPopupMenu`, `setRequestFocusEnabled`, `setVerifyInputWhenFocusTarget`, `setDoubleBuffered`, `setDebugGraphicsOptions`, `setAlignmentX`, `setAlignmentY`) plus ghost JDK-dispatch hooks (`processKeyEvent`, `processComponentKeyEvent`, `processKeyBinding`, `processMouseEvent`, `processMouseMotionEvent`).
3. **Bucket C — Unimplemented stub.** Methods that would fire real events / perform real work we haven't wired; calling them logs WARN via `SHelper.onUnimplemented` and feeds `warnHook`. Today: mouse + input-method listeners (see SD_awt_listener_storage), programmatic `dispatchEvent`, focus-traversal + `hasFocus` / `isFocusOwner` / `setFocusable`, `setCursor` with a `CUSTOM_CURSOR`, and `setComponentPopupMenu` non-null (cross-cutting — graduates when the JPopupMenu surrogate lands).

**Rationale.**
- Uniform `onUnimplemented` hides the distinction between "we deliberately do nothing here because Vaadin already does it" (paint, validate, addNotify) and "we haven't wired this yet" (dispatchEvent, mouse listeners). The migrator watching WARN logs can't tell which WARNs are regressions and which are noise; lumping them together makes the WARN-inventory gates over in `:testapps/phase*` weaker.
- Bucket B's DEBUG-level logging gives the "hit it in a debugger if you suspect it's firing" affordance without flooding production logs. No varargs in `onNoop` keeps the hot path allocation-free — these methods get called repeatedly from Swing migration patterns (repaint-in-constructor, validate-in-loop).
- Bucket A's specific implementations collapse several Swing methods onto the same underlying Vaadin property: all three preferred/minimum/maximum families route to `HasSize`, because Vaadin has only one dimension per axis. "Last setter wins" — writing `setPreferredSize(Dimension(10,10))` then `setSize(100, 50)` leaves `getPreferredSize()` returning (100, 50). Weird against pure Swing semantics but it's the only axis we actually have, and it's more honest than storing three separate Dimensions the layout never consults.
- Location was originally Bucket C on the rationale that "we don't pixel-position, the WARN is honest." It was reclassified to Bucket B: per CLAUDE.md R_layouts_close_enough the project explicitly defers pixel-accurate layout to a future separate library — there's no upstream-blocked counterpart we're waiting on, so the WARN was structurally indistinguishable from "we forgot to implement this." Same logic applies to the `JComponentMixin` paint-hint setters (`setAutoscrolls` / `setInheritsPopupMenu` / `setRequestFocusEnabled` / `setVerifyInputWhenFocusTarget` / `setDoubleBuffered` / `setDebugGraphicsOptions` / `setAlignmentX` / `setAlignmentY`) and the ghost JDK-dispatch overloads (`processKeyEvent` family, `processMouseEvent` family) folded in by the same reclassification.
- Mirrors `:emulators`' own partition: `vaadinx.EHelper.onNoop` already exists (paint / validate / addNotify demoted to DEBUG), and our bucketing here broadly aligns with what `vaadinx.awt.Component.java` does today. `:surrogates` goes a bit further on size because the mixin requires `HasSize`, which `:emulators`' base class doesn't.

**Alternatives.**
- **Keep everything as `onUnimplemented`** — uniform but loses the signal. Migrators see WARN for paint-every-frame calls alongside WARN for real gaps; neither stands out.
- **Promote bucket B to silent no-op** (no log at all) — gives up the ability to trace a suspicious "did this call reach us?" in a debugger. Cheap at DEBUG level, zero-cost at INFO+.
- **Push preferred/min/max into Bucket B** instead of routing through `HasSize` — works (Swing's "null means not set" convention is honoured if the getter returns null), but migrated code that `setPreferredSize(Dimension(100, 50))` and later reads `getPreferredSize().width` stops getting its own value back. The HasSize route preserves that round-trip property.
- **Store preferred/min/max separately from actual** — matches Swing's three-slot semantics but layout doesn't consult them, so the extra storage is dead weight. "Last setter wins on the one dimension Vaadin has" is the pragmatic map.

**Accepted limitation.**
- `setPreferredSize(a)` followed by `setSize(b)` both leave `getPreferredSize()` returning `b`. Migrated code that relied on preferred-size-as-layout-hint-separate-from-actual-size won't observe the distinction.
- `parsePxOr1` returns `1` (not `0`) for non-pixel sizes ({@code "100%"}, {@code "10em"}) and for unset dimensions. Migrated code that reads {@code getWidth()} as literal pixel count gets a slight lie when the underlying Vaadin size is a percent — but the alternative (returning 0) would trip common {@code if (comp.getWidth() > 0)} guards and break more code than the approximation. See `SHelper.parsePxOr1`'s Javadoc for the full rationale.
- Bucket B methods don't emit to `warnHook`, so testapp-level WARN-inventory assertions remain clean. The gate's meaning sharpens: it now asserts "no *regression* to a stubbed call," not "no logs of any kind."

---

<a id="SD_size_cursor_from_vaadin"></a>
## SD_size_cursor_from_vaadin. Size and cursor source of truth is Vaadin state, not a server-side cache

> **Contrast with `AppearanceStore` is historical** — SD_vaadin_first_binding later extended the no-cache stance to appearance too, accepting the lossy round-trip that SD_size_cursor_from_vaadin's rationale had used as a reason *not* to drop the cache. The core decision (size + cursor parse-on-read) still stands.

**Decision.** `getSize()` / `getBounds()` parse `HasSize#getWidth()` / `HasSize#getHeight()` on every read via `SHelper.parsePxOr1(String)`. `getCursor()` reads the element's CSS `cursor` property and reconstructs the AWT `Cursor` via `SHelper.cursorFromCss(String)`. Neither uses a server-side store analogous to `AppearanceStore`'s round-trip cache for colors/fonts.

**Rationale.**
- HasSize accepts arbitrary CSS strings (`"100px"`, `"100%"`, `"calc(50% - 10px)"`). A migrated app that does `comp.setWidth("100%")` via the Vaadin API, then reads `comp.getSize()` via the Swing API, should see an integer derived from *actual* current state — not a stale {@code Dimension} we cached the last time someone called {@code setSize(int, int)}. The DOM style (or Vaadin's mirror of it) is the source of truth.
- For cursor specifically, there are only 14 predefined AWT cursors (+ CUSTOM_CURSOR we can't support), and CSS keyword → Cursor is a perfect inverse of Cursor → CSS keyword. So `cursorFromCss` gives us a lossless round-trip without any storage. Anything unknown → `Cursor.getDefaultCursor()`, which is non-null per Swing convention.
- The color/font analogy doesn't apply: `Color(r, g, b, alpha)` → `rgba(r, g, b, α)` loses precision on alpha (string formatting drops bits), and `Font` → CSS drops metrics that don't survive the trip. Caching avoids those losses. Cursor and pixel-size are discrete values with exact CSS representations; no precision loss to defend against.
- One less state holder to maintain, one less `ComponentUtil.setData` cost, one less place that can drift out of sync with the DOM. Cheaper by every axis.

**Alternatives.**
- **Cache `Cursor` and `Dimension` server-side** (mirror `AppearanceStore`) — adds a holder per mixin for values we can reconstruct exactly from the DOM. Dead weight.
- **Only-set-via-Swing, ignore-Vaadin-side-writes** — breaks round-trip when migrated code uses both APIs (common during incremental migration). The mixin's whole premise is that Vaadin-side writes are legitimate.
- **Parse lazily but cache-on-read** — first read parses, subsequent reads return the cached value. Wrong: Vaadin-side writes between reads would return stale data. Parsing is cheap enough that the optimisation isn't worth the staleness risk.

**Accepted limitation.** `getSize()` returns `(1, 1)` for any non-pixel CSS size — a small lie compared to the real layout, but documented and preferable to `(0, 0)` which would trip migration-code visibility guards (see SD_api_three_buckets's accepted limitations). `CUSTOM_CURSOR` (image-backed cursor) isn't representable as a CSS keyword; `setCursor` on a custom cursor logs `onUnimplemented` and does not write CSS — `getCursor` then reads back `Cursor.getDefaultCursor()` since the element's `cursor` property stays untouched.

---

<a id="SD_vaadin_first_binding"></a>
## SD_vaadin_first_binding. Vaadin-first direct-binding stance (supersedes the store-only parts of SD_event_port_stance/SD_size_cursor_from_vaadin/SD_awt_listener_storage)

**Decision.** Every Swing method on a mixin routes directly to its Vaadin counterpart when one exists; where Vaadin has no direct analog we warn + noop rather than carrying a server-side shadow cache. Specifically:

- Appearance (`setForeground` / `setBackground` / `setFont`): write CSS, reconstruct by re-parsing CSS on every getter call. No `AppearanceStore`. Lossy round-trip accepted (alpha precision to one bit via string formatting; font weight/style collapse to plain/bold and italic/normal; non-px font sizes fall back to 12).
- Tooltip (`setToolTipText` / `getToolTipText`): delegate to `HasTooltip` when the peer implements it; warn + null getter otherwise. No `ToolTipTextStore`. JComponent's `"ToolTipText"` PCE fire stays (user-visible contract), but it reads the old value through `HasTooltip` at fire time.
- Border (`setBorder` / `getBorder`): dispatch by `instanceof` to `BorderCss.applyBorderCss` which writes CSS; `BorderCss.borderFromCss` reconstructs the closest JDK `Border` by reading CSS back. Lossy per SD_border_css_lossy. No `BorderStore`.
- InputVerifier: warn on set, no store, null getter. Vaadin has no direct analog and the JDK signature demands `JComponent` we aren't.
- Listener families: `addXListener` wires a Vaadin subscription eagerly when a peer analog exists (`FocusNotifier`/`BlurNotifier` for focus; attach/detach for SComponent + SHierarchy; nothing for KeyListener — SKeyEvent port deferred — and nothing for Mouse/InputMethod). The unified `Registrations` registry maps Swing listener → Vaadin `Registration` so `removeXListener` teardown is one-map-lookup, and `getXListeners()` filters registry keys by type. Supersedes the six per-family `*Support` holders.
- Default locale statics: `JComponentMixin.getDefaultLocale()` / `setDefaultLocale` read/write `VaadinSession.getCurrent().getLocale()`; NPE when no session. Matches Swing's "default locale is addressable" expectation but honest about where it lives in the web model (per-session, not per-classloader AppContext).

Stores survive only for concepts Vaadin genuinely doesn't have: `PceSupport` (JDK PropertyChangeSupport — no Vaadin analog), `NameStore` (Swing `name` ≠ Vaadin `id`), `LayoutStore` (no Swing-LayoutManager analog, kept inert for future CSS dispatch), `ClientPropertyStore`, `InputMapStore` (lazy-alloc JDK types; browser-side shortcut wiring still deferred). (`OpaqueStore` was added here originally and dropped subsequently — opaque state lives in CSS `background-color` directly with lossy R_vaadin_first readback.)

**Rationale.**
- The two-stance design (store-then-fire) asked for carrying-state-until-we-wire-it infrastructure that produced silent failures: listeners registered but never fired, tooltips round-tripping via a store that drifted when Vaadin-side writes happened. The Vaadin-first stance makes failures *loud* (WARN on no-analog, real firing on analog) and deletes a whole class of drift bugs.
- One stance across the module is cheaper to reason about than two. Mixing "here we cache, here we don't" produced stance-boundary bugs — a migrated app that used both Swing and Vaadin APIs on the same surrogate would see different round-trip semantics per call.
- The extra parse-CSS-on-read cost is small (2–5 string ops per read) and the path is cold — getters aren't on any hot loop, and the correctness win is absolute: Vaadin is the authoritative store, period.
- Mouse + input-method listeners stay WARN for the same reason SD_awt_listener_storage had them in Bucket C: Vaadin doesn't surface them uniformly, and a silent store would lie about the future.

**Alternatives.**
- **Keep the store-then-fire model.** Already evaluated — produced listener-registered-but-never-fires silent failures, and required a separate Path 2 slice we kept deferring. Switching now, while little depends on it yet, is cheaper than switching after a dozen surrogates have shipped.
- **Partial adoption — rewrite JComponent only.** Leaves ComponentMixin on the old stance; migrators would see different round-trip semantics when reading Swing state on a ContainerMixin vs a JComponentMixin surrogate. Worse than two modules.
- **Cache with cache-invalidation on Vaadin-side writes.** Possible with `ElementPropertyChangeListener` but adds a per-holder wiring layer. Not worth it for gains that are one-bit-of-alpha-precision and one-font-weight-quantisation small.

**Accepted limitation.**
- Alpha round-trips to within one bit; font weight and style collapse; custom JComponent `Border` subclasses that JDK-land paintBorder against a `Graphics` don't render. Documented in SD_border_css_lossy.
- `getComponentListeners()` etc. iterate the `Registrations` map and filter by type — O(n) in total listeners, not O(n) in per-family listeners. Negligible for the counts Swing components see in practice.
- Adding the same listener instance twice collapses to a single entry (the second `add` replaces the first registration). Swing's contract allows double-fire; we diverge by squashing. Revisit if a migration target relies on it.
- `JComponent.getUI()` is excluded from the mixin because its return type clashes with Vaadin's `Component.getUI()` (`ComponentUI` vs `Optional<UI>`). Same signature-clash rule as the hierarchy methods in SD_dropin_stance: Vaadin-first wins.

---

<a id="SD_border_css_lossy"></a>
## SD_border_css_lossy. Border CSS round-trip is lossy

**Decision.** `JComponentMixin.setBorder` dispatches the installed `javax.swing.border.Border` instance to CSS via `BorderCss.applyBorderCss`; `JComponentMixin.getBorder` reconstructs the closest `Border` from current CSS via `BorderCss.borderFromCss`. The round-trip is lossy:

- `LineBorder` / `EmptyBorder` / `MatteBorder` (color-only) / `BevelBorder` (LOWERED/RAISED mapped to CSS `inset`/`outset`) / `EtchedBorder` (LOWERED/RAISED mapped to CSS `groove`/`ridge`) / `CompoundBorder(outer, EmptyBorder)` all round-trip to a *fresh instance* that `.equals()` the original (for border types that implement `equals` by fields — LineBorder, EmptyBorder, MatteBorder). Identity is lost.
- `SoftBevelBorder` renders like `BevelBorder` (no CSS `soft-*-bevel` analog); reconstructs as the non-soft form.
- `CompoundBorder` with a non-`EmptyBorder` inner, `TitledBorder`, icon-backed `MatteBorder`, and custom user `Border` subclasses log `onUnimplemented` and render nothing.
- `setBorder(null)` clears the full set of border-related CSS keys (`border*`, `padding*`, `border-radius`) — the complete list is tracked in `SHelper.BORDER_CSS_KEYS` so a new border writer can't silently leak state across a `setBorder` swap.

**Rationale.**
- The Vaadin-first stance (SD_vaadin_first_binding) says: Vaadin owns the state. For borders, Vaadin's analog is CSS; reconstructing on read keeps user code that writes CSS directly in sync.
- TitledBorder specifically needs pseudo-element rendering (`::before` for the title text) which isn't addressable via inline `Style` — we'd need to inject a global stylesheet + wrap the element in a structural change. That's out of scope for a mixin; WARN is the honest answer.
- CompoundBorder(outer, EmptyBorder inner) is the common Swing idiom ("give the component a line border with padding") and maps cleanly to CSS `border` + `padding`. Other compound nestings don't — CSS has only one border layer plus `outline`, and faking a second visible border via `outline` misrepresents the visual.
- Accepting identity loss (fresh instance per `getBorder()` call) is the price of using CSS as the authoritative store. Code that does `if (border == installedBorder)` breaks; code that does `.equals()` stays correct.

**Alternatives.**
- **Cache the Border server-side.** Was the original plan (`BorderStore`). Dropped per SD_vaadin_first_binding because shadow caches drift when external CSS writes happen.
- **Render TitledBorder via a global stylesheet.** Possible but requires session-scoped CSS injection; the surrogate would need to know when it's being used as a peer vs a standalone Vaadin component. Defer.
- **Render CompoundBorder with `outline` for the outer ring.** `outline` doesn't participate in the box model (no inset/spacing with `border`), and browsers paint it outside the element — visually wrong for nested borders.

**Accepted limitation.**
- Identity comparisons on `getBorder()` results break. `.equals()` stays correct for the stock hierarchy.
- TitledBorder: WARN + no CSS written. Migrated apps lose the title rendering entirely.
- Custom user Borders extending `AbstractBorder` with overridden `paintBorder`: WARN + no CSS written. Same as emulators' current stance for custom borders under D_emulator_surrogate_split.
- Alpha precision in border colors is one bit under `rgba()` serialisation, same as SD_vaadin_first_binding's color-round-trip lossiness.
- CompoundBorder nested more than two levels collapses on read to the flat outer+padding shape.

---

<a id="SD_awt_listener_storage"></a>
## SD_awt_listener_storage. AWT listener storage Path 1: focus/key/hierarchy stored, mouse + input-method stay WARN

Superseded by [SD_vaadin_first_binding](#SD_vaadin_first_binding) — store-only Path 1 replaced by Vaadin-first direct binding: families with a Vaadin analog wire eagerly, families without warn + noop (SD_listeners_without_analog).

---

<a id="SD_listeners_without_analog"></a>
## SD_listeners_without_analog. Listener families without a Vaadin analog: Bucket B `onNoop`, not silent-register

**Decision.** `ContainerMixin.addContainerListener` / `removeContainerListener` / `getContainerListeners`, and `ComponentMixin.addHierarchyBoundsListener` / `removeHierarchyBoundsListener` / `getHierarchyBoundsListeners`, all route to `SHelper.onNoop` — no storage, no round-trip. `getXListeners()` returns an empty array. Neither family has a Vaadin-25 analog that can fire; "register silently but never fire" is the silent-failure pattern SD_vaadin_first_binding exists to replace, and we don't reintroduce it at the mixin level.

**Rationale.**
- Vaadin 25 has no server-side child-list mutation event. Vaadin 8's `ComponentContainer.addComponentAttachListener()` was removed in the Vaadin 10 / Flow rewrite and never reinstated — a generic mixin can't observe its own children being added or removed. Per-surrogate overrides of `HasComponents.add` / `remove` can fire the event for a specific container surrogate (future work under D_emulator_surrogate_split), but that wiring is scope-bound per surrogate, not something the mixin can do.
- Ancestor pixel moves/resizes aren't surfaced server-side at all, and R_layouts_close_enough's close-enough layout stance means we don't track pixel positions either way — a `SHierarchyBoundsListener` has no event to fire on regardless of effort. "Redundant by design in a Vaadin-rendered world" places it squarely in Bucket B (SD_api_three_buckets), same category as `repaint` / `revalidate`.
- `SHelper.onNoop` is DEBUG-level and doesn't feed `warnHook`, so testapp-level WARN-inventory gates stay clean. The DEBUG breadcrumb is available for anyone who wants to trace "did this call reach us?" without polluting production logs.
- `getContainerListeners()` / `getHierarchyBoundsListeners()` returning empty diverges from Swing's contract (Swing would return the previously-added listeners). This is the honest Bucket B answer: migration code that inspects its own listener array sees "no container events here" which matches reality — the listener does not fire, so it may as well not exist. Common fire-and-forget registration patterns are unaffected.

**Alternatives.**
- **Register via `Registrations` with a no-op `Registration`.** Preserves the `getXListeners()` round-trip but recreates the exact silent-failure pattern SD_vaadin_first_binding was written to eliminate — `addListener` succeeds, listener is stored, nothing ever fires, migrator has no signal. Rejected. This was the earlier stance; SD_listeners_without_analog refines it.
- **Formalize a `Registrations.addNoop(listener)` helper.** Names the pattern but still carries the silent failure. Rejected for the same reason; no in-tree caller remains, and adding API surface without a justified caller is the opposite of what SD_vaadin_first_binding was for.
- **Bucket C WARN** (match `addMouseListener` / `addKeyListener`). Loud, but overloaded: Bucket C means "we'd need to build this"; here we *can't* build it generically under the current Vaadin model. Bucket B is the more precise signal.

**Accepted limitation.** Migration code that walks `getContainerListeners()` / `getHierarchyBoundsListeners()` to inspect its own subscriptions sees an empty array instead of the registered listener. The common case (fire-and-forget registration) is unaffected. When a concrete container surrogate wires firing (by overriding `add` / `remove`), the per-surrogate implementation supplies its own storage and these mixin defaults are overridden out of the path.

---

<a id="SD_top_level_ancestor"></a>
## SD_top_level_ancestor. `getTopLevelAncestor()` returns the first Vaadin `Dialog` ancestor

**Decision.** `JComponentMixin.getTopLevelAncestor()` walks `_self().getParent()` upward and returns the first `com.vaadin.flow.component.dialog.Dialog` ancestor, or `null` if none is found. Vaadin's `UI` is deliberately *not* treated as top-level. No WARN on either path. `JComponentMixin.getRootPane()` originally stayed WARN — no `JRootPane` surrogate type existed at the layer — but graduated to a parallel walk once SJRootPane landed (SD_sjframe); the mixin's return type changed from `javax.swing.JRootPane` to `SJRootPane` to surface the surrogate-layer type, same surrogate-vs-JDK divergence stance this entry takes for `getTopLevelAncestor`.

**Rationale.**
- Swing's `JComponent.getTopLevelAncestor()` returns a `Window` (or `Applet`), else `null`. Vaadin's closest analog is `Dialog`: it owns a modal/popup frame the rest of the UI can sit inside. Picking `Dialog` as the stop condition preserves the "null when no enclosing window" half of Swing's contract — a surrogate used at route-level (no Dialog wrapping) returns `null`, matching a detached JPanel in Swing.
- The walk handles the `:emulators` handoff transparently without cross-module awareness: an emulator `JFrame` has a `Dialog` peer ([D_peer_ctor_injection](../emulators/decisions.md#D_peer_ctor_injection)), so a surrogate living inside an emulator `JFrame` walks up through intermediate emulator peers (e.g. a `JPanel`'s `Div`) and lands on the Dialog. The surrogate doesn't know that Dialog came from `:emulators` — it just sees a Vaadin Dialog in the chain, which is exactly the right answer.
- Treating `UI` as top-level was the obvious alternative and was rejected because every attached Vaadin component has a UI ancestor — `getTopLevelAncestor()` would then return non-null for any attached surrogate, including orphan route-level ones where Swing would return `null`. The Vaadin-first stance (SD_vaadin_first_binding) says route through Vaadin analogs, but only when the analog actually matches the Swing semantics; `UI` doesn't.
- The migration scenario motivating this: an incremental migration first replaces `javax.swing.JComponent` with emulators, then starts replacing emulator peers with surrogates, then finally drops the emulator layer entirely — at which point surviving user code calls `surrogate.getTopLevelAncestor()` directly. The walk supports that end-state without requiring `:emulators` to stay in the picture.

**Alternatives.**
- **Keep WARN.** Safe default until a caller materialises, but the walk is cheap (a handful of map reads per call, no state) and supporting it now avoids one more thing to revisit during the emulator-to-surrogate handoff.
- **Return farthest `ContainerMixin` ancestor.** User's initial suggestion. Rejected because `ContainerMixin` is a Vaadin-mixin concept, not a Swing Window analog — two nested SJPanels would make the outer SJPanel look top-level, diverging from Swing's "Window only" contract. Also misses the emulator handoff: emulator `JPanel`'s `Div` peer is not a `ContainerMixin`, so the walk skips every emulator peer and lands on UI or nothing.
- **Return `UI.getCurrent()` when attached.** Matches Vaadin's notion of "top of the tree" but makes every attached surrogate non-null, including orphans Swing would call null. Divergence for no gain.
- **Also implement `getRootPane()`.** Was outside this slice's scope when SD_top_level_ancestor landed; later closed by SD_sjframe (which introduced SJRootPane) plus a follow-up that changed the mixin signature to return SJRootPane and added the parent-walk.

**Accepted limitation.**
- Nested Dialogs: the walk returns the innermost Dialog ancestor, not the outermost. Swing's `JComponent.getTopLevelAncestor()` returns the innermost `Window` too, so this matches — recorded here because it's the one place "first ancestor" vs "farthest ancestor" differs.
- Non-Dialog popups (Vaadin `Notification`, `Popover`, etc.) don't count as top-level. A surrogate inside a Notification walks past it and returns `null`. These aren't Swing Window analogs — Swing's `JPopupMenu` also doesn't register as top-level for `getTopLevelAncestor` — so the behaviour is consistent.
- `getRootPane()` initially WARNed pending an SJRootPane port. Closed in two steps: SD_sjframe introduced SJRootPane; a follow-up wired the walk and changed the mixin signature to return SJRootPane.

---

<a id="SD_sjslider"></a>
## SD_sjslider. `SJSlider` surrogate — fat surrogate, thin emulator; CSS-rendering for orientation/inverted; server-side snapToTicks; ticks/labels/track deferred upstream

**Decision.** First concrete surrogate. `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJSlider extends com.vaadin.flow.component.slider.Slider implements JComponentMixin` owns the full Swing-flavoured surface — `BoundedRangeModel` source-of-truth, `ChangeListener` fan-out with `source=SJSlider`, R_swing_is_truth preventPeerEvents guard, R_callswing_envelope `SHelper.callSwing` peer→model mirror — and `:emulators.JSlider` becomes a thin delegating shell. The three-way split with `vaadin-slider`'s current public surface:

- **Implemented on SJSlider.** `setValue(int)`, `setMinimum` / `setMaximum` / `setExtent` / `setValueIsAdjusting`, `setModel`, `addChangeListener` family (emitting `source=SJSlider`), `setOrientation` (VERTICAL via CSS `transform: rotate(-90deg)` on the host; HORIZONTAL clears the rule), `setInverted` (CSS `scaleX(-1)`; composes with VERTICAL into one `transform` rule), `setSnapToTicks` (pure server-side rounding in `syncValueFromPeer`; minor wins over major per JDK contract; grid anchored at `minimum`), `majorTickSpacing` / `minorTickSpacing` field-store (non-visual — drives snap logic and BeanInfo introspection, no WARN), `getUIClassID()` returns `"SliderUI"`. The full `JComponentMixin` surface (border/opaque/client properties/tooltip PCE/etc.) is inherited for free.
- **Deferred on SJSlider behind** [vaadin/flow-components #9181](https://github.com/vaadin/flow-components/issues/9181). `setPaintTicks(true)`, `setPaintLabels(true)`, `setPaintTrack(false)`, `setLabelTable(non-null)`, `createStandardLabels`. All five need addressable CSS parts or slots on `vaadin-slider` that the component doesn't expose today; field-stored + PCE fires regardless so BeanInfo-style probes round-trip, and a WARN fires on the visual-effect direction (truthy paint flags, non-null labelTable) to surface the gap to migrators.
- **Signature-clash carve-out.** `getValue()` on Vaadin `Slider` returns `Double`; JDK `JSlider.getValue()` returns `int`. Java can't resolve the return-type conflict so SJSlider **does not** expose `int getValue()` — users route through `getModel().getValue()`. `setValue(int)` coexists with `Slider.setValue(Double)` as an overload (different parameter type), so `ssurrogate.setValue(42)` takes the Swing path. The signature-clash rule (SD_vaadin_first_binding's tail) extends to this case: Vaadin-first wins.

`:emulators.JSlider` delegates everything through `(SJSlider) peer`, maintains its own `listenerList`, and installs a single peer-ChangeListener bridge that re-fires `ChangeEvent` with `source=this` (the emulator) — migrated code casting `(JSlider) e.getSource()` still sees the emulator, not the underlying SJSlider. `:emulators.JSlider` also keeps its own `changeSupport` for `firePropertyChange` on the Swing-shaped setters, so users who registered `PropertyChangeListener` via the emulator hear the events (emulator and surrogate keep independent PCE listener lists by design — no cross-fire). The thin-down drops ~330 LoC of plumbing from the emulator to the surrogate.

**Rationale.**
- **Fat surrogate, thin emulator** matches architecture.md's framing that "a surrogate used as a plain Vaadin component (without `:emulators` on top) renders and behaves like a richer Vaadin component." Users who arrive via the Vaadin side (`new SJSlider(0, 100, 50).addChangeListener(…)`) get the full Swing-style behaviour without touching `vaadinx.*`. The alternative (thin surrogate, emulator calls instanceof-gated setters) would have left SJSlider a half-animal — extends Slider but doesn't materially enrich it.
- **CSS transform for orientation/inverted**: browser-level rendering primitive, zero state carried, zero feature-flag dependence. Drag coordinates still map correctly post-transform because the browser applies the transform after event routing — user drags right in VERTICAL mode, the shadow-DOM thumb moves up in its pre-rotation coordinate system, value increases. Same for `scaleX(-1)` — user drags thumb leftward under inversion, browser sees rightward, value increases, which IS Swing's "drag toward MAX end" contract regardless of which end MAX sits on.
- **Server-side snapToTicks**: pure logic, no rendering path, no Vaadin changes needed. JDK's `JSlider` snaps values on user input; we do the same in `syncValueFromPeer` before the model ever sees the value. No bidirectional guard needed since the snap runs on browser-originated writes only; programmatic `setValue(int)` writes the exact int through the model.
- **Ticks / labels / track hiding all share a blocker**: `vaadin-slider`'s shadow DOM doesn't expose the track as a named part, doesn't slot tick or label overlay elements, and has no CSS custom property for "hide track." Filing the upstream request once covers all three — the root cause is the same, so three separate workarounds would each carry the same risk of breaking when upstream eventually lands a cleaner API. Field-round-trip + PCE still fires so BeanInfo-style code doesn't regress; the WARN surfaces the gap. If upstream adds the API before we need it, flipping the stubs to real rendering is a mechanical swap.
- **Emulator re-fires PCE + ChangeEvent locally** (rather than exposing the surrogate's listeners directly) because the emulator's listener list was already populated by migration code registering against `JSlider`. Repointing those listeners at the surrogate would change the `source` on the event they see (surrogate vs. emulator) and break migrated casts. Re-sourcing at the emulator boundary keeps the existing contract intact.

**Alternatives.**
- **Thin surrogate, fat emulator** (option (b) in the pre-implementation sketch): SJSlider only adds rendering knobs; emulator keeps BoundedRangeModel plumbing and calls instanceof-gated setters. Rejected — the "richer Vaadin component when used standalone" story falls apart, `:emulators → :surrogates` direction stays one-way but the dependency becomes trivial, and the division of labour doesn't match the architecture doc's stated handoff framing.
- **Implement tick / label rendering ourselves via session-scoped stylesheet injection**: possible but the stylesheet-injection mechanism is the same deferral blocking `TitledBorder` (SD_border_css_lossy), so it's a bigger slice that should land once and serve both callers. Not worth gating SD_sjslider on it.
- **Override `Slider.setValue(Double)` to round-trip through the model** (instead of just overloading with `setValue(int)`): would unify Vaadin-API and Swing-API value writes but also rounds pure-Vaadin-API Double writes to the nearest int, which surprises users who arrive via Vaadin and expect fractional values to stick. The peer-listener path already handles the round-trip — no benefit.
- **Emit WARN via `vaadinx.EHelper.warnHook`** (shared hook with `:emulators`): surrogates can't reach across the module boundary (SD_shelper_statics); each module's `warnHook` stays module-scoped. Consequence: the `:emulators` test that swallows `vaadinx.EHelper.warnHook` doesn't affect SJSlider's WARNs — but that's actually a feature (warnings are attributable to the module that emitted them). Migrators collecting all WARNs need to subscribe to both hooks.

**Accepted limitation.**
- `getValue()` returns `Double` on SJSlider (Vaadin's getter wins per SD_vaadin_first_binding signature-clash). Callers needing an int route via `getModel().getValue()`. The emulator's `JSlider.getValue()` still returns `int`; migrated code reaching the surrogate through the emulator is unaffected.
- Non-integer peer-originated values (browser drag fired with `47.7`) round to the nearest int before reaching the model. Migration from pure-Vaadin-`Slider`-with-fractional-values to SJSlider silently quantises — consistent with JDK `JSlider`'s integer-valued contract and documented in the class javadoc.
- `setOrientation(VERTICAL)` rotates the host via CSS but doesn't re-flow surrounding layout. In a flex/grid container the rotated Slider still claims its pre-rotation height along the flow axis; apps that expect layout reflow after orientation change may need a manual height swap. R_layouts_close_enough close-enough.
- The SJSlider's `ChangeListener` list and the emulator `JSlider`'s `ChangeListener` list are independent. A single `ChangeListener` added to both fires twice. Expected for a two-layer composition; migrators typically add to one layer only.
- `setPaintTicks` / `setPaintLabels` / `setPaintTrack(false)` / `setLabelTable(non-null)` / `createStandardLabels` stay WARN until upstream lands the part/slot API. Fields still round-trip + PCE still fires so inspection code is unaffected, but nothing renders.
- `setLabelTable` stores whatever `Dictionary` the caller passes — no validation that keys are `Integer` or values are `JComponent`. JDK doesn't validate either (raw `Dictionary` signature); match that stance until the deferred rendering lands and a stricter contract earns its keep.
- The emulator `JSlider` still has `int getValue()` at its own layer. Pure-surrogate users who need an int see the SD_vaadin_first_binding-clash and route through the model; pure-emulator users see the familiar int getter — the split is acceptable because the two call sites have different type contexts anyway.

---

<a id="SD_auto_pce"></a>
## SD_auto_pce. Auto-PCE on `ComponentMixin`'s mapped setters (closes the SD_dropin_stance deferral)

**Decision.** `ComponentMixin.setName` / `setEnabled` / `setForeground` / `setBackground` / `setFont` all fire `PropertyChangeEvent` automatically with the JDK property names `"name"` / `"enabled"` / `"foreground"` / `"background"` / `"font"`. Each setter reads the old value via its own getter, skips if `Objects.equals(old, new)`, performs the Vaadin write, and then fires via `firePropertyChange`. `setEnabled` additionally delegates to `HasEnabled.super.setEnabled` so the peer's element enablement is still written.

Mapped setters on `JComponentMixin` already fire — `setOpaque` ("opaque"), `setBorder` ("border"), `setToolTipText` ("ToolTipText"), `putClientProperty` (key-named). This entry closes the corresponding gap on the AWT half of the mixin stack; after SD_auto_pce the rule across the module is "every mapped setter fires its JDK-named PCE."

**Rationale.**
- Swing's `java.awt.Component` contract fires these events; migrated code that registers `PropertyChangeListener` for any of the five names (`if ("enabled".equals(evt.getPropertyName()))` etc.) expected them to arrive and would silently misbehave without this slice. Closing the gap is a direct behavioural-compatibility win — no new infrastructure required, just wiring through the already-existing `PceSupport`.
- Reading the old value through the getter (rather than through a shadow cache) keeps us on the Vaadin-first stance (SD_vaadin_first_binding): the DOM is the source of truth for color / font / enabled, and the PCE old-value reflects what `getX()` would have returned *before* the write. The listener sees exactly the before/after a reader of the surrogate would have observed.
- Matching JDK's pattern of "equality check before the write" (not just relying on `PropertyChangeSupport`'s internal equality skip) avoids a no-op round-trip through Vaadin on identical sets. Slightly cheaper; more importantly, it matches the surrounding `:surrogates` code style (`SJSlider.setOrientation`, `setInverted`, etc. all skip-on-equal before touching state).
- Scope matches the SD_dropin_stance deferral exactly: the five `Component`-level properties. `setLocale`, `setCursor`, `setFocusable`, `setVisible` are deliberately excluded — `setLocale` has no AWT-parent walk in our model, `setCursor` has no corresponding JDK PCE contract, `setFocusable` / `setVisible` aren't in scope (either WARN'd or Vaadin-owned).

**Alternatives.**
- **Ship a PCE-old-value shadow cache.** Would give pristine equality checks even across CSS lossy round-trips (so `setForeground(c)` twice with a partial-alpha `c` would reliably skip the second fire). Rejected because it re-introduces the two-stores drift problem SD_vaadin_first_binding was written to eliminate — the cache would go stale whenever user code writes CSS directly through `getElement().getStyle().set(...)`. The lossy-roundtrip edge case is small (common cases with integer-alpha colors and generic font families round-trip cleanly) and the PCE it fires is honest — a listener reading `getForeground()` before and after would see the same "change" the PCE reports.
- **Leave the deferral open.** Low-maintenance, but every migrated Swing app that registered a PCE listener on any of these properties silently misbehaves; SD_dropin_stance's note explicitly says this was a simplification, not a stance.
- **Fold `setVisible` into the same slice.** JDK's `Component.setVisible` doesn't fire a `"visible"` PCE in practice (it fires `ComponentEvent` via the hierarchy instead); `:emulators` fires one anyway for introspection convenience, but the surrogate `setVisible` is Vaadin-owned and out of the mixin's reach. Scope-bound to a future slice if a migration target needs it.
- **Constants for property names.** Matched JDK's inlined-string convention (no public `ENABLED_KEY` on `Component`); introducing them here would diverge from JDK and add unused surface. Kept inline like `"opaque"` / `"border"` in `JComponentMixin`.

**Accepted limitation.**
- Lossy-roundtrip re-set. `setForeground(Color(r,g,b,alpha))` followed by the same call may fire PCE twice if the reconstructed `getForeground()` differs by ≤1 bit of alpha (see SD_vaadin_first_binding's color round-trip). Same risk for `setFont` with non-bold-or-normal weights or non-px sizes. Callers doing precise no-op avoidance on these must pre-check via `getForeground() / getFont()` themselves.
- Old-value for `setEnabled` is read through `isEnabled()`, which in Vaadin is the *effective* enabled (disabled ancestor masks the local flag). Swing tracks enabled as a local field. A surrogate nested under an already-disabled ancestor that calls `setEnabled(false)` fires `(false, false)` as the equality check sees no change — JDK's `Component.setEnabled(false)` in the same situation would still fire `(true, false)` because the local flag was `true`. R_best_effort_behaviour close-enough: the observable effective state hasn't changed, which is what listeners most often care about.
- `SJSlider` already fires its own PCEs for `"minimum"` / `"maximum"` / `"orientation"` / `"inverted"` / `"model"` / `"majorTickSpacing"` / `"minorTickSpacing"` / `"snapToTicks"` / `"paintTicks"` / `"paintTrack"` / `"paintLabels"` / `"labelTable"`; those keep their existing names (slider-specific) and are unaffected by SD_auto_pce. The Component-level `"enabled"` / `"foreground"` / ... now fire *in addition* when the corresponding setter is called on an SJSlider — no double-fire because each property has one setter path.
- `:emulators` Component fires these same five PCEs from its own layer (shadow-field storage) independent of the surrogate peer. When an emulator wraps an SJSlider, both layers have independent listener lists — a listener added on the emulator side receives the emulator-level PCE; a listener added on the surrogate side receives the surrogate-level one. No cross-fire, no double-fire on the same listener list. Expected behaviour for a two-layer composition.

---

<a id="SD_sjspinner"></a>
## SD_sjspinner. `SJSpinner` surrogate — CustomField composite with swappable inner peer; `LongField` port to close the Long precision gap

**Decision.** Second concrete surrogate. `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJSpinner extends com.vaadin.flow.component.customfield.CustomField<Object> implements JComponentMixin` carries the full JSpinner-shape API surface and picks its inner Vaadin field dynamically from the installed `SpinnerModel`'s value type:

| Installed `SpinnerModel` shape                                              | Inner field                 |
|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------|
| `SpinnerNumberModel` with `Integer` value                                   | `IntegerField`              |
| `SpinnerNumberModel` with `Long` value                                      | `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.LongField`  |
| `SpinnerNumberModel` with `Double` / `Float` / `BigDecimal` / `BigInteger`  | `NumberField`               |
| `SpinnerDateModel`                                                          | `DatePicker`                |
| `SpinnerListModel`                                                          | `Select<Object>`            |
| any other `SpinnerModel` implementation                                     | read-only `TextField` + WARN|

`setModel(newModel)` with a different-typed model rebuilds the inner field in place (tear down, fresh `add`, rewire the peer listener); with a same-typed model it reuses the existing inner and just pushes the new bounds + value through. `SpinnerModel`'s `ChangeListener` is the single source of truth (R_swing_is_truth): our listener fans out to user `ChangeListener`s (source=SJSpinner), pushes current value + bounds + step to the inner peer under the `preventPeerEvents` guard, and calls `CustomField.updateValue` so Vaadin `HasValue` observers see the change through their channel too. Browser-originated edits funnel through an explicit `HasValue` listener on the inner field wrapped in `SHelper.callSwing` (R_callswing_envelope), coerce the peer-typed value back to the model's type (LocalDate→Date via `ZoneId.systemDefault`; Number→`Double`/`Float`/`BigDecimal`/`BigInteger` matching the model's current value type), and write through the model. `setValue(Object)` is overridden to route through `model.setValue` so JDK's contract (IAE on null / non-Number for `SpinnerNumberModel`, etc.) reaches migrated code verbatim.

Read-only handling: `SJSpinner.isReadOnly()` is the user-facing bit; the inner field's effective read-only is `userReadOnly || fallbackForcesReadOnly`. For the unknown-model TextField fallback `fallbackForcesReadOnly=true` permanently — an app can never type into a field whose input we can't parse back.

Shipped alongside: **`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.LongField`** as a plain Vaadin component — no Swing surface, no mixin. Extends `AbstractNumberField<LongField, Long>` with a `Long.parseLong` parser and reuses `@Tag("vaadin-integer-field")` so spinner arrows + decimal-rejection are inherited from the integer web component. General-purpose: any Vaadin code needing a long-typed numeric field can use it directly.

The `:emulators.JSpinner` emulator thins down to a ~170-LoC delegating shell: constructors hand off to `SJSpinner`, every JSpinner-shape method routes through the surrogate, and a single peer-ChangeListener bridge re-fires `ChangeEvent` with `source=this` (emulator) so migrated code casting `(JSpinner) e.getSource()` still sees the emulator. Independent `PropertyChangeSupport` lists on emulator and surrogate (same shape as JSlider, SD_sjslider). The D_emulator_surrogate_split boundary validation (UOE on non-Integer `SpinnerNumberModel`, on `SpinnerDateModel`, on `SpinnerListModel`) is gone — all JDK `SpinnerModel` variants now work through the same surface.

**Rationale.**
- **CustomField, not a leaf primitive.** JSpinner's API allows swapping the model to a different-typed one at runtime (`spinner.setModel(new SpinnerDateModel())`). No single Vaadin leaf primitive morphs from a numeric input into a date picker into a select — `CustomField` is the Vaadin-native vehicle for composing a value-carrying field from swappable children. SD_sjslider's "leaf primitive" shape happened to fit `JSlider`→`Slider` cleanly; JSpinner's contract doesn't allow the same. The [architecture.md rule 2](./architecture.md) was broadened in this slice to make "richer Vaadin component" explicitly cover both leaf primitives and composites; SD_sjspinner is the reference point for the composite case.
- **`LongField` carries its weight.** `SpinnerNumberModel(Long)` is a normal migration shape and the alternatives both have costs: `IntegerField` clips at ±2<sup>31</sup>, `NumberField` rounds at ±2<sup>53</sup> via double mantissa. A narrow Long-typed sibling of `IntegerField` (about 100 LoC, reuses the integer web component's DOM and client behaviour) gives exact round-trip for the whole Long range at the cost we're already paying for the integer case. Also general-purpose for any future long-typed field need — carries its keep beyond SJSpinner.
- **Route `setValue(Object)` through the model, not through `CustomField.setValue`.** JDK `JSpinner.setValue` delegates to `model.setValue`, so the contract user code was written against is IAE / CCE on invalid input. Bypassing `CustomField.setValue`'s internal `setModelValue` bookkeeping is safe because the subsequent model `ChangeEvent` fans out to `fanOutModelChange` which calls `CustomField.updateValue`; CustomField's own value stays in sync via that path. `generateModelValue` returns `model.getValue()` so `HasValue.getValue()` stays honest.
- **Peer→model coercion matches the model's *current* value type, not the peer's type.** A `NumberField` always returns `Double`; if the installed model is `SpinnerNumberModel(BigDecimal)` we coerce to `BigDecimal.valueOf(d)` before writing, so the model's value-type invariant holds across peer edits. Same for `Float` / `BigInteger`. Lossy for BigDecimal/BigInteger but bounded (Double mantissa), same class of precision loss SJSlider accepts for non-integer writes per SD_sjslider.
- **Independent emulator + surrogate PCE lists.** Same rationale as SD_sjslider: listeners registered via the emulator were the target of migrated code casting `(JSpinner) e.getSource()`; repointing them at the surrogate would change the event source they see and break migrated casts. Emulator and surrogate each fire their own `"model"` PCE; listeners hear only the side they registered on.

**Alternatives.**
- **Multiple surrogates, one per model family** (`SIntegerSpinner extends IntegerField`, `SDoubleSpinner extends NumberField`, `SDateSpinner extends DatePicker`, `SListSpinner extends Select`). Rejected because `setModel(newTypedModel)` on the emulator would have no clean path — the emulator's peer is constructor-chosen and swapping peers breaks the `JSpinner(Component peer)` invariant. The composite approach handles runtime model-type swap naturally.
- **`NumberField` with double-mantissa for Long values.** Simpler (no `LongField`) but rounds at 2<sup>53</sup> — enough for most cases but not all, and "mostly works" isn't a great stance for a numeric field. Rejected; precision-exact path is worth the small new component.
- **`IntegerField` with int-clipping for Long values.** Cheaper still but breaks at 2<sup>31</sup>, inside the realistic range of Long-valued spinners (timestamps, IDs, file sizes). Rejected for the same reason as above.
- **`ComboBox<Object>` for `SpinnerListModel`.** Accepts free-text entry + filtering, closer to JDK `JSpinner.ListEditor`'s editable-text UX. Rejected because the list-mutation invalidation story is worse for ComboBox (our server-side list is a copy from `SpinnerListModel.getList()` at install time; free-text entries that aren't in the list would land silently in the model). `Select<Object>` restricts to list items, which is the common-case JSpinner list use — and our unknown-model fallback path already handles the rare "typed input that doesn't match any list entry" case via the read-only TextField story.
- **Unknown-`SpinnerModel` → throw `UnsupportedOperationException`.** Matches the earlier emulator D_emulator_surrogate_split stance. Rejected because the surrogate's role is to *close* those gaps — shipping a fallback that boots (even read-only) beats making a migrated app crash at construction. The WARN surfaces the incompleteness without forcing user action.

**Accepted limitation.**
- `SpinnerListModel.getList()` is snapshotted at install time into `Select.setItems`; mutating the underlying list after install doesn't refresh the options (neither does JDK JSpinner — `SpinnerListModel` isn't observable on list mutation). Swapping the model via `setModel(new SpinnerListModel(newList))` does refresh.
- `BigDecimal` / `BigInteger`-typed `SpinnerNumberModel` peer edits round-trip through `NumberField`'s `Double`, so values outside Double's exact-integer range (±2<sup>53</sup>) or with precision beyond Double's mantissa get quantised. Programmatic writes (`setValue(bigDecimal)`) are exact — only browser-originated edits lose precision.
- `SpinnerDateModel` peer edits use the date's start-of-day in `ZoneId.systemDefault()` — JDK `JSpinner`'s `DateEditor` uses the `Calendar` default time zone, so the two behave identically when the Calendar default is the system default (which it is unless set otherwise). Time-of-day is always midnight on a peer edit (DatePicker has no time component).
- `setValue(null)` on an `SpinnerNumberModel`-backed spinner throws IAE, matching the JDK. Binder bindings through `HasValue.setValue(null)` also throw — Binder treats this as a validation error rather than a silent reset. Apps that want empty-state spinners should use a model that accepts null (unusual — JDK spinners don't typically).
- Inner-field lifecycle is an implementation detail; CSS applied to the inner field directly (via `spinner.getElement().getStyle().set(...)` on a cast inner) doesn't survive `setModel(differentType)`. Styling via the outer `SJSpinner`'s element persists (CustomField host is unchanged across swaps). User code using the `JComponentMixin` surface (`setBorder` / `setOpaque` / etc.) lands on the outer element and is unaffected.
- The surrogate `SJSpinner`'s `HasValue`-reported value and the model's value stay in sync via `updateValue()` after every model change. A tight race where user code reads `spinner.getValue()` between the model fire and our `updateValue()` would see different values from the two sources; all realistic single-threaded code paths are safe.
- `commitEdit` is a no-op on both layers (surrogate and emulator). JDK `JSpinner.commitEdit` forces the editor's parsed value back into the model; we don't have a `JFormattedTextField`-style editor, and peer edits already mirror on every ValueChange so the model is always consistent at call time. Migrated code that catches `ParseException` from `commitEdit` will just never see the exception fire — acceptable since the semantic outcome (model is up to date) is achieved.

<a id="SD_spinner_date_pattern"></a>**SD_spinner_date_pattern — `setDatePattern(String)` plumbs JDK `SimpleDateFormat`-shaped patterns to `DatePicker.DatePickerI18n`.** Added in [emulators D_jspinner_editor](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jspinner_editor). Package-public method on `SJSpinner`; translates `"yyyy-MM-dd"` / `"MM/dd/yyyy"` / similar JDK patterns into a `DatePickerI18n.setDateFormats(pattern)` and applies via `dp.setI18n(...)`. Pattern is field-shadowed so a subsequent `setModel(SpinnerDateModel)` swap re-applies it to the freshly-built DatePicker; without this, swapping date models would silently revert to the locale default. Time components in the pattern (e.g. `"yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm"`) are stripped at the first space — Vaadin DatePicker is date-only by construction, mirroring SD_maskformatter_regex's "no SJFormattedDateTimePicker" stance. Vaadin pattern-rejection (unsupported letters, etc.) degrades to a `SHelper.onUnimplemented` WARN + locale default per R_match_swing_errors (c). No-op on non-Date inner fields (numeric / list / unknown-model paths) — pattern still stored, takes effect if the model swaps to a date-typed one.
