The SJSpinner editor surface itself (`getEditor` / `setEditor`) stays drop-and-WARN per the original SD_sjspinner stance — D_jspinner_editor's editor port lives on the emulator side and consults `setDatePattern` from the outside.

---

<a id="SD_key_events"></a>
## SD_key_events. `SKeyEvent` port + `KeyListener` wiring — Vaadin `KeyNotifier` trio mapped to KEY_PRESSED / KEY_TYPED / KEY_RELEASED

**Decision.** `ComponentMixin.addKeyListener(SKeyListener)` wires a single Swing listener to all three Vaadin `KeyNotifier` subscriptions:

| Vaadin event      | Vaadin interface method       | Swing `SKeyEvent` id | Swing callback |
|-------------------|-------------------------------|----------------------|----------------|
| `keydown`         | `addKeyDownListener`          | `KEY_PRESSED`        | `keyPressed`   |
| `keypress`        | `addKeyPressListener`         | `KEY_TYPED`          | `keyTyped`     |
| `keyup`           | `addKeyUpListener`            | `KEY_RELEASED`       | `keyReleased`  |

Source retyped from `java.awt.Component` to `com.vaadin.flow.component.Component` per SD_event_port_stance. `SKeyEvent` extends a new `SInputEvent` port of `java.awt.event.InputEvent` (the `*_MASK` / `*_DOWN_MASK` pair kept; static `getMaskForButton` / `getModifiersExText` delegate to AWT). `SKeyListener` + `SKeyAdapter` ride alongside with their two identical-shape AWT ports. **Both classes' constant surfaces — `VK_*`, `KEY_LOCATION_*` and the modifier masks — delegate to AWT rather than carrying values** (`VK_A = java.awt.event.KeyEvent.VK_A`), and the whole block is emitted by `DelegatedConstantsTest` reflecting over AWT's public runtime API. So the values are AWT's by construction rather than by transcription, no constant value is written out in `:surrogates` at all, and a keycode AWT adds needs only a regeneration. The emulator-side `vaadinx.awt.event.KeyEvent` keeps its own transcribed table under D_event_port_policy — the two layers differ here on purpose.

Non-`KeyNotifier` peers log via `SHelper.onUnimplemented(this, "addKeyListener/non-KeyNotifier-peer", l)` and skip wiring. Matches the `addFocusListener` non-focusable branch (SD_vaadin_first_binding) — storing a listener that can never fire would mislead migrated code.

The inbound (Vaadin → AWT) translation helpers land in `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.util.KeyConvert` (extracted from `SHelper`, alongside the outbound direction — see Alternatives):
- `vaadinKeyToVK(Key)` — keys off `key.getKeys().get(0)` (the browser-side `event.key` value Vaadin populates). Letters/digits via range match; named keys in a switch (`"Enter"` / `"Escape"` / `"ArrowLeft"` / `"F1"` / etc.); unknown single chars fall back to `java.awt.event.KeyEvent.getExtendedKeyCodeForChar`; everything else → `VK_UNDEFINED`.
- `keyCharFrom(Key)` — single-character primary name → that char; named keys → `CHAR_UNDEFINED`.
- `keyModifiersToMask(Set<KeyModifier>)` — straight enum → bit-flag mapping (`SHIFT`/`CONTROL`/`ALT`/`ALT_GRAPH`/`META`).
- `keyLocationToAwt(KeyLocation)` — one-for-one semantic alignment, but Vaadin's `STANDARD=0` maps to AWT's `KEY_LOCATION_STANDARD=1` (the integer values differ).

**Rationale.**
- **Map `keypress` to `KEY_TYPED`, not synthesize from keydown.** Browsers fire `keypress` only for character-producing keys, with the effective (shift-resolved) char already in `event.key`. Using that directly matches AWT's `KEY_TYPED` semantics (character delivery) exactly and lets the browser do the modifier-combining work we'd otherwise have to replicate server-side. The alternative — track shift state across keydowns and synthesize KEY_TYPED ourselves — would duplicate what the browser already does correctly and introduce skew on dead keys / IME / Shift-Lock edge cases.
- **`System.currentTimeMillis()` for `when`.** Vaadin's `KeyboardEvent` doesn't surface the browser's `event.timeStamp`. Server-side clock is the best available approximation; AWT's `when` is documented as the time of the event, not a display-render timestamp, so a ~1 RTT offset is within R_best_effort_behaviour close-enough. A future `KeyboardEvent.getTimestamp()` addition upstream would drop straight in here (accepted divergence today).
- **`keyKeys().get(0)` over `getCode()`.** `event.getKey()` returns a Key built from the browser's `event.key` — the shift-resolved, locale-aware key identifier (`"A"` vs `"a"`, `"é"` on an AZERTY keyboard). `getCode()` returns the physical-key identifier (`"KeyA"` always, regardless of layout). For Swing compatibility we want the user's *intent* (which letter they typed), not the physical key position — that's `event.key`. This is the opposite direction from the *outbound* mapping `KeyConvert.vkToVaadinKey` (AWT → Vaadin for Shortcut / accelerator install), which uses `.code` because Shortcuts match physical keys — the two directions differ on purpose. The outbound mapping is now shared with `:emulators` through `KeyConvert` (the SD_shelper_statics-allowed `:emulators → :surrogates` direction); only the inbound `.key` matching described here is surrogate-specific.
- **`KEY_TYPED` dispatch guard.** `SKeyEvent`'s constructor enforces AWT's invariant — `id == KEY_TYPED` requires `keyChar != CHAR_UNDEFINED` and `keyCode == VK_UNDEFINED`. In practice browsers never fire `keypress` for non-character keys, so the guard is belt-and-braces; still, we *skip* the dispatch rather than throw when an edge case produces `CHAR_UNDEFINED` (malformed event, future browser change, custom server-side fire of `KeyPressEvent(component, "Enter")`). Throwing IAE on the Vaadin request thread would crash the UI for a recoverable upstream anomaly.
- **One user listener → one composite `Registration`.** Each `addKeyListener` installs three Vaadin subscriptions; composed into a single lambda that `Registrations.add(listener, lambda)` tracks. `removeKeyListener` walks the one composite and `Registration::remove`s all three. Matches the existing shape used by `addHierarchyListener`'s attach+detach pair (SD_vaadin_first_binding).
- **`isActionKey()` asks AWT instead of carrying a list.** It builds a throwaway `java.awt.event.KeyEvent` (a static bare-`java.awt.Component` source, since `EventObject` rejects a null one; ~152 ns per call, measured) and returns *its* answer. Our `VK_*` values are AWT's, so AWT's answer is correct by definition and stays correct as AWT grows keycodes. This replaced a hand-maintained `||` chain that was **wrong for 30 keycodes** — the whole input-method set (`VK_KANA`, `VK_KANJI`, `VK_CONVERT`, `VK_KATAKANA`, …) and the Sun editing set (`VK_BEGIN`, `VK_COPY`, `VK_PASTE`, `VK_UNDO`, `VK_CUT`, …), every miss a false negative. **None of those keys is reachable from a browser, which is why the list could be wrong for months without a single test noticing** — the same reason a list must not come back. `:emulators`' `vaadinx.awt.event.KeyEvent` does the same, so both layers agree with AWT and therefore with each other; `SKeyEventActionKeyTest` / `KeyEventActionKeyTest` sweep the whole keycode space against AWT as the drift guard. `paramString()` deliberately does *not* delegate: AWT's emits `rawCode` / `primaryLevelUnicode` / `scancode` / `extendedKeyCode`, native-populated fields we can never fill, so delegating would print authoritative-looking zeros.

**Alternatives.**
- **Reuse the emulator-side `vaadinx.awt.event.KeyEvent`.** Rejected per SD_shelper_statics — the module direction is `:emulators → :surrogates`. Cross-importing the *event class* reverses it, tangles the modules, and breaks the surrogates-extract-cleanly-into-its-own-jar story; `SKeyEvent` declares its own `VK_*` surface, delegating each value to AWT's per SD_event_port_stance. **Update (KeyConvert extraction):** the *outbound* AWT → Vaadin translation (`vkToVaadinKey` / `toVaadinKeyBinding` / the `VaadinKeyBinding` record) was originally duplicated verbatim into `vaadinx.EHelper` under this same reasoning — but that framed the choice as reuse-across-the-forbidden-arrow vs. duplicate, missing the third option: extract into a `:surrogates` class that `:emulators` calls (the arrow's *allowed* direction, established later by `CssConvert`). Those copies are now collapsed into `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.util.KeyConvert` and `:emulators` calls straight through. Only the inbound `.code`/`.key` split remains genuinely distinct — and that isn't duplication, since the emulator has no inbound table.
- **Ship only `KEY_PRESSED` / `KEY_RELEASED`; omit `KEY_TYPED`.** Rejected — migrated code that listens for character input (e.g. a live-search field that consumes TYPED events to avoid seeing modifier keydowns) relies on the split. Wiring keypress costs one extra subscription and a ~15-LoC helper.
- **Inherit `SKeyEvent` from `SComponentEvent` directly, skip the `SInputEvent` port.** Rejected — loses the `isShiftDown` / `isControlDown` / `getModifiersEx` accessors that migrated code relies on at the `InputEvent` level, and breaks the `instanceof SInputEvent` chain that a future `SMouseEvent` port will share. ~80 LoC for the shared parent is cheap enough.
- **Fire on any Vaadin `Component` via `getElement().addEventListener("keydown", ...)` for non-`KeyNotifier` peers.** Rejected — bypasses Vaadin's typed event machinery (no `KeyboardEvent` wrapper; we'd have to parse the raw DOM event JSON ourselves), and most peers that aren't `KeyNotifier` aren't *Focusable* either, so there's nothing to type into that could fire key events to begin with. WARN-and-skip is the honest answer.
- **Track shift-state across keydowns and synthesize KEY_TYPED with uppercase letters.** Rejected — browsers already do this and handle dead keys / IME / Shift-Lock / AltGraph correctly. Duplicating the state machine server-side would reintroduce bugs the browser team has already fixed.

**Accepted limitation.**
- `SKeyEvent.setModifiers(int)` is a WARN stub — `SInputEvent.modifiers` is final (the value arrives from the browser; we don't expect user code to retarget events mid-dispatch). Callers rewriting modifiers on an incoming event get the log; add real retarget support when a migration target needs it.
- `keyChar` is single-char on the primary `Key` identifier only. A `Key.of("Enter", "NumpadEnter")` (two-entry Key object from a server-synthesized event) reports `CHAR_UNDEFINED` for keyChar even though Enter has a canonical char in AWT (`'\n'`). Incoming browser events always carry a single-identifier Key, so this only affects synthetic server-side fires — unusual, and the KEY_PRESSED keyCode path still resolves correctly.
- Modifier-key presses (Shift / Control / Alt alone) dispatch as `KEY_PRESSED` with `VK_SHIFT` / `VK_CONTROL` / `VK_ALT` and `keyChar == CHAR_UNDEFINED`. AWT does the same. Migrated code listening for modifier-only presses works; code filtering "real" keystrokes should check `isActionKey()` or compare `keyCode` against the modifier VK_* values.
- `KeyModifier.ALT_GRAPH` maps to `ALT_GRAPH_DOWN_MASK`; browsers on platforms that don't distinguish AltGraph from Alt (e.g. US keyboards) won't set this bit. Matches AWT's own platform-dependent behaviour.
- `when` is the server's receive time, not the browser's event time (~1 RTT off). See Rationale; accepted for R_best_effort_behaviour.
- The emulator's `JSpinner` and the surrogate `SJSpinner` keep independent `ChangeListener` lists. A single `ChangeListener` added to both fires twice. Same two-layer-composition convention as SJSlider / JSlider (SD_sjslider); migrators typically register on one layer only.

---

<a id="SD_sframe"></a>
## SD_sframe. `SFrame` surrogate — one concrete on `Dialog`; `WindowMixin` for the Window listener surface; blocking dialogs stay emulator-only

> **Note (SFrame/SJFrame separation).** SD_sframe's original framing put `java.awt.Window` + `java.awt.Frame` + `javax.swing.JFrame`-minus-root-pane all on a single `SFrame` concrete. After SD_sjframe introduced `SJFrame` for the JRootPane layer, and a follow-up cleanup separated the JFrame-only surface from the Frame analog, the split now reads: **`SFrame` is the `java.awt.Frame` (+`Window`) surrogate; `SJFrame extends SFrame` is the `javax.swing.JFrame` surrogate.** The JFrame-specific bits — `defaultCloseOperation` + `WindowConstants` validation + close-op throw, `contentPane` field + `getContentPane`/`setContentPane`, `rootPaneCheckingEnabled` + `ensureContentPane` bootstrap, add/remove routing through contentPane, `getUIClassID` returning `"FrameUI"`, paramString's defaultCloseOperation report — live on SJFrame; SFrame keeps title / setVisible / dispose / WINDOW_OPENED latch / owner tree / state / resizable / undecorated / static getFrames / WindowMixin + JComponentMixin / addOpenedChangeListener routing. The decision text below is preserved verbatim for cross-reference; treat references to "SFrame's defaultCloseOperation / contentPane / rootPaneCheckingEnabled" as having moved to SJFrame.

**Decision.** Third concrete surrogate. `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SFrame extends com.vaadin.flow.component.dialog.Dialog implements WindowMixin, JComponentMixin` carries the `java.awt.Window` + `java.awt.Frame` + `javax.swing.JFrame`-minus-root-pane surface. `:emulators.JFrame` / `:emulators.awt.Frame` / `:emulators.awt.Window` thin to delegating shells over it (SJSlider/SJSpinner shape from SD_sjslider/SD_sjspinner generalised).

One concrete class, one new mixin:

- **`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.awt.WindowMixin extends ContainerMixin`** carries the three Window listener families (`SWindowListener` / `SWindowFocusListener` / `SWindowStateListener` + fire/process helpers + top-level `processEvent` id router), the stateless Window-level accessors that need no storage (`isFocusableWindow`, `getWarningString`, `isAlwaysOnTopSupported`, `isAutoRequestFocus`, `isActive`, `isFocused`, `getFocusOwner`, `getMostRecentFocusOwner`, `isFocusCycleRoot`, `getFocusCycleRootAncestor`, `isValidateRoot`, `isAlwaysOnTop`, `isLocationByPlatform`, `getShape`, `getOpacity`, `getFocusableWindowState`, empty `getFocusTraversalKeys`, empty `getIconImages`, null `getInputContext`), and the silent / WARN-on-change setters (`toFront`/`toBack`/`pack` silent no-ops; `setAlwaysOnTop(true)` / `setLocationByPlatform(true)` / `setShape(non-null)` / `setOpacity(non-1.0)` / `setFocusableWindowState(false)` / `setAutoRequestFocus(false)` / `setFocusCycleRoot(true)` WARN; `setIconImages` / `setIconImage` silent since favicons are route-level; `applyResourceBundle` / `createBufferStrategy` / `getBufferStrategy` / `setLocationRelativeTo` WARN).
- **`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SFrame`** holds all the state-bearing behaviour on the concrete: `preventPeerEvents` feedback-loop guard, `everShown` first-show latch, `visible` shadow (narrow SD_vaadin_first_binding divergence — see Rationale), `owner` + weak-ref `ownedWindowList`, `defaultCloseOperation`, `title`, lazy `contentPane` Div, `rootPaneCheckingEnabled` routing flag. Peer↔Window sync via an `OpenedChangeListener` on the Dialog (via `this`) wrapped in `SHelper.callSwing`; user-driven `setVisible` / `dispose` / `setDefaultCloseOperation` / `setTitle` manage the flag + fire the corresponding events on SFrame's listener lists.

The Window-family event ports land alongside under `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.awt.event`: **`SWindowEvent`** (10 ids — `WINDOW_OPENED` / `CLOSING` / `CLOSED` / `ICONIFIED` / `DEICONIFIED` / `ACTIVATED` / `DEACTIVATED` / `GAINED_FOCUS` / `LOST_FOCUS` / `STATE_CHANGED` — constants delegating to AWT's per SD_event_port_stance, so ID arithmetic carries over; source + opposite retyped to `com.vaadin.flow.component.Component` per SD_event_port_stance), **`SWindowListener`** (7 non-focus / non-state callbacks), **`SWindowFocusListener`** (2), **`SWindowStateListener`** (1), **`SWindowAdapter`** (empty-override helper covering all three listener interfaces, matching JDK's single `WindowAdapter` shape).

**No separate `SWindow` class.** The two-name sketch ("SWindow / SFrame") captured the scope; the implementation collapses to one concrete since `javax.swing.JWindow` isn't queued and `JDialog` (the likely next top-level after SFrame) will pull out a `DialogMixin` at the point that surrogate is designed. Splitting now would spend design cycles on a `SWindow extends Dialog` shell nothing uses.

**Blocking dialogs stay out of scope in `:surrogates`.** `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SHelper.callSwing(Runnable)` is and will remain a literal `runnable.run()` — UI-fiber wrapping for `dialog.show()` / `JOptionPane.showXxxDialog` lives only in `vaadinx.EHelper.callSwing`. Surrogates can't cross the module boundary without inverting it (SD_shelper_statics), and replicating the machinery would duplicate a fragile hook we intentionally scope to the emulator layer. The `callSwing` seam on the surrogate side survives for uniform peer→Swing funneling (consistency with SD_sjslider/SD_sjspinner/SD_key_events), not for blocking behaviour. Documented on the method javadoc. **Where the emulator hands the surrogate a bare `Runnable` (currently only `MenuNode.onClick` per [SD_sjmenubar](#SD_sjmenubar)), the emulator pre-wraps that Runnable in `vaadinx.EHelper.callSwing` itself before passing it across — same per-seam pattern as the emulator-installed `addActionListener` / `addChangeListener` bridges on SJButton / SJToggleButton (see `:emulators.swing.AbstractButton`).**

**Emulator thin-down.** The three emulator classes become delegating shells:

- `:emulators.awt.Window` keeps its own `listenerList` + `preventPeerEvents` + `everShown`, wires its own `Dialog.OpenedChangeListener` (parallel to SFrame's), and delegates peer-side work (`UI.add`, `setOpened(true/false)`, `element.removeFromParent()`) to `SFrame.setVisible` / `SFrame.dispose`. Each layer's `preventPeerEvents` guards its own callback — under emulator-driven writes, both flags are set; under a peer-originated close, neither is set and both layers fire once each on their respective listener lists (SD_sjslider/SD_sjspinner independent-layer shape). `getWindows` / `getOwnerlessWindows` statics unchanged — `vaadinx.EHelper.getWindows` walks Dialog peers and maps via the emulator-side `Component`-keyed data, still intact since `SFrame IS-A Dialog`.
- `:emulators.awt.Frame.setTitle` delegates the Dialog.setHeaderTitle write to `SFrame.setTitle` (which normalizes null, fires its own surrogate-layer `"title"` PCE). The emulator keeps its own field + PCE so migrated PropertyChangeListener registrations on Frame still hear the event on their own layer.
- `:emulators.swing.JFrame.setDefaultCloseOperation` delegates validation + gesture-disarm + EXIT WARN to `SFrame.setDefaultCloseOperation`. `frameInit` / `contentPane` / `JRootPane` D_rootpane_holder holder unchanged — root-pane integration stays emulator-owned per SD_sframe scope.

**Testapp coverage.** `testapps/phase1` (the canonical "first app that shows a Window/Frame" fixture) grows a `WarnInventoryTest` parallel to phase5's — `inventory_phase1_user_path` drives the MainView JFrame+JButton end-to-end, `inventory_sd17_api_surface` micro-driver locks in zero-WARN coverage on JFrame lifecycle / WindowListener fan-out / title / defaultCloseOperation transitions (HIDE / DISPOSE / DO_NOTHING — EXIT_ON_CLOSE WARNs by design and is excluded) / content-pane routing / getFrames / Frame-state stubs.

**Rationale.**
- **One concrete, not `SWindow` + `SFrame`.** JWindow isn't on the queue; a future `SDialog` will want a `DialogMixin` anyway. Splitting SFrame into a two-class hierarchy now spends design cycles on a shell nothing uses and forces a `SWindow extends Dialog` that adds nothing over `Dialog` itself. Collapsing to one concrete keeps the class/state count low and mirrors SJSlider's single-concrete shape — broader splits happen when a second concrete (SDialog) actually lands.
- **`WindowMixin` exists despite one concrete.** Keeps the mixin pattern consistent with `ComponentMixin` / `ContainerMixin` / `JComponentMixin` so future JDialog / JWindow surrogates don't reinvent the Window listener surface. Listener registration + fire helpers on the mixin means SDialog (when designed) gets them for free.
- **`visible` shadow on SFrame.** Narrow SD_vaadin_first_binding divergence, accepted here. `Dialog.isOpened` starts false pre-attach just like AWT's Window, but a caller doing `new SFrame().setVisible(true)` before attach would lose the intent if we relied on `isOpened` alone. The shadow carries that across attachment. Any time the two disagree after attach, `isOpened` wins — the shadow is read only during `setVisible` / `dispose` for transition detection.
- **Event ports: eager or nothing.** `SWindowEvent` / `SWindowListener` / `SWindowFocusListener` / `SWindowStateListener` / `SWindowAdapter` all land together. Splitting (port `SWindowEvent` / `SWindowListener` but defer focus/state) would break the id-routing switch in `processEvent` and leave `WindowMixin.processWindowFocusEvent` / `processWindowStateEvent` referencing non-existent types.
- **Blocking dialogs in the emulator, not here.** Replicating the UI-fiber wrapper on the surrogate side would duplicate the emulator's EHelper.callSwing hook, which is intentionally a single seam. Having surrogates depend on the emulator inverts the module direction (SD_shelper_statics). The trade-off: a pure-surrogate user calling `new SFrame().setVisible(true)` on a modal dialog doesn't block — acceptable since modal blocking is a migration-bridge convenience, not a Vaadin-idiomatic use case.
- **`:emulators.Window` keeps its own OpenedChangeListener.** Cleaner than bridging through an `SFrame.addWindowListener` subscription. Bridging made the emulator dependent on SFrame's event timing (peer-originated close → SFrame mirrors → SFrame fires → emulator hears → emulator mirrors), which opened an ordering gap: by the time the emulator heard the SFrame event, the emulator's `visible` shadow hadn't mirrored yet. Two parallel `OpenedChangeListener`s with their own `preventPeerEvents` guards each handle their own layer's state transitions independently — simpler and matches the existing `:emulators` shape exactly.
- **JRootPane stays emulator-side (unchanged).** D_rootpane_holder's server-side holder continues to live on the emulator layer. The eventual structural port (layered-pane overlay, glass-pane sibling) is a separate `SJRootPane` slice and belongs in its own decision — SD_sframe specifically does not cover it.
- **Content-pane routing via `rootPaneCheckingEnabled` flag.** Identical to the emulator's existing pattern for JFrame's content-pane redirect. The flag lets `super.add(contentPane)` bootstrap the pane without recursing through our own `add(Collection)` override. Post-Java-5 JFrame.add auto-routes to contentPane in JDK; mirroring that here closes the migration-surprise gap for surrogate users.

**Alternatives.**
- **Two concrete (`SWindow extends Dialog`, `SFrame extends SWindow`).** Rejected for SD_sframe's scope — no JWindow consumer, and the extra inheritance layer costs per-ctor cycles without payback. `SDialog` when designed will re-examine the split.
- **Bundle the `SJRootPane` backfill into this slice.** Rejected — D_rootpane_holder's deferral is specifically about when layered/glass structural rendering earns its keep, which is orthogonal to the Dialog↔Window sync SD_sframe covers. Mixing doubles the slice size and intertwines two independent design questions.
- **Skip `WindowMixin`, put the Window listener family directly on SFrame.** Rejected — breaks the ComponentMixin / ContainerMixin / JComponentMixin consistency pattern and forces duplication when SDialog (or any future top-level surrogate) lands. The one-line cost of extending ContainerMixin in a new interface is trivial.
- **Drop the `visible` shadow, read `isOpened()` everywhere.** Rejected — pre-attach callers lose intent (see Rationale).
- **Validate before setOpened *inside SFrame*.** Rejected — `validate()` walks the emulator-layer child tree (`:emulators.Container.validate`) calling LayoutManager CSS dispatch. That's emulator-owned (`vaadinx.awt.LayoutManager`, `vaadinx.EHelper.applyContainerCss`) and the surrogate can't reach it without inverting the module direction (SD_shelper_statics). The emulator's `Window.setVisible` override calls `validate()` before delegating to `SFrame.setVisible`, preserving the one-way direction.
- **Bridge SFrame events to emulator via an `addWindowListener`.** Rejected — see Rationale §":emulators.Window keeps its own OpenedChangeListener". Each layer having its own peer subscription is simpler and avoids the ordering gap.
- **Override `ComponentMixin.addComponentListener` on SFrame to skip attach/detach wiring.** *Adopted* — the mixin's default wires `attach → COMPONENT_SHOWN` / `detach → COMPONENT_HIDDEN`, which assumes attach ↔ visibility. That's wrong for SFrame: the Dialog stays attached across `setVisible(false)` and detaches only on `dispose()`. The override registers listeners with a no-op Vaadin subscription and drives COMPONENT_SHOWN/HIDDEN from `setVisible` / `dispose` / `onPeerOpenedChanged` directly.

**Accepted limitations.**
- **Pure-surrogate layout is inert.** `SFrame` inherits `ContainerMixin.setLayout` (stored via `LayoutStore`, no CSS dispatch). Children added through a pure-surrogate `SFrame.add(Component)` render in plain block flow. Emulator users are unaffected — `:emulators.JFrame.setLayout` routes through the emulator's own content-pane path, and `LayoutCss.applyContainerCss` writes CSS onto the content pane's Div as today. Lifting layout CSS dispatch into `:surrogates` is a separate cross-cutting slice.
- **Blocking dialogs unavailable at the pure-surrogate layer.** `setVisible(true)` on a modal SFrame returns immediately; `JOptionPane.showXxxDialog` / `dialog.show()` blocking belongs to the emulator's EHelper.callSwing hook (D_blocking_dialogs_deferred). Pure-surrogate apps that need blocking behaviour must migrate through the emulator or wait for that hook to land.
- **Pixel geometry.** `setBounds` / `setSize` / `setLocation` inherited from `ComponentMixin` stay WARN — Dialog auto-sizes to content (R_layouts_close_enough). `reshape` / `setMinimumSize` similarly.
- **Focus / activation / iconification / state transitions.** `isFocused` / `isActive` / `WINDOW_*_FOCUS` / `WINDOW_STATE_CHANGED` all report AWT defaults with no dispatch path. Registered focus/state listeners store + remove correctly but never fire. Ported for API parity so migrated code compiles.
- **Multi-UI scope.** `SFrame.getFrames()` / `SFrame.getWindows()` scope to the current `UI`, not the JVM (D_single_ui_per_session inherited), and stay a **type-rooted graph walk** — they deliberately do *not* follow [D_window_registry](../emulators/decisions.md#D_window_registry)'s emulator-side switch to a retained, creation-keyed registry. Per R_vaadin_first a stage-3 migrator holds a Vaadin tree and this is a Swing-flavoured helper over it, so a retained registry here would be the shadow-store shape R_vaadin_first rejects, and it would buy AWT's leak with none of the faithfulness argument (no stage-3 code is reproducing `java.awt.Window`'s call graph). The two layers answer the same question differently **on purpose** — the D_emulator_surrogate_split stage split, not drift.
- **EXIT_ON_CLOSE.** WARNs at set-time and downgrades to HIDE at close-time. Unchanged from the emulator stance — there's no safe browser mapping (Vaadin session close logs the user out everywhere, tab-close JS is unreliable).
- **Two-layer listener fan-out.** A `WindowListener` registered on both `emulator.JFrame` and its peer `SFrame` fires twice on a peer-originated close (each layer's listener independently mirrors). Same convention as SJSlider's `ChangeListener` (SD_sjslider) / SJSpinner's (SD_sjspinner) — migrators typically register on one layer only.
- **~~No separate `SWindow` concrete.~~** Superseded twice: WindowMixin was flipped into the concrete `SWindow` class (SD_sjdialog), and the JWindow slice landed `SJWindow extends SWindow` per [SD_sjwindow](#SD_sjwindow) — extending SWindow directly rather than this entry's SFrame sketch, with the chrome question resolved by the `emul-undecorated` overlay class instead of a mixin split.
- **Lossy layout-vs-routing distinction in pure-surrogate.** Because `SFrame.add(Component...)` routes into `contentPane` while `SFrame.getElement()` is the Dialog's own element, CSS written via `sf.getElement().getStyle()` applies to the Dialog container, not the content pane — same as JDK `JFrame` where frame-level styling differs from content-pane-level. Migrators accustomed to the JDK distinction already handle this; pure-surrogate users coming from Vaadin may find it surprising. Documented on the class javadoc.

---

<a id="SD_sjframe"></a>
## SD_sjframe. `SJRootPane` + `SJFrame` — non-structural root-pane holder, `SJFrame extends SFrame` for the JRootPane-bearing slice

> **Superseded in part by [SD_sjrootpane_structural](#SD_sjrootpane_structural).** The "non-structural holder" stance in the title and throughout the text below is **reversed**: `SJRootPane` now owns the JDK's pane chain structurally, and the content-pane mirror it describes is gone (the two are one object). Everything else here — the `SJFrame extends SFrame` shape, the default-button Enter shortcut, the typed-holder split with the emulator — still stands.

> **Note (SFrame/SJFrame separation).** SD_sjframe originally framed SJFrame as "SFrame + lazy root pane" — SFrame already carried `defaultCloseOperation` + content-pane routing under SD_sframe, and SJFrame just layered the JRootPane holder on top. After the cleanup that separated the JFrame-only surface from the Frame analog, **SJFrame now also owns `defaultCloseOperation` + `WindowConstants` validation + close-op throw + `contentPane` field + `getContentPane`/`setContentPane` + `rootPaneCheckingEnabled` + add/remove routing + `getUIClassID` returning `"FrameUI"`**. The decision text below preserves SD_sjframe's original "SFrame already owns contentPane" framing for cross-reference; treat those references as describing the pre-separation state. The post-separation state: SJFrame owns the full JFrame surface; SFrame is the bare Frame+Window analog.
>
> The emulator-thin-down section below also reads slightly differently post-separation: `:emulators.swing.JFrame.setDefaultCloseOperation` now delegates to `SJFrame.setDefaultCloseOperation` (not `SFrame.setDefaultCloseOperation`); the SD_sframe emulator-thin-down line about `SFrame.setDefaultCloseOperation` is implicitly redirected.

**Decision.** Fourth concrete surrogate (pair). Closes the last `SFrame`/SD_sframe-deferred gap and the last bare-minimum emulator carve-out for top-level Window+Frame+JFrame handling.

- **`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJRootPane extends com.vaadin.flow.component.html.Div implements JComponentMixin`** carries the `javax.swing.JRootPane` surface: content/layered/glass pane references (typed as Vaadin `Component`), default-button wiring with the Enter-key shortcut install, `"defaultButton"` PCE, the `NONE` / `FRAME` / `PLAIN_DIALOG` / … decoration-style int constants, and `getUIClassID() == "RootPaneUI"`. Null rejection on `setContentPane` / `setLayeredPane` / `setGlassPane` throws `java.awt.IllegalComponentStateException` (matching JDK and the existing emulator). The Div base is deliberately non-structural — nothing renders under it. It exists so SJRootPane is itself a Vaadin component (holds client properties, borders, PCE through `JComponentMixin`) and so the emulator's `JRootPane` can use it as peer under the SJSlider / SJSpinner / SFrame thin-shell pattern.
- **`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJFrame extends SFrame`** adds a lazy `SJRootPane` (built on first `getRootPane()` — AWT contract is not to allocate until read), a `createRootPane()` factory hook for subclasses, and layered/glass-pane pass-through accessors delegating to `getRootPane()`. Content-pane access continues to go through `SFrame` directly — SFrame already owns the peer-attached `contentPane` Div and is the browser-side source of truth — but `setContentPane(Div)` is overridden to mirror into the existing `rootPane` so `frame.getRootPane().getContentPane()` stays in lockstep with `frame.getContentPane()`. `setContentPane` invoked before first `getRootPane()` call doesn't eagerly construct the root pane; the lazy seed happens at read time.

**Enter-shortcut install is the only browser-side concern.** Layered and glass panes are server-side holders only — JDK uses them for z-order and mouse-event overlay, neither of which maps onto Vaadin 25 cleanly (same reasoning D_rootpane_holder gave for the emulator's D_rootpane_holder holder). The `SJRootPane.setDefaultButton(Button)` path installs `Shortcuts.addShortcutListener(button, SHelper.callSwing(button::click), Key.ENTER)` with the Vaadin Button as the lifecycle owner — UI-scoped (WHEN_IN_FOCUSED_WINDOW shape), and torn down when the button detaches or gets collected. Replacing the default button removes the previous shortcut before installing the new one so Enter doesn't briefly double-trigger during the swap.

**Emulator thin-down.** Mirrors SJSlider / SJSpinner / SFrame (SD_sjslider / SD_sjspinner / SD_sframe):

- `:emulators.swing.JRootPane` swaps peer from a plain `Div` to `SJRootPane`. `setDefaultButton(JButton)` now delegates the browser-side shortcut install to the surrogate (`surrogate.setDefaultButton((Button) button.getPeer())`); the emulator keeps its own `defaultButton` field + `"defaultButton"` PCE so migrated PropertyChangeListener registrations on `JRootPane` still hear the event on their own layer (SD_sjslider/SD_sjspinner/SD_sframe independent-layer shape). Content / layered / glass pane accessors continue to return emulator AWT-typed objects (`vaadinx.awt.Container` / `vaadinx.swing.JLayeredPane` / `vaadinx.awt.Component`) from locally-held fields — the surrogate's Vaadin-typed holders are for pure-surrogate users; emulator users see the AWT-typed surface verbatim. Same "typed-holder split" reasoning SJSlider applies to its numeric model (SJSlider owns the `BoundedRangeModel` on the surrogate side; emulator-side listeners fire on their own layer).
- `:emulators.swing.JFrame` swaps peer from `SFrame` to `SJFrame`. `getRootPane()` still returns a `vaadinx.swing.JRootPane` (the emulator wrapper), and the two layers hold one SJRootPane: the emulator's root pane builds it and `JFrame.setRootPane` hands it to `SJFrame.setRootPane` (D_rootpane_containment). Layered/glass pane accessors on JFrame route through the JRootPane wrapper as today; no new code paths, just a peer swap behind the scenes. `frameInit` / `contentPane` / `rootPaneCheckingEnabled` unchanged — content-pane routing keeps working through SFrame's (now SJFrame's) existing add/remove overrides.

**Package split (matches SD_sframe's one-concrete-plus-mixin shape for Window).** One concrete `SJRootPane`, no `RootPaneMixin`. Unlike Window's SD_sframe split, there's no prospective JDK class (JInternalFrame, JApplet) sharing the JRootPane surface that would make a mixin earn its keep — JDK JApplet is a deprecated applet-era class we won't port, JInternalFrame is a distinct MDI surface with its own listener set. If that ever changes, factoring the listener families into a RootPaneMixin is a mechanical refactor (same shape SD_sframe leaves the door open for SDialog).

**Rationale.**
- **SJRootPane is non-structural.** JDK structure is Frame → JRootPane → {layeredPane → contentPane, glassPane}. The browser-side equivalent is a flat Dialog → contentPane tree; SFrame already renders this correctly via its `contentPane` Div. Putting SJRootPane on the DOM between would require restructuring SFrame and buying nothing for users — nobody can observe the difference because we don't z-order and glass-pane mouse overlay isn't plumbed. The server-side holder shape is what D_rootpane_holder already chose for the emulator; SD_sjframe continues that stance for the surrogate layer with the one addition that the holder itself is now a Vaadin component (for JComponentMixin benefits).
- **SJFrame extends SFrame (inheritance), not composition.** Matches the JDK shape — JFrame IS-A Frame IS-A Window. A composition ("SFrame delegates to an inner SJFrame") would invert the direction: SFrame is the Window+Frame base and SJFrame is the JFrame-rooted refinement that adds the JRootPane surface on top. SJFrame uses SFrame's `setContentPane(Div)` / `getContentPane()` seam exactly as the SFrame class javadoc anticipated.
- **Lazy rootPane + content-pane mirror on setContentPane.** AWT contract for JRootPane: accessors lazy-construct on first read (allocating three panes at every JFrame construction is wasteful — most JFrames never reach through `getRootPane()`). SJFrame's override of `setContentPane(Div)` must keep the invariant "once you've read the root pane, its `getContentPane()` matches the frame's" even across content-pane swaps, or migrated code that alternates `frame.getContentPane()` / `frame.getRootPane().getContentPane()` reads will see drift. Mirror-on-write is cheaper than a listener-based reactive propagation and matches what the user would do manually.
- **Emulator-side content/layered/glass holders stay emulator-typed.** Routing these through the surrogate means every access crosses the AWT-Component ↔ Vaadin-Component boundary (the emulator JRootPane would need to unwrap/rewrap `vaadinx.awt.Container` ↔ the surrogate's typed contentPane at every call). That's friction for zero payoff: content/layered/glass pane rendering isn't driven by browser-side behavior (just storage). Keeping the AWT-typed holders on the emulator and the Vaadin-typed holders on the surrogate respects the "typed-holder split" precedent SJSlider set with its BoundedRangeModel (model lives on the surrogate, emulator-side ChangeListeners fire independently). Default-button is the only member with real browser-side wiring, so default-button is the only member the emulator delegates through.
- **Default button PCE fires on both layers.** Same SD_sjslider / SD_sjspinner / SD_sframe convention: emulator and surrogate each carry independent `"defaultButton"` PCE listener lists; a migrator who listens on one layer hears only that layer's fire. Adding the same listener to both fires twice (accepted two-layer cost, documented on the class javadoc).
- **Shortcut install on the Vaadin Button directly, not on SJFrame.** Matches the emulator's existing behavior: the Button is the lifecycle owner so when it detaches or gets GC'd, the Enter shortcut disappears. Scoping on SJFrame would tie the shortcut to the frame's lifetime, which is wrong for a default button that gets replaced mid-frame.
- **No RootPaneMixin.** One concrete class, no prospective second consumer — no JWindow or JInternalFrame on the backfill queue, and JApplet is dead. The default-method surface would be a single interface with one implementor; the indirection buys nothing today and can be factored out mechanically if a second consumer ever lands.

**Alternatives.**
- **Structural SJRootPane** (SJRootPane as the Dialog's child, contentPane as SJRootPane's child, glass-pane as absolutely-positioned sibling). Rejected — requires reshaping SFrame's content-pane plumbing (adding a pane-between step), no observable benefit without a glass-pane overlay use case, and collides with Vaadin's Dialog overlay markup. D_rootpane_holder's server-side-holder stance was chosen against this same alternative on the emulator side; SD_sjframe continues it.
- **Bundle SJRootPane into SFrame directly (no SJFrame).** Rejected — conflates the Window+Frame (JDK AWT) layer with the JRootPane (JDK Swing) layer. SFrame needs to be usable without a JRootPane (e.g. a future `SDialog` surrogate would extend `SFrame` but a JDialog doesn't necessarily need a root-pane backfill either way); forcing root-pane surface onto every SFrame user adds drag for no payoff. Inheritance keeps concerns split.
- **Emulator JFrame delegates getRootPane() through a local JRootPane+SJRootPane-linked pair**, where the emulator's JRootPane both has an SJRootPane peer and holds its own AWT-typed pane fields (the proposed design). Adopted — middle ground between "full delegation everywhere" (too much type-bridging for no value) and "no surrogate at all for this component" (violates the surrogate-first policy for backfills). Same shape SJSlider ended up with: browser-side wiring on the surrogate, emulator-layer convenience typing on the emulator.
- **Make SJRootPane extend a non-Vaadin holder class, not Div.** Rejected — loses JComponentMixin (client properties, border CSS round-trip, PCE via `ComponentUtil.setData`). A non-Vaadin class would need to reimplement PceSupport lookup + ClientPropertyStore etc. The unrendered-Div trade-off (one empty element in the DOM but never reached by the Dialog's contentPane attach path) is negligible — SFrame doesn't `.add` the SJRootPane under the Dialog, so the Div never actually lands in the live DOM.
- **Drop the `createRootPane()` factory.** Rejected — JDK JFrame exposes it as a subclass hook; migrated code that overrides it (custom root-pane implementations, L&F experiments) would fail to recompile without it. Keeping the hook is zero cost for callers who don't use it.
- **Wire shortcut against SJFrame, not the Button.** Rejected — ties the Enter shortcut to the frame's lifetime instead of the button's. Replacing the default button would leak the old shortcut until the frame disposes; detaching the button (e.g. `contentPane.remove(button)`) would leave a dangling Enter→click on a detached button. Vaadin's Shortcuts lifecycle keys off the `listenOn` target, so the Button is the correct owner.

**Accepted limitations.**
- **~~No structural layered / glass-pane rendering.~~** **Glass pane superseded by [SD_glasspane_structural](#SD_glasspane_structural) / [D_glasspane_structural](../emulators/decisions.md#D_glasspane_structural)** — the glass pane is now a structural Dialog-child curtain (`emul-glasspane` CSS + `pointer-events` blocking), the "absolute-positioning machinery" this limitation anticipated. The **layered pane stays a holder**: z-order still doesn't render and `setLayer(...)` is an API round-trip (D_glasspane_structural/SD_glasspane_structural keep it deferred — no consumer, and a structural port needs the content-pane reparenting SD_sjframe rejected).
- **Default-button Enter shortcut covers Enter only, no modifiers.** Matches the emulator's existing behavior and the `Shortcuts.addShortcutListener(..., Key.ENTER)` signature. JDK allows alternate default-key bindings via L&F; we have no L&F and no need for alternate keys.
- **Two-layer default-button PCE fan-out.** See Rationale. A listener registered on both `vaadinx.swing.JRootPane` and `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJRootPane` fires twice on `setDefaultButton`. Migration convention: register on one side only — which matches SJSlider / SJSpinner / SFrame.
- **No second-consumer mixin.** If JInternalFrame / JApplet / custom MDI surrogates eventually want the JRootPane surface, SD_sjframe will be revisited to extract a `RootPaneMixin` from SJRootPane's body. Mechanical refactor; not pre-empted here.
- ~~**Pure-surrogate SJRootPane rendering.**~~ **Retired by [SD_sjrootpane_structural](#SD_sjrootpane_structural).** A standalone `new SJRootPane()` used to render an empty Div because it did not auto-host its own content pane; the root pane now owns the whole chain structurally, so dropping one into any Vaadin container gives a working root-pane-alike with no manual `appendChild`. The one half a standalone pane still cannot supply is the host-side `emul-has-contentpane` class, which sizes the chain against a window body.

---

<a id="SD_sjbutton"></a>
## SD_sjbutton. `SJButton` surrogate + `AbstractButtonMixin` — mixin-first, R_vaadin_first-trimmed surface

**Decision.** Fifth concrete surrogate, first AbstractButton-lineage backfill. Covers `javax.swing.JButton` via `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJButton extends com.vaadin.flow.component.button.Button implements AbstractButtonMixin`, with the AbstractButton API surface lifted into the new `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.swing.AbstractButtonMixin` (extends `JComponentMixin`) ahead of SJToggleButton / SJCheckBox / SJRadioButton. Scoped per [R_vaadin_first](../CLAUDE.md): UI functionality is in (ButtonModel state machine + listener fan-out + Action dispatch + mnemonic install); API round-trip for state Vaadin has no counterpart for is **not** — those setters drop-and-WARN on non-default values.

- **`AbstractButtonMixin`** splits its surface cleanly along R_vaadin_first:
  - *UI-functional (kept as full-behaviour implementations):* `text` (written to + read from `getElement().getProperty("text")` — Vaadin-first), `actionCommand`, `hideActionText`, `mnemonic` (drives Vaadin `Shortcuts` Alt+VK → `doClick`), {@link javax.swing.Action} install + PCL propagation (NAME / MNEMONIC_KEY / ACTION_COMMAND_KEY / SHORT_DESCRIPTION / enabled / SMALL_ICON / LARGE_ICON_KEY / DISPLAYED_MNEMONIC_INDEX_KEY), {@link javax.swing.ButtonModel} as source of truth (default `DefaultButtonModel`) with three model→button listener wires for {@link java.awt.event.ActionListener} / {@link javax.swing.event.ChangeListener} / {@link java.awt.event.ItemListener} fan-out re-sourced to the button.
  - *Drop-and-WARN per R_vaadin_first:* the six state-conditional icon setters (`setPressedIcon` / `setSelectedIcon` / `setDisabledIcon` / `setDisabledSelectedIcon` / `setRolloverIcon` / `setRolloverSelectedIcon` — Vaadin Button has no per-state icon slots); the four visual flags (`borderPainted`, `contentAreaFilled`, `focusPainted`, `rolloverEnabled`); `margin`; `multiClickThreshhold`; `displayedMnemonicIndex`. Plain `setIcon(Icon)` is **not** R_vaadin_first-dropped — Vaadin `Button.setIcon(Component)` is a wireable counterpart via the emulators icon-rendering Path 1 `ImageIcon`→Vaadin `Image` translation, so under SD_vaadin_first_binding it binds Vaadin-first with no shadow store. The four alignment setters (`horizontalAlignment` / `verticalAlignment` / `horizontalTextPosition` / `verticalTextPosition`) and `iconTextGap` are also Vaadin-wired (CSS), not R_vaadin_first-dropped — see "Layout — inline-flex" below. Setter pattern (for the still-R_vaadin_first-dropped set): validate where Swing would (R_match_swing_errors IAE preserved — threshhold ≥ 0, mnemonic-index bounds), then silently no-op if the caller passed the JDK default (so `setAction`'s clear-to-default sweep doesn't trip WARNs on every Action install), else `SHelper.onUnimplemented` + drop. Getter pattern: return the JDK default constant (-1 for displayedMnemonicIndex, true / true / true / false for the four flags, null for margin, 0 for multiClickThreshhold, null for the six state-conditional icons).
- **Layout — inline-flex, lossy CSS round-trip per R_vaadin_first.** Vaadin Button's host is `display: inline-flex` per Lumo, so `flex-direction` / `justify-content` / `align-items` / `gap` written inline on the host control how the icon and text children lay out. Each setter writes its slice of host CSS directly; getters lossy-parse CSS back — same shape as `setBorder` / `getBorder` per [SD_border_css_lossy](#SD_border_css_lossy). `installButtonBindings` seeds JDK AbstractButton's defaults (CENTER / CENTER / TRAILING / CENTER + 4px) into CSS once. Flex-direction logic: vertical text-position non-CENTER promotes to column / column-reverse (overrides horizontal); CENTER keeps the row axis with TRAILING/RIGHT → `row` (icon-then-text, JDK default) or LEADING/LEFT/CENTER → `row-reverse`. **No shadow store**: LEFT and LEADING both write `flex-start` and read back as LEADING (canonical); RIGHT and TRAILING write `flex-end` and read back as TRAILING; column-shape flex-direction reports `getHorizontalTextPosition() == TRAILING` (the row-axis value can't be reconstructed from column CSS alone). The lossy round-trip is exactly what R_vaadin_first names "minor round-trip loss is accepted and expected" — comparable to `setText(null)` reading back as `""` or `setBorder` lossy CSS-parse. Future AbstractButton-lineage surrogates whose Vaadin host doesn't honor host flex (e.g. `SJCheckBox` on Vaadin `Checkbox`'s shadow DOM) override the layout setters to write CSS parts or skip the dispatch.
- **`ButtonStateStore`** (in `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.internal`) carries only UI-functional fields: `actionCommand`, `hideActionText`, `mnemonic` + its `mnemonicRegistration`, `action` + its PCL, `model` + the model→button listener triple, the shared `EventListenerList`. No text field (Vaadin's element property holds it); no icon / visual-flag / margin / displayedMnemonicIndex / multiClickThreshhold shadows; **no layout-field shadows** — alignment / text-position / iconTextGap live in CSS only per R_vaadin_first.
- **`SJButton`** adds only the JButton-specific slice on top of the mixin: the five JDK constructors (routing through `installButtonBindings()` and then `setText` / `setIcon` per `AbstractButton.init`), `defaultCapable` + `"defaultCapable"` PCE, `isDefaultButton()` returning `false` (default-button identity lives on SJRootPane per SD_sjframe), `getUIClassID() == "ButtonUI"`, a JButton-flavoured `paramString`, and the three explicit overrides (`getText` / `setText` / `setEnabled`) that redirect into the mixin to defeat Java's "classes beat interfaces" resolution rule (Vaadin `Button` declares concrete versions of those three).
- **Peer click → Swing ActionEvent** flows through `installButtonBindings()`: registers `ClickNotifier.addClickListener(e -> SHelper.callSwing(this::synthesizePeerClick))`. `synthesizePeerClick()` drives the installed `ButtonModel` through the JDK armed↔pressed↔armed pulse so the model's internal `fireActionPerformed` reaches our model-action-listener wire, which re-sources to `SJButton` and fans out to the button-level ActionListener list. Programmatic `doClick()` takes the same path (minus the peer visit), so user-click and `doClick` deliver identical ActionEvent shapes.

**Rationale.**
- **Mixin-first from day one** (departure from SJSlider's inline-first shape). AbstractButton has three confirmed future consumers on the backfill queue — SJToggleButton, SJCheckBox, SJRadioButton — each of which would force an inline-to-mixin refactor of SJButton. Extracting the surface upfront spares that refactor. SJSlider's inline shape was right for JSlider because no second consumer was lined up; SJButton's is different.
- **ButtonModel as source of truth, DefaultButtonModel by default.** JDK's AbstractButton wires its fire-helpers through the model (`createActionListener` / `createChangeListener` / `createItemListener`); user code that installs its own `ButtonModel` expects the model's state to drive the button. Flattening to fields would require reimplementing DefaultButtonModel's pulse semantics and diverge from `setModel`-using migrated code. **Whose model it is depends on the stage:** a stage-3 SJButton builds and owns its own; at stage 2 the emulator owns the model and hands it over through `setModel`, so the surrogate's click pulses and renders the emulator's ([D_emulator_button_model](../emulators/decisions.md#D_emulator_button_model)). `setModel` has the component take the model's enabled state, as the JDK's does, so handing a model over never writes into it. Reusing `DefaultButtonModel` gets the full selected/armed/pressed/rollover state machine for free. This is a clear example of R_vaadin_first-protected UI state: the model drives event delivery, it's not "API round-trip for its own sake."
- **R_vaadin_first trim — drop-and-WARN instead of shadow cache (initial), with subsequent graduations.** Initial SJButton shipped with a full `ButtonStateStore` that shadowed every AbstractButton field. R_vaadin_first review trimmed it: state-conditional icons / visual flags / margin / multiClick / displayedMnemonicIndex have no Vaadin counterpart and dropped to drop-and-WARN; setters only WARN on non-default values so `setAction`'s clear-to-default sweep doesn't spam WARNs. Subsequent graduations under R_vaadin_first's "Vaadin counterpart materialised → wire it" rule: *plain `setIcon(Icon)`* (D_icon_rendering — `Button.setIcon(Component)` via the icon-rendering bridge); *the four alignment setters + `iconTextGap`* (Vaadin Button's host is `display: inline-flex` per Lumo, so `flex-direction` / `justify-content` / `align-items` / `gap` written inline drive icon-text layout). Both graduations bind Vaadin-first with no shadow store: setters write CSS, getters lossy-parse CSS back. The layout fields' lossy round-trip (LEADING ↔ LEFT both as `flex-start`, vertical text-position masking horizontal) follows the [SD_border_css_lossy](#SD_border_css_lossy) `setBorder` precedent — R_vaadin_first explicitly accepts "minor round-trip loss." An earlier shape of this graduation shadow-stored the layout fields in `ButtonStateStore` framed as an `OpaqueStore`-shape carve-out; that was retracted on R_vaadin_first re-review — `OpaqueStore` itself was subsequently dropped on the same reasoning (opaque state lives in CSS `background-color`, no shadow cache needed; "delayed internal state not yet resolvable from Vaadin state" turned out to have no surviving instance once each candidate was reviewed). Visual flags, state-conditional icons, margin, multiClickThreshhold, displayedMnemonicIndex still have no Vaadin counterpart and stay drop-and-WARN.
- **`text` is mixin-abstract; concrete surrogates implement via their HasText.** Initial post-R_vaadin_first implementation read/wrote `getElement().getProperty("text")` / `setProperty("text", ...)` from the mixin's default to avoid a virtual-dispatch loop with `SJButton`'s override. **Latent bug:** Vaadin Button reads its rendered caption from an internal `Text` component (`textNode` / `textSupport`), not from a `text` element property — round-trip via the property worked but the browser caption never updated. A subsequent fix attempted to wire HasText from the mixin via an `instanceof` cast guarded by a `ButtonStateStore.bypassTextDispatch` reentry flag — correct, but conceptually heavy: three invocations of SJButton.setText per user call, distinguished by the flag's state. **Final shape:** `getText()` and `setText(String)` are *abstract* on the mixin; the concrete surrogate provides them. SJButton's inherited `Button.getText()` (textNode-backed) satisfies the abstract `getText` automatically; SJButton.setText overrides Button.setText to call super (which renders correctly via textSupport) and then fires the `"text"` PCE. No flag, no loop, no three-deep dispatch — each surrogate owns its text path end-to-end. Future SJToggleButton / SJCheckBox / SJRadioButton each implement setText similarly (call super + fire PCE). `setText(null)` still round-trips as `""` per R_vaadin_first lossy direction; fresh `SJButton().getText()` returns `""` (Vaadin's empty textNode), not `null`.
- **`getIcon()` / `getUI()` dropped from the mixin.** Vaadin `Button.getIcon()` returns `com.vaadin.flow.component.Component`; JDK `AbstractButton.getIcon()` returns `javax.swing.Icon`. The JVM forbids two methods with the same erased signature and different return types, and `Component` / `Icon` are not covariantly related — a concrete surrogate `extends Button` simply cannot declare both. Same story for `getUI()` (Vaadin returns `Optional<UI>`, JDK returns `ButtonUI`). **The JDK-typed setter overload survives** because `setIcon(javax.swing.Icon)` is overload-safe against Vaadin's `setIcon(Component)` (parameter types differ) — pure-surrogate users access the icon as Vaadin-typed `Component` through the inherited Button getter, and reading back the JDK `Icon` would have to reverse-translate Vaadin Image → JDK Icon (lossy enough that a parallel API path adds no surface migrators rely on). Recording the limitation here so future surrogates extending Vaadin classes with getter-name clashes know the convention: drop the getter, keep the setter as an overload, document the Vaadin-typed access path for pure-surrogate users.
- **SJButton overrides for `setText` / `setEnabled`.** Java's resolution rule picks a concrete class method over a default interface method, and Vaadin `Button` declares both concretely. `setText` overrides to add the `"text"` PCE on top of Vaadin Button's textSupport-based write (mixin declares the method abstract; SJButton's override IS the implementation). `setEnabled` overrides to redirect into the mixin chain so SD_auto_pce's auto-PCE fires (`HasEnabled`'s default doesn't). `getText` doesn't need an override — Button's inherited `getText()` satisfies the mixin's abstract method directly, and JDK doesn't fire PCE on getText. Future AbstractButton-subclass surrogates on Vaadin classes that declare concrete `setText` / `setEnabled` carry the same two-method override mechanically.
- **Mnemonic via Vaadin `Shortcuts` on the button peer.** Matches SJRootPane's default-button Enter-install shape. The Vaadin Button is the lifecycle owner — detach or GC tears the shortcut down. Alternative attach points (the frame, the UI) tie the shortcut to the wrong lifetime. Scope is UI-wide by default (Shortcuts' default `listenOn` = the UI); that's closer to JDK's `WHEN_IN_FOCUSED_WINDOW` than either `WHEN_FOCUSED` or `WHEN_ANCESTOR_OF_FOCUSED_COMPONENT` for mnemonics on buttons in a single top-level Frame.
- **`isDefaultButton()` returns false — default-button identity lives on SJRootPane.** Per SD_sjframe the root pane owns default-button state (the Enter-shortcut install is keyed off the button, but the identity "which button is the default" lives in `SJRootPane.defaultButton`). A button has no server-side back-ref to its owning root pane, so SJButton can't answer `isDefaultButton()` authoritatively; returning false matches the emulator and keeps the R_best_effort_behaviour trade-off minimal (migrated code branching on `isDefaultButton` takes the non-default path, which is the same thing Swing would do before `setDefaultButton` is called).

**Alternatives.**
- **Shadow-cache every AbstractButton field for setter/getter round-trip** (the pre-R_vaadin_first initial shape). Rejected — R_vaadin_first names this as the wrong stance: API round-trip is not a goal when Vaadin has no counterpart; storage exists only for UI-functional state or delayed-internal-state carve-outs. The pre-R_vaadin_first store had ~25 fields, 15 of which were pure setter-echo shadows with no UI effect. Trimmed to ~10 per R_vaadin_first's direct-binding default.
- **Inline AbstractButton surface in SJButton, extract mixin when second consumer lands** (SJSlider's shape). Rejected — three known next consumers (SJToggleButton, SJCheckBox, SJRadioButton) all queued in the backfill. The mixin extraction would happen later regardless; doing it now spares the refactor pass. SJSlider-style inline is correct when the scope is "one concrete subclass in the foreseeable future"; SJButton's isn't.
- **Flatten ButtonModel into ButtonStateStore fields.** Rejected — user code installing a custom `ButtonModel` expects model state to drive the button (armed/pressed/selected transitions firing through `ChangeListener`, ActionEvent source routing through the model's `fireActionPerformed`). Reimplementing DefaultButtonModel's state machine locally would diverge from that contract, and the model drives UI (event delivery) which R_vaadin_first explicitly keeps in scope.
- **Synthesize ActionEvent directly from peer click** (skip the model pulse, mirror the emulator's pre-surrogate shape). Rejected for the same reason — `ChangeListener` and `ItemListener` subscribers would get no state-transition events since the model never sees the click. The pulse path delivers all three event families off one peer click; the direct path would need to re-fire each family separately at different points in SJButton's internal state machine.
- **WARN on every call to the drop-and-WARN setters, including default values.** Rejected — `setAction`'s `configurePropertiesFromAction` normalises values to JDK defaults on every install (clears icon to null, mnemonic to 0, displayedMnemonicIndex to -1, etc.), so a WARN-on-every-call policy would trip a burst of WARNs every time a user attaches an Action with no icon/mnemonic. Gating WARN on non-default values silences the common "Action without those keys" path while still surfacing "user tried to configure something we don't render."
- **Rename the mixin's clashing getters** (`getSwingIcon()` / `getSwingUI()`). Rejected — breaks the import-swap contract (migrated code calls `btn.getIcon()`). The drop-and-document path is closer to JDK-source-compatible at the cost of Vaadin-Component-typed return from `getIcon()` on pure-surrogate users, which is a narrower break than a rename affecting every migrator.

**Accepted limitations.**
- **`getIcon()` and `getUI()` on SJButton return Vaadin types, not JDK types.** See Rationale. The JDK-typed setters exist as overloads but are drop-and-WARN (R_vaadin_first), so pure-surrogate users lose nothing functionally. Migration impact: migrated code doing `Icon i = btn.getIcon()` against an SJButton won't compile; against a `:emulators.JButton` it works unchanged.
- **`setText(null) → getText() == ""`** and **fresh-construction `getText() == ""`** (not `null`). R_vaadin_first-accepted lossy round-trips, both surfaced by the switch from element-property to HasText-based text dispatch. Emulator preserves null; pure-surrogate use accepts `""`. Migrated code that null-checks text on SJButton needs `""`-checking too, or access via the emulator layer.
- **R_vaadin_first drop-and-WARN setters do not round-trip.** A user calling `sjButton.setSelectedIcon(icon); sjButton.getSelectedIcon()` would get back `null`, not `icon`. Same for the other five state-conditional icons, the four visual flags, margin, multiClickThreshhold, and displayedMnemonicIndex. The WARN surfaces the drop; the getter returns the JDK default. A user-code-side cache is the workaround if round-trip is needed. Plain `setIcon(Icon)`, the four alignment setters, and `iconTextGap` round-trip via Vaadin (CSS), with R_vaadin_first-accepted lossy mapping — see "Layout — inline-flex" above.
- **Mnemonic is Alt+VK on the Button peer, not the owning window.** JDK's `WHEN_IN_FOCUSED_WINDOW` scopes the accelerator to the entire focused window; Vaadin's Shortcuts default-scopes to the UI (typically one active UI per window in practice). Close enough for R_best_effort_behaviour — Alt+S fires the Save button whether the button itself is focused or the focus is on a text field in the same frame — but a multi-UI / multi-window app could see `Alt+VK` trigger buttons in a non-focused window. Documented on the class javadoc; not fixed upstream-ready.
- **`isDefaultButton()` returns false even when SJRootPane does install this button as its default.** See Rationale. A migrator relying on the query can bridge through the root pane (`frame.getRootPane().getDefaultButton() == btn`). The false return never causes a wrong-branch crash — `setDefaultButton` is inert on the button side, so "not default" is a safe conservative answer.
- **Pure-surrogate click does not drive mnemonic on a detached button.** The mnemonic shortcut is installed via Vaadin `Shortcuts` on the peer — if the SJButton is never attached to a UI, the accelerator doesn't fire. Programmatic `doClick` still works. Migrated code that builds buttons in a container and then attaches the container to a UI gets the accelerator as soon as the attach propagates, same shape Swing would have.
- **Two-layer PCE fan-out.** A PropertyChangeListener registered on both the emulator's JButton and its SJButton peer fires twice per change for UI-functional properties (`"text"` / `"action"` / `"mnemonic"` / `"actionCommand"` / `"model"` / `"defaultCapable"`). Drop-and-WARN setters fire no PCE on the surrogate side (no state, no change), so those don't multi-fire. Migration convention: register on one layer only — matches SJSlider / SJSpinner / SFrame / SJRootPane.

---

<a id="SD_sjlabel"></a>
## SD_sjlabel. `SJLabel` surrogate — `NativeLabel` base, manual DOM children for icon + text coexistence

**Decision.** Sixth concrete surrogate, third backfill (after SJButton). `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJLabel extends com.vaadin.flow.component.html.NativeLabel implements JComponentMixin` — chosen over `Span` because NativeLabel renders as `<label>` and exposes `setFor(Component)` for the JDK `labelFor` accessibility wiring. The DOM model:

- A stable inner `Span` (`textSpan`) holds the text content. SJLabel's `setText`/`getText` overrides write/read through it.
- An optional second child element holds the icon (a Vaadin `Image` from the icon-rendering bridge). Inserted at index 0 so the visual order is icon-then-text — matches JDK's `horizontalTextPosition == TRAILING` default.

The R_vaadin_first split:

- **UI-functional / wired:** `text` (via `textSpan.setText/getText`), `icon` (via [`Icons.toVaadinIconComponent`](../emulators/decisions.md#D_icon_rendering) — Path 1), `labelFor` (via `NativeLabel.setFor(String)` after auto-minting an id on the target if missing), and the four alignment / text-position setters + `iconTextGap` (driven through `display: inline-flex` CSS — see "Layout — inline-flex" below).
- **Bucket B / deliberate-noop ([SD_api_three_buckets](#SD_api_three_buckets), `SHelper.onNoop`):** `displayedMnemonic` and `displayedMnemonicIndex`. R_match_swing_errors IAE on out-of-range index is preserved. The visual underline has no Vaadin counterpart and the labelFor-targeted Alt+VK accelerator needs focus integration that Vaadin's `<label for>` doesn't surface client-side.

Emulator-side JLabel swaps peer from `Span` to `SJLabel`. `setText` and `setIcon` push through to the surrogate; `setLabelFor` and the alignment / mnemonic / iconTextGap setters round-trip on the emulator's own field shadow (preserving JDK contract — null text, alignment values, etc.) without pushing to the surrogate (the surrogate would drop them anyway). Two-layer field shadow + R_swing_is_truth sync keeps null text round-trip honest on the emulator side; pure-surrogate users see the R_vaadin_first lossy `null → ""` pattern.

**Rationale.**
- **`NativeLabel` over `Span`.** JLabel's primary structural relationship beyond text/icon is `labelFor` — a `<label for="…">` accessibility wire that screen readers + form-association logic depend on. NativeLabel's `setFor` is the direct counterpart; Span doesn't carry `for=`. Visual rendering is identical for inline text (display: inline by default), so the labelFor wire is the deciding factor with no downside.
- **Manual DOM children, not `HasText.setText`.** Vaadin `HasText.setText` calls `Element.setText` which removes *all* children (text nodes and elements). Naive prepend-Image-then-setText would clobber the image on the next text update. The inner `textSpan` strategy keeps text writes scoped to the span's text node, leaving any icon sibling untouched. This is the pattern the SJLabel javadoc names as "the load-bearing slice"; tests guard the regression both directions (`setIcon then setText keeps both`, `setText then setIcon keeps text`).
- **Auto-mint id for `labelFor` target.** Vaadin `NativeLabel.setFor(Component)` requires the target to have an explicit `id` set; JDK `JLabel.setLabelFor(Component)` does not. To match the JDK contract under the import-swap rule, SJLabel auto-mints `"emul-labelfor-N"` ids on targets without one. The counter is class-static `AtomicLong` — collisions across labels in the same session are avoided; cross-restart collisions don't matter. Storage of the original `Component` reference is the JDK-contract-honoring shadow; the auto-id is downstream plumbing.
- **Layout — inline-flex, lossy CSS round-trip per R_vaadin_first.** Host `<label>` runs as `display: inline-flex` so all four alignment / text-position setters and `iconTextGap` map directly onto flex CSS. `verticalTextPosition` non-CENTER promotes the layout to `column` / `column-reverse` (overrides horizontalTextPosition); CENTER stays on the row axis with `flex-direction: row` (TRAILING/RIGHT, JDK default) or `row-reverse` (LEADING/LEFT/CENTER). `horizontalAlignment` → `justify-content`; `verticalAlignment` → `align-items`; `iconTextGap` → CSS `gap`. **No shadow store**: each setter writes its slice of host CSS; each getter lossy-parses CSS back — same shape as `setBorder` / `getBorder` per [SD_border_css_lossy](#SD_border_css_lossy). LEFT and LEADING both round-trip as LEADING (canonical), RIGHT and TRAILING as TRAILING; column-shape flex-direction reports `getHorizontalTextPosition() == TRAILING` (the row-axis value can't be reconstructed from column CSS alone — accepted lossy round-trip per R_vaadin_first's "minor round-trip loss is accepted and expected"). Constructors seed JDK JLabel defaults (LEADING / CENTER / TRAILING / CENTER + 4px gap; icon-only ctor overrides h-align to CENTER) into CSS once. R_match_swing_errors IAE preserved before any state mutation.
- **Mnemonic Bucket B.** JLabel's `displayedMnemonic` does two things in JDK: paints an underline under the indexed character, and (when `labelFor` is set) registers an Alt+VK accelerator that focuses the labelled component. Vaadin `<label>` has no underline rendering and no client-side accelerator hook for the for-relationship. Both halves are out of scope; the displayed-mnemonic-index is field-validated (R_match_swing_errors IAE for out-of-range) and dropped via `SHelper.onNoop`.
- **Two-layer storage shape mirrors SJSlider / SJButton.** The emulator keeps its own field shadow for text / icon / alignment / mnemonic / labelFor; the surrogate has its own layer only for the narrow set of fields R_vaadin_first actually justifies — `textSpan` (inner Span holding text), `iconChild` (current icon Component reference), `labelForTarget` (the Component ref `NativeLabel.getFor()` can't return). Alignment / text-position / iconTextGap live in CSS only on the surrogate side, lossy round-trip per R_vaadin_first; the emulator's own field shadow preserves the JDK-faithful values for stage-2 migrated code. Two-layer PCE fan-out (the SJButton accepted-limitation generalises): a listener registered on both layers fires twice. Migration convention stays "register on one layer only".

**Alternatives.**
- **Extend `Span` instead.** Rejected — labelFor wouldn't work without writing the `for=` attribute manually, and a `<span for=…>` is non-conformant HTML (`for=` is only valid on `<label>` and `<output>`). Screen-reader detection of label-control association would also miss; users of accessibility tooling against migrated apps would silently regress.
- **Inline icon as `setText`-friendly Unicode glyph (e.g., a leading char).** Rejected — JDK Icon contract is image data, not text; users passing an `ImageIcon("logo.png")` expect an image rendered, not a substituted character. Path 2's UIManager-keyed VaadinIcon does the closest thing for the OptionPane use case, and that's a separate slice anyway.
- **Render label via a wrapper `Div` with text+icon as named slots.** Rejected — pulls in custom CSS (slot definitions, named-slot ordering rules) and breaks the `labelFor` accessibility wire because the wrapping div isn't a `<label>`. The NativeLabel + manual children approach uses standard HTML semantics.
- **Flatten icon and text into a single `Element.setText`-driven content with a separate icon overlay.** Rejected — would require positioning hacks (CSS `::before` pseudo-element pushing the icon, or absolute positioning) and still wouldn't survive `setText` clobbering. The inner-Span approach is structurally simpler.

**Accepted limitations.**
- **`getText() == ""` on a default-constructed SJLabel.** The inner Span returns `""` for an empty text node; pure-surrogate `getText` echoes that. Emulator JLabel preserves null at its own field shadow, so two-layer migrated code sees null on the emulator side and `""` on the surrogate side. R_vaadin_first lossy-Vaadin-first round-trip per the same shape as SJButton's `setText(null) → ""`.
- **Alignment / text-position visible only with explicit label size.** `justify-content` and `align-items` are no-ops on a shrink-wrap container — a label without `setSize` lays out flush regardless. JDK has the same caveat (alignment only matters when the label's bounds extend beyond its content). Migrators depending on right-aligned label text get it as soon as a width is set on the label. R_match_swing_errors IAE on bad axis still fires so programming errors surface.
- **Mnemonic does not affect rendering.** `displayedMnemonic` / `displayedMnemonicIndex` setters silently drop via `SHelper.onNoop` (Bucket B / SD_api_three_buckets) — no underline, no accelerator. R_match_swing_errors IAE on out-of-range index preserved.
- **`labelFor` target gets a synthetic id.** Migrators inspecting the target component's id post-`setLabelFor` see the auto-minted `"emul-labelfor-N"` form; explicitly-set ids are preserved (the auto-mint only fires when the target has no id). Downstream code that depends on a specific id should set it before calling `setLabelFor`.
- **`getFor()` returns the Vaadin attribute string, not the original Component.** SJLabel's own `getLabelFor()` returns the originally-passed Component reference (shadow-stored). Inspecting via inherited `NativeLabel.getFor()` returns the resolved id string. R_vaadin_first lossy-direction analog.
- **Two-layer PCE fan-out for `text` / `icon` / `labelFor`.** Same shape as SJButton's accepted limitation. Listeners registered on both layers fire twice; the convention is to register on one.

**Validation.** Surrogate test suite: `SJLabelTest` covering ctors (text-only LEADING + icon-only CENTER), text+icon coexistence (both directions), Path 1 install + null clear + non-ImageIcon WARN, PCE shape (Vaadin Component-typed values for icon, JDK-int-typed for alignment / text-position), labelFor delegate to `NativeLabel.setFor` with id auto-mint + null clear, inline-flex layout dispatch (default row + flex-start + center; horizontalAlignment → justify-content; verticalAlignment → align-items; verticalTextPosition non-CENTER → column / column-reverse override; horizontalTextPosition → row / row-reverse; iconTextGap → CSS gap), Bucket B silence on mnemonic (R_match_swing_errors IAE preserved), happy-path zero-WARN. Emulator JLabelTest: existing 18 tests retained; updated 3 ("peer is an SJLabel surrogate", "setText null lands as empty string on the SJLabel peer", "setText reaches the rendered SJLabel"); added 3 ("setIcon ImageIcon installs Vaadin Image on the SJLabel peer per Path 1", "setIcon null clears the SJLabel peer's icon slot", "setText after setIcon keeps both rendered on the peer").

---

<a id="SD_toggle_checkbox_first_cut"></a>
## SD_toggle_checkbox_first_cut. `SJToggleButton` + `SJCheckBox` surrogates — `Checkbox` peer, `ToggleButtonModel` source of truth, ValueChange-only browser wire

**Decision.** Seventh + eighth concrete surrogates, second AbstractButton-lineage backfill (after SD_sjbutton's SJButton). Covers `JCheckBox` and `JToggleButton`. Both surrogates land in one slice; `SJCheckBox extends SJToggleButton` mirrors the JDK `JCheckBox extends JToggleButton` shape and avoids near-duplicate decision text. **Restructured by [SD_sjtogglebutton_button_peer](#SD_sjtogglebutton_button_peer):** SJToggleButton later moved onto a `Button` peer (pressed chrome) and SJCheckBox became a standalone Checkbox-peer surrogate, so the shared-base structure described below is historical — the Checkbox-peer toggle wiring itself is unchanged, it just lives on SJCheckBox directly now.

- **`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJToggleButton extends com.vaadin.flow.component.checkbox.Checkbox implements AbstractButtonMixin`.** Vaadin `Checkbox` is the only stock toggleable primitive Vaadin 25 ships and matches the existing emulator's R_best_effort_behaviour-accepted "JToggleButton renders as checkbox glyph" divergence. Provides `HasValue<Boolean>` (selection round-trip + browser-side toggle), `ClickNotifier<Checkbox>`, `Focusable<Checkbox>`, and `setLabel`/`getLabel` for the text surface. Does **not** implement `HasText`, has no `setIcon`, and renders its label inside shadow DOM — three properties that drive every override the surrogate makes against the mixin.
- **`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJCheckBox extends SJToggleButton`.** Mirrors JDK `JCheckBox extends JToggleButton`. Adds the JCheckBox-specific surface — `borderPaintedFlat` field + `"borderPaintedFlat"` PCE, `getUIClassID() == "CheckBoxUI"`, JCheckBox-flavoured `paramString` — and nothing else. The peer is still a Vaadin `Checkbox` (no separate concrete class); the inheritance + override surface is identical to SJToggleButton's.

**Override surface against `AbstractButtonMixin`** (the SD_sjbutton mixin lifted ahead of these consumers):

- **`installButtonBindings()` — fully overridden.** The mixin's default installs `DefaultButtonModel`, wires `ClickNotifier` to `synthesizePeerClick`, and seeds inline-flex CSS for the `display: inline-flex` Vaadin Button host. None of those fit a Checkbox peer. The override:
  1. Installs `javax.swing.JToggleButton.ToggleButtonModel` (JDK's static inner class) instead of `DefaultButtonModel`. ToggleButtonModel.setPressed(false) while armed flips selected, fires ItemEvent + ChangeEvent, then ActionEvent — exactly the "click toggles selection and fires Action" semantics user code expects from JToggleButton/JCheckBox.
  2. Skips the `ClickNotifier` wire and installs a `HasValue<Boolean> ValueChangeListener` instead. Browser-originated toggles arrive as ValueChangeEvent (reliable for both mouse-click and keyboard-Space on `<input type="checkbox">`); listening for `ClickEvent` would either double-fire (when both events arrive) or miss the keyboard path. The ValueChangeListener guards on `preventPeerEvents` and `model.isSelected() == newValue` (idempotency — avoid pulsing if model already in sync), then funnels through `SHelper.callSwing(this::synthesizePeerClick)` to drive the same model pulse a programmatic `doClick` would. Result: user-click and `doClick` deliver identical Item+Change+Action sequences.
  3. Skips the inline-flex CSS seed. Vaadin Checkbox's host element wraps a shadow-DOM `<input>` + label structure; host-level `flex-direction` / `justify-content` / `align-items` / `gap` don't propagate through the shadow root, so seeding them would write CSS the user can't visually achieve. The `getter` family below returns the JDK default constants directly instead.
- **`setText(String)` / `getText()` — fully overridden.** Mixin declares both `abstract` per [SD_sjbutton](#SD_sjbutton)'s "concrete owns its HasText path" rationale. Vaadin Checkbox is **not** `HasText` (it's *labelled*, not *texted*); the override routes through `setLabel(String)` / `getLabel()`, normalises null → `""` per R_vaadin_first lossy direction, and fires the `"text"` PCE. Same shape JDK has — Swing JCheckBox's "text" is what Vaadin calls a "label" — but the call goes to a different Vaadin method.
- **Model→peer push wire installed in `setModel`.** AbstractButtonMixin's `setModel` attaches three model→button bridge listeners (Change / Action / Item fan-out re-sourced to the button) but does **not** push selection to the peer — that's the concrete's job. SJToggleButton overrides `setModel` to additionally install a model→peer ItemListener that mirrors `model.isSelected()` into `Checkbox.setValue(...)` under `preventPeerEvents`, and tracks its teardown in a `Registration` so a subsequent `setModel` swap detaches cleanly.
