Programmatic `setSelected` (mixin → model.setSelected → ItemEvent → push wire → cb.setValue) and `doClick` (synthesizePeerClick → model pulse → ItemEvent → push wire → cb.setValue) both keep model + peer in lockstep without the user needing to do anything.
- **`setIcon(Icon)` — inherited mixin default already drops correctly.** AbstractButtonMixin's `setIcon` checks `if (_self() instanceof Button btn)` and binds Vaadin-first; the `else if (icon != null)` branch WARNs-and-drops. Since SJToggleButton extends `Checkbox` (not `Button`), the mixin's else branch hits naturally — no override needed. R_vaadin_first-accepted: Vaadin Checkbox has no icon slot; per-state icons inherited from the mixin stay drop-and-WARN as on SJButton.
- **Layout setters — fully overridden to drop-and-WARN.** Same shadow-DOM reasoning as `installButtonBindings`. `setHorizontalAlignment` / `setVerticalAlignment` / `setHorizontalTextPosition` / `setVerticalTextPosition` / `setIconTextGap` validate per R_match_swing_errors (IAE on invalid SwingConstants) then drop-and-WARN on non-default values; default values silently no-op so `setAction`'s clear-to-default sweep doesn't trip WARNs. Getters return the JDK default constants (CENTER / CENTER / TRAILING / CENTER + 4px gap, matching AbstractButton). The mixin's CSS-readback path is bypassed entirely — no inline-flex CSS gets written, so reading from CSS would just return defaults anyway, but the override makes the R_vaadin_first "no Vaadin counterpart" stance explicit at the API surface.
- **`setEnabled(boolean)` — overridden** (same Java "classes beat interfaces" defeat as SJButton). Vaadin Checkbox declares concrete `setEnabled` (via `HasEnabled`); the override redirects into `AbstractButtonMixin.super.setEnabled(...)` so the mixin's chain (and SD_auto_pce auto-PCE) fires. Mixin's `setEnabled` also pushes to `model.setEnabled(...)` so the model's gating stays in sync — important because ToggleButtonModel's `setPressed` short-circuits when disabled.

**SJCheckBox-specific surface.** Adds three things on top of SJToggleButton:
- `setBorderPaintedFlat(boolean)` + `isBorderPaintedFlat()` — **drop-and-WARN per R_vaadin_first.** Vaadin Checkbox owns its chrome and exposes no flat-border toggle, so there's no Vaadin counterpart and no UI behaviour to preserve. Setter validates nothing (JDK doesn't either) then `SHelper.onUnimplemented` + drops on non-default (true); default value (false) is a silent no-op so a `new SJCheckBox()` ctor sweep doesn't WARN. Getter returns the JDK default `false`. No PCE fires (no state, no change — same shape as SJButton's per-state icon setters per [SD_sjbutton](#SD_sjbutton)). The emulator-side `:emulators.JCheckBox` keeps its own field shadow + `"borderPaintedFlat"` PCE for full JDK round-trip per R_swing_is_truth (same two-layer split SD_sjbutton uses for state-conditional icons and SD_sjlabel uses for `displayedMnemonic`).
- `getUIClassID() == "CheckBoxUI"` (override the inherited `"ToggleButtonUI"`).
- `paramString()` appending `,borderPaintedFlat=false` (always JDK default — surrogate doesn't store the value).

**`ButtonStateStore` reuse, no new store.** SJToggleButton/SJCheckBox don't add new mixin state — `actionCommand` / `hideActionText` / `mnemonic` / `action` / `ButtonModel` / `EventListenerList` all carry from SJButton's existing store. The `preventPeerEvents` flag and the model→peer push `Registration` live as plain instance fields on `SJToggleButton` (not in `ButtonStateStore`) since the surrogate class itself is a perfectly good place for state — `ComponentUtil.setData` is for mixin-level state that has no surrogate class to attach to, which doesn't apply here. `borderPaintedFlat` lives as a plain field on `SJCheckBox` for the same reason.

**Emulator thin-down.** Mirrors the JSlider / JSpinner / SFrame / SJButton pattern:

- `:emulators.swing.JToggleButton` peers on `SJToggleButton` and hands it the emulator's own `ToggleButtonModel`, so a browser toggle pulses that model and every event reaches the emulator's listeners through its own `Handler`, with no bridge between layers ([D_emulator_button_model](../emulators/decisions.md#D_emulator_button_model)).
- `:emulators.swing.JCheckBox` swaps default peer from raw `Checkbox` to `SJCheckBox`. Otherwise unchanged — JCheckBox already extends JToggleButton, so all the wiring above carries through.
- `:emulators.swing.JToggleButton.setText` continues to push `cb.setLabel(...)` (existing override; the surrogate's setText already does this, but the emulator-side push keeps the emulator's R_swing_is_truth contract honest for users who never touch the surrogate API).

**Rationale.**
- **Checkbox peer over Button-with-CSS-toggle.** The "JToggleButton shares the Checkbox peer" question had three options on the table: (a) Vaadin `Checkbox` (current emulator's choice — visual mismatch for JToggleButton's "looks pressed" chrome); (b) Vaadin `Button` with `aria-pressed` / `data-selected` + CSS for the pressed look (visually closer for JToggleButton, requires custom selection-state machinery on a Button that isn't `HasValue<Boolean>`); (c) wait for a Vaadin `ToggleButton` add-on. (a) wins: the CRUD baseline ("the form has a toggleable boolean") doesn't depend on the JToggleButton-specific pressed-button chrome, the existing emulator's R_best_effort_behaviour-accepted divergence has shipped without complaint, and reusing Checkbox lets `SJCheckBox extends SJToggleButton` mirror the JDK shape. (b) is reachable later as a sub-class swap (`SJToggleButton2 extends Button`) if a migration target needs the chrome; that future work doesn't block this slice.
- **`ToggleButtonModel` instead of `DefaultButtonModel` as default.** JDK's `JToggleButton.ToggleButtonModel.setPressed(false)` while armed has the toggle-and-fire-events logic baked in. Reusing it directly gives us correct toggle semantics for free — no need to reimplement "press → flip selected → fire ItemEvent → fire ChangeEvent → fire ActionEvent" on top of `DefaultButtonModel`. R_vaadin_first explicitly keeps `ButtonModel` as source of truth in scope (the model drives event delivery, it's not API round-trip for its own sake), so installing the JDK-supplied subclass that gives the right behaviour is an obvious win. SJButton uses `DefaultButtonModel` because its press→release pulse fires Action only (no toggle) — different semantics need different default models.
- **ValueChange-only wire, not ClickNotifier.** Two reasons. (1) Vaadin Checkbox fires both ValueChangeEvent and ClickEvent when the user toggles via mouse; double-wiring would double-fire the model pulse. (2) Keyboard activation (Space on a focused checkbox) is processed natively by the `<input type="checkbox">` element and reliably fires ValueChangeEvent but doesn't always surface through ClickNotifier on the host. Wiring only ValueChange catches both modalities through one path. The idempotency guard (`m.isSelected() == newValue → return`) handles the corner case where a programmatic `cb.setValue(...)` echoes back through the listener — without the guard, the pulse would re-toggle and corrupt state.
- **Model→peer push lives in setModel, not in setSelected.** Hooking on `setSelected` would catch only direct mixin calls; user code that holds a `ButtonModel` reference and does `myModel.setSelected(true)` directly would bypass. Hooking the model with an ItemListener catches every selection transition regardless of who triggered it. Teardown via `Registration` on `setModel` swap mirrors the mixin's own bridge-listener `modelInstall` shape — same pattern, separate handle.
- **SJCheckBox extends SJToggleButton, not a parallel sibling.** Mirrors JDK and lets the `:emulators.JCheckBox extends JToggleButton` chain stay structurally identical at the surrogate layer. The borderPaintedFlat surface is small enough that a separate concrete saves the trivial 4-method overhead and gets free inheritance of the entire toggle wiring (model, ValueChangeListener, setText override, setEnabled override). Splitting would force re-listing the override surface on SJCheckBox.
- **No new store — instance fields on the concrete are the right home.** `preventPeerEvents` and the model→peer push registration are SJToggleButton-specific (not shared with SJButton), and SJToggleButton is a class we own. `ComponentUtil.setData` exists to give mixins shared state when there's no class to attach fields to ([SD_mixin_mechanism](#SD_mixin_mechanism)) — but here we have one. Same call SJButton's `defaultCapable` field made.
- **Drop-and-WARN on layout setters, not silent CSS write that no-ops visually.** R_vaadin_first names "minor round-trip loss is accepted" — but a setter that writes CSS the user can never see take effect isn't lossy round-trip, it's silent failure. Drop-and-WARN surfaces the gap at the call site; defaults silently no-op so `setAction`'s clear-to-default doesn't WARN-spam. This is the same shape SJButton's state-conditional icon setters take (per [SD_sjbutton](#SD_sjbutton)).
- **The emulator shares the model rather than bridging from it.** An earlier shape gave each layer its own model and subscription and bridged ActionEvent across; superseded by [D_emulator_button_model](../emulators/decisions.md#D_emulator_button_model), where the emulator owns the one model and the surrogate renders it. `SJCheckBox` / `SJRadioButton` re-show the model's state after a click it declined (a ButtonGroup keeping its lone selection), and `setModel` shows the new model's selection.

**Alternatives.**
- **Vaadin `Button` with `aria-pressed` + CSS pressed-look.** Rejected — see Rationale. Reachable as `SJToggleButton2 extends Button` later.
- **Wire both ClickNotifier and ValueChangeListener with dedup logic.** Rejected — double-firing risk + keyboard-path coverage gap. ValueChange-only is simpler and complete.
- **Use `DefaultButtonModel` and override the toggle behaviour locally.** Rejected — reimplements ToggleButtonModel's already-correct logic. Gain: zero. Cost: maintenance burden + drift risk if JDK behaviour evolves.
- **Skip setSelected in setModel push wire, hook setSelected directly.** Rejected — bypasses user code that mutates the model directly. The ItemListener-on-model approach catches every transition.
- **SJCheckBox as a separate sibling of SJToggleButton (both extend Checkbox + AbstractButtonMixin directly).** Rejected — duplicates the entire override surface. JDK's inheritance shape is the right model.
- **New `ToggleButtonStateStore` for `preventPeerEvents` + push registration.** Rejected — the surrogate is a class we own; ComponentUtil.setData is for mixin-level state without a class anchor, not state that naturally belongs on the concrete.
- **Inherit the mixin's inline-flex CSS write and accept "visually no-op but readback works".** Rejected — silent failure with a getter that lies about what the user can see. Drop-and-WARN is more honest per R_vaadin_first.
- **Override `setIcon` to drop-and-WARN explicitly.** Rejected as redundant — the mixin's default already takes the non-Button else branch and WARNs on non-null. Adding an explicit override would just duplicate the WARN call. JDK's `setIcon(Icon)` on a JCheckBox is rarely used in practice (checkbox icons aren't a common Swing pattern); the dropped slot is understood by migrators.

**Accepted limitations.**
- **JToggleButton renders as a checkbox glyph, not a pressed button.** *Resolved by [SD_sjtogglebutton_button_peer](#SD_sjtogglebutton_button_peer)* — SJToggleButton now peers over a Vaadin `Button` and renders the stays-pressed chrome (and gains icon + real layout). SJCheckBox keeps the Checkbox glyph, which is correct for a checkbox.
- **`setIcon` and the six state-conditional icons WARN-and-drop.** Vaadin Checkbox has no icon slot. Migrated code that decorates a toggle/checkbox with an icon loses the visual; events still fire. Same R_vaadin_first stance as SJButton's per-state icons; difference is plain `setIcon` doesn't have the Path 1 wireable-counterpart escape it has for SJButton.
- **Layout (alignment / text-position / iconTextGap) is drop-and-WARN.** Shadow DOM doesn't honor host inline-flex CSS. Setters validate (R_match_swing_errors IAE on invalid SwingConstants) then WARN-and-drop on non-default; getters return JDK defaults. Migrators relying on left-aligned label text on a JCheckBox lose the visual but the program runs.
- **`getText() == ""` on a default-constructed SJToggleButton/SJCheckBox.** R_vaadin_first lossy direction — Checkbox's `getLabel()` returns `null` for an unset label, but `setText(null)` writes `""` (since Vaadin Checkbox's `setLabel(null)` would NPE on the underlying DOM write); reading back gives `""`. Same SD_sjbutton shape (`SJButton.getText()` post-`setText(null)` is `""`). Emulator JToggleButton/JCheckBox preserve null on their own field shadow per R_swing_is_truth.
- **Two-layer event fan-out.** A listener registered on both `:emulators.JToggleButton` and its `SJToggleButton` peer fires twice. Same SJButton convention — register on one layer.
- **Mnemonic install via Vaadin `Shortcuts` is UI-scoped.** Inherited from AbstractButtonMixin / SD_sjbutton. Close to JDK's `WHEN_IN_FOCUSED_WINDOW` for single-UI apps.
- **Pure-surrogate `setVisible(false)` then `setVisible(true)` doesn't reset the model.** Standard Checkbox behaviour — the value carries across visibility transitions. JDK's JToggleButton same.
- **`indeterminate` not exposed on the AbstractButton surface.** Vaadin Checkbox supports a third "indeterminate" state (mixed-checked); JDK AbstractButton has no equivalent. Pure-surrogate code can call `cb.setIndeterminate(true)` directly via the inherited Checkbox API; emulator users can't reach it through the AbstractButton surface. Not in R_vaadin_first scope (Vaadin-only feature with no JDK counterpart on the AbstractButton API; if a migration target wants it, surface it later as a non-standard helper).

**Validation.** Surrogate test suite: `SJToggleButtonTest` covers ctors (no-arg / text / text+selected / Action), default ToggleButtonModel install, `setSelected` programmatic path (Item + Change events, no Action), `doClick` (full Item + Change + Action sequence per ToggleButtonModel pulse), browser-originated toggle via `cb.setValue` triggering the same sequence, ButtonModel swap (push wire torn down + reinstalled, model bridge listeners detached), `setAction` wiring (NAME / mnemonic / enabled propagation), `setText` routes through `Checkbox.setLabel`, R_match_swing_errors IAE on invalid SwingConstants for alignment + text-position setters, drop-and-WARN on non-default alignment / text-position / iconTextGap / setIcon / state-conditional icons, happy-path zero-WARN. `SJCheckBoxTest` adds: `setBorderPaintedFlat(true)` WARNs and `isBorderPaintedFlat()` returns `false` (R_vaadin_first drop), `setBorderPaintedFlat(false)` is silent (default), `getUIClassID() == "CheckBoxUI"`, paramString contains the JCheckBox-specific tail, all inherited toggle behaviour green. Emulator suite: existing `JCheckBoxTest` 19 tests retained — 4 updated to assert peer is now `SJCheckBox` and that ItemEvent / ChangeEvent / ActionEvent flow through the emulator's listeners with source = emulator (not surrogate); added 2 ("doClick now fires ChangeEvent through the bridged surrogate model pulse" + "ButtonModel selection state survives setText/setEnabled cycles"). The emulator-side `borderPaintedFlat` round-trip + PCE test stays green — emulator preserves full round-trip independently of the surrogate's R_vaadin_first drop. Sampler `FormWarnInventoryTest` + `ButtonsView` remain green — Karibu locator `_get(Checkbox.class, ...)` still resolves since SJCheckBox / SJToggleButton IS-A Checkbox.

---

<a id="SD_sjpanel"></a>
## SD_sjpanel. `SJPanel` surrogate — thin `Div`-backed container, JDK `LayoutManager` accepted directly

**Decision.** Ninth concrete surrogate, third backfill (after SJButton + SJLabel + SJToggleButton/SJCheckBox). Covers `JPanel`. Genuinely thin: `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJPanel extends com.vaadin.flow.component.html.Div implements JComponentMixin`. No new mixin, no new state-holder, no override surface against the inherited mixins beyond the L&F drop-and-WARN bucket and the four-ctor JDK shape.

- **Peer base: Vaadin `Div`.** A bare HasComponents container, the same peer the existing emulator already uses. No shadow DOM (so the inline-flex / border / opaque CSS the inherited mixin writes lands on the host element where it actually renders), no Vaadin-specific value/selection/event behaviour to override.
- **Constructors mirror JDK** — four public forms (`SJPanel()` / `SJPanel(boolean)` / `SJPanel(LayoutManager)` / `SJPanel(LayoutManager, boolean)`) all funnelling through the two-arg form. The boolean `isDoubleBuffered` parameter is accepted-and-dropped (no Vaadin counterpart per [R_layouts_close_enough](../CLAUDE.md), same shape the emulator takes).
- **Default layout is JDK `java.awt.FlowLayout`** (not `vaadinx.awt.FlowLayout` — `:surrogates` can't depend on `:emulators` per [SD_shelper_statics](#SD_shelper_statics)). The mixin's `ContainerMixin.setLayout` ([D_layout_css_on_content](../emulators/decisions.md#D_layout_css_on_content)) accepts the JDK class and applies the equivalent CSS via `LayoutCss.flowLayoutCss` to the surrogate's host element. No fork of the LayoutManager hierarchy needed at the surrogate layer (see "LayoutManager handling" below).
- **No `setOpaque(true)` in the ctor.** JDK JPanel installs `opaque=true` via L&F at construction, so a fresh JDK JPanel reports `isOpaque() == true`. The mixin's `setOpaque` writes `background-color` CSS and reads the same back per [SD_vaadin_first_binding](#SD_vaadin_first_binding) — *no shadow store*. Calling `setOpaque(true)` in the SJPanel ctor with no background set writes nothing useful (the `null → remove("background-color")` branch does effectively the same as not calling it), and the readback `isOpaque()` still returns `false` (no `background-color` CSS = false per the mixin's lossy rule). Skipping the call is cleaner: no PCE noise during construction, no R_vaadin_first-violating shadow store. **Accepted divergence: `new SJPanel().isOpaque() == false`**, where `new javax.swing.JPanel().isOpaque() == true`. Same R_vaadin_first lossy direction the mixin already documents on `setOpaque`. Emulator-side `:emulators.JPanel` preserves the JDK `isOpaque() == true` via its field shadow per R_swing_is_truth (two-layer split, same shape SD_sjbutton takes for state-conditional icons and SD_toggle_checkbox_first_cut takes for `borderPaintedFlat`).
- **L&F surface** (`setUI` / `updateUI` / `getUIClassID`) — drop-and-WARN / no-op pattern. `getUIClassID` returns `"PanelUI"`; `updateUI` is a deliberate no-op (Vaadin owns the DOM); `setUI` `SHelper.onUnimplemented`-and-drops. **JDK `getUI()` is dropped** — Vaadin `Component.getUI()` returns `Optional<UI>`, JDK `JComponent.getUI()` returns `ComponentUI` (or `PanelUI` for JPanel), and the JVM forbids two methods with the same erased signature and different return types. Same call SD_sjbutton makes for `Button.getIcon()` vs JDK `getIcon()`. The JDK-typed `setUI(PanelUI)` survives as a plain method since Vaadin Div has no `setUI` to clash with on the parameter side.
- **No accessibility port.** `getAccessibleContext` WARNs and returns null (pre-existing pattern across all surrogates per the audit's permanent sub-bucket (c)).

**LayoutManager handling at the `:surrogates` layer.** Worth recording explicitly since SJPanel is the first surrogate that exercises the layout dispatch as core functionality (SJButton/SJLabel touch it via the inherited mixin defaults but don't depend on it; SFrame/SJFrame have their own contentPane routing). The shape:

- The surrogate's `ContainerMixin.setLayout(LayoutManager)` accepts the **JDK** `java.awt.LayoutManager` (not the emulator's `vaadinx.awt.LayoutManager`).
- Recognised JDK layouts (today: `java.awt.FlowLayout`, `java.awt.BorderLayout`) are pattern-matched via `instanceof` and translated to CSS on the surrogate's host element. Adding more (`GridLayout`, `BoxLayout`, `GridBagLayout`, …) is one `instanceof` branch + one CSS builder in `SHelper`, mechanically.
- Custom user `LayoutManager` subclasses (anything not in the recognised set) store the layout via `LayoutStore` (so `getLayout()` round-trips per JDK contract) but apply no CSS — the container falls back to default block flow. Same accepted limitation `:emulators` carries per CLAUDE.md §"Custom LayoutManager support".
- **No `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.awt.LayoutManager` parallel hierarchy.** The emulator's `vaadinx.awt.LayoutManager` interface fork was forced by the `addLayoutComponent(String, vaadinx.awt.Component)` / `removeLayoutComponent(vaadinx.awt.Component)` / `layoutContainer(vaadinx.awt.Container)` signatures — the JDK methods take `java.awt.Component`, but emulator user code passes emulator components, not real AWT components. **At the surrogate layer those Component-typed methods are dead surface — never invoked.** The surrogate's `setLayout` treats the LayoutManager as a *shape descriptor*: read public fields (`getAlignment()`, `getHgap()`, …), translate to CSS, done. The Component-arg methods are skipped entirely.
- **Per-child constraint CSS** (BorderLayout's NORTH/SOUTH/EAST/WEST → `grid-area`, future GridBagLayout's `gridx/gridy/anchor`) needs a `ContainerMixin.add(Component, Object constraint)` API that doesn't exist today — Vaadin's `HasComponents.add(Component...)` has no constraint slot. This gap is noted; SJPanel doesn't trigger it (FlowLayout has no per-child constraints, and BorderLayout-using migrated code reaches SJPanel through `:emulators.JPanel`, where the emulator's `vaadinx.awt.BorderLayout.addLayoutComponent` writes per-child CSS to the surrogate's underlying element). A separate slice closes this when a pure-surrogate code path needs BorderLayout's region anchoring (likely a later obligation).

**Emulator thin-down** (mirror SJSlider / SJSpinner / SFrame / SJButton / SJToggleButton):

- `:emulators.swing.JPanel` swaps default peer from a raw `Div` to `SJPanel`. The protected `JPanel(Component peer)` ctor stays as the escape hatch for custom peers. `setLayout` and `setOpaque` continue to fire on the emulator side; the emulator's `vaadinx.EHelper.applyContainerCss` writes CSS on the SJPanel's element (since SJPanel IS-A Div). No two-layer event bridging needed — JPanel has no event surface to bridge (no model, no clicks, no actions).

**Rationale.**
- **Peer choice: Div.** A Div-shaped container is the natural candidate; it's the same peer the existing emulator already uses and the most flexible HasComponents primitive Vaadin ships. No reason to prefer `HtmlContainer` (abstract, less direct) or a custom Vaadin component (over-design for a thin holder).
- **JDK FlowLayout default.** Matches JDK JPanel's `LayoutManager` default. The surrogate can't reach `vaadinx.awt.FlowLayout` per SD_shelper_statics (module direction), and the JDK class carries the same data the surrogate's `LayoutCss.flowLayoutCss` builder consumes.
- **Skip `setOpaque(true)` in ctor.** Calling it would write the same CSS as not calling it (no background → no `background-color` rule) but fire a "opaque" PCE during construction. The `isOpaque() == false` divergence on a fresh SJPanel is R_vaadin_first-accepted lossy direction, identical in shape to the lossy round-trips the mixin already documents (SJButton's `setText(null) → ""`, SJLabel's `getText()` empty default, SJToggleButton's `borderPaintedFlat` drop). Two-layer split: emulator field-shadows `opaque=true` per R_swing_is_truth; surrogate's `isOpaque()` reads CSS per R_vaadin_first and returns false. Migration convention stays "register PropertyChangeListener on one layer only."
- **No new state holder, no override surface beyond L&F + ctors.** SJPanel is the first surrogate where the inherited mixin chain (`JComponentMixin extends ContainerMixin extends ComponentMixin`) provides essentially everything: `setLayout` + CSS dispatch, `setOpaque` + CSS readback, `setBorder` + CSS round-trip ([SD_border_css_lossy](#SD_border_css_lossy)), `add`/`remove` via inherited `HasComponents`, focus / key / ancestor / mouse / property-change / client-property surface from JComponentMixin. The surrogate adds the four ctors + L&F surface + paramString tail and stops there. This is the small-thin shape SJButton couldn't be (event surface) and SJLabel couldn't quite be (DOM-children-for-text+icon coexistence); SJPanel's lack of UI behaviour is exactly what makes it tiny.
- **Container event firing — explicitly NOT in this slice.** `ContainerMixin.addContainerListener` is Bucket B (`SHelper.onNoop`) per [SD_listeners_without_analog](#SD_listeners_without_analog), and a cross-cutting note leaves the door open for per-surrogate firing: "Per-surrogate overrides of `HasComponents.add` / `remove` can fire the event for a specific container surrogate (e.g. an `SJPanel`) — scope-bound per surrogate, not mixin-wide." Deferred from this slice — keeping the SJPanel shape tight; the cost-benefit needs a real consumer to evaluate (most JPanel-using migrated code doesn't register ContainerListener, and the emulator side doesn't fire it either today).

**Alternatives.**
- **`HtmlContainer` peer (abstract base).** Rejected — Div is concrete and identical functionally.
- **Build a `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.awt.LayoutManager` parallel hierarchy.** Rejected — the surrogate doesn't invoke the Component-typed methods, so the parallel interface buys nothing while doubling the layout-class porting work.
- **`setOpaque(true)` in ctor anyway, accept the noise.** Rejected — would fire a "opaque" PCE during ctor (annoying for any pre-add listener), still doesn't fix the R_vaadin_first readback (CSS still says false), and adds a no-op call. Skip is cleaner.
- **Add a `ContainerEvent`-firing override on `add` / `remove` here.** Rejected as out of slice scope. Reach for it when a real migration target needs it (likely a later obligation if at all). The mixin's onNoop covers the API-compat shape today.
- **Drop the `boolean isDoubleBuffered` ctor variants entirely.** Rejected — JDK JPanel ships them; migrated code that calls `new JPanel(true)` or `new JPanel(layout, false)` should compile unchanged per [D_import_swap_target](../emulators/decisions.md#D_import_swap_target). Accepting + dropping the parameter matches the emulator's existing stance.

**Accepted limitations.**
- **`isOpaque()` returns false on a fresh SJPanel.** R_vaadin_first lossy direction — JDK JPanel reports `true`, surrogate reads CSS which is unset → false. Emulator JPanel keeps the JDK-faithful `true` via field shadow. Two-layer split per R_swing_is_truth/R_vaadin_first.
- **`isDoubleBuffered` parameter is accepted-and-dropped.** No Vaadin counterpart per R_layouts_close_enough; matches emulator behaviour. `isDoubleBuffered()` still returns the JDK default false after `new SJPanel(true)`.
- **No ContainerEvent firing on `add` / `remove`.** Inherited from `ContainerMixin.addContainerListener` Bucket B. Pure-surrogate code that adds children to an SJPanel and listens for ContainerEvent gets no events. Same gap the emulator carries today; closure scope-bound per surrogate when a migration target needs it.
- **Custom `LayoutManager` subclasses don't dispatch CSS.** Same gap the emulator carries per CLAUDE.md §"Custom LayoutManager support". Container falls back to block flow.
- **L&F `setUI` WARNs-and-drops; `getUI` is not declared on SJPanel.** Pure-surrogate `getUI()` calls invoke the inherited Vaadin `Component.getUI()` (returns `Optional<UI>`); migrated `JPanel.getUI()` against an SJPanel won't compile. Through `:emulators.JPanel` it works unchanged via the emulator's WARN-and-null surface. Same shape SD_sjbutton records for SJButton's `getIcon`.
- **Two-layer PCE fan-out for `border` / `opaque` / `name` etc.** Same shape SJButton / SJLabel / SJToggleButton document — listeners registered on both layers fire twice. Migration convention: register on one layer only.

**Validation.** Surrogate test suite: `SJPanelTest` covers ctors (no-arg defaults to FlowLayout; boolean / layout / layout+boolean variants), `getUIClassID == "PanelUI"`, paramString chain (no JPanel-specific tail), R_vaadin_first lossy `isOpaque()` returns false on fresh and after `setOpaque(true)` with no background, becomes true after `setBackground(...)` + `setOpaque(true)` round-trip, FlowLayout / BorderLayout dispatch via inherited mixin (asserts the host CSS lands), border CSS round-trip via inherited mixin (LineBorder happy-path), `add` / `remove` via inherited `HasComponents`, custom `LayoutManager` subclass stores but doesn't dispatch (smoke test), L&F getUI / setUI WARN-and-drop, happy-path zero stub WARNs. Emulator suite: existing `JPanelTest` retained — added 1 test asserting peer is now `SJPanel` and that emulator-side `setLayout` CSS lands on the surrogate's element. Sampler routes (FormView / ButtonsView / InputsView all use JPanel) remain green — `_get`/locator paths resolve through the Vaadin `Div` IS-A relationship.

---

<a id="SD_sjtextfield"></a>
## SD_sjtextfield. `JTextComponentMixin` + `SJTextField` surrogate — mixin-first text surface, R_swing_is_truth Document↔peer sync lifted, R_vaadin_first-trimmed

**Decision.** Tenth concrete surrogate, fourth backfill (after SJButton + SJLabel + SJToggleButton/SJCheckBox + SJPanel). Covers the `SJTextField` half of the text-component backfill. SJPasswordField + SJTextArea are explicitly *queued consumers* of the new mixin and land later as thin extends-the-mixin slices, mirroring the SD_sjbutton / SD_toggle_checkbox_first_cut pattern (mixin lifted ahead of multiple consumers, first concrete shipped, others follow without refactoring the mixin).

- **`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.swing.text.JTextComponentMixin extends JComponentMixin`** carries the `javax.swing.text.JTextComponent` API surface — Document model integration, R_swing_is_truth Document↔peer sync, editable propagation, getText/setText, copy/cut/paste (drop-and-WARN), caret/highlighter/keymap (drop-and-WARN), L&F surface. Lifted directly from `:emulators.swing.text.JTextComponent`'s inline-fix carve-out (where the same R_swing_is_truth sync was lifted out of JTextField into JTextComponent so JPasswordField + JTextArea inherit it transparently). Same shape `AbstractButtonMixin` takes for the AbstractButton-lineage surrogates per [SD_sjbutton](#SD_sjbutton).
- **`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJTextField extends com.vaadin.flow.component.textfield.TextField implements JTextComponentMixin`** — first concrete consumer of the mixin. JDK-shaped ctors (5 forms — no-arg / String / int / String+int / Document+String+int) all funnelling through the three-arg root form. JTextField-specific surface: `columns` + `setColumns` (negative → IAE per R_match_swing_errors, `setWidth(CssConvert.columnsToCssWidth(N))` on the peer per R_layouts_close_enough close-enough), ActionListener wiring on Enter via `addKeyPressListener(Key.ENTER, ...)`, narrow `setAction` (enabled / SHORT_DESCRIPTION / ACTION_COMMAND_KEY only — not NAME / mnemonic / icons, mirrors emulator's narrower-than-AbstractButton shape), `getUIClassID == "TextFieldUI"`. `horizontalAlignment` drops-and-WARNs per R_vaadin_first (TextField shadow DOM doesn't honor host text-align — same call SJToggleButton's layout setters make per SD_toggle_checkbox_first_cut).
- **`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.internal.TextStateStore`** carries mixin state per [SD_mixin_mechanism](#SD_mixin_mechanism): `document` (current installed Document), `docToPeer` (single shared DocumentListener instance, attached on setDocument swap), `preventPeerEvents` (R_swing_is_truth feedback-loop guard, used by both directions of the sync). No `editable` field — the mixin reads `peer.isReadOnly()` directly when peer is `TextFieldBase`, R_vaadin_first Vaadin-first per [SD_vaadin_first_binding](#SD_vaadin_first_binding). For the rare non-`TextFieldBase` peer (Div fallback), `isEditable()` always returns `true` — accepted lossy direction documented on the mixin.

**R_swing_is_truth Document↔peer sync — lifted shape from `:emulators.JTextComponent`:**

- `installTextComponentBindings()` (called from concrete's ctor) installs the default `PlainDocument` (via `createDefaultDocument()` factory hook) + attaches the internal `docToPeer` `DocumentListener`. If the peer is a `TextFieldBase`, sets `ValueChangeMode.EAGER` (so each keystroke fires) and wires `addValueChangeListener` to `SHelper.callSwing(() -> syncDocumentFromPeer(...))` per R_callswing_envelope.
- `onDocumentMutated()` (private mixin helper, fired by `docToPeer` on insert/remove): if `preventPeerEvents` is set, bail; else set the flag, call `peer.setValue(readDocument())`, clear in `finally`.
- `syncDocumentFromPeer(String)` (private mixin helper, fired by ValueChangeListener): if `preventPeerEvents` is set, bail; else set the flag, `document.remove(0, length) + document.insertString(0, peerValue, null)`, clear in `finally`.
- `setDocument(Document)` swaps the doc, removes `docToPeer` from the old, attaches it to the new, fires `"document"` PCE, then `onDocumentMutated()` so the new doc's text reaches the peer.
- `readDocument()` (private mixin helper) reads the raw Document text (bypasses `getText()` overrides — matters for SJPasswordField, which masks `getText()`; the peer always sees cleartext).

Same code shape the emulator's JTextComponent.java already proves works end-to-end across TextField + PasswordField + TextArea peers; the lift just puts it behind the mixin so pure-surrogate users get it too.

**Queued consumers:**

- **`SJPasswordField extends Vaadin PasswordField implements JTextComponentMixin`**. Adds: `echoChar` field + `setEchoChar` + `echoCharIsSet` + `getEchoChar` (drop-and-WARN — Vaadin PasswordField's `<input type="password">` uses the browser's native default mask glyph, no per-instance override exposed by Vaadin); `char[] getPassword()` + `getText()` override that masks with echoChar (load-bearing — JPasswordField's reason for existing). The `readDocument()` mixin helper bypasses the masked `getText()` so peer sync works cleartext. `getUIClassID == "PasswordFieldUI"`. No mixin changes needed — SJPasswordField just extends + adds the password-specific surface.
- **`SJTextArea extends Vaadin TextArea implements JTextComponentMixin`**. Adds: `rows` / `setRows` / `getRows` (R_match_swing_errors IAE on negative, peer.setHeight via "lh"/"em" or analogous), `columns` / `setColumns` (lifts to mixin? — see open question), `tabSize` / `lineWrap` / `wrapStyleWord` (lineWrap → peer's wrap attribute or CSS; wrapStyleWord likely drop-and-WARN), `append` / `insert(String, int)` / `replaceRange(String, int, int)` (Document mutations — could lift to mixin), `getLineCount` / `getLineOfOffset` / `getLineStartOffset` / `getLineEndOffset` (line-oriented Document queries — could lift to mixin). `getUIClassID == "TextAreaUI"`. May surface a need to lift the line-oriented helpers to the mixin retroactively if SJTextArea ends up needing them; current call is to keep the mixin scoped to JTextComponent's actual surface and put line-oriented + columns helpers on SJTextArea itself.

**`columns` and `horizontalAlignment` are NOT in the mixin.** JDK declares both on JTextField (and JTextArea has its own `columns`), not on JTextComponent. Each concrete owns its own. Same precedent SD_sjpanel follows — concrete-specific surface stays on the concrete.

**R_vaadin_first trim audit — what drops:**

- **Caret / Highlighter / Keymap / Selection / NavigationFilter** — all WARN-and-drop. Vaadin TextFieldBase has no server-side caret model, no highlighter, no Swing Keymap analog. Selection-related (`getSelectionStart`/`End`, `select`, `replaceSelection`) is reachable via Vaadin `Element.executeJs` for a future slice but not in scope today.
- **Copy / cut / paste** — WARN-and-drop. Clipboard is a cross-cutting capability handled separately; same drop the emulator carries today.
- **Drag-and-drop / DropMode** — WARN-and-drop. A separate cross-cutting obligation.
- **Print / Read / Write / InputMethod** — WARN-and-drop. Out of scope per CLAUDE.md.
- **Caret/Selection/Disabled/SelectedText colors** — WARN-and-drop. No Vaadin counterpart for color-of-selected-text-pixels.
- **`setMargin` / `getMargin`** — WARN-and-drop (mirrors the AbstractButtonMixin's same drop per SD_sjbutton — no Vaadin counterpart for the JDK Insets margin shape).
- **`setFocusAccelerator` / `getFocusAccelerator`** — WARN-and-drop. Mnemonic-style shortcut to focus the field; could wire via Vaadin Shortcuts later but not in scope here.
- **`getActions`** — returns empty array (matches emulator). EditorKit actions aren't modelled.
- **`viewToModel` / `modelToView` / `viewToModel2D` / `modelToView2D`** — WARN-and-drop. Pixel-level coordinate math we don't model per R_layouts_close_enough.
- **`setComponentOrientation`** — chains to `JComponentMixin.setComponentOrientation` (which writes the `dir` attribute on the peer per the SD_vaadin_first_binding-driven base). The JDK JTextComponent override additionally handles caret direction; we don't model caret per R_layouts_close_enough.

**`isEditable()` returns the peer state directly — no shadow store.** When peer is `TextFieldBase`, `isEditable()` returns `!peer.isReadOnly()`. Programmatic `setEditable(b)` writes `peer.setReadOnly(!b)` and fires `"editable"` PCE. R_vaadin_first Vaadin-first; same shape SD_sjpanel's `isOpaque()` takes (Vaadin reads back from CSS, no shadow). Non-`TextFieldBase` peers always report editable=true (no reflection, can't store) — accepted lossy direction.

**`setUI` survives, `getUI` is dropped.** Same SD_sjbutton / SD_sjpanel precedent — Vaadin `Component.getUI()` returns `Optional<UI>`, JDK `JTextComponent.getUI()` returns `TextUI`, signatures clash. `setUI(TextUI)` survives as overload-safe (no Vaadin `setUI` to clash with on the parameter side).

**Emulator thin-down (SJTextField only — SJPasswordField + SJTextArea peers stay raw until those surrogates land):**

- `:emulators.swing.JTextField` swaps default peer from raw `TextField` to `SJTextField`. Existing `JTextField` extends `vaadinx.swing.text.JTextComponent` (which carries the emulator's own R_swing_is_truth Document↔peer sync) — the surrogate now has its own parallel R_swing_is_truth sync at the surrogate layer. **Two-layer feedback-loop guards**: the emulator's `preventPeerEvents` (in `vaadinx.swing.text.JTextComponent`) handles emulator-driven writes; the surrogate's `preventPeerEvents` (in `TextStateStore`) handles surrogate-driven writes. Both bail their listener when their flag is set — same SD_sjslider/SD_sjspinner/SD_sframe independent-layer-feedback-guards shape generalised. Programmatic emulator `setText("X")` → emulator's docToPeer fires → emulator preventPeerEvents=true → peer.setValue("X") → surrogate's ValueChangeListener fires → surrogate preventPeerEvents check (false) → surrogate's syncDocumentFromPeer writes to *surrogate's* Document → surrogate's docToPeer fires → surrogate preventPeerEvents=true → peer.setValue("X") (idempotent, same value) → emulator's ValueChangeListener bails (emulator preventPeerEvents=true). Net result: both layers' Documents end up with "X", no infinite loop, no double-fire of user-registered DocumentListener (each layer's listeners fire on their own Document only).
- `:emulators.swing.JPasswordField` and `:emulators.swing.JTextArea` peer choices stay unchanged (raw `PasswordField` / `TextArea`) until SJPasswordField / SJTextArea land. The mixin's R_swing_is_truth sync only activates when the peer is a `TextFieldBase` — which raw PasswordField / TextArea both are — so the surrogate-layer sync wouldn't fire for them at all (no mixin-implementing peer). Until those concretes land, only SJTextField gets the surrogate-side R_swing_is_truth sync; the others continue to rely on the emulator's existing JTextComponent sync.
- AbstractButton's existing SJButton/SJToggleButton bridge branches in its ctor are unaffected — JTextField's ctor doesn't go through AbstractButton.
- `:emulators.JTextField`'s ActionListener-on-Enter wiring stays — the surrogate also wires its own ActionListener-on-Enter. Two-layer fire (same as the existing JCheckBox PCE convention SD_toggle_checkbox_first_cut documents): a listener registered on both layers fires twice. Migration convention: register on one layer.

**Rationale.**

- **Mixin-first from day one.** Three confirmed future consumers — SJTextField (this slice), SJPasswordField, SJTextArea — same calculus SD_sjbutton made for AbstractButtonMixin. Lifting upfront spares the inline-to-mixin refactor when SJPasswordField + SJTextArea land. The mixin's R_swing_is_truth sync code and Document lifecycle lift cleanly because the emulator's inline-fix already factored it (the lift here mirrors that same factoring in surrogate-land).
- **TextStateStore on `ComponentUtil.setData`, not concrete instance fields.** The mixin's R_swing_is_truth sync code reads/writes `preventPeerEvents` and `document` from default-method bodies — instance fields on the concrete would force every consumer (SJTextField, SJPasswordField, SJTextArea) to expose abstract getters/setters as boilerplate. The store keeps the mixin self-contained. Same precedent ButtonStateStore takes for AbstractButtonMixin's `model` and `mnemonic` state. SJSlider's instance-field `preventPeerEvents` is a different case — there the mixin doesn't reach back into the concrete's state (`SJSlider.modelListener` IS the listener; mixin doesn't know about it). For SJTextComponentMixin the mixin owns the listener body, so the state must be mixin-reachable.
- **`isEditable` reads peer directly, no shadow store.** Vaadin `TextFieldBase.isReadOnly()` is exposed bidirectionally — read it back, negate, done. R_vaadin_first Vaadin-first, same call SD_sjpanel makes for `isOpaque`. The non-`TextFieldBase` always-true fallback is accepted lossy: the only path to a non-`TextFieldBase` peer is a custom subclass passing it through the protected ctor (explicitly opted in to non-standard wiring).
- **`columns` / `horizontalAlignment` stay on the concrete, not the mixin.** JDK declares both on JTextField (not JTextComponent), and JTextArea has its own `columns` with different defaults / semantics (combined with `rows`). Lifting to the mixin would force a "shared columns" contract that isn't really shared in JDK — same precedent SD_sjpanel follows for JPanel-specific surface.
- **Two-layer R_swing_is_truth sync is correct without coordination.** Each layer's `preventPeerEvents` is independent: when the emulator drives a write, the emulator's flag suppresses its own re-entry; the surrogate's flag is unset, so the surrogate's listener fires once and runs its own sync (writing to the surrogate's Document, which is a different object from the emulator's Document). Net: emulator's Document and surrogate's Document both reach the same value through different code paths; both layers fire their own DocumentListeners on their own Document; no infinite loop because each preventPeerEvents flag suppresses *that* layer's reflection back. Verified by reasoning through the four cases (emulator setText / surrogate setText / browser typing / programmatic peer.setValue) — same shape SD_sframe documents for the SFrame / emulator.JFrame OpenedChangeListener two-layer pattern.

**Alternatives.**

- **Inline JTextComponent surface in SJTextField, extract mixin when SJPasswordField lands.** Rejected — SJSlider's inline shape worked because no second consumer was queued; here three consumers are confirmed and the refactor cost is concrete. Same SD_sjbutton reasoning.
- **Single-layer R_swing_is_truth sync (emulator delegates to surrogate's Document).** Rejected — couples the two layers' Document instances and breaks the "each layer owns its own listener list" invariant. Migrators registering DocumentListener on the emulator wouldn't get a Document they can mutate independently; testability suffers.
- **Lift `columns` to the mixin.** Rejected — JDK doesn't put it there, and JTextArea's `columns` semantics differ (combined with `rows`). Two concretes isn't enough overlap to justify a shared mixin field.
- **Drop the surrogate's R_swing_is_truth sync entirely; let migrated code reach the Document only via the emulator.** Rejected — pure-surrogate users need the Document↔peer sync to use SJTextField as a real Vaadin component. The mixin lift makes it available without forcing migration through the emulator.
- **Wire `horizontalAlignment` via `Element.executeJs` to set CSS on the shadow-DOM input part.** Rejected — it's a real follow-up but SJToggleButton's drop-and-WARN precedent (SD_toggle_checkbox_first_cut) is the right stance here. Lift if a migration target needs it.

**Accepted limitations.**

- **`getText() == ""` on a default-constructed SJTextField.** R_vaadin_first lossy direction — the mixin's PlainDocument starts empty, `getText()` reads the empty Document, returns `""`. Same shape SD_sjbutton documents for SJButton's `setText(null) → ""`.
- **`isEditable() == true` for non-`TextFieldBase` peers always.** No reflection back from a Div fallback peer; can't observe a stored state. R_vaadin_first lossy direction.
- **Caret / Highlighter / Keymap / Selection — all WARN-and-drop.** Vaadin TextFieldBase doesn't expose server-side equivalents. Pure-surrogate code that branches on `getCaretPosition()` etc. sees zero/null/empty defaults. Accepted permanent gap.
- **Copy / cut / paste WARN-and-drop.** Clipboard is post-Sampler.
- **`horizontalAlignment` setter validates per R_match_swing_errors (IAE on bad axis) but drops-and-WARNs on non-default values; getter returns JDK default LEADING.** Same SD_toggle_checkbox_first_cut precedent for layout setters that have no Vaadin counterpart through the shadow DOM. Migrators relying on right-aligned text in a JTextField lose the visual but the program runs.
- **`focusAccelerator` WARN-and-drop.** Could wire via Vaadin Shortcuts later (same machinery as AbstractButtonMixin's mnemonic), not in scope here.
- **JDK `getUI()` not declared on SJTextField.** Same SD_sjbutton / SD_sjpanel pattern (Vaadin `Optional<UI>` clash). Pure-surrogate `getUI()` calls reach the inherited Vaadin `Component.getUI()`; migrated `JTextField.getUI()` against an SJTextField won't compile (against `:emulators.JTextField` works via the emulator's WARN-and-null surface).
- **Two-layer DocumentListener fan-out.** A DocumentListener registered on `:emulators.JTextField.getDocument()` fires on the emulator's Document; the same listener registered on `sjt.getDocument()` fires on the surrogate's Document. They're different Document instances; both reach the same text via the two-layer R_swing_is_truth sync, but listeners are per-layer. Migration convention: register on one layer (the emulator side for migrated code).
- **Two-layer ActionListener fan-out for Enter.** A listener registered on both layers fires twice. Same SJButton / SJSlider / SJSpinner convention.
- **`setAction` propagates only `enabled` / `SHORT_DESCRIPTION` / `ACTION_COMMAND_KEY`.** JDK JTextField's actual contract — narrower than AbstractButton's. Doesn't copy NAME (would overwrite form data) or icons or mnemonics.
- **`isValidateRoot` always false.** No JViewport modelling.
- **SJTextArea concrete landed subsequently.** SJPasswordField shipped per [SD_sjpasswordfield](#SD_sjpasswordfield); SJTextArea shipped per [SD_sjtextarea](#SD_sjtextarea). All three queued consumers of `JTextComponentMixin` are now landed.

**Validation.** Surrogate test suite: `SJTextFieldTest` covers ctors (no-arg / String / int / String+int / Document+String+int), R_swing_is_truth sync (programmatic setText writes peer; peer setValue writes Document under preventPeerEvents — both directions, no infinite loop), Document swap via setDocument fires "document" PCE + new doc's text reaches the peer, setEditable propagates to peer.isReadOnly(), R_vaadin_first lossy isEditable() reads peer state, ActionListener on Enter (Karibu key-press simulation), ActionListener actionCommand fallback to text, setColumns IAE on negative + writes peer width "Nch", horizontalAlignment R_match_swing_errors IAE on bad axis + drop-and-WARN on non-LEADING, setAction narrow propagation (enabled / tooltip / actionCommand only — NAME doesn't overwrite text), copy/cut/paste WARN-and-drop, getUIClassID, happy-path zero stub WARNs. `JTextComponentMixinTest` (smaller — sanity-checks the mixin via SJTextField as the test bed since it's the only concrete in this slice): document lifecycle, setEditable round-trip, getText null defaults, paramString tail. Emulator suite: existing `JTextFieldTest` retained — added 1-2 tests asserting peer is now `SJTextField` and that the two-layer R_swing_is_truth sync delivers consistent text on both layers. Sampler `FormView` (uses JTextField for the email/password/etc fields) remains green — `_get(TextField.class, ...)` still resolves since SJTextField IS-A TextField.

---

<a id="SD_sjpasswordfield"></a>
## SD_sjpasswordfield. `SJPasswordField` surrogate — second `JTextComponentMixin` consumer, JTextField surface duplicated (not lifted)

> **Correction, 2026-09-01 — the masking limb of this entry was wrong and is reversed.** From 2026-04-22 (`02d68dd`, the original emulator) to 2026-09-01, SB-Emulators' `getText()` / `getText(int, int)` returned a run of `echoChar`, on the stated premise that "JDK JPasswordField's whole point is `getText()` masking to discourage accidental cleartext logging." **That premise is inverted.** Real JDK 25, headless: `new JPasswordField(); setText("ADMIN"); getText()` → `"ADMIN"`. `javax.swing.JPasswordField.getText()`'s entire body is `return super.getText();` — it is declared only to carry `@Deprecated`, and `echoChar` has never been read by any getter, only by `BasicPasswordFieldUI`'s view. The deprecation exists *because* the method hands out cleartext as an immutable `String` that cannot be zeroed, unlike `getPassword()`'s `char[]`; it was read as evidence for the opposite of what it says. Both mask loops are deleted; the emulator keeps the override pair as `return super.getText(...)` + `@Deprecated`, exactly as the JDK does, so a migrator recompiling keeps the javac warning their Swing build already emitted. `getPassword()` was always correct. `echoChar` **state** survives per [R_decline_effect_only](../CLAUDE.md#hard-rules) — `getEchoChar` / `echoCharIsSet` / the `setEchoChar` boundary WARN keep their meaning, and the WARN is in fact strengthened: the write now reaches nothing observable at all. Where the false premise had propagated, and what it cost:
>
> - **It compounded into a second, opposite-signed divergence.** The "ActionEvent payload is cleartext" rationale below declined to match a JDK "misbehaviour" — a masked `actionCommand` fallback — **that does not exist**, invoking [R_best_effort_behaviour](../CLAUDE.md#hard-rules) to license it. It reached the right observable behaviour by fabricated reasoning. `fireActionPerformed` is now SJTextField's one-liner verbatim (`actionCommand != null ? actionCommand : getText()`), which is what the JDK inherits. R_best_effort_behaviour excuses *approximating* what the JDK does; it never licensed *inventing* what it does not — the upward cap that says so is [R_no_silent_improvements](../CLAUDE.md#hard-rules), added later and independently.
> - **It seeded a decision entry's motivating example.** [D_sync_reads_raw_model](../emulators/decisions.md#D_sync_reads_raw_model) was introduced in the same commit, citing this masking as the concrete case that forced "the sync reads the model, never the public accessor." The rule is right and stands; its example is retired and replaced with `JFormattedTextField`'s formatter.
> - **The exit gate could not catch it.** Six tests across both layers asserted the masking as the contract. A suite written from the implementation's own premise re-states the premise; it does not test it. The generalisable check — assert shadowed getters against values *measured from a real JDK run* — was the open `Q_audit_gap` item, and was worth more than this fix: it is built, and is now a build gate, per [D_return_value_audit](../emulators/decisions.md#D_return_value_audit).
> - **`Q_security_intent_survives` — open.** The guard removed above was wrong *here*; the intent behind it was not obviously wrong. If a migrator genuinely wants a "don't log cleartext" guard, is there anywhere legitimate to put it? Not on `getText()` — [R_swing_is_truth](../CLAUDE.md#hard-rules) and [R_no_silent_improvements](../CLAUDE.md#hard-rules) both forbid it, which is the whole content of this correction. A lint is a different conversation and must not be smuggled into a fidelity fix.
> - **Cost.** Found 2026-08-27 by the `testapps/inventory` stage-2 probe as "no user can log in": `LoginPanel:118` calls `passWord.getText().trim()`, so the app SHA-256'd `"*****"`. No WARN, no throw, clean inventory, green build — the silently-plausible-return failure shape, and `getText()` on a password field is the *common* older-Swing idiom.
>
> Everything else in this entry — ctors, the mixin-consumer shape, duplicate-not-lift, per-layer state, peer lock-down — is unaffected and stands as written. The rejected-alternative "Make `getText()` return cleartext on the surrogate (skip masking)" below is **now the accepted behaviour**; it is kept in place, struck, as the record of a belief that was documented and confidently argued for four months.

**Decision.** Eleventh concrete surrogate, second consumer of [SD_sjtextfield](#SD_sjtextfield)'s `JTextComponentMixin`. Covers the SJPasswordField half of the text-component backfill; the SJTextArea half closes per [SD_sjtextarea](#SD_sjtextarea).

- **`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJPasswordField extends com.vaadin.flow.component.textfield.PasswordField implements JTextComponentMixin`** — peer is Vaadin `PasswordField` (extends `TextFieldBase`, renders `<input type="password">` with the browser's native mask glyph plus optional reveal button). JDK-shaped ctors (5 forms) all funnelling through the three-arg root form.
- **JPasswordField-specific surface.** `echoChar` field (default `'*'`) + `setEchoChar` (fires nothing — the JDK's setter is not bound, per SD_property_fanout_audit) + `echoCharIsSet` (true iff `echoChar != 0`); `char[] getPassword()` returns a fresh copy of the Document's text for the caller to zero after use. **`getText()` / `getText(int, int)` are not overridden** and return the Document verbatim via the mixin's defaults, matching the JDK (see the correction note above). `getUIClassID == "PasswordFieldUI"`. `validate()` visibility-widening override required per the SJTextField precedent.
- **JTextField-shape surface duplicated, not lifted.** SJPasswordField carries a copy of SJTextField's JTextField-specific code: `columns` + `setColumns` (R_match_swing_errors IAE on negative, peer.setWidth(CssConvert.columnsToCssWidth(N)) per R_layouts_close_enough), `horizontalAlignment` (drop-and-WARN per R_vaadin_first — same call SJTextField makes per SD_sjtextfield), ActionListener wiring on Enter via `addKeyPressListener(Key.ENTER, ...)`, narrow `setAction` (enabled / SHORT_DESCRIPTION / ACTION_COMMAND_KEY only — no NAME / icon / mnemonic per JDK JTextField's contract), `actionCommand` field + `postActionEvent` + `fireActionPerformed` (`actionCommand != null ? actionCommand : getText()` — JDK JTextField's fallback, inherited unchanged).
- **No `JTextFieldMixin` lift today.** JDK `JPasswordField extends JTextField` so the JTextField-shape surface is part of JPasswordField's contract. At the Vaadin level `PasswordField extends TextFieldBase` (not `TextField`), so SJPasswordField *cannot* extend SJTextField — the surface lives as duplicate concrete code instead. Two consumers don't justify a `JTextFieldMixin` lift today; if a third arrives the lift becomes the same SD_sjbutton/SD_sjtextfield mixin-first refactor.

**Emulator thin-down.**

- `:emulators.JPasswordField` swaps default peer from raw `PasswordField` to `SJPasswordField`. Two-layer R_swing_is_truth sync matches SJTextField's shape per SD_sjtextfield — independent `preventPeerEvents` flags on each layer; Vaadin's idempotent `setValue` short-circuits the surrogate's docToPeer echo back to the peer so the chain converges naturally. Each layer's Document holds the same text; each layer's `getText()` reads its own.
- `:emulators.JTextField`'s `JTextField(Component peer)` ctor widened the Enter-keypress check from `peer instanceof TextField` to `peer instanceof TextField || peer instanceof PasswordField` so emulator JPasswordField gets Enter-as-submit on its own listenerList (matching JDK JPasswordField inheriting JTextField's behaviour). The check stays restricted to the {TextField, PasswordField} pair rather than widened to `TextFieldBase` to avoid accidentally giving Enter-as-submit to a custom subclass passing a TextArea through the protected ctor — TextArea's contract is Enter-inserts-newline. Two-layer Enter ActionListener fan-out matches SJTextField's documented behaviour per SD_sjtextfield.
- **The emulator owns `echoChar`, and `getPassword()` reads the emulator's own Document** — the JDK's field and the JDK's body — so no password-surface getter reads the peer, and every one answers on any thread. That is the case that matters here: a login `SwingWorker` reads `getPassword()` in `doInBackground()` ([D_emulator_owned_state](../emulators/decisions.md#D_emulator_owned_state)). `setEchoChar` stores the field, then pushes the value on to the surrogate through `withPeer`, which keeps the surrogate's copy equal to it and raises the masking WARN there; the push is one-way, since the surrogate never observes the emulator. `getText()` likewise reads its own layer's Document via `super`, so a `BadLocationException` bound belongs to the Document the caller is holding.
- **Peer lock-down.** Nothing in this codebase or in the JDK extends `JPasswordField` — it is a leaf class in `javax.swing`'s hierarchy. With no subclass needing the [D_peer_ctor_injection](../emulators/decisions.md#D_peer_ctor_injection) custom-peer escape hatch, the protected `JPasswordField(Component peer)` ctor is removed and the root public ctor calls `super(new SJPasswordField())` directly. Result: every `JPasswordField` instance — through any public ctor or any future subclass — peers over an `SJPasswordField`. The class itself stays non-final to match JDK shape per R_swing_is_truth, but the peer type is locked at the type system level: a subclass can only reach `super` through the public ctors, all of which hard-code `SJPasswordField`. The same lock-down isn't applied to `:emulators.JTextField` because `JPasswordField` extends it — `JTextField`'s protected `(Component peer)` ctor is the seam `JPasswordField` uses to pass `SJPasswordField` up the chain. JCheckBox is the other JDK swing leaf today; its peer wiring is more entangled with JToggleButton inheritance, so the same lock-down is reachable but not urgent.

**Rationale.**

- **Duplicate-not-lift for the JTextField surface.** Two consumers (SJTextField, SJPasswordField) is below the bar SD_sjbutton set for AbstractButtonMixin (three queued consumers — SJToggleButton + SJCheckBox + SJRadioButton). The duplicated code is ~150 LoC of straight-line method bodies with no shared state holder requirement; refactoring is one mechanical pass when SJTextArea or a third consumer arrives. Lifting now would force a `JTextFieldMixin extends JTextComponentMixin` with a state holder for `columns` + `actionCommand` + `listenerList` + `action` + `actionPropertyChangeListener` + `horizontalAlignment` to keep the mixin self-contained, plus a `validate()` widening shape on every consumer — material complexity for a two-consumer base.
- ~~**Mask in `getText()`, not `readDocument()`.**~~ Retired with the masking; see the correction note. The mixin's `readDocument()` bypass survives on D_sync_reads_raw_model's own terms (the public accessor is an override seam regardless of who overrides it), not on this class's behalf.
- ~~**ActionEvent payload is cleartext** — "for JPasswordField that fallback would yield masked stars … rather than match that misbehaviour bug-for-bug per R_best_effort_behaviour".~~ Struck: the JDK's fallback already yields the entered value, because its `getText()` does. The surrogate now calls `getText()` like SJTextField, reaching the same behaviour without the fabricated justification. Kept struck rather than deleted — this is where a false premise visibly produced a *second* documented divergence, which is the point worth remembering.
- **Widen the emulator ctor check, don't bridge.** Two options for the emulator-side Enter wiring on JPasswordField: (a) widen `instanceof TextField` to `instanceof TextField || instanceof PasswordField` so the emulator wires its own keypress directly, OR (b) bridge from surrogate's listenerList to emulator's listenerList (shape SD_sjbutton takes for SJButton). Picked (a) for consistency with SJTextField's existing two-layer fan-out and to keep the emulator+surrogate listener wiring symmetric (each layer owns its own keypress listener; user clicks fire both). Bridging would special-case JPasswordField vs JTextField for no benefit.
- **A copy per layer, the emulator's pushed on to the surrogate's.** Two copies can disagree only when something writes the surrogate's behind the emulator's back, and at stage 2 nothing does: the emulator's setter is the only writer, and it pushes. The alternative, delegating the getter to the peer, makes `getEchoChar()` a peer read, which is what the emulator-owned-state rule removes.

**Alternatives.**

- **Lift JTextField surface into a `JTextFieldMixin` ahead of SJPasswordField.** Rejected today, accepted-when-third-consumer. Two consumers and ~150 LoC of duplication don't clear SD_sjbutton's three-consumer bar. The lift is mechanical when needed.
- **Bridge from surrogate's ActionListener fan-out to emulator's listenerList instead of widening the emulator's ctor check.** Rejected — adds asymmetry between SJTextField and SJPasswordField, and the widened check is a single-line change consistent with the documented "TextField + PasswordField submit on Enter; TextArea inserts newline" comment already in the emulator code.
- ~~**Make `getText()` return cleartext on the surrogate (skip masking).** Rejected — JDK JPasswordField's whole point is `getText()` masking to discourage accidental cleartext logging. Pure-surrogate users would lose this guard.~~ **Reversed 2026-09-01 — this is now the behaviour.** The premise is false in every clause: the JDK does not mask, its "whole point" is the `char[]` on `getPassword()`, and there was no guard to lose because Swing never had one. Left in place as the record; see the correction note at the top of this entry.
- **Single-source echoChar on the surrogate, the emulator delegating to it.** Shipped from `4287d91` until the emulator-owned-state rollout. Rejected because the delegating getter reads the peer, so `getEchoChar()` / `getPassword()` needed the peer on a worker thread. The one-way push costs one line per setter, and a two-way mirror is not needed because the surrogate never changes the value on its own.
- **Drop the ActionListener / setAction / horizontalAlignment surface from SJPasswordField entirely (keep just JTextComponent + password-specific).** Rejected — JDK JPasswordField is-a JTextField, so a pure-surrogate user gets the JTextField API expectations. Dropping would surprise migration code that uses `pf.addActionListener(...)` against a pure-surrogate SJPasswordField (the emulator-side path keeps working through emulator inheritance, but pure-surrogate breaks).

**Accepted limitations.**

- **JTextField surface duplicated.** ~150 LoC of straight-line method bodies repeated across SJTextField and SJPasswordField. Refactor cost is concrete when a third consumer arrives.
- **`getText() == ""` on a default-constructed SJPasswordField.** Same R_vaadin_first lossy direction SD_sjtextfield documents for SJTextField — empty Document → empty string.
- **`echoChar` is inert.** It round-trips through its own getter and nothing else: no server-side renderer consults it, and Vaadin exposes no way to hand it to the browser. Kept as state per R_decline_effect_only (the JDK stores it before any support check), and the `setEchoChar` boundary WARN tells a migrator whose "show password" UX depends on `echoChar=0` that it will not work.
- **Default `echoChar` is `'*'`, where a real JDK reports `8226` (`'•'`).** The JDK's value is not a constant — it comes from `UIManager`'s `PasswordField.echoChar`, i.e. the installed L&F, which SB-Emulators has none of (R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (b)). `'*'` is `javax.swing`'s own field initialiser and a defensible pick with no L&F to consult; a migrator comparing `getEchoChar()` against a hard-coded `8226` measured under Metal will see a divergence. Nothing observable depends on it.
- **Two-layer ActionListener fan-out for Enter.** Same as SJTextField per SD_sjtextfield — a listener registered on both layers fires twice. Migration convention: register on one layer.
- **`copy()` / `cut()` inherited from mixin's drop-and-WARN.** JDK JPasswordField beeps and refuses; our WARN-and-drop is the closest equivalent and keeps clipboard-safe semantics under R_match_swing_errors. Same shape `:emulators.JPasswordField` carries.
- **Vaadin PasswordField's browser-side mask glyph is fixed.** The browser renders whatever `<input type="password">` chooses, and `setEchoChar` cannot reach it. Same accepted divergence `:emulators.JPasswordField` carries.

**Validation.** Surrogate test suite: `SJPasswordFieldTest` covers ctors (no-arg / String / int / String+int / Document+String+int), R_swing_is_truth sync both directions (programmatic setText writes the peer; peer setValue writes Document), echoChar default + setter + fires-no-PCE + boundary WARN, **`getText()` returns cleartext and is unmoved by echoChar in either direction** (pinned against a real-JDK-25 measurement, per the correction note), getPassword returns fresh char[] per call, setEditable propagates to peer.isReadOnly(), ActionListener on Enter via postActionEvent (payload is the entered text), setColumns IAE on negative + writes peer width "Nch", horizontalAlignment R_match_swing_errors IAE + drop-and-WARN on non-LEADING, setAction narrow propagation (enabled / actionCommand / NAME-not-overwrite-text), copy / cut / paste WARN-and-drop, getUIClassID, happy-path zero stub WARNs. Emulator suite: `JPasswordFieldTest` asserts peer is `SJPasswordField`, two-layer R_swing_is_truth sync delivers consistent text, peer value change reaches both layers' Documents, and — the regression pins for this entry's correction — `getText()` returns cleartext, echoChar does not move it, and `getText(offs, len)` bounds-checks against the emulator's *own* Document rather than the surrogate's.

---

<a id="SD_sjtextarea"></a>
## SD_sjtextarea. `SJTextArea` surrogate — third `JTextComponentMixin` consumer, JTextArea-specific line-oriented helpers on the concrete

**Decision.** Twelfth concrete surrogate, third and final consumer of [SD_sjtextfield](#SD_sjtextfield)'s `JTextComponentMixin`. The three text-component consumers (SJTextField + SJPasswordField + SJTextArea) are all landed.

- **`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJTextArea extends com.vaadin.flow.component.textfield.TextArea implements JTextComponentMixin`** — peer is Vaadin `TextArea` (extends `TextFieldBase` alongside `TextField` / `PasswordField`, renders `<vaadin-text-area>` backed by HTML `<textarea>` with native browser wrapping). JDK-shaped ctors (6 forms — no-arg / String / rows+cols / String+rows+cols / Document / Document+String+rows+cols) all funnel through the four-arg root form.
- **JTextArea-specific surface.** `rows` / `setRows` / `getRows` (R_match_swing_errors IAE on negative, `peer.setMinRows(N)`, no PCE per JDK), `columns` / `setColumns` / `getColumns` (R_match_swing_errors IAE on negative, `peer.setWidth(CssConvert.columnsToCssWidth(N))` per R_layouts_close_enough close-enough, no PCE per JDK), `tabSize` (default 8) / `lineWrap` / `wrapStyleWord` (field-only round-trip with PCE per JDK; Vaadin TextArea wraps by default with no toggle to disable, tabSize uses browser default — accepted divergence per R_best_effort_behaviour), `append(String)` / `insert(String, int)` / `replaceRange(String, int, int)` (Document mutation helpers — null str is no-op for append/insert per JDK; null str means "delete only" for replaceRange; bad bounds throw IAE per R_match_swing_errors, each keeping the JDK's own message. **All three are bounds checks in front of one private `spliceDocument`** — an append being a splice of the empty span at the end, an insert a splice of the empty span at `pos`. That is not `javax.swing.JTextArea`'s shape and is not meant to be: three separate JDK-shaped bodies are carried JDK expression, which is what put this file on `LicenseHeaderTest.DERIVED_WITHOUT_NAME` under Header A, so re-splitting them for familiarity's sake reopens that. See PROVENANCE.md § "The exceptions"), line-oriented Document queries `getLineCount` / `getLineOfOffset(int)` / `getLineStartOffset(int)` / `getLineEndOffset(int)` (PlainDocument's default root element holds one child per line — exactly Swing's line model; BadLocationException on out-of-range matches JDK). `getUIClassID == "TextAreaUI"`. `validate()` visibility-widening override required per the SJTextField / SJPasswordField precedent.
- **No JTextField-shape surface.** JDK JTextArea inherits from JTextComponent (not JTextField), so SJTextArea has no ActionListener-on-Enter (Enter inserts a newline — that's the whole point of a multi-line editor), no `setAction(Action)`, and no `horizontalAlignment`. The `columns` field exists but with rows-combined semantics, not the JTextField shape; no shared codepath with the SJTextField/SJPasswordField duplicated surface.
- **Mixin re-used unchanged.** No mixin changes were needed — SD_sjtextfield forecast that the line-oriented helpers and rows/columns would land on the concrete (not lifted to the mixin), and that call held: only one of the three text-component consumers carries them, so lifting wouldn't reduce duplication. Same precedent SJPanel-specific surface follows per SD_sjpanel, and SJTextField/SJPasswordField's columns + horizontalAlignment follow per SD_sjtextfield.

**Emulator thin-down.**

- `:emulators.JTextArea` swaps default peer from raw `TextArea` to `SJTextArea`. **Two-layer R_swing_is_truth sync** matches SJTextField's shape per SD_sjtextfield — independent `preventPeerEvents` flags on each layer; Vaadin's idempotent `setValue` short-circuits the surrogate's docToPeer echo back to the peer so the chain converges naturally. Each layer's Document is independent; both reach the same text via the two-layer convergence.
- **The emulator owns its state where the JDK keeps it, and pushes it on to the surrogate.** `rows` / `columns` / `wrap` / `word` are emulator fields under the JDK's names, and `tabSize` is the emulator Document's `PlainDocument.tabSizeAttribute` property, as in the JDK. So installing a new Document resets `getTabSize()` to that Document's own value (8 for a fresh `PlainDocument` or a `DefaultStyledDocument`), and a `putProperty` on the Document is visible through the getter; both are measured on JDK 25 and pinned by `JTextAreaTest.stateMatchesTheJdk`. No getter reads the peer. Each setter pushes on to the surrogate through `withPeer`: `rows` / `columns` become its CSS sizing, and the other three are field-only there. The push happens only in the setters, so a Document swap leaves the surrogate's `tabSize` behind; that has no effect, because the surrogate renders none of it. **Two-layer PCE fan-out** for `tabSize` / `lineWrap` / `wrapStyleWord`: the surrogate fires its own PCE on its PropertyChangeSupport, and the emulator fires its own. `rows` / `columns` fire no PCE, as in the JDK.
- **Document mutation helpers duplicated, not delegated.** `append` / `insert` / `replaceRange` route through the *emulator's* own Document (via `getDocument()`) so emulator-registered DocumentListeners fire on the emulator's Document. The R_swing_is_truth sync mirrors the change into the surrogate's Document via the peer's `setValue` echo path; both layers' Documents converge. Delegating these to the surrogate would have skipped the emulator's DocumentListener fan-out for migrators registering on the emulator side (the listener-per-layer convention SD_sjtextfield documents). Same shape the line-helper queries (`getLineCount` etc.) take — they read from the emulator's Document, which is observationally identical to the surrogate's after R_swing_is_truth sync.
- **Peer lock-down per R_leaf_peer_lockdown.** `JTextArea` is a leaf class in `javax.swing`'s hierarchy (nothing in this codebase or in the JDK extends it). With no subclass needing the [D_peer_ctor_injection](../emulators/decisions.md#D_peer_ctor_injection) custom-peer escape hatch, the protected `JTextArea(Component peer)` ctor is removed and the root public ctor calls `super(new SJTextArea())` directly via `vaadinx.swing.text.JTextComponent`'s `(Component peer)` ctor. Result: every `JTextArea` instance — through any public ctor or any future subclass — peers over an `SJTextArea`. The class itself stays non-final to match JDK shape per R_swing_is_truth. Same lock-down precedent SD_sjpasswordfield records for `JPasswordField` (also a leaf); the seam at `JTextField` (a non-leaf, JPasswordField extends it) stays open per [R_leaf_peer_lockdown](../CLAUDE.md#hard-rules).

**Rationale.**

- **Line-oriented helpers stay on the concrete, not lifted to the mixin.** SD_sjtextfield forecast this and the call held: only SJTextArea uses `getLineCount` / `getLineOfOffset` / `getLineStartOffset` / `getLineEndOffset` / `append` / `insert` / `replaceRange`. Lifting to the mixin would force SJTextField + SJPasswordField to either inherit dead-but-callable surface or override every line helper to drop-and-WARN — material complexity for one consumer. Same precedent SJPanel-specific layout dispatch follows per SD_sjpanel.
- **`rows` / `columns` stay on the concrete, two-getter convention.** JDK JTextArea has its own `getColumns()` / `setColumns(int)` semantics (combined with rows for sizing) that differ from JTextField's standalone columns. Sharing a mixin field would force one or the other's semantics onto the other; per-concrete fields are honest. Same shape SD_sjtextfield records for SJTextField's columns + horizontalAlignment.
- **`tabSize` / `lineWrap` / `wrapStyleWord` PCE-bearing field-only round-trip.** JDK JTextArea fires PCE for all three even though they don't reach the rendered text on a Vaadin TextArea (browser default tab width; always-on wrapping). Field-only round-trip with PCE keeps migrator code that reads them back honest, and the PCE fan-out keeps BeanInfo-style probes working. Same R_best_effort_behaviour best-effort stance the emulator carried pre-thin-down.
- **No ActionListener-on-Enter wiring.** Enter inserting a newline is the JDK JTextArea contract — wiring Enter→ActionEvent here would break multi-line input. The wiring lives only on JTextField + JPasswordField (the {TextField, PasswordField} restricted-pair check in `:emulators.JTextField`'s ctor explicitly excludes TextArea per SD_sjpasswordfield's rationale).
- **append / insert / replaceRange duplicated on the emulator side, not delegated.** Each layer's DocumentListener fires only on that layer's Document (the per-layer-listener convention SD_sjtextfield documents). If the emulator delegated these to the surrogate, mutations would only fire the surrogate's DocumentListener; the R_swing_is_truth sync would mirror into the emulator's Document and fire the emulator's listener too, but only as the *secondary* mutation (surrogate's listener fires first, on a different Document). Migrators registering DocumentListener on the emulator would see the right events but with surprising ordering — clearer to keep the emulator's helpers operating on the emulator's Document directly (the surrogate's helpers do the same on the surrogate's side, both reaching the same text via the convergent R_swing_is_truth sync).

**Alternatives.**

- **Lift line-oriented helpers + append/insert/replaceRange into the mixin.** Rejected — only one of three consumers uses them; lifting would force the other two to inherit dead surface or drop-and-WARN every method. SJTextField/SJPasswordField stay focused on their JTextField-shape surface.
- **Delegate emulator's append/insert/replaceRange to surrogate.** Rejected — would change the order of DocumentListener fan-out across layers (surrogate's listener fires first, emulator's listener fires as the R_swing_is_truth-sync echo). Per SD_sjtextfield the per-layer listener convention is "each layer fires on its own Document"; the emulator path mutating the emulator's Document directly preserves that.
- **Make `setLineWrap(true)` actually disable wrap on the peer via custom CSS.** Rejected — Vaadin TextArea's browser textarea has no straightforward toggle, custom CSS would touch shadow DOM. R_best_effort_behaviour best-effort, field-only round-trip with PCE is the SD_sjtextfield precedent for visual-flag setters that lack a Vaadin counterpart.
- **Drop `rows` / `columns` peer mapping entirely (since `setMinRows` / `setWidth` only approximate).** Rejected — R_layouts_close_enough close-enough has been the consistent stance on dimension hints; the existing `peer.setMinRows(N)` / `peer.setWidth(CssConvert.columnsToCssWidth(N))` pattern is what JTextField + emulator JTextArea both use, and migrators expect a 5-row text area to render at least 5 rows tall.

**Accepted limitations.**

- **`tabSize` / `lineWrap` / `wrapStyleWord` are observably ignored on the peer.** Vaadin TextArea wraps by default (no toggle); browsers render tabs at their default width. Field round-trip + PCE matches JDK so migrator code that reads them back stays honest; the visual reality differs. R_best_effort_behaviour best-effort.
- **`rows` dimension approximated via `setMinRows`.** A JTextArea with 5 rows under JDK Swing renders exactly 5 rows tall by default. Vaadin's `setMinRows(5)` renders at least 5 rows but allows the textarea to grow. R_layouts_close_enough close-enough.
- **`columns` dimension approximated via `setWidth("calc(<n>ch + 2em)")`** — one shared mapping, `CssConvert.columnsToCssWidth`, called from all four sites (SJTextField / SJPasswordField / SJTextArea and the emulator's own `JTextField`). Same R_layouts_close_enough stance on the character unit: `"ch"` is the width of a `"0"` glyph, close to the JDK's "character column" but not pixel-identical (Swing measures `m`-width-class characters). **The `2em` term is not slack, it is the field's chrome** — Vaadin fields are `border-box`, so a bare `n + "ch"` sizes the host to the text alone and the input-field padding, borders and inner `<input>` padding eat the entire budget: measured in-browser at 22px against a 12px font, which left a 3-column field with *negative* room for text, rendering about one digit wide. Browser-verified at `calc(3ch + 2em)` → 3.3 characters usable. Nothing server-side can catch this class of error (the CSS is valid either way and Karibu asserts only the string), which is why the Sampler `Focus` route's 3-digit phone fields are the standing visual check.
- **No PCE for `rows` / `columns`.** Matches JDK (which fires no PCE for either); migrator code listening on `"rows"` / `"columns"` PCE wouldn't have worked under real Swing either.
