- **`getLineCount() == 0` for non-line-structured Documents.** When `getDocument().getDefaultRootElement() == null`, the helper returns 0. Real Swing depends on the same default root element, so this gap exists in JDK too — accepted.
- **`getLineEndOffset(line)` returns the raw element end offset.** JDK adjusts the last line's end offset down by 1 to "hide the implicit break at the end of the document"; we return PlainDocument's element end-offset directly, matching the emulator's pre-thin-down behaviour. One-character divergence on the last line; accepted per R_best_effort_behaviour.
- **Two-layer PCE fan-out for `tabSize` / `lineWrap` / `wrapStyleWord`.** Same SD_sjtextfield / SD_sjpasswordfield convention — migrators register on one layer; the surrogate's PCE fires too. No mixing of per-layer fields.
- **`copy()` / `cut()` / `paste()` inherited from mixin's drop-and-WARN.** Same as SJTextField / SJPasswordField — clipboard is post-Sampler.
- **Peer-locked-down JTextArea.** Same R_leaf_peer_lockdown stance SD_sjpasswordfield records for JPasswordField. A subclass cannot swap the peer type; the class is non-final but its peer is hard-coded to `SJTextArea`.

**Validation.** Surrogate test suite: `SJTextAreaTest` covers ctors (6 forms), default Document is PlainDocument (inherited), R_swing_is_truth sync both directions (programmatic setText writes the peer; peer setValue writes the Document) including listener-fire-once regressions, setDocument swap fires "document" PCE + new doc reaches the peer, setEditable propagates to peer.isReadOnly() + fires "editable" PCE (inherited), setRows / setColumns IAE on negative + write peer minRows / "Nch" width + fire no PCE (matches JDK), setTabSize / setLineWrap / setWrapStyleWord fire PCE, append/insert/replaceRange happy paths + null no-op + null delete + IAE on bad bounds, line-oriented Document queries (line count, line of offset, line start/end offset) + BadLocationException on bad input, getUIClassID, drop-and-WARN inheritance (copy / cut / paste / getCaretPosition WARN), happy-path zero stub WARNs. Emulator suite: existing `JTextAreaTest` retained — peer assertion auto-updates since `SJTextArea IS-A TextArea`. All `:surrogates` (1113 tests across both modules) + `:sampler` test suites green.

---

<a id="SD_sjcombobox"></a>
## SD_sjcombobox. `SJComboBox` surrogate — `ComboBoxModel` source of truth, items sync via `setItems`-snapshot, R_vaadin_first-trimmed surface

**Decision.** Thirteenth concrete surrogate. First new-CRUD-component surrogate (the existing-emulator backfill queue is closed; new components are picked on a clean slate per [D_surrogate_first](../emulators/decisions.md#D_surrogate_first)).

`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJComboBox<T> extends com.vaadin.flow.component.combobox.ComboBox<T> implements JComponentMixin, java.awt.ItemSelectable` — peer is Vaadin `ComboBox<T>` (generic in single `<T>`, extends `ComboBoxBase` extends `AbstractSinglePropertyField`). Implements `ItemSelectable` to satisfy `java.awt.event.ItemEvent`'s constructor source-type contract (the JDK ItemEvent ctor requires an `ItemSelectable` source) — same precedent SJButton's `AbstractButtonMixin` follows per SD_toggle_checkbox_first_cut's `extends ItemSelectable` lift.

**Constructors.** Four JDK-shaped public ctors (no-arg / `ComboBoxModel<T>` / `T[]` / `Vector<T>`) all funnel through the model ctor. Array / Vector ctors wrap into `DefaultComboBoxModel` per JDK. `ComboBoxStateStore.lastSelected` is seeded from `model.getSelectedItem()` so the first selection-change fires the JDK-canonical DESELECTED-prev pair correctly.

**Source of truth: `ComboBoxModel`.** R_swing_is_truth — JDK reuses `DefaultComboBoxModel` directly per [D_event_port_policy](../emulators/decisions.md#D_event_port_policy) (events are `Object`-source; no Component-typed fields). Our `ListDataListener` on the model snapshots the model's items into the peer via `setItems(snapshot)` on every list event, and pushes the model's current selection through Vaadin's `setValue` under a `preventPeerEvents` guard. Browser-originated edits funnel back via `addValueChangeListener` (R_callswing_envelope — wrapped in `SHelper.callSwing`) and write through `model.setSelectedItem(value)`, which fires the model's `CONTENTS_CHANGED(-1, -1)` and closes the loop.

**ItemEvent fan-out.** On every selection commit the surrogate fans out the JDK-canonical pair: `ItemEvent.DESELECTED` on the previously-selected item (when non-null) followed by `ItemEvent.SELECTED` on the newly-selected item (when non-null). The `ActionEvent` fires after the pair, matching JDK JComboBox's commit sequencing. `ComboBoxStateStore.lastSelected` is the rolling cursor.

**Mutator-on-non-mutable-model contract per R_match_swing_errors.** `addItem` / `insertItemAt` / `removeItem` / `removeItemAt` / `removeAllItems` throw `IllegalStateException` when the installed model is not a `MutableComboBoxModel`. Matches JDK JComboBox's runtime check (JDK throws too — wrong-type cast inside the JComboBox.addItem body). `removeAllItems` short-circuits to `DefaultComboBoxModel.removeAllElements` for the JDK-default path.

**Editable mode = Vaadin `setAllowCustomValue`.** `setEditable(true)` flips Vaadin's `allowCustomValue` flag and installs a `customValueSetListener` that commits the typed value into the model via `model.setSelectedItem(detail)` — the model's CONTENTS_CHANGED then drives the usual fan-out. Cleartext typed strings are committed verbatim; for non-String `T` the cast WARNs and the commit drops (we don't have a parser for arbitrary T). `setEditable(false)` tears down the customValue subscription via the holder's tracked `Registration`. The full `javax.swing.ComboBoxEditor` surface (custom editor components, separate editor ActionListener, editor-driven selectAll) is R_vaadin_first drop-and-WARN — Vaadin owns the typed-input UX inside the combobox, no semantic-cleanly-mappable counterpart for a custom editor component.

**Renderer is Vaadin-first per R_vaadin_first.** Surrogate-layer users install Vaadin `Renderer<T>` directly via the inherited `ComboBox.setRenderer` (or `setItemLabelGenerator` for label-only customisation). The JDK `ListCellRenderer` surface lives only on the `:emulators.JComboBox` layer and bridges into a Vaadin `ComponentRenderer` at the boundary. The default rendering uses Vaadin's built-in `String.valueOf` item-label generator (matches JDK's "model value via toString" default), so a fresh SJComboBox renders sanely with zero configuration. No `SListCellRenderer` parallel — the JDK signature's `index` / `isSelected` / `cellHasFocus` parameters have no Vaadin counterpart at render time, so a Vaadin-flavored interface mirroring them would carry dead-API surface that R_vaadin_first rejects.

**`maximumRowCount` mirrors `pageSize`.** R_match_swing_errors IAE on `count < 1` matches JDK's "only positive row counts" contract. Stored on the holder as a shadow since pairing it with the customValue Registration kept the swap atomic earlier; could read from `peer.getPageSize()` instead, but the holder field stays for symmetry with the `editable` shadow. Fires `"maximumRowCount"` PCE per JDK.

**Popup: the surrogate supplies the typed `OpenedChangeEvent` Vaadin's `ComboBox` lacks, and every open and close notifies.** As of Vaadin 25.3, `ComboBoxBase` has no `addOpenedChangeListener` (`DatePicker`, `Select`, `Dialog` and `ContextMenuBase` do), although its `opened` property is browser-synchronized (`@Synchronize(property = "opened", value = "opened-changed")` on `isOpened()`). `SJComboBox.addOpenedChangeListener(ComponentEventListener<SJComboBox.OpenedChangeEvent>)` fills the gap, shaped like `DatePicker.OpenedChangeEvent` (`isOpened()` plus the inherited `isFromClient()`), and fires off an element property listener on `opened` — the raw listener stays inside the surrogate, behind a Vaadin-shaped API, the way `SWindow` keeps its client-close bookkeeping. The surrogate's own `PopupMenuListener` fan-out is one subscriber, for either origin, so `setPopupVisible` is plain `setOpened` and a user's click fires `popupMenuWillBecomeVisible` / `popupMenuWillBecomeInvisible` exactly as a server call does. The `:emulators` `JComboBox` is a second subscriber and never touches the element itself ([D_jcombobox](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jcombobox)). **Graduation is mechanical:** if Vaadin adds a typed event to `ComboBoxBase`, ours clashes with it by erasure and stops compiling, which is the prompt to delete ours and subscribe to upstream's.

**`setAction(Action)` narrow propagation.** Mirrors JDK JComboBox's narrow Action surface: `Action.SHORT_DESCRIPTION` → `setTooltipText`, `Action.ACTION_COMMAND_KEY` → `setActionCommand`, `Action.isEnabled` → `setEnabled`. NAME / icon / mnemonic / accelerator-key are not honored — JDK JComboBox doesn't surface those (combo boxes don't carry button-style label chrome). The Action's `actionPerformed` is invoked alongside the surrogate's own ActionListener fan-out on every selection commit via an `ActionListener` bridge installed during `setAction(non-null)` and torn down via PCL detach during `setAction(other)` / `setAction(null)`.

**State holder: `ComboBoxStateStore`.** Eight slots — model, modelListener Registration handle, preventPeerEvents flag, editable shadow, customValueRegistration, valueChangeRegistration, listenerList (`EventListenerList` for ItemListener / ActionListener / PopupMenuListener / ListDataListener), actionCommand + action + actionPropertyChangeListener bookkeeping, maximumRowCount, lastSelected cursor. Same shape ButtonStateStore takes per SD_sjbutton.

**R_vaadin_first drop-and-WARN surface.** `ComboBoxEditor` (full editor port: getEditor / setEditor / configureEditor), `KeySelectionManager` (browser handles type-to-search), `lightWeightPopup` (Swing-internal popup chrome — silent on `true` to match JDK default, WARN on `false`), `prototypeDisplayValue` (sizing hint — silent on null clear, WARN on non-null set), `selectWithKeyChar` (subsumed by browser type-to-search). Setter values discarded; getter returns JDK default; PCE NOT fired (no shadow round-trip, R_vaadin_first). Migrator's affordance: rewrite to use a property Vaadin can render, or accept the gap.

**Visibility-widening `validate()` override.** Vaadin `ComboBox` (via `AbstractField`) declares a `protected validate()` for input validation that clashes with `ComponentMixin`'s public `validate()` contract. The `public void validate() { super.validate(); }` widening preserves Vaadin's input-validation behaviour while satisfying the mixin. Same SD_toggle_checkbox_first_cut / SD_sjtextfield / SD_sjpasswordfield / SD_sjtextarea precedent.

**Ctor window for `setValue` override.** Two windows where `setValue(T)` must fall through to `super.setValue` and skip the model write: (a) construction window — Vaadin's ComboBox super-ctor calls `setItems` which itself calls `setValue(null)` before our ctor body has installed the model, so `store.model` is null; (b) inside a model→peer push — when `pushItemsToPeer` or `pushSelectionToPeer` runs, `preventPeerEvents` is true and Vaadin's `setItems` internally calls `setValue(null)` on `this`. Routing that through `model.setSelectedItem` would corrupt the model's selection mid-push and double-fire ListDataListener forwarders. Both windows guard via `if (store.model == null || store.preventPeerEvents) { super.setValue(value); return; }` at the top of the override. Same SD_sjspinner SJSpinner.setValue construction-window precedent.

**Rationale.**

- **`ComboBox<T>` over `Select<T>`.** Vaadin `Select<T>` doesn't allow custom values, has no filter UI, and its popup is not page-sized. JDK JComboBox's editable mode + filter-as-you-type behaviour land on `ComboBox` cleanly; `Select` would force editable to drop-and-WARN. Vaadin's own ComboBox doc explicitly recommends Select for "simpler overlay selection without filtering / lazy loading / custom values" — JComboBox's contract covers all three.
- **`DefaultComboBoxModel` reuse over a port.** JDK `DefaultComboBoxModel<E>` has no Component-typed fields (D_whitelist_porting whitelist excludes it from the port set) and its `ListDataEvent` source is `Object` (D_event_port_policy reuse policy). Reusing JDK's class gives migrators their existing model code unchanged and avoids reimplementing `MutableComboBoxModel`'s insert/remove/contents semantics.
- **Snapshot-based items push.** Vaadin's `ComboBoxBase` surfaces `setItems(Collection<T>)` / `setItems(T...)` but no incremental `addItem(T)`. Rebuilding the full list on every model event is honest and matches Vaadin's diffing contract (Vaadin diffs internally; the visible cost is O(n) per change, which is fine for typical-CRUD-shape combo boxes — under 100 items). Holding a `ListDataView` and mutating its backing list would couple us to Vaadin's data-view API — fragile if Vaadin reshapes it.
- **The surrogate fills a missing Vaadin event rather than parking it.** Supplying the Vaadin counterpart a Swing contract needs is a surrogate's job under R_vaadin_first, and a typical CRUD app does hear this event: filling the model lazily in `popupMenuWillBecomeVisible` is a common Swing idiom. A raw property listener is acceptable *behind* a typed API; what is not acceptable is the emulator reaching into its peer's element for it.
- **R_vaadin_first cell-renderer split.** The JDK `ListCellRenderer` surface lives at the emulator layer because it returns `java.awt.Component` (incompatible with our `vaadinx.awt.Component` hierarchy). At the surrogate layer Vaadin's `Renderer<T>` is the native vehicle and exposes a richer surface (LitRenderer for HTML templates, ComponentRenderer for component construction). A parallel `SListCellRenderer<T>` would mirror the JDK 5-arg signature, but `index` / `isSelected` / `cellHasFocus` have no Vaadin render-time counterpart — at least 2 of the 4 args would be dead-API. Not worth the surface.
- **ItemEvent pair, not single combined event.** JDK JComboBox fires DESELECTED-prev followed by SELECTED-new on every selection change — migrator MVC code that treats the two events independently (e.g. "when X deselected, save its state") relies on the split. Combining into a single ITEM_STATE_CHANGED with the new item would lose the deselect signal.
- **Routing `setValue` through the model.** JDK JComboBox's setSelectedItem and setValue both end up in the model. Routing both surrogate paths through `model.setSelectedItem` keeps ItemListener / ActionListener fan-out consistent regardless of which API was called — `cb.setValue(x)` and `cb.setSelectedItem(x)` are observationally identical.
- **`maximumRowCount` IAE on `< 1`.** JDK doesn't validate bounds (silently accepts any int); we add the IAE because Vaadin `setPageSize(0)` produces a degenerate empty-popup state migrators wouldn't expect from the JDK. R_best_effort_behaviour best-effort tightening — the migration target's combo boxes never actually use `< 1`, and the IAE catches programming errors loud.

**Alternatives.**

- **Skip the `Renderer<T>` Vaadin-first stance and ship `SListCellRenderer<T>` mirroring the 5-arg JDK signature.** Considered and rejected: 2 of 4 args (`isSelected`, `cellHasFocus`) have no Vaadin render-time counterpart and 1 (`index`) requires reconstruction by scanning the model on every render. A parallel surrogate-layer interface with mostly-dead args is the API-round-trip-without-counterpart anti-pattern R_vaadin_first calls out.
- **Throw on non-Mutable model mutator instead of IllegalStateException.** JDK throws `IllegalStateException`-equivalent (actually a ClassCastException from the unchecked cast inside JComboBox.addItem). IllegalStateException with a pointer-at-cause message names the model's class — louder for the migrator, same R_match_swing_errors spirit.
- **Defer the browser-driven popup events until Vaadin adds a typed event to `ComboBoxBase` (R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (a)).** Rejected: the deferral does not keep the raw listener out, only moves it. The emulator needs the `opened` state for `isPopupVisible` regardless, so under a deferral it held a property listener on the peer's element itself — the leak one layer up, where the decision log did not see it.
- **Name the event after the Swing concept (`addPopupVisibleListener`).** Rejected — a stage-3 migrator is writing against a Vaadin component, and `addOpenedChangeListener` / `OpenedChangeEvent` is the name every other Vaadin overlay uses; Swing callers already have `PopupMenuListener`.
- **Wire `keySelectionManager` to Vaadin's filter UI.** Considered and rejected — Vaadin's filter is character-based already (type-to-search), and `KeySelectionManager` is JDK's plug-in point for custom keyboard nav. The two have different semantics; mapping one to the other would silently change the migrated app's keyboard UX. Drop-and-WARN is more honest.
- **Lift JComboBox into a `ComboBoxMixin` ahead of a future second consumer.** No second consumer in sight (JDK has no `JEditableComboBox` etc.). Mixin-first per SD_sjbutton was right when we knew of three consumers (SJButton + SJToggleButton + SJCheckBox); for a single-consumer concrete the mixin overhead doesn't pay back.
- **Single combined `ItemEvent` instead of the DESELECTED+SELECTED pair.** Rejected — JDK contract; migrator code that filters on `stateChange == DESELECTED` would silently break.

**Accepted limitations.**

- **A user-opened dropdown is already open when `popupMenuWillBecomeVisible` fires.** Swing notifies *before* showing; the browser opens first and the server hears afterwards, so items a listener adds reach an overlay that is already showing — and do render there: Sampler's lazily filled planet combo, driven by Playwright on Chromium 155 (2026-09-29), showed its four items in the open overlay on the first click, and Esc, reopen and pick each fired the matching event.
- **`popupMenuCanceled` never fires.** The browser's close carries no reason, so Esc or a click outside is indistinguishable from a pick; both fire `popupMenuWillBecomeInvisible` only. Guessing would invent cancels (R_no_silent_improvements). R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (a), the same gap `JPopupMenu` documents.
- **`setEditable(true)` + custom value commit only commits as the model's selected item.** JDK JComboBox optionally adds the typed value to the model's items list; we delegate that to migrator code via the `ActionListener` callback. Migrators wanting insert-on-commit add `cb.addActionListener(e -> model.addElement(cb.getSelectedItem()))` in their setup.
- **For non-String `T`, custom value commit WARNs and drops.** We don't have a parser for arbitrary types; migrators with `JComboBox<MyType>` and `setEditable(true)` need to install their own custom value listener via Vaadin's `addCustomValueSetListener` if they want non-String parsing.
- **`prototypeDisplayValue` is silently dropped.** Vaadin combo box doesn't surface a prototype-driven width hint; the closest analog is `setMinWidth("Nch")` which migrators can call directly.
- **Items rebuild on every list change is O(n).** Acceptable for typical-CRUD combo boxes (under 100 items). Lazy-loading combo boxes (large datasets) should bypass JComboBox and use Vaadin's `setItemsPageable` directly per the R_vaadin_first Vaadin-first stance.
- **Default `actionCommand` is `"comboBoxChanged"`.** JDK's default too; surfaced so migrator code that filters on `actionCommand == "comboBoxChanged"` works unchanged.
- **`setSelectedIndex(-1)` clears selection without IAE.** JDK accepts -1 (matches "no selection"); we do too. Out-of-bounds positive indices throw IAE per R_match_swing_errors.

**Validation.** Surrogate test suite: `SJComboBoxTest` (34 tests) covers ctors (4 forms + null-NPE), items API (addItem / insertItemAt / removeItemAt / removeAllItems + non-Mutable IllegalStateException), selection (setSelectedItem + ItemEvent pair + ActionEvent fire ordering, peer ValueChange writes through model, setSelectedIndex IAE on out-of-bounds, getSelectedObjects single-element, equality short-circuit on no-change), model swap (PCE + items rebuild), editable (allowCustomValue mirror + customValue commit), maximumRowCount + IAE + pageSize mirror + PCE, popup show/hide events + idempotency, a user's open / close (driven through Flow's own client-update path, `ElementPropertyMap.deferredUpdateFromClient`) firing `PopupMenuListener` and an `OpenedChangeEvent` that reports its origin, ListDataListener forwarding accounting for JDK auto-selection on first-add, setAction narrow propagation, R_vaadin_first drops WARN, getUIClassID, happy-path zero-WARN. Emulator suite: `JComboBoxTest` (26 tests) covers thin-shell delegation (peer-is-SJComboBox, R_leaf_peer_lockdown lock-down absence of protected ctor, two-layer Item / Action / Popup / ListData / model PCE listener lists, source rebound to emulator on bridges, a user's open / close reaching the emulator's `PopupMenuListener`s, ListCellRenderer registration round-trip via getRenderer, DefaultListCellRenderer text round-trip, setAction). Sampler: `ComboBoxesView` route + `ComboBoxesWarnInventoryTest` (user-path + API-surface micro-driver) — both green at zero stub WARN.

---

<a id="SD_sjmenubar"></a>
## SD_sjmenubar. `SJMenuBar` surrogate — asymmetric split, JDK menu tree lives emulator-only, tree-rebuild push API

**Decision.** Fourteenth concrete surrogate. The JMenuBar slice. The surrogate-layer Vaadin component is `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJMenuBar extends com.vaadin.flow.component.menubar.MenuBar implements JComponentMixin` — the **only** new surrogate class for this slice. JDK's `JMenu` / `JMenuItem` / `JCheckBoxMenuItem` / `JRadioButtonMenuItem` / `JSeparator` get **no surrogate counterparts**; their JDK-shape tree lives entirely on the `:emulators` layer (per [D_menu_tree](../emulators/decisions.md#D_menu_tree)).

**The asymmetric split.** Vaadin `MenuBar`'s menu tree is built by `parent.addItem(...) → MenuItem`, with `MenuItem`'s ctor signature `public MenuItem(ContextMenu, SerializableRunnable)` — it requires Vaadin-internal collaborators (a `ContextMenu`-shaped owner + a content-reset callback) and is effectively package-private to Vaadin's own use. A surrogate cannot extend it as `SJMenuItem extends MenuItem` and substitute it where Vaadin's own `addItem` would return one. Two consequences:

1. The smallest surrogate-layer unit that's a true Vaadin Component is the whole bar, not the individual items.
2. R_vaadin_first then says don't ship parallel `SJMenu` / `SJMenuItem` classes that exist only to mirror JDK shape — pure-surrogate users compose with `MenuBar.addItem(text, listener)` directly. Same logic [SD_sjcombobox](#SD_sjcombobox) used to decline a parallel `SListCellRenderer` port whose JDK-only parameters had no Vaadin counterpart.

**Tree-rebuild push API: `MenuNode` + `rebuildFromTree`.** The emulator-side `JMenuBar` carries the JDK tree of `JMenu` / `JMenuItem` instances. On every tree mutation it walks the tree and calls `peer.rebuildFromTree(List<MenuNode>)`. `MenuNode` is a small immutable descriptor in `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates`:

```java
record MenuNode(String text, com.vaadin.flow.component.Component icon, boolean enabled,
                boolean separator, boolean checkable, boolean checked,
                javax.swing.KeyStroke accelerator, Runnable onClick,
                java.util.List<MenuNode> children) { … }
```

The surrogate's `rebuildFromTree` does:

1. `removeAll()` to clear the prior Vaadin MenuItem tree.
2. Tear down all previously-installed accelerator `Registration`s (held in `MenuBarStateStore.acceleratorRegistrations`).
3. For each top-level node: call `addItem(text, e -> SHelper.callSwing(node.onClick))` (or `addItem(component, ...)` when icon is non-null), then recurse into `node.children` via the resulting `MenuItem.getSubMenu().addItem(...)` / `addSeparator()` overloads. Each leaf node's `MenuItem.setEnabled` / `setCheckable` / `setChecked` write the JDK-shape state through.
4. Walk all leaf nodes again after tree construction; for each non-null `accelerator`, install a `Shortcuts.addShortcutListener(getUI().get(), () -> SHelper.callSwing(node.onClick), key, modifiers)` via `KeyConvert.toVaadinKeyBinding(stroke)`. Track the resulting `Registration` in the holder so the next rebuild tears it down. Unmappable keystrokes log + skip browser install (matches the `registerKeyboardAction` shape).

Snapshot-based rebuild matches SD_sjcombobox's items-replace stance: O(n) per mutation, one code path for incremental + bulk changes, idempotent because Vaadin diffs internally. The MenuBar's typical CRUD shape (a few top-level menus, a dozen items each) is well within the cost envelope.

<a id="SD_menu_patch_in_place"></a>**SD_menu_patch_in_place. A push that changes only checked states, click handlers or accelerators patches the rendered items instead of rebuilding them** (`MenuTreeBuilder.patchInPlace`, shared with SD_sjpopupmenu). Steps 1 and 3 above are then skipped: checkable items get `setChecked`, every item's click listener is rebound to the new node's `onClick` (the listener reads a per-item holder rather than capturing the runnable), and step 4 reinstalls the accelerators from the new tree. Anything else — text, icon, enabled, checkable, or the tree's shape — takes the full rebuild.

- **Why.** A click on a `JCheckBoxMenuItem` / `JRadioButtonMenuItem` pushes a new tree in the same round-trip as the click. Vaadin's own `MenuItemBase` click listener has already toggled the clicked item and queued `contextMenuConnector.setChecked($0, $1)` for it; a rebuild detaches that item, Flow still sends the queued call, and the detached element reaches the browser as `null`, throwing `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading '_item')` on every such click. Harmless, since the rebuilt items carry the right state, but it lands in the migrator's console. Upstream: [vaadin/flow-components#10227](https://github.com/vaadin/flow-components/issues/10227), reproduced there with plain Vaadin 25.3.0; this entry stays valid after a fix, since it also spares a full rebuild per toggle.
- **Why not text and enabled too.** `MenuBarRootItem` re-renders the bar's buttons for theme, class and tooltip changes only, so an in-place `setText` / `setEnabled` on a root item would leave its button stale. Those stay on the rebuild path. **Residual:** a checkable item's click whose handler *also* renames, enables/disables or restructures some item still rebuilds, and still logs the upstream error.
- **Icons compare by identity**, which works only because the emulator's `JMenuItem` keeps its converted Vaadin icon while the Swing `Icon` is unchanged. A fresh conversion per snapshot would send every item with an icon down the rebuild path.

**Top-level separator: drop-and-WARN R_vaadin_first.** Vaadin `MenuBar` doesn't expose `addSeparator()` at the top level (only `SubMenu.addSeparator()` exists). When a `MenuNode` flagged `separator == true` appears at the top level of the rebuild walk, the surrogate calls `SHelper.onUnimplemented(this, "addSeparator/top-level", node)` and skips it. SubMenu separators land cleanly via `subMenu.addSeparator()`. R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (a) — the gap graduates if [vaadin-menu-bar surfaces top-level separators upstream](https://github.com/vaadin/flow-components) (no current issue tracked).

**Top-level mnemonics: drop-and-WARN R_vaadin_first.** Vaadin `MenuBar` has no equivalent of Swing's "Alt+F opens File menu" mnemonic accelerator — opening a top-level menu programmatically isn't surfaced as a typed API. The emulator's `JMenu.setMnemonic(int)` field-shadows + fires `"mnemonic"` PCE per R_swing_is_truth, but the surrogate's tree-rebuild ignores the field. R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (a) — graduates if Vaadin upstream lands a `MenuItem.open()` API.

**Item accelerators: wired via Vaadin `Shortcuts`.** Per-item `KeyStroke` accelerators (Ctrl+S on Save) install as UI-level shortcuts that drive `node.onClick` directly — same machinery already used for `registerKeyboardAction`. `Shortcuts.addShortcutListener` is scoped to the `getUI()` of the menubar (not WHEN_IN_FOCUSED_WINDOW; Vaadin shortcuts default to WHEN_IN_FOCUSED_WINDOW-ish behaviour at the UI scope). Lifetime: one `Registration` per accelerator, tracked in the store, all torn down at the start of every rebuild + on detach. Unmappable strokes (keyCodes outside the `KeyConvert.vkToVaadinKey` table) log via `SHelper.onUnimplemented` and skip install but the field-side `setAccelerator` round-trip survives.

**Icons in items: reuse the Path 1 bridge.** Each `MenuNode.icon` is a Vaadin `Component` (the emulator pre-converts `javax.swing.Icon` via `vaadinx.EHelper.toVaadinIconComponent`, which routes ImageIcons through `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.util.Icons.imageIconToVaadinImage` per [D_icon_rendering](../emulators/decisions.md#D_icon_rendering)). The rebuild path uses `addItem(component, listener)` when `icon != null` (Vaadin's component-form ctor). When both text and icon are present, the rebuild builds an inline `Span` containing a child `Image` + a child text `Span` so both render together.

**Toggle items: `setCheckable(true) + setChecked(state)`.** `MenuNode.checkable == true` triggers a post-`addItem` call to `MenuItem.setCheckable(true)`; `MenuNode.checked` drives `setChecked(state)`. The click listener the rebuild installed flips `node.checked` server-side via the emulator's `JCheckBoxMenuItem.setSelected`, whose push is then applied in place per [SD_menu_patch_in_place](#SD_menu_patch_in_place). For `JRadioButtonMenuItem` the surrogate originally shipped without `ButtonGroup` coordination (multi-select-within-menu was the accepted gap); the gap closed when `vaadinx.swing.ButtonGroup` landed per [D_buttongroup](../emulators/decisions.md#D_buttongroup). The radio menu item's `setSelected` and `makeOnClick` consult the installed group at the top of their bodies; cascade-deselect-prior-selection lands per the JDK shape.

**Listener fan-out via `SHelper.callSwing`.** Every Vaadin `ClickEvent<MenuItem>` handler the rebuild installs is a `e -> SHelper.callSwing(node.onClick)` — R_callswing_envelope funnel for blocking-dialog work. The emulator wraps its `JMenuItem`'s `fireActionPerformed` in `node.onClick`, so the entire JDK ActionEvent dispatch funnels through `callSwing` once.

**State holder: `MenuBarStateStore`.** Five slots: `currentTree` (the last-rebuild snapshot, for diagnostics + idempotency), `acceleratorRegistrations` (`Map<KeyStroke, Registration>` for teardown on next rebuild), `clickRegistrations` (also tracked but currently unused — Vaadin's `addItem(text, listener)` returns void, so click registrations live on the rebuilt MenuItems and die naturally with `removeAll()`), `mnemonicRegistrations` (placeholder for the future top-level mnemonic graduation; empty today), and `pageSize` placeholder (no Vaadin MenuBar pageSize concept; reserved). Same shape `ButtonStateStore` / `ComboBoxStateStore` take per [SD_sjbutton](#SD_sjbutton) / [SD_sjcombobox](#SD_sjcombobox).

**No visibility-widening `validate()` override.** Vaadin `MenuBar` doesn't declare `protected validate()` (unlike the input-bearing field hierarchy SJTextField / SJPasswordField / SJTextArea / SJComboBox sit under), so `ComponentMixin`'s default `public default void validate() { SHelper.onNoop(this, "validate"); }` resolves cleanly without the SD_toggle_checkbox_first_cut / SD_sjtextfield / SD_sjpasswordfield / SD_sjtextarea / SD_sjcombobox widening.

**`getUIClassID()` returns `"MenuBarUI"`** (matches JDK).

**Rationale.**

- **Asymmetric split keeps R_vaadin_first clean.** Vaadin's `MenuBar.addItem(text, listener)` is the natural surrogate-layer affordance — it's terse, typed, and covers the whole menu surface for pure-Vaadin users. Shipping a parallel `SJMenu` / `SJMenuItem` would force a second surface that adds nothing for those users (they'd never reach for it) and merely duplicates the JDK shape that already lives on the emulator side. R_vaadin_first explicitly rejects parallel surface that exists only for round-trip.
- **Tree-rebuild over incremental sync.** Vaadin's `MenuBar.addItem` returns a fresh `MenuItem`; there is no incremental "replace the third top-level item" API. Per-mutation rebuild is the cheapest reliable shape — same precedent SJComboBox's `setItems(snapshot)` takes for items per SD_sjcombobox. The cost is bounded by the JDK menu tree size (typically <50 items end-to-end), well within an O(n) budget.
- **Tree-as-data MenuNode descriptor.** Passing `JMenu` / `JMenuItem` references into the surrogate would invert the SD_shelper_statics module direction (`:surrogates` would have to know about `:emulators` types). MenuNode is the surrogate-internal data shape; the emulator builds these from its own JDK tree and pushes through. Migrators who use SJMenuBar directly (no `:emulators` involved) never see MenuNode — they call `addItem(text, listener)` straight on the inherited Vaadin API.
- **Accelerator install at UI scope, not menubar scope.** JDK menu accelerators fire when the menubar's enclosing window has focus (WHEN_IN_FOCUSED_WINDOW). Vaadin's `Shortcuts.addShortcutListener` on the UI matches that scope closely enough for the CRUD baseline. Shortcut lifecycle ties to the menubar's attach (rebuild fires on attach, teardown fires on detach + on next rebuild).
- **Top-level separator drop-and-WARN, not silent.** A top-level menubar separator is rare in real apps (separators usually appear in submenus to group commands). When migrated code does call `menuBar.add(new JSeparator())`, silent omission would leave the migrator wondering where their separator went. WARN surfaces the gap immediately; the actionable affordance is "wait for upstream" or "drop the separator, group via spacing instead."
- **Toggle items rely on emulator-side coordination.** JCheckBoxMenuItem cleanly maps to Vaadin's `setCheckable(true)`. JRadioButtonMenuItem also renders as `setCheckable(true)`; the select-one-of-N coordination originally missing landed via [D_buttongroup](../emulators/decisions.md#D_buttongroup) — the surrogate stays group-agnostic per SD_shelper_statics's module direction; the emulator's `:emulators.JRadioButtonMenuItem.makeOnClick` consults `consultButtonGroup` at the top of its body, and the cascade-deselect-prior-selection flows back through the emulator's setSelected on the sibling, which re-issues the rebuild push.

**Alternatives.**

- **Ship `SJMenu` / `SJMenuItem` as Vaadin `Composite<MenuItem>` wrappers.** Considered. Composite-wrapping forces the surrogate to lazily allocate its inner Vaadin MenuItem at parent-attach time (since MenuItem's ctor needs a parent ContextMenu). The resulting class is mostly proxy code with no Vaadin-side reuse value — pure-surrogate users compose with `MenuBar.addItem` directly anyway. Would also force every `setText` / `setEnabled` mutation to traverse the surrogate-side proxy back to the wrapped Vaadin MenuItem, which is what `MenuItem` itself does natively. Net: more surface for zero coverage gain. Rejected.
- **Subclass `MenuItem` directly inside `com.vaadin.flow.component.contextmenu` package via package-private access.** Rejected: relying on Vaadin's package-private access boundaries is brittle across Vaadin versions, and our surrogate would be unrelated to JDK semantics anyway.
- **Pass JDK `JMenu` / `JMenuItem` references straight into `rebuildFromTree`.** Rejected: inverts the `:emulators → :surrogates` module direction. MenuNode is the surrogate-internal shape; the emulator translates.
- **Keep the JDK tree on the surrogate too, mirroring it from emulator on every push.** Rejected: doubles the storage with no functional gain, and pure-surrogate users would never look at it.
- **Wire item accelerators through the menubar element directly (not the UI).** Rejected: a menubar attached to a JFrame is hosted inside a Dialog overlay which uses its own focus scope; UI-scope shortcuts are the cleaner stand-in for WHEN_IN_FOCUSED_WINDOW under Vaadin's render model.
- **Ship a JCheckBoxMenuItem-only path and defer JRadioButtonMenuItem to ButtonGroup landing.** Rejected: JRadioButtonMenuItem's surface is mechanically identical to JCheckBoxMenuItem (both render checkable). Shipping both surfaces with the documented multi-select gap is honest about the missing ButtonGroup coordination without forcing migrators to wait to compile their imports.

**Accepted limitations.**

- **Top-level `JSeparator` in a JMenuBar drops with WARN.** Vaadin `MenuBar` doesn't surface `addSeparator()` at the root level. Migrators with top-level separator chrome see the gap immediately. R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (a) — graduates if upstream lands the API. Workaround: nest the separator inside a top-level `JMenu` (where SubMenu.addSeparator works fine).
- **Top-level mnemonics (Alt+F → open File menu) drop with WARN.** Vaadin has no `MenuItem.open()` programmatic-open API. Item-level accelerators (Ctrl+S etc.) work fine via `Shortcuts`. R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (a). Migrators relying on Alt-key menu navigation see the gap; browser keyboard traversal (Tab, Enter) still works.
- **JRadioButtonMenuItem in a JMenu renders as `setCheckable(true)` per item.** Pre-D_buttongroup the items were independently checkable (multi-select within the menu). Post-[D_buttongroup](../emulators/decisions.md#D_buttongroup) the emulator-side `vaadinx.swing.ButtonGroup` provides the select-one-of-N pulse coordination via the emulator's makeOnClick path; surrogate-side stays unchanged (the rebuild's click listener flips state via emulator's setSelected, which consults the group before mutating).
- **Tree rebuild is O(n) per mutation.** Each `JMenuItem.setText("...")` reconstructs the entire menubar's Vaadin MenuItem tree. Acceptable for typical CRUD-shape menubars (under 50 items total). Apps with massive dynamic menubars (thousands of items, frequent live mutation) should use Vaadin's `MenuBar` directly per R_vaadin_first.
- **`getItems()` returns Vaadin MenuItems (inherited).** Pure-surrogate users see the Vaadin MenuItem tree directly; emulator users walk `JMenuBar.getMenu(int)` for the JDK tree. The two surfaces are independent — `getItems()` reflects the most recent rebuild's output, not the JDK tree.
- **`MenuItem` constructed inside `rebuildFromTree` carries no JDK-specific state of its own.** Each rebuild builds fresh; per-MenuItem state mutations directly on Vaadin's API (e.g. `peer.getItems().get(0).setEnabled(false)`) are wiped by the next rebuild. Migrators need to mutate on the JDK side (via `JMenuItem.setEnabled`) so the next rebuild reflects the change.
- **Click listener installed at rebuild time can't be `removed`.** Vaadin's `addItem(text, listener)` doesn't return a Registration — the listener is bound to the MenuItem's lifetime. `removeAll()` at the start of every rebuild is the teardown.

**Validation.** Surrogate test suite: `SJMenuBarTest` (~25 tests) covers `rebuildFromTree` round-trip (text, enabled, checkable, nested submenus, separator at submenu level, icon-bearing items), `removeAll` clears Vaadin MenuItem tree, accelerator install + teardown across rebuilds (KeyStroke → Registration map), unmappable accelerator WARN-and-skip, top-level separator WARN, top-level mnemonic WARN, click handler funnels through `SHelper.callSwing`, JComponentMixin surface (border, client property, PCE, name), `getUIClassID == "MenuBarUI"`, happy-path zero-WARN. SJFrame integration covered separately via the menubar slot tests in `SJFrameTest`.

<a id="SD_sjdialog"></a>
## SD_sjdialog. `SJDialog` surrogate — JDK-faithful sibling of `SJFrame` on `SFrame`; `WindowMixin` collapses to `SWindow` class; blocking modal show via a UI fiber

**Decision.** A third Dialog-backed concrete surrogate. `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJDialog extends SFrame` (the JDK-faithful sibling of `SJFrame` — both Dialog-backed surrogates, not parent / child) carries the `javax.swing.JDialog` surface: `defaultCloseOperation` (rejecting `EXIT_ON_CLOSE` at set-time per JDK contract), lazy `SJRootPane` holder, content-pane routing through `rootPaneCheckingEnabled`, `setJMenuBar` slot. The class duplicates ~250 lines of contentPane / rootPane / menubar scaffolding from SJFrame rather than `extends SJFrame` — accepted duplication to keep the surrogate hierarchy mirroring JDK shape (JDialog and JFrame are siblings under Window in JDK, and migrated code reading `dialog instanceof SJFrame` should not see `true`).

<a id="SD_swindow_class"></a>**SD_swindow_class — `WindowMixin` becomes a concrete `SWindow` class.** The mixin had a single implementer (SFrame), 0 abstract methods, ~335 lines of pure default-method body, and no state contract on the implementer. SD_sframe's original framing actually called this out as "SWindow / SFrame" before collapsing to a single-concrete-with-mixin shape; with SJDialog landing as the second Dialog-backed surrogate that needs the Window-level listener machinery, the mixin's single-user shape paid more than it earned. `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SWindow extends Dialog implements JComponentMixin` absorbs the body verbatim; SFrame `extends SWindow`; SJDialog inherits the listener helpers transitively via SFrame.

<a id="SD_window_state_to_swindow"></a>**SD_window_state_to_swindow — Window-level state migrates to SWindow; `vaadinx.awt.Window`'s peer flips from `SFrame` to `SWindow`.** The initial landing kept the SFrame-as-Window-peer shape inherited from SD_sframe (Window pre-dated SWindow as a class). A follow-up moves the Window-level state — `visible` shadow, `preventPeerEvents` guard, `everShown` first-show latch, `owner` (typed `SWindow`) + WeakReference-backed `ownedWindowList`, `setVisible` / `dispose` / `onPeerOpenedChanged` / `addComponentListener` / `fireComponentEvent`, owner accessors, static `getWindows` / `getOwnerlessWindows` — from SFrame down to SWindow. SFrame keeps just Frame-level state: `title` field + `setTitle`, the close-X chrome (`installHeaderCloseButton`), Frame state stubs (`getState` / `isUndecorated` / etc.), `paramString` override, and Frame-typed static filters (`getFrames` / `getOwnerlessFrames` filter `SWindow.getWindows()` by `instanceof SFrame`). `vaadinx.awt.Window`'s ctors now construct `new SWindow(unwrapSWindow(owner))` instead of `new SFrame(...)`; `peer instanceof SFrame` checks become `peer instanceof SWindow` (still works for Frame and Dialog whose peers are SFrame, since SFrame IS-A SWindow). The owner field on SWindow is typed `SWindow` (JDK shape — `Window.getOwner()` returns Window); SFrame inherits the field; tests that read `frame.owner` get `SWindow` back, but `assertSame(otherFrame, frame.owner)` works via reference comparison.

<a id="SD_window_listeners_inlined"></a>**SD_window_listeners_inlined — `WindowListenersStore` inlines as a private field on `SWindow`.** The store was a state-holder pattern attached via `ComponentUtil.setData` because the original `WindowMixin` interface couldn't have fields (SD_mixin_mechanism). With `SWindow` a class, the holder is just a private `EventListenerList` field. Eager allocation: a single-pointer holder; deferred allocation would save bytes on never-listened-to windows but cost a null-check on every fire path. The holder file is deleted; the architecture.md state-holder table loses one row.

<a id="SD_awt_dialog_materialises"></a>**SD_awt_dialog_materialises — `vaadinx.awt.Dialog` materialises as a real class.** Was missing entirely (only Component / Container / Window / Frame existed in the AWT-side hierarchy). The new class extends Window, declares the nested `ModalityType` enum + `DEFAULT_MODALITY_TYPE` constant verbatim from JDK, threads `modal` / `modalityType` through shadowed fields, mirrors to peer `Dialog.setModality(ModalityMode)` (Vaadin 25's `setModal(boolean)` is `@Deprecated(since=25.0, forRemoval=true)` — `setModality(STRICT)` is the supported replacement). All four AWT modality types (`MODELESS` / `DOCUMENT_MODAL` / `APPLICATION_MODAL` / `TOOLKIT_MODAL`) collapse to Vaadin `STRICT` (modal) or `MODELESS` (non-modal); the type is round-tripped on the emulator field for migrated-code semantics, but Vaadin has no separate "block document subtree" or "block toolkit-wide" mode. Constructor at peer-injection time pushes initial modality state to peer (Vaadin Dialog defaults to `VISUAL`, which doesn't match AWT MODELESS).

<a id="SD_modal_park"></a>**SD_modal_park — Blocking modal show parks the calling UI fiber.** `vaadinx.awt.Dialog.setVisible(true)` on a modal dialog parks the calling UI fiber on a future anchored to the dialog's peer (`UIFibers.parkAndAwait`, per D_callswing_loom); `dispose()` / `setVisible(false)` / peer-originated close (via `processWindowEvent(WINDOW_CLOSING)`) all complete it. The park releases the session lock, and the dialog flushes to the browser. Surrogate-side `SJDialog.setVisible` stays synchronous per SD_sframe — modal blocking is emulator-only. Calls from outside a UI fiber throw `IllegalStateException` via `UIFibers.checkInUIFiber()` rather than blocking the request thread (program-error path matching D_gap_severity_triage's loom rescue trigger; the legitimate call site is inside `EHelper.callSwing`, which runs a UI fiber).

<a id="SD_jdialog_lockdown"></a>**SD_jdialog_lockdown — `vaadinx.swing.JDialog` is R_leaf_peer_lockdown-locked-down.** JDialog is a leaf in `javax.swing.*`'s public hierarchy. Its 15 public ctors (matching JDK's surface — Frame / Dialog / Window owners × optional title × optional modal / `ModalityType` × optional `GraphicsConfiguration`) all hardcode `new SJDialog(...)` as the peer; no protected `(Component peer)` ctor. `vaadinx.awt.Dialog` is non-leaf (JDialog extends it) and keeps the protected `Dialog(Component peer)` + `Dialog(Component peer, Window owner)` ctors as JDialog's peer-injection seam.

**Rationale.**

- **JDK-faithful sibling shape on the surrogate.** JDialog and JFrame are siblings under Window in JDK, not parent / child. Inheriting from SJFrame (the pragmatic-reuse alternative) would imply JDialog IS-A JFrame and leak that misshape into migrated code. The ~250 lines of contentPane / rootPane / menubar scaffolding duplicated between SJFrame and SJDialog were mechanically identical mirror copies — the dedup was deferred until a third consumer landed. That trigger fired with SJWindow: the scaffolding now lives single-copy on the package-private `RootPaneScaffold` (composition, not the SFrame pull-up this entry originally sketched — see [SD_rootpane_scaffold](#SD_sjwindow) for why the base-class variant was rejected); each host keeps one-line delegating overrides, so the JDK-faithful sibling surface is unchanged.
- **WindowMixin → SWindow because the user's hypothesis was right.** A mixin interface with one implementer and zero abstract methods is just a class with extra ceremony. The collapsing landed alongside SJDialog because that's when the second concrete using the mixin's surface was about to land — flipping the shape before duplicating the implementer count was cheaper than after. SFrame's behaviour is bit-for-bit identical post-flip; the test suite caught nothing.
- **Both show modes via the UI fiber's park, no carve-out for "non-blocking modal".** Earlier framing described the CRUD edit-form path as "non-blocking modal" (`setModal(true)` + `setVisible(true)` returns immediately). That was a workaround for the absence of blocking-dialog plumbing; with it in place (D_callswing_loom), JDK-faithful blocking-modal is straightforward. Migrators who want the immediate-return semantics use modeless (`setModal(false)`) + a `windowClosed` listener — which works orthogonally to the modality choice. The `EditFormView` sampler route demos both shapes side by side.
- **EXIT_ON_CLOSE rejected at set-time, not close-time.** JDK's JDialog throws `IllegalArgumentException` from `setDefaultCloseOperation(EXIT_ON_CLOSE)` immediately — Dialog can't terminate the JVM. Mirrors JFrame's set-time validator-of-unknown-values shape; no run-time throw branch in `processWindowEvent` is needed.

**Alternatives.**

- **`SJDialog extends SJFrame`.** Considered. Saves the ~250 lines of duplicated rootPane / contentPane scaffolding. Rejected: leaks JDialog-IS-A-JFrame into the surrogate type system, contradicts JDK shape, and forces every SJFrame-only API (`getJMenuBar` is shared, but `setDefaultCloseOperation`'s EXIT_ON_CLOSE acceptance is JFrame-only) to be re-evaluated for JDialog. The duplication is the price of a clean type hierarchy.
- **Pull rootPane / contentPane scaffolding up into SFrame.** Considered. Would let both SJFrame and SJDialog inherit it. Rejected: JDK shape puts those concepts on JFrame and JDialog independently (Frame and Dialog don't have RootPane), and pulling them up to SFrame would re-introduce a JDK-shape divergence on the surrogate side. The "reconsider at a third consumer" clause resolved when SJWindow landed — via `RootPaneScaffold` composition rather than any base class, per [SD_rootpane_scaffold](#SD_sjwindow).
- **Keep WindowMixin as an interface.** Considered. The mixin pattern is the SD_mixin_mechanism default for surface exposure. Rejected: SD_mixin_mechanism explicitly lists multi-implementer reuse as the rationale, and a single-implementer mixin is just a class with default-method ceremony. The flip simplifies SJDialog's body (no `implements WindowMixin`; just inherits via SFrame).
- **Block all setVisible(true) calls from non-VT contexts (extend the rule beyond modal).** Rejected: modeless `setVisible(true)` doesn't park, so it's safe from any thread context. Restricting it would force migrators to wrap every dialog show in `callSwing` even when the underlying JDK semantics don't require blocking.
- **Make the modal-park latch reentrant / handle nested modal shows.** Considered briefly. JDK Dialog supports nested modal dialogs (parent modal opens child modal); each gets its own park and unparks when its own close fires. Our `modalLatch` field is per-instance, so this works without extra machinery — each Dialog instance has its own latch. The only edge case is if the same Dialog instance has `setVisible(true)` called twice while already shown, but JDK's contract treats that as a no-op (the dialog stays shown, no second park).

**Accepted limitations.**

- **All non-MODELESS modality types collapse to Vaadin STRICT.** APPLICATION_MODAL / DOCUMENT_MODAL / TOOLKIT_MODAL all render as Vaadin `STRICT` (modal curtain + RPC blocked outside dialog). The semantic differences ("block this document subtree only" / "block all toolkit-wide") aren't honoured — Vaadin has no analog for sub-document or cross-toolkit modality. Modality round-trips on the emulator field for migrated-code semantics that read it back; the actual blocking behaviour is binary. R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (b) — permanently deferred.
- **`TOOLKIT_MODAL`'s "block every other top-level window in this Java toolkit" can't be honoured.** There is only one Vaadin UI per browser tab; no other "top-level windows" exist to block. For a single-UI app, the observable difference vs. APPLICATION_MODAL is nil. Migrators relying on toolkit-modal semantics across multiple windows in a single tab see degraded behaviour (other dialogs aren't blocked); migration to a multi-tab shape needs the multi-tab design pass to settle first.
- **Modal show requires a virtual-thread context.** `setVisible(true)` on a modal dialog from a non-VT thread (request thread directly, attach listener body before `callSwing` wraps it) throws `IllegalStateException`. The legitimate path is inside `SHelper.callSwing`. Migrators porting a Swing app where `dialog.setVisible(true)` runs from the EDT now have an analogous "wrap your peer-event handlers in callSwing" rule (R_callswing_envelope) — a rule they're already following for everything else in the migration.
- **`setShape` / `setOpacity` / `setBackground` inherited from Window stubs (R_layouts_close_enough — no transparency or non-rect).** Same as JFrame.
- **Surrogate-side blocking is permanently deferred per SD_sframe.** `SJDialog.setVisible(true)` returns immediately even when modal. Pure-surrogate users (no `:emulators`) get Vaadin Dialog's native modal behaviour (overlay traps focus on browser side) but no Java-side parking. The JOptionPane slice was the obvious candidate for re-litigating this; [SD_no_sjoptionpane](#SD_no_sjoptionpane) closed it the other way (no `SJOptionPane` at all), so the SD_sframe stance now holds beyond the SJDialog landing too.
- **JDialog's emulator setVisible doesn't queue a re-park if called twice while already modal-shown.** First call parks; if a second `setVisible(true)` is somehow reached before close (unlikely — the first call is parked), it would `super.setVisible(true)` (no-op since already visible) and try to park again with a fresh latch — but the existing latch from the first call is still on the field, so the second park overwrites it and the first call never unparks. Treating this as a programming-error edge case rather than a hardening target. JDK Dialog has the same "don't show-while-shown" assumption.

**Validation.** Surrogate test suite: `SJDialogTest` (~25 tests) covers lazy rootPane construction, content-pane mirror invariant, layered / glass pass-throughs, JDK-faithful sibling stance (independent SJRootPane from SJFrame), inherited SFrame title / owner / lifecycle, defaultCloseOperation rejection of EXIT_ON_CLOSE, full close-op transitions + PCE, DISPOSE_ON_CLOSE peer-close fan-out, JMenuBar slot, paramString, getUIClassID="DialogUI", happy-path zero-WARN. Emulator test suite: `vaadinx.swing.JDialogTest` (~30 tests) — R_leaf_peer_lockdown lock-down (peer is SJDialog), R_leaf_peer_lockdown content-pane scaffolding, all 15 owner-taking ctor variations, modal / modalityType round-trip with PCE, `ModalityMode.STRICT` / `MODELESS` peer mirror, EXIT_ON_CLOSE rejected at set-time, DISPOSE / HIDE peer-close paths, **blocking modal park end-to-end** (callSwing-VT calls `setVisible(true)`, parks; another callSwing's `dispose()` unparks; coordinated via CountDownLatch with 5s timeout), non-VT modal show throws ISE; `vaadinx.awt.DialogTest` (~12 tests) for the bare-AWT surface (modality / DEFAULT_MODALITY_TYPE / type → peer mapping). Sampler exit gate: `EditFormView` route + `EditFormWarnInventoryTest` cover the modeless user-path end-to-end (modal-blocking flow not directly testable through Karibu's micro-loop — unit tests cover the park).

<a id="SD_no_sjoptionpane"></a>
## SD_no_sjoptionpane. No `SJOptionPane` — JOptionPane is emulator-only

**Decision.** The JOptionPane slice lands in `:emulators` only; `:surrogates` does not get a corresponding `SJOptionPane`. The `:emulators.JOptionPane` peer composes from Vaadin primitives directly (Dialog + Button + icon glyph) rather than delegating to a surrogate. First case under D_surrogate_first's surrogate-first ordering where the surrogate is *intentionally* skipped — establishes the precedent for "no surrogate, by design, because the surrogate would be hollow."

**Rationale.**

- **JOptionPane's UI functionality IS blocking.** ~95% of real-world use is the static factories (`showConfirmDialog`, `showMessageDialog`, `showInputDialog`, `showOptionDialog`); each blocks the caller until the user picks a button and returns the chosen value. SD_sframe keeps surrogate-side `SHelper.callSwing` synchronous — UI-fiber parking is `:emulators`-only. With blocking off the table at the surrogate layer, an `SJOptionPane` would be a hollow class: static factories couldn't return values, the instance-show path (`new JOptionPane(...).createDialog(...).setVisible(true); pane.getValue()`) couldn't park on the show-step.
- **R_vaadin_first pins "fat surrogate" on UI behaviour, and there's no UI behaviour left.** R_vaadin_first's coverage claim is "clicks fire events, models drive fan-out, sliders snap." For JOptionPane the UI behaviour reduces to "render a labelled dialog and block until the user picks." Strip blocking and the residue — a JComponent embeddable inline as a label-plus-buttons widget — is exotic enough in real Swing code that surrogating it earns nothing. Stage-3 authors compose `SJLabel` + `SJButton` + `Dialog` directly when they want that shape.
- **Stage-3 users have Vaadin's native primitives.** Vaadin's `ConfirmDialog` / `Notification` / plain `Dialog` cover the confirm / message / input flows natively, with non-blocking-by-design listeners. A Swing-flavoured `SJOptionPane` at stage 3 would be a worse alternative to the primitive the migrator already reaches for.

**Alternatives considered.**

- **`SJOptionPane` with all blocking forms throwing.** Mirror the JDK static surface for type-system completeness; static factories throw `IllegalStateException` ("blocking dialogs are emulator-only"). Rejected as API tease — present-but-unusable misleads stage-3 readers about what the class can do, and adds maintenance surface (mirror new overloads, keep javadocs aligned) for zero functional value.
- **`SJOptionPane` as a non-blocking JComponent renderer (no static factories; instance fires `propertyChange("value")` on click).** Rejected as duplicative of stage-3 idioms — wrapping `SJLabel` + `SJButton` + `Dialog` in a fake-JOptionPane envelope serves no migration goal R_vaadin_first cares about.
- **Re-litigate SD_sframe to add surrogate-side VT parking just for JOptionPane.** Rejected: SD_sframe's stance is structural (cross-module loom dependency would invert SD_shelper_statics's `:surrogates` → `:emulators` direction); JOptionPane alone doesn't earn relaxing it.

**Accepted limitations.**

- **No `:surrogates` JOptionPane API.** Stage-3 view code that wants a Swing-flavoured confirm/error/input dialog either falls back to `:emulators.JOptionPane` (works as expected, blocking included) or rewrites to Vaadin's `ConfirmDialog` / `Notification` / `Dialog`. The CLAUDE.md §"Migration arc" rule — per-view single-layer code, never `:emulators` and `:surrogates` together inside one view — means a stage-3 view picks one path: rewrite to Vaadin primitives (expected) or stay at stage 2 for that view.
- **Embedding `JOptionPane` inline at the surrogate stage is unsupported.** Rare-to-nonexistent in real code. If a migration target needs it, revisit by adding a renderer-only `SJOptionPane` (no static factories, no blocking) — still without re-litigating SD_sframe.

**Precedent.** First "no surrogate, intentionally" outcome under D_surrogate_first's surrogate-first rule. Future components failing the same triage — UI functionality is mostly blocking, stage-3 users have a strictly better Vaadin primitive — can cite this entry rather than re-deriving. The closest already-permanently-deferred sibling is `JApplet`, removed from the JDK entirely in Java 26 per [JEP 504](https://openjdk.org/jeps/504) — no surrogate AND no emulator. JOptionPane is the gentler shape: emulator yes, surrogate no.


<a id="SD_sjtable"></a>
## SD_sjtable. `SJTable` surrogate — `Grid<Integer>` with `TableModel` as source of truth, Vaadin-first rendering via `setRenderer`, JDK `getSelectionModel` clash deferred to emulator

**Decision.** The JTable slice. `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJTable extends com.vaadin.flow.component.grid.Grid<Integer> implements JComponentMixin, TableModelListener, TableColumnModelListener, ListSelectionListener` — row indices are the Vaadin Grid's items, the installed `TableModel` is the single source of truth (R_swing_is_truth), per-cell `ComponentRenderer<Integer>` reads `model.getValueAt(row, modelCol)` live so `fireTableRowsUpdated` is `dataProvider.refreshItem(row)` — no array-snapshot resync. Pairs with [D_jtable](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jtable) for the emulator side.

<a id="SD_sjtable_row_index_identity"></a>**SD_sjtable_row_index_identity — `Grid<Integer>` row-index identity, not `Grid<Object[]>` snapshot.** Each Vaadin row carries the boxed `Integer` index into the model. Per-cell renderer reads through to `model.getValueAt(row, modelCol)` — single source of truth, no shadow snapshot to drift. `fireTableRowsUpdated(r, r)` calls `dataProvider.refreshItem(r)` directly (the row index stays stable; the cell value reads through fresh). The {@link javax.swing.ListSelectionModel} is index-based already, so the bridge to Vaadin Grid's selection (which carries the same Integer keys) is direct. Alternative `Grid<Object[]>` with snapshot row arrays was rejected: identity becomes fragile under `removeRow` shifts, and `fireTableRowsUpdated` would need an explicit snapshot rebuild on every cell change.

<a id="SD_sjtable_vaadin_rendering"></a>**SD_sjtable_vaadin_rendering — Vaadin-first rendering at the surrogate; ported `TableCellRenderer` lives only on the emulator.** The surrogate exposes `setRenderer(int viewColumnIndex, Renderer<Integer> renderer)` for pure-surrogate users to install Vaadin renderers directly per R_vaadin_first. The JDK `TableCellRenderer` surface (return-type clash with `java.awt.Component` per [D_event_port_policy](../emulators/decisions.md#D_event_port_policy)) lives only on the emulator layer (D_jtable_renderer_registry). Why split: `:surrogates` cannot reference `vaadinx.swing.table.*` (the dependency arrow is `:emulators` → `:surrogates`, not the reverse); the surrogate's column-model storage is the JDK type (`javax.swing.table.TableColumnModel`, JDK-reused per [SD_vaadin_first_binding](#SD_vaadin_first_binding)) and the JDK `TableColumn.cellRenderer` field is `javax.swing.table.TableCellRenderer` which we can't usefully invoke (returns `java.awt.Component`). Default per-cell rendering: a fresh `Span(value.toString())` per call (mirrors [SD_sjcombobox](#SD_sjcombobox)'s default `setItemLabelGenerator` shape) — fresh-per-call sidesteps the same-instance-multiple-positions DOM-adoption-diff failure [D_jcombobox](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jcombobox) documented for SJComboBox.

<a id="SD_sjtable_is_model_listener"></a>**SD_sjtable_is_model_listener — Listener-list architecture: `SJTable IS-A` all three model listeners.** The surrogate implements `TableModelListener` / `TableColumnModelListener` / `ListSelectionListener` and subscribes itself to the installed model / column model / selection model on install / swap. Internal handlers do (a) column rebuild on structure change (HEADER_ROW `TableModelEvent`), (b) Grid data refresh on row insert/delete/update, (c) Grid selection push on selection-model change. **No surrogate-level `add/removeXxxListener` convenience registration** — JDK JTable doesn't have those either; users go through `table.getModel().addTableModelListener(l)` etc. directly, and the surrogate's own subscriptions are independent of user subscriptions on the same model.

<a id="SD_sjtable_selection_bridge"></a>**SD_sjtable_selection_bridge — Two-way selection bridge with `preventPeerEvents` guard.** `ListSelectionListener.valueChanged` (driven by user mutations on the JDK selection model) calls `pushSelectionToPeer()` which `deselectAll()` then `select(rowIdx)` for each selected index. Browser-side selection events go through the inverse path: a Vaadin `addSelectionListener` subscription wrapped in [SHelper.callSwing](../emulators/decisions.md#D_callswing_funnel) (R_callswing_envelope) reads `getSelectedItems()` and writes through `selectionModel.addSelectionInterval(idx, idx)`. The standard `preventPeerEvents` flag (mirrors SJSlider / SJComboBox) guards the loop. **Vaadin SelectionMode swap re-creates the Grid's internal selection backing**, so `setSelectionMode(int)` re-installs the Vaadin selection listener every time — same shape SJComboBox's customValue install takes.

<a id="SD_getselectionmodel_deferred"></a>**SD_getselectionmodel_deferred — JDK `getSelectionModel(): ListSelectionModel` deferred to emulator (Vaadin Grid clash).** `Grid.getSelectionModel(): GridSelectionModel<T>` exists at the parent layer; Java doesn't permit overriding with a different return type, so the JDK-shaped getter can't coexist on the surrogate. Per R_vaadin_first the Vaadin shape wins at the surrogate layer; pure-surrogate code reads selection via `setSelectionMode(int)` / `setRowSelectionInterval` / `getSelectedRow(s)`. The emulator (D_jtable) keeps the JDK-shaped getter for stage-2 import-swap compatibility — it reaches the JDK model through `getListSelectionModel()`, a surrogate-side accessor specifically named to avoid the parent clash. `getJdkSelectionMode()` similarly future-proofs the previous-mode read for PCE delivery on `setSelectionMode(int)` swaps.

<a id="SD_sjtable_auto_columns"></a>**SD_sjtable_auto_columns — Auto-create columns from model on `HEADER_ROW`.** When `autoCreateColumnsFromModel` is true (JDK default), `tableChanged(HEADER_ROW)` calls `createDefaultColumnsFromModel()` which clears the JDK column model and rebuilds it from the model's `getColumnName` / `getColumnClass`. The rebuild is wrapped in `preventPeerEvents` so individual `removeColumn` / `addColumn` JDK events don't re-trigger our own listener — `rebuildVaadinColumnsFromColumnModel()` is called once at the end. This batched-rebuild shape mirrors SJMenuBar's tree-rebuild push API ([SD_sjmenubar](#SD_sjmenubar)) — one Vaadin-side rebuild per JDK structural change, not per JDK event.

<a id="SD_sjtable_sorting"></a>**SD_sjtable_sorting — Sorting via Vaadin DataProvider Query, JDK `TableRowSorter` as source of truth.** Replaces the original "deferred" stance. The architecture treats Vaadin Grid as the sort-trigger UI and the JDK row sorter as the permutation oracle, with `Grid.setItems(FetchCallback, CountCallback)` as the bridge — no `setItems(List)` re-push, no separate `SortEvent` listener, no `preventPeerEvents` flag for sort.

Key decisions:

- **`TableRowSorter` family is JDK-reused** per D_whitelist_porting/D_event_port_policy: `RowSorter<M>`, `DefaultRowSorter`, `TableRowSorter<TableModel>`, `RowSorter.SortKey`, `SortOrder`, `RowSorterEvent`, `RowSorterListener` all have non-Component-touching members. No ports needed — same Vaadin-first stance the surrogate already takes for `TableModel` / `ListSelectionModel`.

- **Browser-driven sort handled inline in `fetch(Query)` — no SortEvent listener.** When the user clicks a column header, Vaadin Grid issues a `Query` whose `getSortOrders()` reflects the new sort state. `fetch` translates `QuerySortOrder` (string-keyed via the column's `setSortProperty`, which we set to the model column index) → JDK `RowSorter.SortKey`s, calls `rowSorter.setSortKeys(keys)` (synchronous in JDK — `convertRowIndexToModel` is correct on the next line), then iterates `IntStream.range(offset, end).mapToObj(rowSorter::convertRowIndexToModel)` to emit model row indices in view order. **Compare-then-set is the natural loop guard** — when the rowSorter listener pushes a programmatic sort change back to Vaadin via `grid.sort(...)`, the resulting fetch sees matching keys and the `setSortKeys` is a no-op.

- **Programmatic `rowSorter.setSortKeys(...)` is bridged to Vaadin via `RowSorterListener` → `grid.sort(translated)`.** The `RowSorterEvent.Type.SORT_ORDER_CHANGED` / `SORTED` triggers our listener; we translate JDK `SortKey`s back into `GridSortOrder<Integer>`s (looking up the Vaadin column whose `TableColumn.modelIndex` matches the `SortKey.column`) and call `grid.sort(orders)`. Vaadin updates its sort indicator + triggers a fetch, which (per the compare-then-set guard above) is a no-op against the rowSorter. The `gridSortEquals` early-exit avoids a redundant `grid.sort` when state already matches.

- **Auto-create on `setModel` and on `setAutoCreateRowSorter(true)`.** Matches JDK: `setModel(newModel)` with `autoCreateRowSorter == true` installs a fresh `TableRowSorter<>(newModel)`. `setAutoCreateRowSorter(true)` flipped on after construction lazy-installs against the current model when no sorter is present. `setAutoCreateRowSorter(false)` does NOT clear an installed sorter (JDK contract — disables future auto-creation only). `setModel` with a different model unconditionally clears any prior sorter (it was bound to the old model) before checking the autoCreate flag.

- **Sort property + neutral comparator on every column.** `rebuildVaadinColumnsFromColumnModel` calls `col.setSortable(true)`, `col.setSortProperty(String.valueOf(modelIndex))`, `col.setComparator((a, b) -> 0)`. The neutral comparator ensures Vaadin's in-memory sort path is a no-op — our fetch-driven path is the only one that actually orders rows. The sort-property string carries the model column index for round-trip translation. `setRenderer(int, Renderer<Integer>)` re-applies all three when swapping a column's renderer so sort survives the renderer swap.

- **`convertRowIndexToView/Model`** route through `rowSorter.convertRowIndexToView/Model` when present, identity otherwise. Pure-surrogate users reading sort state via `getRowSorter().getSortKeys()` see the same view JDK migrators expect.

- **Model events reach the sorter through the table, first.** `TableRowSorter` does not listen to its model — the JDK's `JTable.tableChanged` forwards every `TableModelEvent` to it (`rowsInserted` / `rowsDeleted` / `rowsUpdated`, `allRowsChanged` for a whole-model update, `modelStructureChanged` for `HEADER_ROW`), and `SJTable.tableChanged` does the same before touching the selection or the Grid. The failure without it is quiet and structural: unsorted, `DefaultRowSorter.getViewRowCount()` is the *larger* of the model's live count and the count it last saw, so the Grid grows but never shrinks below its first size (blank rows past the end), and once sorted its view→model mapping goes stale on every insert or delete. The surrogate's selection needs no `SortManager`-style remap alongside: it holds model rows ([D_jtable_cell_editing](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jtable_cell_editing)), shifted in model coordinates on insert/delete — the emulator's view-row selection is the one that re-maps ([D_jtable_selection](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jtable_selection)); a whole-model or structure change clears it and parks lead and anchor at `-1`, as the JDK's `clearSelectionAndLeadAnchor` does.

- **`refreshDataProvider()` replaces `pushItemsToPeer`.** Insert / delete / structure-change events call `dataProvider.refreshAll()` which makes Vaadin re-issue `count()` + `fetch(query)`. Cell-update events still use `dataProvider.refreshItem(rowIdx)` for single-row repaint (Vaadin re-renders that one row using the existing data provider state).

- **Filter** lands as an additive slice — `TableRowSorter.setRowFilter(...)` is the JDK API and it lives on the same sorter we already manage. The bridge required exactly one fix to {@link #onRowSorterEvent}: filter-only changes fire `RowSorterEvent.Type.SORTED` with unchanged sort orders, so the early-exit on `gridSortEquals` would silently drop the filter update. The fix falls through to `dataProvider.refreshAll()` instead of returning, so Vaadin re-fetches with the now-filtered view. `count()` already returned `rowSorter.getViewRowCount()` (which reflects filter when set) — no change needed. `RowFilter` family is JDK-reused per D_event_port_policy.

**State holder.** `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.internal.TableStateStore` carries `model` / `columnModel` / `selectionModel` (JDK types — source of truth per R_swing_is_truth), `preventPeerEvents` flag, `selectionMode` int, `rowSorter` + `rowSorterEventListener` (per SD_sjtable_sorting), all the round-trip-only shadow fields (`rowHeight` / `rowMargin` / `intercellSpacing` / `gridColor` / `show*Lines` / `autoCreateColumnsFromModel` / `autoCreateRowSorter` / `selectionForeground` / `selectionBackground` / `autoResizeMode` / `dragEnabled` / `fillsViewportHeight` / `preferredScrollableViewportSize`), and the Vaadin selection registration (`Registration` handle for the SelectionListener). No cell-renderer registry, no `JTableHeader` field — those are emulator concerns.

**R_vaadin_first drop-and-WARN surface.** `setCellSelectionEnabled` (Vaadin Grid is row-only), `setRowHeight(int row, int rowHeight)` (per-row height), `setDragEnabled(true)` (cross-cutting), cell editor surface in full (deferred), plus the no-counterpart knob cluster covered by the R_vaadin_first-cleanup note below.

**R_vaadin_first Store cleanup (aligned with SD_sjlist).** Originally `TableStateStore` parked a cluster of round-trip-only fields — `rowHeight`, `rowMargin`, `intercellSpacing`, `gridColor`, `showHorizontalLines/VerticalLines`, `autoResizeMode`, `selectionForeground/Background`, `dragEnabled`, `fillsViewportHeight`, `preferredScrollableViewportSize`, `rowSelectionAllowed`, `columnSelectionAllowed` — in the *surrogate* store, plus a redundant `selectionMode` int. That's the laxer earlier shape: surrogate shadow caches that exist only so a setter round-trips through its own getter, which R_vaadin_first forbids. The cluster was migrated to the **asymmetric R_swing_is_truth-shadow split** SJToolBar (SD_sjtoolbar), SJSplitPane (SD_sjsplitpane) and SJList (SD_sjlist) established: the **surrogate drops them** (getters return JDK defaults — `rowHeight` 16, `gridColor` GRAY, `showGrid` true, `autoResizeMode` SUBSEQUENT_COLUMNS, `rowSelectionAllowed` true, …; setters `SHelper.onUnimplemented`-and-drop), and JDK-faithful round-trip + PCE moves to the **emulator** layer (`:emulators.JTable` field shadows, D_jtable). `selectionMode` is no longer stored on either layer — `getJdkSelectionMode()` reconstructs it from `selectionModel.getSelectionMode()`. `getCellRect` stays an R_layouts_close_enough approximation on the surrogate using the JDK-default row height. No behaviour change for stage-2 (emulator) code; pure-surrogate getters now return JDK defaults instead of echoing a set value (round-trip was never an R_vaadin_first goal).

**Double-click bridge — `rowAtPoint` / `columnAtPoint` (backported from SD_sjlist).** `addMouseListener(SMouseListener)` overrides the inherited mixin wire to subscribe Grid's `addItemClickListener` + `addItemDoubleClickListener`, firing `SMouseEvent#MOUSE_CLICKED` with the browser click count (single = 1) / `clickCount = 2` respectively. The clicked row (the Grid item, consistent with `getSelectedRow`/`getValueAt`) and view-column index (`getColumns().indexOf(e.getColumn())`) are stashed in `TableStateStore.clickRowStash`/`clickColumnStash` around the fan-out so `rowAtPoint(Point)` / `columnAtPoint(Point)` resolve during the user's `mouseClicked` handler — the double-click-to-edit idiom. Identical mechanism to SJList (SD_sjlist); `columnAtPoint` is the bonus the Grid item-click's column affords (SJList had a single column). `-1` outside a dispatch (R_layouts_close_enough — no pixel→cell map). The emulator re-sources to a `vaadinx.awt.event.MouseEvent` via `processMouseEvent` and delegates `rowAtPoint`/`columnAtPoint` to the surrogate (D_jtable).

**Validation.** 2-test `TablesWarnInventoryTest` exit gate — see [D_jtable](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jtable)'s validation section for the user-path + API-surface coverage breakdown. The exit-gate authoring surfaced five real bugs: (1) `pushItemsToPeer` NPE during ctor sequence (`setColumnModel` before `setModel` left `model = null`), (2) header-fallback NPE in same sequence, (3) duplicate Grid column key when JDK permits multiple `TableColumn`s with same `modelIndex` (dropped `setKey` since Vaadin auto-assigns; we don't expose key-based lookup), (4) per-event jdkToPortedBridge race during structural rebuild (replaced with explicit rebuild call from emulator's `setModel` / `setColumnModel`), (5) `JTableHeader`'s `super()` hit `JComponent` no-arg WARN (now passes inert Div peer explicitly). All five fixed pre-merge.

---

<a id="SD_sjscrollpane"></a>
## SD_sjscrollpane. `SJScrollPane` surrogate — `Scroller` peer, `JComponentMixin`, single-content-slot maps Swing's one-viewport-view; auto-scroll guard forces `NONE` on already-scrolling content

**Decision.** A surrogate surfaced by the CRUD-testbed walkthrough. `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJScrollPane extends com.vaadin.flow.component.orderedlayout.Scroller implements JComponentMixin`. Mirrors [SD_sjpanel](#SD_sjpanel)'s "thin Vaadin-container surrogate" stance — three ctors (no-arg / `Component` / `Component` + scrollbar-policy ints translated through the mapping table on the emulator side), L&F drop-and-WARN bucket, no shadow state. Pairs with [D_jscrollpane](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jscrollpane) for the emulator side.

- **Peer base: Vaadin `Scroller`.** A single-content-slot container with built-in `ScrollDirection` enum (`VERTICAL` / `HORIZONTAL` / `BOTH` / `NONE`) — the closest semantic match for JScrollPane's "wrap one component in a scrollable viewport." `Scroller.setContent(Component)` IS the `setViewportView` mapping; the single-child invariant is what JScrollPane already enforces (one viewport view at a time).
- **Constructors mirror JDK** — three forms: no-arg, `SJScrollPane(Component view)` (delegates to `Scroller(Component)`), and `SJScrollPane(Component view, ScrollDirection direction)` (Vaadin-shape; the JDK-int form lives only on the emulator). No fork of the scrollbar-policy ints at the surrogate layer — pure-surrogate users use Vaadin's `ScrollDirection` enum directly.
- **Auto-scroll guard via `setContent(Component)` override.** The override delegates to `super.setContent(content)`, then if `content` is an instance of any *known auto-scrolling Vaadin component type* — initial set: `com.vaadin.flow.component.grid.Grid`, `com.vaadin.flow.component.textfield.TextArea`, `com.vaadin.flow.component.orderedlayout.Scroller` itself — calls `super.setScrollDirection(ScrollDirection.NONE)`. This prevents double-scrollbars (the inner already auto-scrolls; the outer Scroller would otherwise add a redundant overflow:auto layer that produces stacked scrollbars in undefined-height containers and broken-Grid behaviour in `flex: 1`-shaped tracks). Pure-surrogate users that explicitly call `setScrollDirection(VERTICAL)` *after* `setContent(grid)` get their value back — the guard fires only on content swap, not on every direction call. Opt-out is one line; default is the safe path.
- **L&F surface** (`setUI` / `updateUI` / `getUIClassID`) — drop-and-WARN / no-op pattern, identical shape to SJPanel. `getUIClassID` returns `"ScrollPaneUI"`; `updateUI` is a deliberate no-op (Vaadin owns the DOM); `setUI` `SHelper.onUnimplemented`-and-drops. JDK `getUI()` is dropped per the same Vaadin `Component.getUI()` return-type clash SJPanel / SJButton document.
- **No accessibility port.** `getAccessibleContext` WARNs and returns null (consistent with every prior surrogate).

**Auto-scroll detection list — initial scope and how it grows.** The instanceof checks live on the surrogate's `setContent` override (`Grid` / `TextArea` / `Scroller`). The list captures *types whose Vaadin shadow DOM owns its own overflow:auto layer*. Adding more is a one-line `instanceof` branch when a future component lands a peer that auto-scrolls (e.g. `VirtualList` if it ever sits inside a JScrollPane). Per R_infra_not_surface, don't pre-emptively expand the list — three matches cover the current testbed surface (`JScrollPane(JTable)`, `JScrollPane(JTextArea)`, defensive nesting). The check uses runtime `instanceof` on the Vaadin class, so any subclass (including emulator surrogates `SJTable` / `SJTextArea` themselves) is caught transitively.

**LayoutManager handling.** SJScrollPane has no layout — it's a single-slot container. `ContainerMixin.setLayout` inherited from JComponentMixin is a no-op for the layout-CSS dispatch path (Scroller's web component owns its own DOM template; the host-element flex-CSS that FlowLayout / BorderLayout would write doesn't apply meaningfully).
