Pure-surrogate code that calls `scrollPane.setLayout(new FlowLayout())` stores the layout via `LayoutStore` (so `getLayout()` round-trips) but no CSS lands — same accepted limitation SJPanel documents for unrecognised LayoutManagers. JDK JScrollPane uses a `ScrollPaneLayout` internally to manage viewport / row-header / column-header / corners; we don't reproduce that orchestration since the corner / header surface is R_vaadin_first drop-and-WARN at the emulator (D_jscrollpane) and pure-surrogate users compose with `setContent` directly.

**Emulator thin-down** (mirror SJPanel / SJTable):

- `:emulators.swing.JScrollPane` lands as a thin shell over SJScrollPane. R_leaf_peer_lockdown-locked-down (JDK leaf — no class in `javax.swing.*` extends JScrollPane). The protected `(Component peer)` ctor is omitted; all public ctors funnel through a private `(SJScrollPane peer)` ctor that hard-codes the surrogate.
- The scrollbar-policy ↔ `ScrollDirection` mapping table lives on the emulator side (D_jscrollpane). Field-shadows H/V policy ints for JDK round-trip; the mapping translates to a single `ScrollDirection` push to the surrogate (skipped when content is auto-scrolling, so the field-shadowed ints round-trip without driving a contradictory direction).
- JViewport / JScrollBar are emulator-only shadow JDK components per the SD_no_sjoptionpane precedent (no SJOptionPane). The surrogate exposes only `Scroller.setContent` / `getContent` / `setScrollDirection` / `getScrollDirection` for pure-surrogate users.

**Rationale.**
- **Peer choice: `Scroller`.** Vaadin 25's native scroll container — single-content-slot semantic matches JScrollPane's one-viewport-view contract directly. `ScrollDirection` enum collapses the JDK two-axis policy cross-product into a 4-value enum that's expressive enough for the testbed cases and small enough to map without a shadow store. Alternative: a custom `Div` with `overflow: auto` CSS — rejected; `Scroller` is the canonical Vaadin primitive and ships ScrollDirection + scroll-position helpers (`scrollToTop` / `scrollToBottom`) we can lift through later.
- **Auto-scroll guard at surrogate layer, not emulator only.** The user's stance is "pretend the scrollbar was set, don't actually render double." The detection has to fire when content is set — both via the emulator's `setViewportView` (which calls the surrogate's `setContent`) AND via direct pure-surrogate `setContent(grid)`. Putting the guard at the surrogate's `setContent` override catches both paths in one place; emulator-only would miss pure-surrogate users.
- **Three-class detection list, not user-extensible.** The testbed needs Grid + TextArea + Scroller. User-extensible mechanisms (`SHelper.registerAutoScrollingType(Class<?>)`) are over-design for three matches; if a future component surfaces a fourth class, add the `instanceof` branch then. R_infra_not_surface.
- **Pure-surrogate `getScrollDirection()` lossy round-trip after `setContent(autoScrollingType)`.** `setScrollDirection(VERTICAL)` then `setContent(grid)` makes `getScrollDirection()` return `NONE`. R_vaadin_first-accepted lossy direction — the surrogate owns "what's actually rendering"; the JDK-shaped policy ints live on the emulator side via field shadow per R_swing_is_truth. Migration convention: read scrollbar policy through the emulator (`JScrollPane.getVerticalScrollBarPolicy()`), not by inspecting the surrogate's Scroller direction.

**Alternatives.**
- **Always-Scroller, no auto-scroll guard.** Rejected — wraps a Grid in `overflow: auto`, producing stacked scrollbars or zero-height grids depending on parent sizing. The testbed both broke this way; the guard is the user-accepted fix per the design pre-flight.
- **Conditional peer (Scroller for non-scrolling content; Div for already-scrolling content).** Rejected — an SJScrollPane's peer type changing based on its first content swap breaks the 1:1 emulator↔peer invariant and the `instanceof Scroller` checks downstream (Karibu locators, ScrollDirection round-trip on the surrogate).
- **CSS `overflow: visible` override on the host element when content auto-scrolls.** Rejected — Vaadin Scroller's web component owns its DOM template and overflow rules; CSS overrides on the host don't reliably reach the inner template. `setScrollDirection(NONE)` is the documented Vaadin-supported "off" switch.

**Accepted limitations.**
- **`getScrollDirection()` returns `NONE` after `setContent(grid)` regardless of what user set.** Documented above; R_vaadin_first lossy. Workaround: read JDK ints on the emulator's JScrollPane.
- **No support for `JScrollPane.setColumnHeaderView` / `setRowHeaderView` / `setCorner`.** Surrogate-side has no slot for these — Vaadin Scroller is single-content-slot. Emulator-side WARNs per R_vaadin_first (D_jscrollpane).
- **No `JViewport` surrogate.** `JViewport` is emulator-only (SD_no_sjoptionpane precedent — Vaadin has no separate viewport object; `Scroller.getContent()` IS the viewport view). Pure-surrogate users access content via Vaadin's own `getContent()`.
- **No surrogate-level `addChangeListener` for scroll position.** Vaadin Scroller doesn't fire server-side scroll-position events without a custom DOM listener stack — out of scope here. JDK JViewport's `addChangeListener` lives on the emulator's shadow JViewport (stores listeners in a list that never fires; documented).
- **`setLayout(LayoutManager)` stores but doesn't dispatch.** SJScrollPane's host element doesn't honor flex-CSS in a useful way (Scroller owns the DOM template). Same shape SJPanel documents for unrecognised LayoutManagers.

**Validation.** Surrogate test suite: `SJScrollPaneTest` covers ctors (no-arg / Component / Component+ScrollDirection), `getUIClassID == "ScrollPaneUI"`, the auto-scroll guard fires for Grid / TextArea / Scroller content (all three force NONE after setContent), the guard does NOT fire for non-scrolling content (a `Div` keeps the user's set ScrollDirection), `setContent(null)` clears the slot without errors, post-content `setScrollDirection(VERTICAL)` overrides the guard's NONE (opt-out works), L&F getUI / setUI WARN-and-drop, happy-path zero stub WARNs. Emulator test (D_jscrollpane) covers the JDK-shape policy round-trip + the cross-product mapping table.

---

<a id="SD_sjformatted_family"></a>
## SD_sjformatted_family. `SJFormatted*` family — one surrogate per Vaadin input control, JFormattedTextField-specific surface lifted into a shared mixin

**Decision.** Five surrogates land together for [emulators D_jformattedtextfield](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jformattedtextfield)'s strategy dispatch:

- `SJFormattedDatePicker extends DatePicker` — DateStrategy peer.
- `SJFormattedIntegerField extends IntegerField` — IntegerStrategy peer.
- `SJFormattedLongField extends LongField` — LongStrategy peer.
- `SJFormattedNumberField extends NumberField` — NumberStrategy peer.
- `SJFormattedTextField extends TextField` — DefaultStrategy + MaskStrategy peer.

Each is-a different Vaadin input component; together they cover the formatter-driven input-shape range JFormattedTextField needs. Surrogate-first per [D_surrogate_first](../emulators/decisions.md#D_surrogate_first) — the emulator JFormattedTextField uses one of these as peer based on the strategy chosen at ctor time.

<a id="SD_formatted_no_base_class"></a>**SD_formatted_no_base_class — Five concrete surrogates, no shared base class.** Vaadin's DatePicker / NumberField / IntegerField / LongField / TextField don't share a stateful supertype that exposes `setValue(Object)` polymorphically. A single base class is impossible. The JTextField-shaped surface (`columns` / `horizontalAlignment` / `actionCommand` / `EventListenerList` for ActionListener fan-out / `setAction` propagation) follows the [SD_sjpasswordfield](#SD_sjpasswordfield) precedent — duplicated per surrogate, not lifted. Surface is small, leaf-stable, and on non-TextField peers (DatePicker / NumberField / …) most of it collapses to drop-and-WARN; per-surrogate explicit WARN sites are easier to read than a mixin with `instanceof TextField`-shaped branches.

<a id="SD_formatted_native_value"></a>**SD_formatted_native_value — Value source-of-truth: peer's native Vaadin type, per surrogate.** Each surrogate's source of truth for the value is its native Vaadin getter (`DatePicker.getValue() : LocalDate`, `IntegerField.getValue() : Integer`, etc.) plus a per-surrogate converter where the JDK type and the Vaadin type differ. R_swing_is_truth sync from peer ↔ surrogate value: `ValueChangeListener` on the peer fires `SHelper.callSwing` → emit `PropertyChangeEvent("value", old, new)` on the surrogate. The Object-typed `getValue()` and `setValue(Object)` on the surrogate side coerce between JDK type (Date / Integer / Long / Number / String) and Vaadin type (LocalDate / Integer / Long / Double / String). DatePicker conversion reuses [SJSpinner](#SD_sjspinner)'s `toLocalDate(Date)` / `toDate(LocalDate)` helpers, promoted from SJSpinner-private to a shared `SHelper.dateConversions` utility class so SJFormattedDatePicker doesn't reinvent them.

<a id="SD_sjformattedfieldmixin"></a>**SD_sjformattedfieldmixin — `SJFormattedFieldMixin` for the JFormattedTextField-specific surface.** Non-text-base peers (DatePicker, NumberField, …) cannot use [JTextComponentMixin](#SD_sjtextfield)'s Document↔peer-text sync — the mixin's body gates on `_self() instanceof TextFieldBase`. A new `SJFormattedFieldMixin` (interface, default methods, holder via `ComponentUtil.setData` per [SD_mixin_mechanism](#SD_mixin_mechanism)) carries the JFormattedTextField-specific surface that all five surrogates share: `value` (Object) source-of-truth field, `valueClass` introspection, focus-lost-behavior int (`REVERT` / `COMMIT` / `COMMIT_OR_REVERT` / `PERSIST`), `commitEdit()` hook. Document is held emulator-side only — surrogates don't consume Document events, just the displayed string — so the mixin exposes a `setDisplayText(String)` setter the emulator pushes to after `formatter.valueToString` runs. `valueToString` itself is *not* on the mixin (it would force surrogates to know about formatters; they don't): the emulator runs the formatter, then pushes the resulting String through the mixin.

<a id="SD_maskformatter_regex"></a>**SD_maskformatter_regex — Browser-side regex from MaskFormatter on SJFormattedTextField only.** SJFormattedTextField receives an optional pattern attribute via a package-private `setBrowserPattern(String regex)` hook; the emulator's MaskStrategy converts the MaskFormatter's mask string to a regex and writes it. The other four SJFormatted* surrogates have no equivalent — DatePicker / NumberField / IntegerField / LongField own their own client-side parsers and don't benefit from a regex layer. Pattern attr is set via `getElement().setAttribute("pattern", regex)`; cleared by passing null.

<a id="SD_formatted_listener_storage"></a>**SD_formatted_listener_storage — Listener storage: ActionListener + PropertyChangeListener per R_vaadin_first.** ActionListener fan-out on commit (focus-loss / Enter / `commitEdit()`) follows SJTextField's shape — `EventListenerList` per surrogate. `PropertyChangeEvent("value", old, new)` fires from `setValue(Object)` whenever the Object value changes; uses the existing `ComponentMixin.firePropertyChange` infrastructure. Every ActionListener / PropertyChangeListener registration is backed by a Vaadin event subscription (`KeyNotifier.addKeyPressListener(Key.ENTER, …)`, `BlurNotifier.addBlurListener(…)`, `HasValue.addValueChangeListener(…)`) — no listener storage without a Vaadin source.

**Rationale.**

- **Separate surrogates per Vaadin input class.** Vaadin's parallel-not-shared peer hierarchy already drove SJTextField + SJPasswordField apart (SD_sjpasswordfield); the JFormatted* family extends the same shape across three more peer types. Trying to unify behind a single CustomField composite (SJSpinner shape, SD_sjspinner) would pay with extra DOM nesting + Karibu locator drilling and a more fragile peer-type-swap mechanism that still doesn't generalize across rendering shapes — five thin surrogates with strategy dispatch on the emulator side are cleaner.
- **`SJFormattedFieldMixin` lifted; JTextField surface duplicated.** Two surfaces, two stances. The JFormattedTextField-specific surface (value, focusLostBehavior, commitEdit) is identical across all five surrogates and unlikely to grow leaf-specific overrides — lift. The JTextField surface (columns, alignment, actionCommand, setAction) is mostly drop-and-WARN on non-TextField peers and the per-peer WARN sites read more clearly when explicit — duplicate, per the SD_sjpasswordfield precedent.
- **Date↔LocalDate helpers shared across SJSpinner + SJFormattedDatePicker.** SJSpinner's `toLocalDate` / `toDate` helpers were already private; promoting them to `SHelper.dateConversions` avoids a near-identical re-implementation. No new public surface — the helpers stay package-private under `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates`.

**Alternatives.**

- **Single CustomField composite SJFormattedField with swappable inner peer (SJSpinner shape).** Considered. Rejected per Rationale.
- **Lift JTextField surface into a shared mixin.** Rejected — only five consumers, surface is mostly drop-and-WARN on non-TextField peers; a mixin with `instanceof TextField`-shaped branches reads worse than per-surrogate explicit WARN sites.
- **Use bare `SJDatePicker` / `SJNumberField` (no `Formatted` prefix), reusable beyond JFormattedTextField.** Rejected — bare versions would have to conform to a more general Vaadin-API contract (no JTextField surface) and JFormattedTextField's bridge would have a fatter emulator. The `SJFormatted` prefix scopes the surrogate's responsibilities tightly to the JFormattedTextField family.
- **Document↔peer sync on non-TextFieldBase peers.** Rejected — DatePicker has no String-typed value; pushing Document text into LocalDate is meaningless. The emulator-side Document holds the displayed string; the surrogate consumes only the post-formatter String via `setDisplayText`.

**Accepted limitations.**

- **Five surrogates land together.** The JFormattedTextField slice can't ship per-strategy in isolation without leaving the unimplemented strategies WARN-throwing on ctor. They land in dependency order (DatePicker first to unblock the user-stated date-picker need, then DefaultStrategy, then number family, then mask); the in-tree state between increments has incomplete coverage — typical incremental progression.
- **No `SJFormattedDateTimePicker`.** A DateFormatter with time component (`"yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm"`) renders as `SJFormattedDatePicker` (date-only); the time portion is dropped silently. Add `SJFormattedDateTimePicker extends DateTimePicker` if a real migration target uses time-bearing date formats; deferred per R_infra_not_surface. Mirrors SJSpinner's existing limitation under SD_sjspinner.
- **`MaskFormatter` overwrite mode is not modeled.** Typed input behaves as insert mode; "type over" patterns lose the JDK behavior. R_match_swing_errors (c) drop-and-WARN.
- **`SJFormattedTextField.setBrowserPattern(String)` is package-private,** intended for emulator-side wiring only. Pure-surrogate users compose with Vaadin's standard validation API instead.
- **No `valueToString` on the surrogate side.** The mixin exposes a `setDisplayText(String)` hook the emulator pushes to after running `formatter.valueToString`. Pure-surrogate users who set the value directly without a formatter get the Vaadin-native string representation (DatePicker uses its locale formatter; NumberField uses Double.toString-style); to match a Swing app's exact formatted display they need to go through the emulator.

**Validation.** Per-surrogate test suites under `:surrogates/src/test/java`:

- `SJFormattedDatePickerTest` — ctor variants (no-arg, Date initial value), `setValue(Date)` updates LocalDate peer, peer-side LocalDate change fires `PropertyChangeEvent("value", oldDate, newDate)`, Date↔LocalDate round-trip via shared helpers, focus-lost-commit shape (value pulled from peer, displayed text recomputed via emulator-side formatter hook), R_vaadin_first drop-and-WARN sweeps (setColumns / setHorizontalAlignment), L&F WARN-drops, accessibility WARN-and-null.
- `SJFormattedIntegerFieldTest` / `SJFormattedLongFieldTest` / `SJFormattedNumberFieldTest` — same shape with appropriate Number subtypes.
- `SJFormattedTextFieldTest` — ctor variants, `setValue(String)` round-trip, `setBrowserPattern` writes / clears the pattern attribute, ActionListener Enter wiring, R_vaadin_first drop-and-WARN per the JTextField duplicated surface.

Combined with the emulator suite in [D_jformattedtextfield](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jformattedtextfield) and the Sampler `FormattedFieldsPanel` exit-gate, the strategy table → surrogate dispatch is verified end-to-end.


<a id="SD_browser_timezone"></a>
## SD_browser_timezone. `BrowserTimeZone` — Date↔LocalDate uses browser zone, throws if `BrowserTimeZone.fetch()` not called during UI init

**Decision.** `SHelper.toLocalDate(Date)` and `SHelper.toDate(LocalDate)` — used by `SJSpinner` (SpinnerDateModel + DatePicker peer) and the `SJFormatted*` family's `SJFormattedDatePicker` (DateFormatter + DatePicker peer) — read the time zone from a new `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.BrowserTimeZone` cache instead of `ZoneId.systemDefault()`. The cache is populated from Vaadin's `ExtendedClientDetails` via `BrowserTimeZone.fetch()`, called from a UI init listener. `BrowserTimeZone.get()` throws `IllegalStateException` when the cache is empty — third enumerated case in the R_match_swing_errors / D_gap_severity_triage case-1 family of programming-error throws. Slimmed Java port of [karibu-tools' `BrowserTimeZone.kt`](https://github.com/mvysny/karibu-tools/blob/main/karibu-tools/src/main/kotlin/BrowserTimeZone.kt): we cache the derived `ZoneId` directly rather than the whole `ExtendedClientDetails`, since the surrogate layer only ever reads the zone.

**Rationale.**

- **Browser zone, not server JVM zone.** A Swing desktop app's `ZoneId.systemDefault()` IS the user's zone — JVM and user share a machine. A Vaadin web app's `ZoneId.systemDefault()` is the *server* JVM, which sits in UTC (or whatever the deployment environment picks) while the browser is on the user's machine. Date↔LocalDate round-trips that go through `systemDefault()` therefore disagree with what the user sees in their DatePicker. The `:surrogates` Vaadin-first stance — "the Vaadin peer is the visible truth" — extends to time-zone interpretation: the peer is in the browser, so the conversion uses the browser's zone.
- **The zone's arithmetic is `java.util.TimeZone`'s, not `java.time.ZoneId`'s.** `BrowserDateUtils.toLocalDate` / `toDate` read and build the wall-date with a default (Julian/Gregorian hybrid) `GregorianCalendar` in `TimeZone.getTimeZone(BrowserTimeZone.get())` — the computation `SimpleDateFormat` and `Calendar` run, and the one a Swing app's own `JSpinner` DateEditor ran on the desktop. The two disagree before standard time — `TimeZone` applies today's raw offset to every pre-1900 instant, `ZoneId` the real local mean time — and midnight in one is the previous day in the other: measured on the `testapps/crud` kit round (2026-09-24), a Los Angeles browser picking 1867-11-07 saw the app print 1867-11-06, and a Berlin browser opening Ada Lovelace's 1815-12-10 saw 1815-12-09 in the picker. Agreeing with `TimeZone` makes every `Date` the pickers hand back identical to what the migrator's date code builds and prints, in both stages. The hybrid calendar, not a proleptic one, so that before 1582-10-15 the picker shows the same label `SimpleDateFormat` prints; the ten dates the cutover skipped come back ten days later. The cost is hand-written `java.time` over a pre-1900 `Date` (`date.toInstant().atZone(zoneId)`), which can disagree by a day — the population already told to convert through `BrowserDateUtils`. **Rejected: moving the emulators onto `ZoneId` rules instead** (a `TimeZone` subclass over `ZoneRules`). It cannot be done faithfully: for any zone that is not the JDK's internal `ZoneInfo`, `GregorianCalendar.computeTime` takes the offset from the package-private `TimeZone.getOffsets`, which is `getRawOffset()` plus DST — one value per zone — so `Calendar.set(...).getTime()` and `SimpleDateFormat.parse` would keep the old offsets while `format` moved, and the emulators would stop agreeing with themselves.
- **Throw, don't UTC-fallback.** Karibu-tools' equivalent falls back to UTC if `extendedClientDetails` is null; we throw. Reasoning: a missing zone is always a programming error (forgot to call `fetch()` from UI init), and a silent UTC fallback produces dates an hour off in subtle ways — much harder to diagnose than a loud `IllegalStateException` pointing at the missing wiring. Pairs with R_match_swing_errors's general programming-error stance: "doing something silly beats crashing the host app" applies to *runtime gaps* (no Vaadin counterpart for a Swing API contract); "throw with a pointer at the cause" applies to *contract violations*. Forgetting the UI init wiring is the latter.
- **Slim port — `ZoneId`, not `ExtendedClientDetails`.** Karibu-tools stores the whole `ExtendedClientDetails` in the session because it exposes other derived properties (current date/time, locale). The surrogate layer's only consumers (`SHelper.toLocalDate` / `SHelper.toDate`) read the zone and nothing else. Storing only the derived `ZoneId` keeps the contract narrow, makes the test fixture trivial (`BrowserTimeZone.setZoneId(ZoneOffset.UTC)` instead of fabricating a fake `ExtendedClientDetails` with its long constructor), and avoids implying a wider API than the surrogate layer needs.
- **`@PreserveOnRefresh` makes the round-trip fast enough.** The user-facing concern with throw-on-missing is the very-first-render timing: `Page.retrieveExtendedClientDetails` queues a JS round-trip whose callback fires in the *next* request, not synchronously. With `@PreserveOnRefresh`, however, Vaadin's first response includes a window-name / ECD probe that the browser answers *before* the route handler runs — by the time the view's constructor reaches a `JSpinner(SpinnerDateModel)`, the cache is populated. Apps that don't use `@PreserveOnRefresh` and land directly on a Date-bearing view get a deterministic throw on first render rather than silently-wrong dates; the migrator either adds `@PreserveOnRefresh` (cheap) or moves the Date-bearing render off the entry route.

**Alternatives considered.**

- **Karibu-tools verbatim — store `ExtendedClientDetails`, fall back to UTC in `get()`.** Rejected per the throw stance above. The slim-down is a related-but-separate simplification (storing only the derived `ZoneId` instead of the whole ECD), kept here because the surrogate layer never needs the rest of ECD.
- **Lazy `fetch()` from inside `SHelper.toLocalDate` / `SHelper.toDate`.** Considered: the first call to `toLocalDate` would queue a fetch and return a UTC default; the second call would see the populated cache. Rejected — the call sites are typically inside Swing-event paths under `SHelper.callSwing`, which run in a UI fiber. Queuing browser round-trips from that depth complicates the deadlock model (R_callswing_envelope / D_callswing_loom) for marginal benefit; an explicit UI-init `fetch()` is cleaner.
- **Per-call `UI.getCurrent().getPage().retrieveExtendedClientDetails(...)` synchronously.** Vaadin has no synchronous accessor — `retrieveExtendedClientDetails` is always a JS round-trip. The only synchronous path is reading the session-cached value, which is what `BrowserTimeZone.get()` does.
- **Push the throw deeper — make `SHelper.toLocalDate` / `SHelper.toDate` accept an explicit `ZoneId` parameter from each call site.** Rejected as API noise. The surrogate's job is to hide the time-zone plumbing from `:surrogates`-and-`:emulators` consumers; threading a `ZoneId` through every Date-bearing surrogate would scatter the contract instead of centralizing it.

**Accepted limitations.**

- **First-render dependence on `@PreserveOnRefresh`.** Apps that don't annotate their entry route with `@PreserveOnRefresh` AND land directly on a Date-bearing view AND haven't called `fetch()` synchronously elsewhere will see the throw on first render. Documented as the migrator's responsibility per CLAUDE.md §R_match_swing_errors case (3); not silently swallowed.
- **Tests need an explicit fixture.** MockVaadin has no real browser to round-trip with, so `fetch()` is a no-op in tests and the cache stays empty. Test bases (`AbstractKaribuTest` in `:surrogates` and `:emulators`, `MockVirtualThreadAwareServlet.setupMockVaadin` in `:sampler`) plant `ZoneOffset.UTC` directly via `BrowserTimeZone.setZoneId`. UTC over a DST-bearing zone so tests don't break twice a year.
- **Subsequent `fetch()` calls are no-ops once the zone is set.** A session whose zone has been planted (production callback or test fixture) won't re-trigger a browser round-trip if `fetch()` is called again. Aligns with karibu-tools' shape and keeps fetch idempotent across UI re-init / session-refresh paths.

**Validation.** `SJSpinnerTest`'s SpinnerDateModel round-trip (line 275) and `SJFormattedDatePickerTest`'s `jdkDate(...)` helper (line 278) compare against `BrowserTimeZone.get()` — same zone source production code now uses, so the round-trip stays apples-to-apples. The `:emulators` `JSpinnerTest` SpinnerDateModel scenario inherits the fixture via that module's `AbstractKaribuTest`. `:sampler` tests inherit via `MockVirtualThreadAwareServlet.setupMockVaadin`, which keeps every sampler test's `MockVaadin.setup` call site one line shorter. The arithmetic bullet: `BrowserDateUtilsTest` (agreement with `SimpleDateFormat` across eight zones on both sides of their standard meridian, 1815–2026, plus the Julian label and BC years) and `SJSpinnerTest`'s two pre-1900 cases, the round's Los Angeles and Berlin symptoms.

<a id="SD_sjeditorpane_div"></a>
## SD_sjeditorpane_div. `SJEditorPane` surrogate — `Div` peer, read-only HTML only (superseded by SD_sjeditorpane_rte)

> **Superseded by [SD_sjeditorpane_rte](#SD_sjeditorpane_rte).** The `Div`-backed read-only `SJEditorPane` is **deleted**; `JEditorPane` peers over a single `SJEditorPane extends RichTextEditor` for both read-only and editable modes. The synchronous `setPage(URL)` fetch (`URL.openStream` → best-effort charset decode → `setText`, `"page"` PCE, server-side reach) carries over onto the RTE surrogate; its rationale and accepted divergences live in SD_sjeditorpane_rte and the `SJEditorPane` class javadoc.

<a id="SD_sjtoolbar"></a>
## SD_sjtoolbar. `SJToolBar` surrogate — `Div` peer with pinned flex CSS, orientation via `flex-direction`, `addSeparator` pushes perpendicular thin Div, `setMargin` → CSS padding, drop-and-WARN on user `setLayout`

Emulator-side decision: [D_jtoolbar](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jtoolbar).

**Peer choice — `Div`, not `HorizontalLayout`.** The initial sketch was `extends HorizontalLayout`; the design pass picked `Div` instead. Two reasons:

1. **Orientation symmetry.** A toolbar's `setOrientation(VERTICAL)` flips CSS `flex-direction: row` → `column`. On a Vaadin `HorizontalLayout` that flip would render correctly but produce the "a HorizontalLayout that's actually vertical" misnomer for any Vaadin-shape consumer reading the class name. `Div` is direction-agnostic — same shape SJPanel / SJRootPane / SJEditorPane already follow.
2. **CSS flex-direction lives inline, not via a Vaadin component-property.** Both peer types end up writing `flex-direction` to the host element either way (HorizontalLayout's own setter would be a hard-coded "row" we'd have to override on every `setOrientation`); `Div` skips the indirection.

The toolbar's pinned flex CSS — `display:inline-flex; align-items:center; gap:var(--lumo-space-xs); flex-direction:row|column` — is written from the ctor and on every `setOrientation`. We do not go through `ContainerMixin.setLayout`'s CSS dispatch (that's parameterised on JDK `FlowLayout` / `BorderLayout` / BoxLayout / GridBagLayout instances; the toolbar wants none).

**User `setLayout` drops-and-WARN per R_vaadin_first.** Migrators calling `toolbar.setLayout(new BorderLayout())` would otherwise have the Border-grid CSS clobber the toolbar's flex CSS — a silent visual regression. The override:

- `setLayout(null)` is silently accepted (Container.removeAll teardown path; no point WARNing on it).
- `setLayout(non-null)` writes `SHelper.onUnimplemented` and preserves the pinned flex CSS.

Lifts the restriction if a real migration target needs custom toolbar layouts.

**Orientation — field source-of-truth + CSS write on every change.** `setOrientation` validates per R_match_swing_errors (`IllegalArgumentException` matches JDK), fires `"orientation"` PCE on actual change, and rewrites the pinned-flex CSS block whole (composed each time rather than diffing which key changed so a future addition can't leave stale keys behind). Runtime flip is supported — no peer swap needed because the surrogate is direction-agnostic at the type level.

**`addSeparator` pushes a perpendicular thin `Div`-shaped bar.** A horizontal toolbar gets a `min-width: 1px` child styled with `align-self: stretch` and `background-color: var(--lumo-contrast-20pct)`; a vertical toolbar gets `min-height: 1px`. The bar is built as a plain Vaadin `Div` with a `data-emul-toolbar-separator` attribute — no surrogate-side `SJSeparator` class (would be a one-method drop-and-WARN repeat of the JSeparator emulator's existing stretching trick); the toolbar owns its separators' CSS inline. `addSeparator(Dimension)` accepts the dimension and drops it per R_layouts_close_enough (no pixel-accurate layout); the visual output matches the no-arg form.

**`setMargin(Insets)` → CSS `padding: t r b l`.** Straight write to the host element's style; `null` removes the property (matches JDK's "use L&F default" semantics, which here means no padding). Round-trips via the stored `Insets` field (CSS read-back would require parsing `"2px 4px 2px 4px"` shorthand; the field is cheaper). Fires `"margin"` PCE on actual change.

**API partition.**

- **Bucket A (real impl).** Ctors (`SJToolBar()` / `SJToolBar(int orientation)`), `setOrientation` / `getOrientation`, `addSeparator()` / `addSeparator(Dimension)`, `setMargin` / `getMargin`, child management via inherited `HasComponents` / `ContainerMixin`, L&F `getUIClassID = "ToolBarUI"` + `updateUI` noop. Pinned flex CSS lifecycle in the ctor.
- **Bucket B / C (dropped — emulator-only on the JToolBar shell side).** `setFloatable` / `isFloatable`, `setRollover` / `isRollover`, `setBorderPainted` / `isBorderPainted` — no method declared on the surrogate. Pure-surrogate Vaadin-shape users don't reach for these; if they do, they get a compile error (and a hint to use the JToolBar emulator). Emulator-side shadow per [D_jtoolbar](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jtoolbar).
- **Bucket C (drop-and-WARN).** `setUI(ToolBarUI)`, `setLayout(non-null)`.

The asymmetric split (no fields on the surrogate, field-shadow on the emulator) is the same shape SD_sjbutton takes for state-conditional button icons and SD_toggle_checkbox_first_cut takes for `borderPaintedFlat`. Each shipping field reflects a Swing-only concept with no Vaadin counterpart whose round-trip matters only to JDK-shaped consumers.

**No `JToolBar.Separator` counterpart here.** The stage-2 class is the emulator's `vaadinx.swing.JToolBar.Separator`, a `JSeparator` child peered on `SJSeparator` ([D_jtoolbar](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jtoolbar)); stage-3 code calls `addSeparator()`, whose bar is a plain `Div` rather than a component, or adds an `SJSeparator` itself.

**Accepted limitations.**

- **No theme variant default.** Vanilla styling. Per the design pass — migration neutrality > out-of-box visual treatment; users style via `setBorder` / `setBackground`.
- **CSS gap from `--lumo-space-xs`.** Pulls Lumo's CSS variable for inter-button spacing rather than a fixed pixel value. Migrators on a non-Lumo theme see the CSS variable fall through to browser default (typically 0); accepted divergence.
- **`paramString` empty tail.** No round-trip surface for floatable/borderPainted on this side (they live on the emulator); margin lives here but renders via CSS rather than a printable property. The pure-surrogate `paramString` tail is empty.

**Validation.** `SJToolBarTest` (22 tests) — ctor variants, orientation validation, pinned flex CSS on the host, runtime orientation flip + PCE, addSeparator perpendicular bars on both orientations, addSeparator(Dimension) accept-drop, setMargin → CSS padding round-trip + PCE, setLayout drop-and-WARN with pinned-CSS survival, L&F + getUIClassID, Vaadin Button children land as flex items, data-swing-class stamp. `JToolBarTest` covers the emulator side ([D_jtoolbar](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jtoolbar)). `ToolBarsWarnInventoryTest` on the Sampler side asserts zero stub WARN across the user-path (3-demo nav + 4 button clicks including the add(Action) path) and the API-surface micro-driver.

<a id="SD_sjsplitpane"></a>
## SD_sjsplitpane. `SJSplitPane` surrogate — `SplitLayout` peer, slot setters mirror primary/secondary, no-counterpart cluster present-but-drops-and-WARNs

Emulator-side decision: [D_jsplitpane](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jsplitpane).

**Peer choice — `SplitLayout` direct.** Vaadin's two-slot resizable splitter is exactly the JDK shape; orientation enums share naming convention (HORIZONTAL = side-by-side, VERTICAL = stacked); the primary/secondary slot pair maps directly onto JDK's left/right (HORIZONTAL_SPLIT) and top/bottom (VERTICAL_SPLIT) alias pairs. No CSS-layer divergence needed.

**API surface — JDK-shaped where Vaadin can reach, present-but-drops where it can't (per R_vaadin_first).** The surrogate exposes the full JDK setter surface for the no-counterpart knobs (`setResizeWeight`, `setOneTouchExpandable`, `setContinuousLayout`, `setDividerSize`, `setLastDividerLocation`, `setDividerLocation(int)`, `setDividerLocation(double)`'s `setLastDividerLocation` companion) — `SHelper.onUnimplemented` + drop. Their getters return the JDK default unconditionally (no `Store` shadow per R_vaadin_first). The reverse — omitting the setters entirely as SD_sjtoolbar does for floatable/rollover/borderPainted — would push pure-surrogate users to learn which JDK methods are silently absent class-by-class; presence-with-drop keeps the migration-shape API discoverable. Round-trip + PCE for these knobs lives on the emulator side per [D_jsplitpane](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jsplitpane).

**Why the present-with-drop split differs from SD_sjtoolbar's omit-entirely.** Two situational distinctions:

1. SD_sjtoolbar's cluster (floatable/rollover/borderPainted) reflects platform concerns that don't exist in a browser (detachable toolbars, JDK rollover state). The surrogate omitting them is a "this concept doesn't apply at this layer" signal.
2. SD_sjsplitpane's cluster (resize weight, one-touch, continuous layout, divider size) reflects splitter UX knobs that *do* apply but Vaadin's SplitLayout doesn't expose Java-side. The user-intent is meaningful; we just can't honour it. Present-with-WARN matches that shape.

The contract for future surrogates: drop-entirely when the *concept* is foreign to the browser (R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket b); present-with-WARN when the concept applies but Vaadin lacks the knob (R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket a or c).

**`setDividerLocation(int pixel)` — R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (a) blocked-upstream.** SplitLayout's only divider API is `setSplitterPosition(double percent)`. We can't faithfully translate pixels without container width (R_layouts_close_enough: no server-side coordinates), so we drop-and-WARN. Classified blocked-upstream (lift if Vaadin grows a pixel-based splitter API), same shape as SJSlider's tick deferral. The companion `setDividerLocation(double 0–1)` is fully wired — multiplies by 100 and forwards to `setSplitterPosition`.

**Slot setters — `setLeftComponent` / `setRightComponent` (+ top/bottom aliases).** Map directly onto `addToPrimary(Component)` / `addToSecondary(Component)` — both replace any existing slot occupant (Vaadin's `setComponent(c, slotName)` semantic). Getters return the current `getPrimaryComponent()` / `getSecondaryComponent()`. The setters take Vaadin `Component`s (stage-3 shape); emulator side bridges through `peer.getPeer()` extraction.

**`resetToPreferredSizes()` — best-effort 50/50.** JDK computes divider position from child preferred sizes; we have neither preferred-size machinery (R_layouts_close_enough) nor pixel coordinates. The behavioural intent — "snap divider back to a neutral position" — translates to `setSplitterPosition(50.0)`. Real impl on the surrogate; zero-WARN. Migrators who expect "snap to child sizes" get "snap to 50/50" — accepted R_best_effort_behaviour best-effort divergence. The companion `getDividerLocation()` getter on the surrogate always returns `-1` (the JDK sentinel for "not set / compute from children"); the int round-trip lives on the emulator.

**Orientation — JDK int constants ↔ Vaadin `Orientation` enum.** `setOrientation(int)` validates per R_match_swing_errors (`IllegalArgumentException` matches JDK on garbage int), translates to `SplitLayout.Orientation.HORIZONTAL` (JDK `HORIZONTAL_SPLIT = 1`) or `VERTICAL` (JDK `VERTICAL_SPLIT = 0`), forwards to the inherited `super.setOrientation(Orientation)`, fires `"orientation"` PCE on actual change. Reverse mapping on `getOrientation()`.

**API partition.**

- **Bucket A (real impl).** Ctors (`SJSplitPane()` / `SJSplitPane(int orientation)`), `setOrientation(int)` / `getOrientation()` (JDK shape over inherited Vaadin enum API), `setLeftComponent` / `setTopComponent` / `setRightComponent` / `setBottomComponent` + getters (addToPrimary/Secondary aliases), `setDividerLocation(double 0–1)` → `setSplitterPosition(d*100)`, `resetToPreferredSizes()` → `setSplitterPosition(50.0)`, L&F `getUIClassID = "SplitPaneUI"` + `updateUI` noop.
- **Bucket C (present-with-drop-and-WARN).** `setDividerLocation(int pixels)` (blocked-upstream per R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket a); `setResizeWeight`, `setOneTouchExpandable`, `setContinuousLayout`, `setDividerSize`, `setLastDividerLocation` (no-counterpart, R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket c). All five setters log via `SHelper.onUnimplemented` + drop; matching getters return JDK defaults (`0.0`, `false`, `false`, `0`, `-1`).
- **Bucket C (drop-and-WARN, return JDK sentinel).** `getDividerLocation()` (-1), `getMinimumDividerLocation()` / `getMaximumDividerLocation()` (-1), `setUI(SplitPaneUI)`, `getUI()`, `getAccessibleContext()`.

**Drag-end PCE dropped.** Vaadin's `SplitterDragEndEvent` carries `primaryComponentFlexBasis` as a percent string (`"32.5%"`); JDK's `DIVIDER_LOCATION_PROPERTY` PCE fires with `Integer` pixels. Without container width we can't translate — same gap as `setDividerLocation(int)`. Drop entirely rather than fire with a fabricated value or sentinel. Re-litigates if a real migration target listens for the PCE.

**Accepted limitations.**

- **No "draggable" / "lock-divider" knob.** Vaadin's SplitLayout has no Java-side toggle; JDK has no such method either. No-op.
- **Per-slot styling** (Vaadin's `setPrimaryStyle` / `setSecondaryStyle`) not exposed as a JDK surface — pure-surrogate users reach for the inherited Vaadin methods directly; no Swing-flavoured counterpart needed.
- **No `JSplitPane.Separator` / `BasicSplitPaneDivider` shape ports.** JDK code reaching into the divider's UI delegate (`getUI().getDivider()`) bottoms out at the L&F dispatch surface which is R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (b) out of scope.

**Validation.** `SJSplitPaneTest` — ctor variants + orientation validation, orientation flip (Vaadin enum and JDK int round-trip), slot setters with replace semantics, `setSplitterPosition` round-trip and `setDividerLocation(double)` percent translation, `resetToPreferredSizes()` 50/50 snap + sentinel reset, the six no-counterpart setters all WARN + drop with JDK-default getter readout, sentinel reads on `getDividerLocation` / `getMinimum/MaximumDividerLocation`, `setUI` WARNs, `data-swing-class` stamp.

---

<a id="SD_sjtabbedpane"></a>
## SD_sjtabbedpane. `SJTabbedPane` surrogate — `TabSheet` peer, children-based tab header render (never `setLabel`), JDK-faithful `SingleSelectionModel` bridge, asymmetric WARN-vs-noop on `setTabLayoutPolicy`

Emulator-side decision: [D_jtabbedpane](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jtabbedpane).

**`SJTabbedPane extends TabSheet implements JComponentMixin`.** Vaadin's `TabSheet` (24+) couples a tab strip with a content-swap area — closer to JTabbedPane's shape than any prior component. Selection lives on the peer (R_vaadin_first source of truth); per-tab metadata (title / icon / tooltip / custom tab header / enabled) lives in `TabbedPaneStateStore.tabMetas` and is rendered into each Vaadin `Tab`'s **children** on every change.

**Why children-based, never `Tab.setLabel`.** Design probe (`_TabSheetProbe.kt`, deleted) revealed a footgun: `tab.setLabel("X")` wipes the Tab's children. So `tab.add(icon)` followed by `tab.setLabel(title)` loses the icon. The children-based render strategy avoids this entirely:

```
TITLE_ONLY:     tab.removeAll() + tab.add(Span(title))
TITLE_AND_ICON: tab.removeAll() + tab.add(iconComp) + tab.add(Span(title))
ICON_ONLY:      tab.removeAll() + tab.add(iconComp)
CUSTOM:         tab.removeAll() + tab.add(userComponent)
EMPTY:          tab.removeAll()
```

`Tab.setAriaLabel(title)` is always called (or `""` for empty) — accessibility is preserved regardless of which render mode runs, including the CUSTOM case where the user's custom header may not contain readable text. Header rebuild is single-method (`renderTabHeader(int)`) called by every per-tab mutation; no incremental child-edits, no state-machine tracking of "which child is title."

**JDK-faithful `SingleSelectionModel` bridge.** The store holds a `DefaultSingleSelectionModel` and an internal `ChangeListener` (the "modelListener") registered on it. The bridge has two legs:

1. **Peer → model** (TabSheet's `SelectedChangeEvent` listener, installed in ctor): wraps body in `SHelper.callSwing` per R_callswing_envelope, writes the peer's `getSelectedIndex()` into the model unconditionally. The peer is the source of truth; the model trails. Guarded by `preventPeerEvents` — set true by leg 2 around its peer-push so this listener bails when the change originated server-side.
2. **Model → world** (modelListener): user code calling `tabbedPane.setSelectedIndex(int)` or `tabbedPane.getModel().setSelectedIndex(int)` reaches us through this listener. Pushes to peer iff peer disagrees (the equality guard prevents the peer-originated round-trip from re-entering `super.setSelectedIndex`), then fires user-registered `ChangeListener`s on the surrogate's own list. Single fan-out point — both peer-clicks and JDK-side setSelectedIndex hit the same listener fire path.

This matches JDK JTabbedPane's structure: model fires `stateChanged`, an internal listener re-fires through `tabbedPane.addChangeListener` registrations. `setModel` swaps the model as the JDK allows — unhook the modelListener from the old instance, hook the new, sync the peer to the new model's selection. The model is JDK-side machinery (the R_vaadin_first model carve-out), so the swap is pure rewiring; it was originally dropped-and-WARNed on the mistaken ground that the wiring "assumes the original instance", but the wiring is three lines to re-point, and the emulator's [D_field_write_reconcile](../emulators/decisions.md#D_field_write_reconcile) `model` field needs the swap to be real.

**Bound-check on emulator (`setSelectedIndex`, `insertTab`).** Probe found Vaadin's `add(Tab, c, -1)` treats -1 as "append" (silently). JDK throws `IndexOutOfBoundsException` for negative `insertTab` indices and for `setSelectedIndex` outside `[-1, tabCount)`. We validate explicitly per R_match_swing_errors — the Vaadin permissiveness doesn't leak through. `setSelectedIndex(-1)` is legal (deselect); `setSelectedIndex(>= tabCount)` throws.

**`removeTabAt` selection-shift: no bridge needed.** Probe confirmed Vaadin's `remove(int)` reshuffle is JDK-identical: removing middle/selected stays at index (now next tab); removing last/selected shifts to i-1; removing below-selected decrements; removing above-selected leaves unchanged; removing only-tab → -1. We forward straight to `super.remove(int)`. The `syncModelFromPeer` post-call handles the one case Vaadin DOESN'T fire `SelectedChangeEvent` for: when the selected Tab instance is unchanged but its index shifted (remove-below-selected). Idempotent if Vaadin DID fire (model.setSelectedIndex dedupes on same-value). **The one exception is removing the selected tab:** Vaadin moves the selection onto the neighbour, and when that neighbour is disabled it falls back to the removed tab and throws `Tab to select must be a child`. So `removeTabAt` deselects first (behind `preventPeerEvents`), removes, then selects the JDK's target, which Vaadin refuses if it is disabled, leaving no tab selected. `removeAll` deselects first for the same reason, which also leaves one model change, to -1, as the JDK's `removeAll` makes.

**`setComponentAt` keeps the selection and fires nothing,** as the JDK's does. Vaadin has no content swap, so the tab is removed and re-added at its index; Vaadin moves the selection off the removed tab and then, through `addTabAtIndex`, past the re-inserted one. Both moves stay out of the model behind `preventPeerEvents`, and the selected index is put back afterwards.

**Under the emulator the model is private.** `JTabbedPane` owns its own `SingleSelectionModel` and does not share it (D_jtabbedpane): this class's structural methods shift the model themselves, which is right for a stage-3 caller and would be a second writer on a shared one. The emulator pushes the selected index through `setSelectedIndex` after each change, so in stage 2 the private model only mirrors the emulator's.

**Tab placement: `TOP` only — non-TOP WARNs.** Vaadin TabSheet exposes no orientation knob (the underlying `Tabs` has `setOrientation(VERTICAL)` but TabSheet doesn't surface it as of 25.1.6). `setTabPlacement(TOP)` is silent; `setTabPlacement(LEFT/RIGHT/BOTTOM)` WARNs + round-trips. Garbage placement throws IAE per R_match_swing_errors. Field shadow lives on this layer (`store.tabPlacement`); emulator fires `"tabPlacement"` PCE per R_swing_is_truth.

**Asymmetric `setTabLayoutPolicy` — `SCROLL` silent, `WRAP` WARNs.** Vaadin auto-scrolls on overflow already, with no wrap mode. The asymmetric WARN-vs-noop partition codifies user-intent:

- `setTabLayoutPolicy(SCROLL_TAB_LAYOUT)` → `SHelper.onNoop` (DEBUG only, no WARN). User asked for what Vaadin already does — intent met, no surprise to surface.
- `setTabLayoutPolicy(WRAP_TAB_LAYOUT)` → `SHelper.onUnimplemented`. User asked for wrap; we can't deliver. Surface the gap.

Default-ctor value is `WRAP_TAB_LAYOUT` (JDK default) but we don't WARN at ctor time — only on **explicit** setter calls, where user intent is unambiguous. Same shape as SD_api_three_buckets's "Bucket B = deliberate noop" partition.

**No-counterpart per-tab cluster — drop-and-WARN both layers.** `setMnemonicAt`, `setDisplayedMnemonicIndexAt`, `setBackgroundAt`, `setForegroundAt`, `setDisabledIconAt` all R_vaadin_first drop-and-WARN here. Storage-for-storage's-sake (without a Vaadin pulse behind them) is the anti-pattern R_vaadin_first names. Lift criterion when a real migration target needs round-trip: build the JSplitPane-style emulator-side field-shadow cluster — but pre-emptively building it violates R_infra_not_surface.

**Per-tab `Container.components` lives on the emulator only.** SJTabbedPane doesn't extend `Container`; the awt-side tree-shape concern is emulator-only (JComponentMixin reaches into Vaadin's component-children which is enough for surrogate-layer code). The emulator layer maintains `Container.components` via `addSlotChild` / `removeSlotChild` from the very first slice per D_jtabbedpane (folded in upfront after the D_jsplitpane_slot_tree_shape / D_shadow_children_tree_shape retro-fix lessons).

**Custom tab header (`setTabComponentAt`).** The JLawyer-critical method — multi-case-open with close-button tabs is the canonical use. JDK contract: setting a non-null custom component visually replaces the title/icon; the title shadow stays queryable via `getTitleAt` and powers `setAriaLabel(title)` for screen readers. Setting back to null reverts to the (title, icon) render. Maps to children-based render via the CUSTOM mode above. The emulator keeps the awt component in its per-tab state so `getTabComponentAt` returns the same instance the user passed — surrogate alone can't, because the user supplied an awt component but the surrogate only sees its Vaadin peer.

**API partition.**

- **Bucket A (real impl, JDK-faithful).** Three ctors with placement / policy validation; addTab × 3 overloads; insertTab; removeTabAt; removeAll; setComponentAt; setSelectedIndex / getSelectedIndex / setSelectedComponent / getSelectedComponent; setTitleAt / setIconAt / setToolTipTextAt / setEnabledAt / setTabComponentAt + getters; indexOfTab / indexOfComponent / indexOfTabComponent / getComponentAt / getTabCount / getTabRunCount; addChangeListener / removeChangeListener / getChangeListeners; getModel; setTabPlacement / setTabLayoutPolicy (with asymmetric WARN); JComponentMixin surface (border, tooltip, name, PCE, etc.).
- **Bucket B (silent noop).** `updateUI`, `setTabLayoutPolicy(SCROLL_TAB_LAYOUT)`.
- **Bucket C (drop-and-WARN).** `setMnemonicAt`, `setDisplayedMnemonicIndexAt`, `setBackgroundAt`, `setForegroundAt`, `setDisabledIconAt`, `getBoundsAt`, `indexAtLocation`, `setUI`, `getAccessibleContext`, `setTabPlacement(LEFT/RIGHT/BOTTOM)`, `setTabLayoutPolicy(WRAP_TAB_LAYOUT)`, `setComponentAt(null)`. (`setModel` graduated out of this bucket — see the model-bridge section above.)

**Accepted limitations.**

- **Visual placement is always TOP.** Per the upstream gap above. Visual + ariaLabel still correct for accessibility; field round-trip on emulator preserves Java-side state.
- **No wrap mode.** Per the upstream gap above. Tabs auto-scroll on overflow.
- **Disabled tab can't be programmatically selected.** Vaadin TabSheet refuses; JDK allows. R_best_effort_behaviour best-effort divergence; document.
- **No per-tab bg/fg/mnemonic/disabledIcon round-trip.** R_vaadin_first — would be storage-for-storage's-sake without a Vaadin pulse.
- **Image-icon render is AWT-load-dependent.** `setIconAt(ImageIcon)` works correctly in production where AWT can decode the raster; in headless test mode the image may not load and the Tab renders title-only. Visual verification lives in the Sampler route; surrogate test asserts meta-storage round-trip only.

**Validation.** `SJTabbedPaneTest` (61 tests) — ctor matrix + validation; add/insert/remove cycle with bounds checks; removing the selected tab beside a disabled one; `removeAll` firing once; `setComponentAt` keeping the selection; selection bridge across peer-driven + model-driven + same-value-dedupe paths; removeTabAt selection-shift across all five JDK corners (middle / last / below / above / only-tab); per-tab metadata round-trip with children-based render verification; custom tabComponent + title-revert; index lookups; placement / policy WARN/silent partition; R_vaadin_first cluster WARNs; L&F surface; data-swing-class stamp; JComponentMixin smoke.

<a id="SD_sjlist"></a>
## SD_sjlist. `SJList` surrogate — `Grid<Integer>` peer (row-index identity), `ListSelectionModel` bridge reused from SJTable, item-click→`SMouseEvent` double-click wire

Emulator-side decision: [D_jlist](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jlist). The driving use is the "per-case document list."

**`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJList<E> extends com.vaadin.flow.component.grid.Grid<Integer> implements JComponentMixin, ListSelectionListener, ListDataListener`** — SJTable's row-index identity, narrowed to a single headerless column.

**Peer choice — Grid, not VirtualList / ListBox.** The brainstorm fork. JList's three runtime-switchable selection modes (`SINGLE` / `SINGLE_INTERVAL` / `MULTIPLE_INTERVAL`, default the last) only map onto a *single* Vaadin component via `Grid.setSelectionMode` (`NONE`/`SINGLE`/`MULTI`). `VirtualList` carries **no native selection** (it's a pure virtualised renderer — picking it means hand-rolling click/range/keyboard selection, the weakest R_vaadin_first fit); `ListBox` / `MultiSelectListBox` are *two* classes (one per mode) a surrogate is-a can't swap between at runtime, and `MultiSelectListBox` renders checkboxes. Grid also (a) reuses SJTable's proven `ListSelectionModel`↔Grid two-way bridge verbatim, (b) rides the existing SJScrollPane auto-scroll guard (which already detects `Grid` content) so the universal `new JScrollPane(jlist)` idiom needs zero new work, and (c) keys rows by `Integer` index — which is why the items are indices, not `E`: JList permits duplicate `.equals()` elements, and Vaadin Grid keys items by identity (equal `E` values would collide). The accepted cost is a **checkbox column in MULTI mode** (R_best_effort_behaviour/R_layouts_close_enough — JList shows highlight-only; suppressing it is exactly what would have justified VirtualList, so we keep Grid's native selection chrome).

**Source of truth = `ListModel` + `ListSelectionModel`** (both JDK-reused per D_event_port_policy — `ListDataEvent` / `ListSelectionEvent` are `Object`-sourced). SJList IS-A both listeners and subscribes itself on install/swap (JDK JList's UI-delegate pattern). In-memory `setItems(fetch, count)` reads `model.getElementAt` live (no `TableRowSorter` — JList has none, so the fetch is trivial `0..size-1`); `intervalAdded`/`intervalRemoved` shift the selection model (the effective behaviour the dropped `BasicListUI` provided) + `refreshAll` — unless `setSelectionAdjustedByOwner(true)` says an owner shifts it itself, as the `JList` emulator does on the model's own thread (D_jlist); `contentsChanged` does per-index `refreshItem`. `setSelectionModel` shows the new model's selection on the Grid.

**Selection bridge** — copied from SJTable: `valueChanged` → `pushSelectionToPeer` (deselectAll + select per index); browser → us via `addSelectionListener` wrapped in `SHelper.callSwing` (R_callswing_envelope) under `preventPeerEvents`; the Vaadin selection listener re-installs on every `setSelectionMode` (Grid re-creates its selection backing). Mode mapping: `SINGLE`→`SINGLE`; both interval modes→`MULTI` with the `ListSelectionModel` enforcing the JDK contract (the SJTable divergence: browser ctrl-click past a single-interval silently reverts). `getSelectionModel()` clash with Grid's same-named getter resolved exactly as SD_sjtable: the JDK `ListSelectionModel` rides `getListSelectionModel()`, the emulator reaches it there.

**Renderer Vaadin-first (R_vaadin_first).** `setRenderer(Renderer<Integer>)` replaces the single column; default is a fresh-per-call `Span(value.toString())` (D_jcombobox DOM-adoption guard). The JDK `ListCellRenderer` surface lives only on the emulator (D_jlist + D_jcombobox).

**Double-click bridge — the headline.** `addMouseListener(SMouseListener)` overrides the inherited mixin wire (Grid dispatches *item* clicks carrying the row index, not generic component clicks). It installs **two** Grid subscriptions combined into one `Registration`: `addItemClickListener` → `MOUSE_CLICKED` with the browser click count (single = 1), `addItemDoubleClickListener` → `MOUSE_CLICKED` with `clickCount = 2` (`ItemDoubleClickEvent extends ItemClickEvent`, so one translation serves both). The clicked index is stashed in `ListStateStore.clickIndexStash` (a **field**, not a thread-local — the emulator's re-source may drain the dispatch in a UI fiber, and a field survives that boundary) around the fan-out so `locationToIndex(Point)` returns the clicked row during the user's `mouseClicked` handler — the canonical double-click-to-open idiom (R_layouts_close_enough — there is no server-side pixel→index map otherwise; `-1` outside a dispatch). R_best_effort_behaviour: a real browser double-click also fires intervening cc=1 events, matching AWT's escalation closely but not its exact 1-then-2 sequence. The same `addItemDoubleClickListener` + stash retrofit was **subsequently backported to SJTable** (`rowAtPoint` / `columnAtPoint` — see the SD_sjtable double-click note + D_jtable), so the two surrogates' mouse stories are now symmetric.

**No-Vaadin-counterpart knobs — asymmetric R_swing_is_truth-shadow split (not a surrogate shadow cache).** `layoutOrientation` (wrap modes), `visibleRowCount`, `fixedCellWidth/Height`, `selectionForeground/Background`, `dragEnabled` have no peer property (Grid virtualises / auto-sizes / theme owns chrome). Per R_vaadin_first the **surrogate drops them**: getters return JDK defaults, setters `SHelper.onUnimplemented`-and-drop (no shadow cache) — `getLayoutOrientation()` is always `VERTICAL`, wrap WARNs; `setPrototypeCellValue(non-null)` WARNs. JDK-faithful round-trip + PCE for these lives on the **emulator** layer (`vaadinx.swing.JList` field shadows, D_jlist), exactly the asymmetric pattern SJToolBar (SD_sjtoolbar/D_jtoolbar) and SJSplitPane (SD_sjsplitpane/D_jsplitpane) established. SJTable's `TableStateStore` originally parked analogous round-trip-only fields (`rowHeight`/`gridColor`/…) in the *surrogate* store (the laxer, earlier shape); it was **subsequently aligned to this same asymmetric split** — see the SD_sjtable R_vaadin_first-cleanup note. The JDK selection-mode int is likewise **not** shadowed: `getJdkSelectionMode()` reconstructs it from `selectionModel.getSelectionMode()` (the source of truth). **R_best_effort_behaviour:** `valueIsAdjusting` drag-coalescing collapses to a single final selection event (Vaadin fires discrete events; we can't observe the mid-drag stream). `dragEnabled(true)` is deferred to the drag-and-drop slice.

**Validation.** `SJListTest` (26 tests) — ctors (incl. non-mutable array/Vector model), selection-mode default/round-trip/IAE, model↔peer selection both directions + no-loop, `setSelectedIndices`/`getSelectedValuesList`/`setSelectedValue`, ListDataListener growth/shrink-with-selection-shift, `setModel` clears selection, single + double click bridge with `locationToIndex` resolution + stash clear, `setRenderer` column replace, no-counterpart knobs drop-on-surrogate + wrap WARN + IAE, `getUIClassID`.

<a id="SD_sjpopupmenu"></a>
## SD_sjpopupmenu. `SJPopupMenu` surrogate — `ContextMenu` peer, shared `MenuTreeBuilder` with `SJMenuBar`, `setComponentPopupMenu(SJPopupMenu)` as a `setTarget`-backed Swing-flavoured helper

**Decision.** The JPopupMenu slice — `JPopupMenu` plus the cross-cutting `setComponentPopupMenu` plumbing deferred earlier ([SD_api_three_buckets](#SD_api_three_buckets)). The surrogate is `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJPopupMenu extends com.vaadin.flow.component.contextmenu.ContextMenu implements JComponentMixin`. The same menu-item family (`JMenuItem` / `JMenu` / `JCheckBoxMenuItem` / `JRadioButtonMenuItem` / `JSeparator`) that [SD_sjmenubar](#SD_sjmenubar) keeps emulator-only stays emulator-only here too — `SJPopupMenu` ships **no** per-item surrogate classes, for the identical R_vaadin_first reason (Vaadin `MenuItem`'s ctor needs internal collaborators; pure-surrogate users compose with the inherited `ContextMenu.addItem(...)` directly).

**`SJPopupMenu` reuses SD_sjmenubar's tree-rebuild engine wholesale.** `JPopupMenu` is, structurally, a menu container exactly like `JMenuBar` — the only difference is the Vaadin peer it renders into (`ContextMenu` vs `MenuBar`). The emulator walks its JDK tree on every mutation and pushes a `List<MenuNode>` snapshot via `rebuildFromTree`; the surrogate tears down the prior Vaadin `MenuItem` tree + accelerator `Registration`s and rebuilds. Identical `MenuNode` descriptor, identical accelerator-via-`Shortcuts` install, identical `onAttach` re-install hook, identical re-entrancy guard.

**Shared `MenuTreeBuilder` (the refactor).** Vaadin `MenuBar`, `ContextMenu`, **and** `SubMenu` all implement `com.vaadin.flow.component.contextmenu.HasMenuItems` (`addItem(String|Component, listener) → MenuItem`, items return `getSubMenu() → SubMenu`). The byte-identical walk SD_sjmenubar inlined into `SJMenuBar` is extracted to `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.internal.MenuTreeBuilder`, keyed on `HasMenuItems` — recursion into sub-menus, `addItem` overload selection (icon → `addItem(Component, …)` with an icon+text `Span` per SD_sjlabel; else `addItem(String, …)`), per-item `setEnabled`/`setCheckable`/`setChecked`, leaf→peer map collection, accelerator install (`KeyConvert.toVaadinKeyBinding` → `Shortcuts.addShortcutListener` at UI scope), and the attach-time leaf-map recovery walk. Both `SJMenuBar` and `SJPopupMenu` route through it. `SJMenuBar` keeps its `MenuBarVariant.TERTIARY` theming + `getUIClassID="MenuBarUI"`; `SJPopupMenu` returns `"PopupMenuUI"`.

**The one asymmetry — top-level separators.** `addSeparator()` lives on `ContextMenuBase` / `SubMenuBase`, **not** on the `HasMenuItems` interface. So `MenuTreeBuilder` doesn't call it directly; the top-level separator is delegated to a per-caller callback. `SJMenuBar` passes a "`SHelper.onUnimplemented` + skip" callback (SD_sjmenubar's accepted gap — Vaadin `MenuBar` has no top-level `addSeparator`), but **`SJPopupMenu` passes `ctx::addSeparator` — so a top-level `JPopupMenu` separator renders cleanly**, a strict improvement over the menubar case. Sub-menu separators are uniform (`SubMenu.addSeparator()` always available) and live inside the builder.

**`setComponentPopupMenu(SJPopupMenu)` — a real `setTarget`-backed helper, not a drop.** The cross-cutting `JComponentMixin.setComponentPopupMenu` graduates from its SD_api_three_buckets conditional-WARN to a working Swing-flavoured helper whose **parameter type changes from the JDK `javax.swing.JPopupMenu` to the surrogate `SJPopupMenu`** (R_vaadin_first — the surrogate layer is Vaadin-shaped; a stage-3 user holds an `SJPopupMenu`, never a JDK one):

```java
default void setComponentPopupMenu(SJPopupMenu popup) {
    SJPopupMenu prev = ComponentPopupStore.of(_self()).popup;
    if (prev != null) prev.setTarget(null);
    ComponentPopupStore.of(_self()).popup = popup;
    if (popup != null) popup.setTarget(_self());   // the Vaadin binding behind the stored ref
}
default SJPopupMenu getComponentPopupMenu() { return ComponentPopupStore.of(_self()).popup; }
```

The stored reference passes R_vaadin_first's storage test because it is **backed by** `ContextMenu.setTarget` — it drives the live context-menu binding (like an installed `Action` or a `ButtonModel`), not a shadow cache whose only job is round-tripping its own getter. The getter's return-type split (`SJPopupMenu` surrogate-side vs `vaadinx.swing.JPopupMenu` emulator-side, per [D_jpopupmenu](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jpopupmenu)) is the same two-layer divergence `getIcon()` already exhibits (`javax.swing.Icon` vs Vaadin `Component`).

**The predicted "fan-out edit across every surrogate" dissolves.** Earlier design passes (SD_api_three_buckets included) flagged `setComponentPopupMenu` as a fan-out edit touching every existing surrogate. It isn't: `ContextMenu.setTarget(Component)` attaches to **any** peer uniformly, so the entire wire-up is a single `JComponentMixin` default method + a tiny `ComponentPopupStore`. No per-surrogate work. The emulator drives the binding by delegating through this default (`peer instanceof JComponentMixin m → m.setComponentPopupMenu(sjPopup)`), so both layers share one code path.

**Trigger semantics.** `JPopupMenu` is Swing's right-click context menu; `ContextMenu`'s default open trigger is right-click (long-press on touch) — they match, so no `setOpenOnClick`. The common migrated idiom — `comp.setComponentPopupMenu(popup)` then right-click — works natively end to end.

**`show(invoker, x, y)` / `setVisible(true)` — `setTarget` + WARN on programmatic open.** Vaadin `ContextMenu` has **no** server-side programmatic `open()` (confirmed against 25.1 — `ContextMenuBase` exposes `setTarget` / `setOpenOnClick` / `isOpened` but no open). So the emulator's `JPopupMenu.show(invoker, x, y)` calls `setTarget(invoker)` (lazily binding the menu so the *next* right-click on the invoker opens it) and `SHelper.onUnimplemented` for the immediate programmatic open-at-coordinates. R_layouts_close_enough position loss + R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (a) — graduates if Vaadin lands a programmatic open API.

**State holder: `ComponentPopupStore`.** One slot (`SJPopupMenu popup`), `ComponentUtil`-backed per the `MenuBarStateStore` pattern. `SJPopupMenu`'s own cross-rebuild bookkeeping (accelerator `Registration`s, `currentTree`, `preventRebuild`) reuses the existing `MenuBarStateStore` shape (renamed/shared as appropriate — both menu surrogates carry identical cross-rebuild state).

**Rationale.**

- **One engine, two peers.** The rebuild walk is the genuinely hard part of the menu surrogates (accelerator lifetime, attach-time recovery, icon+text composition, re-entrancy). Now that a second consumer exists, extracting it to `MenuTreeBuilder` is the R_infra_not_surface "implement the mechanism properly" call — a duplicate would drift. `HasMenuItems` is the natural seam Vaadin itself uses to unify menubar + context-menu + sub-menu construction.
- **`setComponentPopupMenu` is exactly the "Swing-flavoured helper where R_vaadin_first allows" the migration arc promises.** Stage-3 users get a terse Swing idiom (`comp.setComponentPopupMenu(popup)`) that compiles to the Vaadin-native `popup.setTarget(comp)` underneath — no JDK types leak into the surrogate API, and the binding is real.
- **Top-level separators land for free.** ContextMenu's `addSeparator()` removes SD_sjmenubar's menubar-only gap at this layer; surfacing it via the per-caller callback keeps the shared builder honest about where the JDK contract is and isn't met.

**Alternatives.**

- **Surrogate `setComponentPopupMenu` stays `onNoop` / keeps the WARN.** Considered (the original design-pass framing). Rejected on review: a stage-3 surrogate user calling it would expect attachment, and the Vaadin counterpart (`setTarget`) is right there. `onNoop` would be storage-free but functionality-free — the opposite of R_vaadin_first's "reproduce the UI functionality where Vaadin has a counterpart." The `setTarget`-backed helper is the correct R_vaadin_first outcome.
- **Take a JDK `javax.swing.JPopupMenu` on the surrogate.** Rejected — inverts the Vaadin-shaped surrogate API and gives a stage-3 user no way to build the argument without reaching into JDK Swing.
- **Duplicate the rebuild walk into `SJPopupMenu` (SD_sjdialog precedent).** Rejected for this slice: SD_sjdialog duplicated `SJFrame` scaffolding because the two window shapes genuinely diverge (modal park, window-listener surface); the menu walk is byte-identical across `HasMenuItems`, so duplication would be drift-prone copy-paste with no offsetting divergence.
- **Programmatic open via `executeJs` synthesizing a contextmenu event.** Rejected — brittle, position-guessing, and outside the CRUD/JLawyer baseline; the right-click-trigger path covers the real usage.

**Accepted limitations.**

- **No programmatic open-at-coordinates.** `show(x, y)` binds the target + WARNs; the menu opens on the next right-click, not synchronously at the requested pixel. R_layouts_close_enough + sub-bucket (a).
- **One live target per popup instance.** `setTarget` binds a single component; `setComponentPopupMenu` of the same `SJPopupMenu` on a second component re-targets it (the first loses its menu). Swing permits a shared `JPopupMenu` across invokers; under Vaadin the last target wins. Rare in practice; documented.
- **O(n) rebuild per mutation** — inherited from SD_sjmenubar; bounded by typical popup size (a handful of items).
- **Menubar mnemonics / menu-key navigation** — unchanged from SD_sjmenubar (sub-bucket (a)).

**Validation.** `SJPopupMenuTest` (17 tests) — `rebuildFromTree` round-trip (text / enabled / checkable / nested submenu / **top-level separator renders, no WARN** / icon item), empty+null clear, accelerator install + teardown across rebuilds + unmappable-keystroke WARN-and-skip, click fires `onClick`, `setTarget` bind/clear round-trip, `getUIClassID == "PopupMenuUI"`, JComponentMixin surface (name / client property), realistic-tree zero-WARN. The `setComponentPopupMenu(SJPopupMenu)` round-trip + re-target + null-clear (asserting `getTarget()` follows) lives in `JComponentMixinTest` on the representative `TestJSurrogate` (the prior conditional-WARN test rewritten to the new binding behaviour). `SJMenuBarTest` (existing ~25 tests) re-run unchanged as the `MenuTreeBuilder` extraction's regression net. Sampler `PopupMenusPanel` route + `PopupMenusWarnInventoryTest` (2 tests: WARN-free panel-swap user path + JPopupMenu API micro-driver with enumerated intentional-WARN paths) + `NavigationSmokeTest` entry.

<a id="SD_sjtree"></a>
## SD_sjtree. `SJTree` surrogate — `TreeGrid<Object>` peer (node-Object identity), custom lazy `HierarchicalDataProvider` live-reading the JDK `TreeModel`, `TreeSelectionModel` path-bridge via an identity parent map

**Status: implemented.** The left-pane case-folder hierarchy in the JLawyer-shape testbed ([`CaseTreeView`](../testapps/jlawyer-shape/swing/src/main/java/com/vaadin/swingbridge/testapps/jlawyershape/swing/CaseTreeView.java)) is the consuming shape. Emulator-side decision: [D_jtree](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jtree).

**Decision.** `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJTree extends com.vaadin.flow.component.treegrid.TreeGrid<Object> implements JComponentMixin, TreeModelListener, TreeSelectionListener` — the JDK `TreeModel`'s node objects are the Vaadin items, the installed `TreeModel` is the single source of truth (R_swing_is_truth), and a custom `HierarchicalDataProvider<Object, Void>` reads `model.getRoot()` / `getChild` / `getChildCount` / `isLeaf` **live and lazily** (children fetched only when a node expands) so `treeNodesChanged` is a `dataProvider.refreshItem(node)` with no snapshot rebuild. Mirrors SJTable ([SD_sjtable](#SD_sjtable)) / SJList ([SD_sjlist](#SD_sjlist)) structurally; it is the third Grid-family surrogate.

<a id="SD_sjtree_node_identity"></a>**SD_sjtree_node_identity — items are the `TreeModel`'s node `Object`s; identity via the node's own `equals`/`hashCode`; not the queue's `TreeGrid<TreeNode>` sketch, not an `Integer`-index dodge.** The queue sketched `TreeGrid<TreeNode>`; corrected to **`TreeGrid<Object>`** — JDK's `TreeModel` contract is node-as-`Object` (`getRoot(): Object`, `getChild(Object,int): Object`), and a custom `TreeModel` may use plain user objects as nodes rather than `TreeNode` instances. The SJTable/SJList `Integer`-index identity dodge **does not transfer**: a tree's data provider must navigate parent⇄child, so a flat index can't be the item. We therefore key the Vaadin items on the node objects directly and rely on node identity being unique — which holds for the overwhelmingly common `DefaultTreeModel` + `DefaultMutableTreeNode` case (the testbed case): `DefaultMutableTreeNode` does **not** override `equals`/`hashCode`, so it is identity-keyed. **Accepted R_best_effort_behaviour limitation:** a custom `TreeModel` returning value-`equals` nodes (e.g. the same `String` at two positions) would collide in Vaadin's key-mapper; documented, not defended — an identity-wrapper item type is the future refinement if a migration target hits it. This is the SJList "Grid, not VirtualList — and here's the cost we accept" shape applied to identity.

<a id="SD_sjtree_lazy_provider"></a>**SD_sjtree_lazy_provider — custom lazy `HierarchicalDataProvider`, not `setItems(roots, childProvider)`; identity parent map is legitimate machinery.** `TreeGrid.setItems(roots, childItemProvider)` eagerly walks the whole tree into a `TreeData` snapshot at set-time — that breaks both live-read (R_swing_is_truth) and the queue's mandated lazy expansion. Instead SJTree installs an `AbstractBackEndHierarchicalDataProvider<Object, Void>`:
- `fetchChildrenFromBackEnd(HierarchicalQuery)` → `query.getParent() == null ? roots() : childrenOf(parent)` as a `Stream`, reading `model.getChild` live;
- `getChildCount(HierarchicalQuery)` → `roots().size()` or `model.getChildCount(parent)`;
- `hasChildren(node)` → `!model.isLeaf(node)`.

`roots()` is `[model.getRoot()]` when `rootVisible` is true (JDK default) and `childrenOf(model.getRoot())` when false — so `setRootVisible(false)` is a data-provider-level reshaping (the synthetic root is hidden, its children become top-level), the only `rootVisible` behaviour we reproduce (R_best_effort_behaviour; the JDK's root-handle-on-top-level nuance is `setShowsRootHandles`, an emulator visual shadow per D_jtree). During every `fetchChildrenFromBackEnd` we record `parentMap.put(child, parent)` in an `IdentityHashMap<Object,Object>` held in `TreeStateStore`. This is **not** a shadow cache (R_vaadin_first): the provider stays on the default `HierarchyFormat.NESTED` and **overrides `getParent(item)` (read from the parent map) and `getItemIndex(item, query)` (→ `model.getIndexOfChild(parent, child)` — the JDK `TreeModel` has exactly this method)** — both defaults throw `UnsupportedOperationException` for a non-in-memory provider, and `TreeGrid.scrollToItem` requires both per its javadoc contract. The selection/path bridge (SD_sjtree_selection_bridge) additionally needs node→`TreePath` reconstruction, and the JDK `TreeModel` has no `getParent`, so the map is the only way to walk up. Vaadin's `TreeData` keeps exactly this parent pointer; we mirror it over the JDK model.

<a id="SD_sjtree_is_model_listener"></a>**SD_sjtree_is_model_listener — `SJTree IS-A TreeModelListener` (source of truth), event→refresh mapping.** The surrogate subscribes itself to the installed `TreeModel` on install/swap (JDK JTree's UI-delegate pattern; **no surrogate-level `add/removeTreeModelListener` convenience** — users go through `tree.getModel().addTreeModelListener(l)` directly, exactly SD_sjtable_is_model_listener). Mapping, all guarded by `preventPeerEvents`:
- `treeNodesChanged(e)` → `dataProvider.refreshItem(node)` per changed child (cell repaint, structure intact);
- `treeNodesInserted` / `treeNodesRemoved(e)` → `dataProvider.refreshItem(e.getTreePath().getLastPathComponent(), /*refreshChildren*/ true)` (subtree re-fetch) + prune/repopulate the affected `parentMap` entries;
- `treeStructureChanged(e)` → `dataProvider.refreshAll()` + clear the relevant `parentMap` subtree.

<a id="SD_sjtree_selection_bridge"></a>**SD_sjtree_selection_bridge — `TreeSelectionModel` ⇄ `TreeGrid` selection bridge; path reconstruction via the parent map.** JDK `TreeSelectionModel` is **path-set**-based; Vaadin Grid selection is **item-set**-based. `SJTree IS-A TreeSelectionListener` and subscribes to the installed `TreeSelectionModel`. JDK→peer: `valueChanged(TreeSelectionEvent)` pushes — `deselectAll()` then `select(path.getLastPathComponent())` for each selected `TreePath`. peer→JDK: a Vaadin `addSelectionListener` wrapped in `SHelper.callSwing` (R_callswing_envelope) reads `getSelectedItems()`, reconstructs each selected node's `TreePath` by walking `parentMap` to the root and reversing, and writes through `selectionModel.setSelectionPaths(...)` under `preventPeerEvents`. Mode mapping: `SINGLE_TREE_SELECTION` → Grid `SINGLE`; `CONTIGUOUS_`/`DISCONTIGUOUS_TREE_SELECTION` → Grid `MULTI` with the `DefaultTreeSelectionModel` enforcing the JDK contract (browser multi-select past a contiguous model silently reconciles — the SJList interval-mode divergence, R_best_effort_behaviour). Mode changes are observed via the model's `"selectionMode"` PCE (`DefaultTreeSelectionModel` fires it from `setSelectionMode`) — JDK code idiomatically calls `tree.getSelectionModel().setSelectionMode(...)`; there is no `JTree.setSelectionMode` for the surrogate to intercept, so the model is watched directly and the watcher moves across `setSelectionModel` swaps. The Vaadin selection listener re-installs on every selection-mode re-map (Grid re-creates its selection backing — SD_sjtable_selection_bridge/SD_sjlist). `getSelectionModel()` **does clash here, exactly as on SJTable**: `TreeGrid extends Grid`, so SJTree inherits `Grid.getSelectionModel(): GridSelectionModel<T>` (verified against vaadin-grid-flow 25.1.6), and a JDK-shaped `getSelectionModel(): TreeSelectionModel` would be an incompatible-return-type override — compile error. The SD_sjtable dodge applies: the surrogate exposes `getTreeSelectionModel()` / `setTreeSelectionModel(TreeSelectionModel)`, and the JDK-shaped `getSelectionModel()` lives on the emulator (D_jtree_two_layer_resource), which isn't a Grid and reads straight through.

<a id="SD_sjtree_expansion"></a>**SD_sjtree_expansion — expansion bridge; vetoable `TreeWillExpandListener` deferred (sub-bucket a).** TreeGrid's native `expand(items)` / `collapse(items)` / `isExpanded` / `addExpandListener` / `addCollapseListener` back the JDK `expandPath` / `collapsePath` / `isExpanded(TreePath)` / `addTreeExpansionListener` surface. peer→JDK expansion events fire `TreeExpansionEvent` through `SHelper.callSwing` (R_callswing_envelope). **`addTreeWillExpandListener` + `ExpandVetoException` drop-and-WARN — D_gap_severity_triage sub-bucket (a) blocked-upstream:** Vaadin has no pre-expansion vetoable hook; the listener stores but never fires, documented in javadoc. (Types reuse from the JDK — see the port note below.)

<a id="SD_sjtree_hierarchy_render"></a>**SD_sjtree_hierarchy_render — Vaadin-first hierarchy-column render via a function-level swap seam; ported `TreeCellRenderer` lives only on the emulator.** Exactly SD_sjtable_vaadin_rendering/SD_sjlist's split, with one TreeGrid-specific wrinkle: **the expand/collapse toggle lives in the renderer, not the column** — `addComponentHierarchyColumn` wraps its provider in `HierarchyColumnComponentRenderer`, whose template emits the `<vaadin-grid-tree-toggle>` + `.level` indentation + expand-on-click wiring, so a later `Column.setRenderer(plain ComponentRenderer)` would silently drop that chrome. The swap seam is therefore **function-level, not renderer-level**: the surrogate installs **one** `addComponentHierarchyColumn(node -> componentProvider.apply(node))` where `componentProvider: Function<Object, com.vaadin.flow.component.Component>` lives in `TreeStateStore` and defaults to a fresh-per-call `Span(textOf(node))` (the D_jcombobox DOM-adoption guard — fresh per call); the emulator swaps the function once (D_jtree_renderer_registry) and the Vaadin renderer is never re-installed. The JDK `TreeCellRenderer` surface (`getTreeCellRendererComponent` returns `java.awt.Component`, the D_event_port_policy return-type clash) lives **only** on the emulator (D_jtree): `:surrogates` cannot reference `vaadinx.swing.tree.*` (dependency arrow is `:emulators` → `:surrogates`). The hierarchy column is the single column — JTree is single-column — and `HierarchyColumnComponentRenderer` owns the expand/collapse toggle + indentation chrome inside it.

<a id="SD_sjtree_row_api"></a>**SD_sjtree_row_api — row-based API over the visible-node walk (R_layouts_close_enough); pixel `getRowForLocation` via the click stash.** JTree's row API (`getRowCount`, `getPathForRow`, `getRowForPath`, `setSelectionRow`, `expandRow`) addresses the *flattened visible* (expanded) order.
