SJTree computes that order by a DFS from `roots()` descending only into nodes where `isExpanded(node)` — `getRowCount()` is the walk length, `getPathForRow(i)` the i-th node's reconstructed path, `getRowForPath(p)` the index (-1 if an ancestor is collapsed). The pixel form `getRowForLocation(x,y)` has no server-side pixel→row map (R_layouts_close_enough): the item-click bridge stashes the clicked node + its visible row index in `TreeStateStore` (a **field**, surviving the UI-fiber boundary — SD_sjlist's stash rationale) so the value is correct during the user's `mouseClicked`/`mousePressed` handler — exactly `CaseTreeView.selectOnRightClick`'s `getRowForLocation(ev.getX(), ev.getY())` idiom; `-1` outside a dispatch. Mouse + popup-trigger: `addMouseListener(SMouseListener)` overrides the inherited mixin wire to dispatch `MOUSE_PRESSED`/`MOUSE_RELEASED`/`MOUSE_CLICKED` from Grid's item-click (carrying `getButton()` so right-click → `isPopupTrigger()`), the SJList double-click-bridge shape; the press/release pair is best-effort R_best_effort_behaviour (Grid reports a click, not the raw press/release stream).

**Port surface — only the renderer family ports; the rest reuse-from-JDK per D_event_port_policy (corrects the queue's "ported `TreeModel`" sketch).** Walking the `javax.swing.tree.*` + tree-event surface against D_whitelist_porting/D_event_port_policy (port only Component-hierarchy classes, interfaces referencing `Component`, and AWT events referencing `Component`):
- **Port** into `vaadinx.swing.tree.*`: `TreeCellRenderer` (returns `java.awt.Component` → clash) and `DefaultTreeCellRenderer` (is-a `JLabel` → extends `vaadinx.swing.JLabel`). That's it for live functionality. `TreeCellEditor` ports as a deferred stub (in-cell editing is D_gap_severity_triage sub-bucket (b), the JTable cell-editor precedent).
- **Reuse from the JDK unchanged** (no `Component` reference anywhere in their public surface): `TreeModel`, `TreeNode`, `MutableTreeNode`, `DefaultMutableTreeNode`, `DefaultTreeModel`, `TreePath`, `TreeSelectionModel`, `DefaultTreeSelectionModel`, `RowMapper`, and the entire event family `TreeModelEvent` / `TreeModelListener` / `TreeSelectionEvent` / `TreeSelectionListener` / `TreeExpansionEvent` / `TreeExpansionListener` / `TreeWillExpandListener` / `ExpandVetoException` (`TreeExpansionEvent`'s ctor takes `Object source`, so it accepts the emulator `JTree` without a port). The queue's "ported tree-model surface (`TreeModel`, …)" was a sketch; D_event_port_policy governs and the port is far smaller than JTable's (which was forced to port `TableColumnModelEvent` because the JDK ctor demanded a `javax.swing.table.TableColumnModel` source — no analogue here).

**State holder.** `TreeStateStore` (under `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.internal`, the `TableStateStore`/`ListStateStore` pattern) holds: `model` (TreeModel), `selectionModel` (TreeSelectionModel), `dataProvider` ref, `parentMap` (IdentityHashMap), `componentProvider` (the SD_sjtree_hierarchy_render hierarchy-column render seam), `rootVisible`, `preventPeerEvents`, `clickNodeStash` + `clickRowStash`, `vaadinSelectionRegistration`, expand/collapse `Registration`s. Only R_vaadin_first-legitimate state (models, the navigation map, behaviour gates, machinery) — no round-trip shadow caches; those live on the emulator (D_jtree_row_path_bridge).

**Alternatives considered.**
- **`TreeGrid<TreeNode>` (the queue sketch).** Rejected — over-narrows the JDK `TreeModel` node-as-`Object` contract; a custom model using plain user objects wouldn't compile against it.
- **`Integer`-row-index identity (the SJTable/SJList dodge).** Rejected — a hierarchical data provider must navigate parent⇄child; a flat visible-row index is unstable under expand/collapse and can't be the data-provider item.
- **`setItems(roots, childProvider)` eager `TreeData` snapshot.** Rejected — eager full-tree walk breaks live-read (R_swing_is_truth) and lazy expansion; the custom `HierarchicalDataProvider` keeps the JDK model as the sole source of truth.
- **An identity-wrapper item type to fully dodge value-`equals` collisions.** Deferred (R_infra_not_surface — unrequested surface): `DefaultMutableTreeNode` is identity-keyed already, covering the testbed + the common case; revisit only if a migration target ships a value-`equals` `TreeModel`.

**Validation.** `SJTreeTest` (36) — ctor + `setModel` install/swap (incl. listener-subscription move + selection clear); lazy fetch (a counting `TreeModel` proves collapsed nodes never fetch) via Karibu's TreeGrid helpers in `GridKt` (`_getRootItems` / `_rowSequence` / `_clickItem` / `_select`); `treeNodesChanged`/`Inserted`/`Removed`/`structureChanged` → correct `DataRefreshEvent`/`refreshAll` mapping + parent-map upkeep; data-provider `getParent`/`getItemIndex` overrides (the `scrollToItem` contract, incl. the hidden-root null-parent case); selection both directions + no-loop + path reconstruction through `parentMap`; mode default→MULTI / `SINGLE_TREE_SELECTION`→SINGLE re-map observed through the model PCE, bridge surviving the swap; `rootVisible` true/false root-level reshaping; expansion bridge fires `TreeExpansionEvent` (+ per-registration teardown); the isExpanded-requires-expanded-ancestors JDK contract; willExpand drop-and-WARN; hierarchy-column default render (fresh `Span` per call) + the SD_sjtree_hierarchy_render function-swap seam keeping the Vaadin renderer instance; visible-row walk (`getRowCount`/`getPathForRow`/`getRowForPath`) across collapsed subtrees and under hidden root; press/release/click mouse bridge with right-click `BUTTON3`/`isPopupTrigger` + `getRowForLocation` stash resolution + clear; `getUIClassID == "TreeUI"`.

---

<a id="SD_sjcolorchooser"></a>
## SD_sjcolorchooser. `SJColorChooser` surrogate — `html.Input(type="color")` peer, `ColorSelectionModel` source-of-truth, alpha dropped

**Decision.** `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJColorChooser extends com.vaadin.flow.component.html.Input implements JComponentMixin` (emulator side in [D_jcolorchooser](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jcolorchooser)). Vaadin 25 ships no color-picker component, so the peer re-purposes the browser-native `<input type="color">` primitive via Vaadin's public `Input` — which is already an `AbstractSinglePropertyField<Input, String>` bound to the `value` property (a `#rrggbb` hex). The ctor calls `setType("color")`. Sub-decisions:

- <a id="SD_colorchooser_input_peer"></a>**SD_colorchooser_input_peer — why `Input`, not a raw `@Tag("input")` re-host.** `Input` is public and does the `value`-property binding + `value-changed` subscription for us, so extending it is strictly less work than the [VaadinRadioButton](../surrogates/src/main/java/com/vaadin/swingbridge/surrogates/VaadinRadioButton.java) raw re-host — that re-host exists only because Vaadin's own `RadioButton` wrapper is package-private. Here nothing forces a re-host.
- <a id="SD_colorchooser_model_truth"></a>**SD_colorchooser_model_truth — `ColorSelectionModel` is the source of truth (R_swing_is_truth), SJSlider shape.** The installed `ColorSelectionModel` (JDK `DefaultColorSelectionModel` reused per D_event_port_policy — no `java.awt.Component` ref) drives everything. One model `ChangeListener` fans out to user ChangeListeners (`source=this`) and pushes the selected color (as hex) to the peer under `preventPeerEvents` (R_swing_is_truth); the peer's `ValueChangeListener` mirrors browser edits back through `SHelper.callSwing` (R_callswing_envelope) into `model.setSelectedColor`. Unlike display-only [SJProgressBar](../surrogates/src/main/java/com/vaadin/swingbridge/surrogates/SJProgressBar.java), the color input has a live browser edit path, so both the guard and the callSwing wrap are needed — the [SJSlider](../surrogates/src/main/java/com/vaadin/swingbridge/surrogates/SJSlider.java)/[SD_sjslider](#SD_sjslider) template verbatim.
- <a id="SD_colorchooser_hex_bridge"></a>**SD_colorchooser_hex_bridge — hex bridge helpers.** `SHelper.toHexColor(Color)` → `#rrggbb` and `SHelper.colorFromHex(String)` → `Color` (handles `#rgb` shorthand, null on unparseable) sit beside the existing `toCss`/`colorFromCss` (which speak `rgb()`/`rgba()`, the wrong dialect for `<input type="color">`).
- <a id="SD_colorchooser_panels_dropped"></a>**SD_colorchooser_panels_dropped — preview panel + chooser panels R_vaadin_first drop-and-WARN.** `setPreviewPanel` and the `setChooserPanels`/`addChooserPanel`/`removeChooserPanel` family are pure custom paint (D_gap_severity_triage sub-bucket (b)) — the native input *is* the chooser UI. They field-round-trip (so getters are honest) but WARN. `setDragEnabled` field-round-trips silently (benign preference, not a dropped capability).

**Rationale.** Extending `Input` gives browser-native color picking with the exact `HasValue.ValueChangeListener` sync shape the fat-surrogate model pattern already uses, at near-zero cost. The model-as-source-of-truth keeps `getColor`/`setColor`/ChangeListener JDK-faithful while the peer stays Vaadin-first (R_vaadin_first).

**Alternatives considered.**
- **Raw `@Tag("input") AbstractSinglePropertyField<…, String>` re-host** (the VaadinRadioButton shape) — rejected; duplicates what public `Input` already provides for no gain (SD_colorchooser_input_peer).
- **Shadow-cache the full `Color` (incl. alpha) so the getter round-trips exactly** — rejected per R_vaadin_first's no-fabricated-`Store` rule; the model already holds the last programmatically-set color, and the peer legitimately can't represent alpha.

**Accepted limitation.** **Alpha dropped** — `<input type="color">` is opaque `#rrggbb`. A model color with `alpha<255` pushes opaque RGB to the peer; a browser round-trip then reads back opaque. The model retains the set alpha until a browser edit overwrites it. Accepted R_best_effort_behaviour/R_vaadin_first divergence (same lossiness class as SD_border_css_lossy borders).

**Validation.** `SJColorChooserTest` (19) — ctor variants seed the model; `setColor` (+ int overloads) writes the model and pushes `#rrggbb` to the peer `value`; ChangeEvent `source=surrogate`; browser value-change writes the model + fires once (no feedback loop) via the `preventPeerEvents` guard; unparseable peer hex ignored; `setSelectionModel` swap re-subscribes + fires PCE + same-instance no-op; translucent color pushes opaque hex; `setDragEnabled` silent round-trip; `setPreviewPanel`/`setChooserPanels` WARN-but-round-trip; `getUIClassID == "ColorChooserUI"`. Emulator `JColorChooserTest` (14) + Sampler `ColorChoosersWarnInventoryTest` (2) green.

---

<a id="SD_sjwindow"></a>
## SD_sjwindow. `SJWindow` surrogate — undecorated top-level on `SWindow`; `RootPaneScaffold` composition dedup; window geometry + undecorated chrome land on `SWindow`

**Decision.** `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJWindow extends SWindow` (emulator side in [D_jwindow](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jwindow)). JDK shape is `JWindow extends java.awt.Window implements RootPaneContainer` — no title, no chrome, no close gestures, no `defaultCloseOperation` (JWindow doesn't implement `WindowConstants`), no menubar — so the surrogate extends `SWindow` directly, **not** `SFrame`, whose title / close-X / close-op state a JWindow must not carry. Typical shapes served: splash screens (centered, progress-driven, auto-dispose) and toasts (explicit corner placement, timer dismissal). Sub-decisions:

- <a id="SD_sjwindow_ctor_defaults"></a>**SD_sjwindow_ctor_defaults — ctor defaults are the entire JWindow delta over SWindow.** Undecorated chrome always on (SD_undecorated_chrome); `ModalityMode.MODELESS` (a splash/toast must never block the page — Vaadin Dialog defaults modal); `setCloseOnEsc(false)` + `setCloseOnOutsideClick(false)` (no chrome → no user close gesture; programmatic `setVisible(false)` / `dispose()` are the only close paths, so no peer-originated `WINDOW_CLOSING` ever fires on a JWindow); draggable / resizable left at Vaadin Dialog's `false` defaults (no title bar to drag, no grips — deliberately unlike `SFrame`'s ctor seeding).
- <a id="SD_rootpane_scaffold"></a>**SD_rootpane_scaffold — `RootPaneScaffold` composition, not a base-class pull-up.** [SD_sjdialog](#SD_sjdialog) anticipated pulling the contentPane / rootPane scaffolding "up" once a third Dialog-backed surrogate landed; SJWindow fired that trigger. Resolved by **composition**: package-private `RootPaneScaffold` (ctor takes the host `SWindow` + a `createRootPane` supplier so the subclass hook still dispatches virtually) owns the lazy contentPane `Div`, lazy `SJRootPane` + the content-pane↔root-pane mirror invariant, the SD_sjmenubar/D_menu_tree menubar slot, and the `rootPaneCheckingEnabled` recursion-guard idiom — single-copy; `SJFrame` / `SJDialog` / `SJWindow` each keep one-line delegating overrides. A literal pull-up into `SWindow` was rejected: it would surface `getContentPane()` / content-pane add-routing on *bare* `SWindow` / `SFrame`, whose direct-add contract is documented JDK shape (`java.awt.Frame#add` has no content-pane indirection — that's JFrame's, post-Java-5). The scaffold's menubar slot stays dormant on SJWindow (JWindow has no `setJMenuBar`); a dormant field beats a third mirror-copy of everything else.
- <a id="SD_swindow_geometry"></a>**SD_swindow_geometry — window geometry lands on `SWindow` (all window surrogates benefit), mapping 1:1 onto Dialog top/left + HasSize.** `setLocation(x, y)` → `setLeft(x+"px")` / `setTop(y+"px")` (takes the overlay out of its default centered placement); `setSize(w, h)` → `setWidth` / `setHeight`; `setBounds` = both; `centerOnScreen()` clears top/left back to the centered default. `setLocationRelativeTo(anything)` maps to `centerOnScreen()` — the browser has no screen geometry to compute component-relative placement from, and viewport-center is the close-enough answer for the "center over my main window" idiom (R_layouts_close_enough, accepted; was WARN). Window *placement* is not layout-manager work, so R_layouts_close_enough's pixel-layout exclusion doesn't apply; getters are Vaadin's own `getTop` / `getLeft` / `getWidth` / `getHeight` per R_vaadin_first (setter writes the Vaadin property, getter reads it back).
- <a id="SD_undecorated_chrome"></a>**SD_undecorated_chrome — undecorated chrome via the `emul-undecorated` overlay class + static `@StyleSheet` CSS.** `SWindow.setUndecoratedChrome(boolean)` toggles the class (Vaadin Dialog syncs class names onto `vaadin-dialog-overlay`); `META-INF/resources/emul/swindow.css` zeroes `::part(content)` padding + `::part(overlay)` border-radius and hides `::part(header)` (title + SFrame's close-X chrome). The **box-shadow is deliberately kept** — in a browser the window floats over live page content, not a desktop wallpaper, and a shadowless white rect is illegible. Served as a plain `<link>` stylesheet from `META-INF/resources` (SJTabbedPane precedent — never enters the Vite bundle, no dev-bundle rebuild). `SFrame.setUndecorated(true)` retires its WARN and drives the same class; unlike JDK, the surrogate setter doesn't throw while displayable (the CSS class toggles live) — the JDK displayable-time throw lives on the emulator layer where the JDK contract is the API surface (D_setundecorated_real).

**Rationale.**

- **`SWindow` base, not `SFrame`.** The WindowMixin→SWindow class flip left `SWindow` as a concrete Dialog-backed window with the full peer↔window sync but no Frame state — exactly JWindow's JDK profile. Extending SFrame (SD_sframe's original sketch for a JWindow backfill) would drag in title + close-X + the header chrome that SD_undecorated_chrome then has to hide, plus `getTitle()` on a window that JDK says has none.
- **Composition matches the house rule and the JDK sibling-shape argument simultaneously.** SD_sjdialog already paid ~250 lines of mirror-copy once to keep JDialog a JDK-faithful sibling; a third copy crosses the line SD_sjdialog itself drew, while a base-class pull-up breaks the very sibling-shape argument SD_sjdialog defended. The scaffold threading between them keeps both properties.

**Alternatives considered.**

- **`SJWindow extends SFrame`** (SD_sframe's sketch) — rejected; inherits title / close-op / close-X state JDK JWindow doesn't have, and `sjWindow instanceof SFrame` leaks the misshape.
- **Flag-gated machinery on `SWindow`** (literal pull-up with an "is a RootPaneContainer" gate) — rejected; least per-class code but `getContentPane()` and the add-routing become visible (inert) on bare SWindow / SFrame — a surface-shape lie relative to `java.awt.Window` / `Frame`.
- **Third mirror-copy on SJWindow** — rejected; SD_sjdialog's stated dedup threshold ("the next-step refactor *if* a third lands") would be ignored at exactly the moment it fires.
- **Shared-owner-frame emulation for the no-arg ctor** — rejected on the emulator side (see D_no_shared_owner_frame); nothing surrogate-side either.

**Accepted limitations.**

- **Box-shadow on an "undecorated" window** — documented R_layouts_close_enough divergence (SD_undecorated_chrome).
- **~~No viewport-relative anchoring.~~** Resolved by [D_toolkit_screen_size](../emulators/decisions.md#D_toolkit_screen_size): `Toolkit.getScreenSize()` now reports the browser viewport (`window.innerWidth/Height`, live-resize-tracked), so the classic `screen.width - w - 20` edge-anchoring math lands on the visible page — no stage-3 rewrite needed. Residual: an already-shown window doesn't re-anchor when the user later resizes the browser (matches Swing, where a desktop-resolution change doesn't move windows either).
- **Two-layer listener fan-out** — same SD_sjslider/SD_sjspinner convention as every window surrogate: a listener registered on both `emulator.JWindow` and its `SJWindow` peer fires once per layer.

**Validation.** `SJWindowTest` (18) — ctor defaults ×4, owner chain, content-pane routing + swap + null rejection + mirror invariant, geometry ×5 (location / size / bounds / recenter ×2), lifecycle OPENED/CLOSED smoke, WARN-free splash flow. `SFrameTest` updated (`setUndecorated` now asserts the class toggle, WARN-free). Emulator-side `JWindowTest` (19) + Sampler `WindowsWarnInventoryTest` (2) per D_jwindow.

---

<a id="SD_glasspane_structural"></a>
## SD_glasspane_structural. Structural glass pane in `RootPaneScaffold`; `SJRootPane` delegates; layered pane stays a holder

**Decision.** Surrogate side of [D_glasspane_structural](../emulators/decisions.md#D_glasspane_structural). The structural glass pane lives in `RootPaneScaffold` — the shared engine already owning the content-pane and menubar Dialog-child slots for `SJFrame` / `SJDialog` / `SJWindow` — so all three hosts get it in one place.

- **`RootPaneScaffold` owns a glass-pane slot.** `getGlassPane()` lazy-creates an invisible `Div`; `setGlassPane(Component)` attaches it as a Dialog-direct child (via `host.add` under a cleared `rootPaneCheckingEnabled` flag, exactly like the content-pane / menubar slots so the host's add-override falls through to Dialog's native add), tags the element with `emul-glasspane`, and adds `emul-has-glasspane` to the host so `::part(content)` becomes the positioned ancestor the absolute pane insets against. Swapping detaches + untags the previous pane. Null throws `IllegalComponentStateException` per JDK.
- **`SJRootPane` delegates when hosted.** `RootPaneScaffold.getRootPane()` hands the pane a back-reference (`_attachScaffold`); with it non-null, `SJRootPane.get/setGlassPane` route into the scaffold's structural slot. Standalone (`new SJRootPane()`, unlinked) keeps the SD_sjframe non-structural holder fallback.
- **CSS** in `emul/swindow.css` (the existing SWindow-family `@StyleSheet`, per the `@StyleSheet`-over-`@CssImport` convention): `.emul-glasspane { position:absolute; inset:0; z-index:1000; pointer-events:auto }` + `vaadin-dialog-overlay.emul-has-glasspane::part(content){position:relative}`. Visibility is the pane's own `setVisible` (`display:none` when hidden), so the default invisible pane neither paints nor blocks.
- **Layered pane stays a holder** on `SJRootPane` (unchanged from SD_sjframe) — see D_glasspane_structural for why.

**Rationale.**
- **Scaffold, not `SJRootPane`, owns the slot** because the DOM host is the Dialog (the scaffold's `host`), and the scaffold already runs the checking-flag bootstrap for its sibling slots. Putting glass-pane attach anywhere else would need a fresh host back-reference and duplicate the flag idiom. `SJRootPane` keeps the JDK-shaped API and forwards.
- **Unconditional detach on swap** (unlike the content-pane / menubar swaps, which guard `removeFromParent` on `isAttached`): `Dialog.add` sets the element parent immediately, so a glass-pane swap performed *before* the window is shown must still unplant the old pane and drop its class — otherwise both render on open and the stale class lingers. The class tag makes correctness observable in a way the untagged content/menubar swaps didn't need.
- **`z-index:1000`** clears any in-content positioned descendant: the content pane is a static `Div` and forms no stacking context, so its children's z-indexes compete in the glass pane's context.

**Alternatives.**
- **Give `SJRootPane` a host back-ref and own the slot itself.** Rejected — duplicates the scaffold's checking-flag bootstrap; the scaffold is the natural owner (D_glasspane_structural option 2a).
- **Structural layered pane too.** Rejected — see D_glasspane_structural (content-pane reparenting cost, no consumer).
- **Second `@StyleSheet` file for the glass pane.** Rejected — it's a SWindow-family concern; folding it into `emul/swindow.css` avoids a second `<link>` and keeps the window CSS in one place.

**Accepted limitations.** Inherit D_glasspane_structural's: mouse-block only (no Tab-focus trap, matching Swing), no overlay painting / event redispatch, standalone panes non-structural, layered pane unchanged.

**Validation.** `SJRootPaneTest` (+3) — hosted structural attach + class wiring + host/rootPane report the same pane, swap re-tags + unplants the old, standalone stays a holder; existing standalone holder tests unchanged. Full `:surrogates` suite green; emulator + Sampler coverage per D_glasspane_structural.

<a id="SD_sjinternalframe"></a>
## SD_sjinternalframe. `SJInternalFrame` surrogate — decorated non-modal top-level on `SWindow`; `InternalFrameListener` family; maximize geometry; minimize is emulator-only

`SJInternalFrame extends SWindow` — the inverse of [SJWindow](#SD_sjwindow): chrome stays *on*. It is the peer for `:emulators` `JInternalFrame` ([D_internal_frames](../emulators/decisions.md#D_internal_frames)).

**Decision.**
- <a id="SD_internalframe_chrome"></a>**SD_internalframe_chrome — ctor delta over `SWindow` = keep the Dialog chrome, stay modeless.** `setHeaderTitle(title)` (mirrors the frame `title`); a **title-bar control group** (minimize / maximize / close icons in the Dialog header, the `BasicInternalFrameTitlePane` analog) each shown/hidden by the matching `iconifiable` / `maximizable` / `closable` flag (default close-only; the emulator flips them from the `JInternalFrame` ctor booleans) — close routes to the internal-frame close seam (SD_internalframe_fire_seams), minimize / maximize route to emulator-supplied `Runnable` handlers so a header click honours the vetoable `icon` / `maximum` properties (a pure-surrogate user with no handler falls back to hiding the overlay / toggling `setMaximum`); `setDraggable(true)` + `setResizable(true)` (title-bar drag + resize grips *are* `JInternalFrame`'s drag/`resizable` affordances — Vaadin gives them free), `setModality(MODELESS)` (an internal frame never blocks the page), `setCloseOnEsc(false)` + `setCloseOnOutsideClick(false)` (JInternalFrame closes only via its close-X, like Swing). This is `SFrame`'s chrome subset called directly on the Dialog base — without inheriting `SFrame`'s Frame-only state.
- <a id="SD_internalframe_fire_seams"></a>**SD_internalframe_fire_seams — `SInternalFrameListener` family via three protected fire seams on `SWindow`.** New ported `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.swing.event.SInternalFrameEvent` / `SInternalFrameListener` / `SInternalFrameAdapter` (server-side-fire-only, SD_listeners_without_analog shape, parallel to the Window listener family). To reuse `SWindow`'s lifecycle without duplicating the `everShown` latch / `preventPeerEvents` guard (both private), `SWindow`'s three fire points are extracted into protected overridable methods — `fireOpenedEvent()` (called from `setVisible` under the first-show latch), `fireClosingEvent()` (called from `onPeerOpenedChanged` on a user close), `fireClosedEvent()` (called from `dispose`), each defaulting to the existing `fireWindowEvent(...)` body (zero behaviour change for `SFrame`/`SJDialog`/`SJWindow`). `SJInternalFrame` overrides all three to fire the `INTERNAL_FRAME_*` equivalents, and routes its close-X to `fireClosingEvent()` so both close paths converge.
- <a id="SD_internalframe_activation"></a>**SD_internalframe_activation — selection drives ACTIVATED / DEACTIVATED.** `setSelected(true/false)` stores the flag, best-effort focuses the overlay, and fires `INTERNAL_FRAME_ACTIVATED` / `DEACTIVATED`. Programmatic front-raise is Vaadin overlay order (R_layouts_close_enough) — no `toFront` guarantee.
- <a id="SD_internalframe_maximize"></a>**SD_internalframe_maximize — maximize via `SWindow` geometry (SD_swindow_geometry).** `setMaximum(true)` → top/left `0` + `100%`/`100%`; `setMaximum(false)` → `centerOnScreen()`. No listener event (bound property only).
- <a id="SD_scaffold_fourth_consumer"></a>**SD_scaffold_fourth_consumer — `RootPaneScaffold` gains a fourth consumer.** `JInternalFrame` is a `RootPaneContainer`; contentPane / rootPane / glassPane bookkeeping is the shared scaffold ([SD_rootpane_scaffold](#SD_sjwindow)) — one-line delegating overrides. The menubar slot stays dormant (JInternalFrame has no `setJMenuBar`), like SJWindow.
- <a id="SD_no_surrogate_iconify"></a>**SD_no_surrogate_iconify — no iconify on the surrogate.** The surrogate has no `setIcon` surface at all — the full iconify *state machine* (state, vetoable change, events, `getDesktopIcon`, overlay hide/restore via the emulator driving `SWindow.setVisible`) lives on the emulator per D_internalframe_minimize. There is therefore no surrogate WARN site; the un-renderable desktop-icon restore visual is a documented divergence, not an `onUnimplemented` call (WARNing would trip the exit gate). `:surrogates` stays off the blocking-dialog machinery — nothing blocks (SD_sframe).

**Rationale.**
- **`SWindow` base, not `SFrame`.** Extending `SFrame` would drag in `defaultCloseOperation`, `iconImages`, static `getFrames()`, window-state that `JInternalFrame` doesn't have. `SWindow` is the bare Dialog-backed window with the peer↔window sync; the small chrome subset SJInternalFrame does want is a handful of direct Dialog calls. Same call SJWindow made.
- **Protected fire seams beat re-plumbing.** The Window↔InternalFrame event mapping is 1:1; the only obstacle to reuse was that the fire calls were inlined against private latch/guard state. Three thin protected methods expose exactly the override points, no behaviour change for existing subclasses.

**Alternatives.**
- **Second `OpenedChangeListener` on the peer in SJInternalFrame.** Rejected — can't see `SWindow.preventPeerEvents` (private), so a programmatic `setVisible(false)` would spuriously fire CLOSING. The seam approach respects the guard.
- **Duplicate the SWindow lifecycle into SJInternalFrame.** Rejected — re-copies the `everShown` / `preventPeerEvents` machinery; the seams are ~6 lines total.

**Accepted limitations.** No desktop-icon visual (SD_no_surrogate_iconify); viewport-relative placement + overlay-order z-stacking (R_layouts_close_enough); box-shadow on the overlay (inherited SD_undecorated_chrome). Inherits SWindow's focus/always-on-top gaps (R_infra_not_surface).

**Validation.** `SJInternalFrameTest` — decorated non-modal Dialog (draggable/resizable/header title, modeless), `InternalFrameListener` fires (opened on first show, closing on close-X, closed on dispose, activated/deactivated on select), maximize geometry, contentPane routing. Emulator + Sampler coverage per D_internal_frames.

<a id="SD_sjseparator"></a>
## SD_sjseparator. `SJSeparator` surrogate — `Div` peer with orientation-driven hairline CSS; no listener surface

`SJSeparator extends Div implements JComponentMixin` — the standalone visual divider, same `Div` peer SJPanel / SJToolBar use. Peer for `:emulators` `JSeparator` ([D_jseparator](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jseparator)).

**Decision.** A separator carries **no R_vaadin_first UI functionality** — no clicks, model, value, or event fan-out — so it is *not* one of the "no surrogate by design" set: those (SJOptionPane, JDesktopPane, Preferences, printing) are emulator-only because a VT-park / JDK-SPI / PDF-pipeline obstacle blocks a clean is-a Vaadin component, and a plain divider has no such obstacle. It follows the D_surrogate_first surrogate-first default like every other component; the surrogate is deliberately thin. One property, `orientation` (HORIZONTAL default), drives `applyOrientationCss()`: `background var(--lumo-contrast-20pct)` + `align-self:stretch` always, plus `min-width:1px` (VERTICAL) / `min-height:1px` (HORIZONTAL) on the main axis. `setOrientation` validates per R_match_swing_errors (`IllegalArgumentException` matches JDK) and fires `"orientation"` PCE on actual change. Because there is no listener surface, there is no `Registrations` holder and no Vaadin subscription to track (R_vaadin_first's user-listener rule is vacuous here). Menu separators are drawn inline by the menu tree (SD_sjmenubar) and `SJToolBar.addSeparator`'s bar by the toolbar itself (SD_sjtoolbar), so neither routes through `SJSeparator`; the emulator's `JToolBar.Separator` is a `JSeparator` and does ([D_jtoolbar](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jtoolbar)).

**Rationale.**
- **`Div`, not Vaadin `Hr`.** `Hr` is `<hr>` — a horizontal rule with no vertical variant, so a VERTICAL separator would need a different element and the orientation flip couldn't stay a single-element CSS change. A `Div` with `align-self:stretch` handles both orientations symmetrically (same reasoning SJToolBar took for its direction-agnostic `Div`).
- **Cross-axis fill needs a flex parent.** `align-self:stretch` fills the cross axis in flex layouts (BoxLayout, FlowLayout, BorderLayout regions). A GridBagLayout cell not told to fill may render the bar at its 1px intrinsic size — an accepted R_layouts_close_enough *cosmetic* gap (the divider is in the right place, just not stretched), not a structural one.

**Accepted limitations.** GridBag non-fill cosmetic gap (above); `getAccessibleContext` deferred WARN + null (surrogate-wide stance).

**Validation.** `SJSeparatorTest` — ctors + orientation validation, orientation CSS (both orientations + flip clears the opposite key), `"orientation"` PCE (fires once on change, no-op on equal), L&F surface. Emulator + Sampler coverage per D_jseparator.

<a id="SD_sjtogglebutton_button_peer"></a>
## SD_sjtogglebutton_button_peer. `SJToggleButton` repurposed onto a `Button` peer (pressed chrome); `SJCheckBox` becomes a standalone Checkbox-peer surrogate

`SJToggleButton extends Button implements AbstractButtonMixin` — a bare JToggleButton now renders as a stays-pressed button (its real Swing look) instead of a checkbox glyph, and gains icon + real layout for free. Peer for `:emulators` `JToggleButton`; **not** inherited by JCheckBox / JRadioButton, which keep their glyph peers.

**Decision.** Closes the standing "dedicated JToggleButton peer" item (was an optional item in TODO.md). [SD_toggle_checkbox_first_cut](#SD_toggle_checkbox_first_cut) originally peered SJToggleButton over Vaadin `Checkbox` (the only stock toggleable) and made `SJCheckBox extends SJToggleButton` — an R_best_effort_behaviour-accepted "JToggleButton looks like a checkbox" divergence. SD_toggle_checkbox_first_cut itself parked the fix as a future `SJToggleButton2 extends Button`. This is that fix, done as a repurpose rather than a new class so the emulator↔surrogate name correspondence stays intact (`:emulators.JToggleButton` → `SJToggleButton`, like every other pair):

- **`SJToggleButton extends Button implements AbstractButtonMixin`** (was `extends Checkbox`). Button is a `ClickNotifier` (not `HasValue<Boolean>`), which flips the browser wire and the selection-display mechanism vs. the Checkbox siblings — see below.
- **`SJCheckBox extends Checkbox implements AbstractButtonMixin`** (was `extends SJToggleButton`). Now standalone, carrying the Checkbox-peer toggle surface directly. This is exactly the parallel-not-shared duplication [SD_sjpasswordfield](#SD_sjpasswordfield) accepted and `SJRadioButton` already lives with: Vaadin `Checkbox`, `Button`, and our `VaadinRadioButton` share no ancestor below `Component`, so a toggle over each needs its own peer-bound wiring. `SJCheckBox` and `SJRadioButton` are now near-identical Checkbox/HasValue-peer toggles — a shared mixin for that wiring is a possible later cleanup, out of scope here.

**Override surface** (differences from the mixin default `installButtonBindings`, which is tuned for a Button peer already):
- **`installButtonBindings`** — the *only* change from the mixin default is installing `JToggleButton.ToggleButtonModel` (toggle-on-release) instead of `DefaultButtonModel` (momentary). The `ClickNotifier` wire and the inline-flex CSS seed are kept verbatim — Button is a `ClickNotifier` and honors host flex. (Contrast the Checkbox siblings, which must swap to a `ValueChangeListener` and skip the CSS seed.)
- **`setModel`** — adds a model→peer push `ItemListener` that mirrors `model.isSelected()` onto the `aria-pressed` host attribute (correct a11y for a toggle button; `emul/sjtogglebutton.css` paints the depressed look off it). **No `preventPeerEvents` guard** — writing an attribute can't echo back as a `ClickEvent`, so the feedback loop the Checkbox siblings guard against doesn't exist here.
- **`setText`** — SJButton shape (`super.setText` + `"text"` PCE); Button is `HasText`.
- **Layout setters + `setIcon`** — *not* overridden. Inherited straight from the mixin: alignment / text-position / iconTextGap bind to host inline-flex CSS, and `setIcon` hits the Vaadin-first Button branch. Bare JToggleButton thus gains real layout and icon rendering the Checkbox-glyph version drop-and-WARNed.

**Emulator side is unchanged in wiring** — the ctor peers on `SJToggleButton`, and every peer-aware path (the model hand-over in `setModel`, `setText`) dispatches on `AbstractButtonMixin` / `HasText` / `HasLabel` per [D_jradiobutton](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jradiobutton), so the Button peer slots straight in. `JToggleButton.setText`'s `HasLabel` branch now serves only the glyph subclasses (SJCheckBox / SJRadioButton peers are `HasLabel`-not-`HasText`); the Button peer is rendered by `super.setText`'s `HasText` branch and skips it. Only comments changed there.

**Rationale.**
- **Repurpose over new class.** A new `SJPushToggleButton` would have left `SJToggleButton` (Checkbox) as a zombie base whose only role is SJCheckBox's parent — a class named after a component it no longer peers for, breaking the one-emulator-one-surrogate naming the rest of the module holds. Repurposing keeps names honest; the cost is making SJCheckBox standalone, which is the SD_sjpasswordfield pattern already in the tree.
- **`aria-pressed` + CSS over a Vaadin theme variant.** No stock Vaadin "toggle/pressed" button variant exists; `aria-pressed` is both the correct accessibility state and a stable style hook only SJToggleButton sets, so nothing leaks onto plain SJButtons. The inset shadow is an ordinary host declaration; the fill is set through `--vaadin-button-background`, the property Aura and Lumo both paint the button from, because a direct `background` declaration loses to Aura's own `background` rule — same specificity, loaded later — and the selected toggle looked unselected on crud until the fill moved onto the property. Exact "sunken bevel" fidelity is close-enough per R_layouts_close_enough.
- **No shadow selection field.** Selection lives in the `ToggleButtonModel` (R_vaadin_first UI-functional state); the attribute is a projection of it, written on every Item transition — no separate `selected` cache.

**Alternatives.**
- **Keep the Checkbox peer, add a new Button-backed sibling.** Rejected — zombie-named base, per Rationale.
- **Wait for a Vaadin toggle-button component.** Rejected — none exists upstream; the Button + aria-pressed path is small and complete.
- **Share the Checkbox-peer toggle wiring between SJCheckBox and SJRadioButton via a new mixin.** Deferred — a real cleanup (they're near-identical now) but orthogonal to this change; left as noted scope for later.

**Accepted limitations.**
- **Pressed appearance is close-enough (R_layouts_close_enough), not a pixel-faithful Swing bevel.** The inset shadow + contrast fill read unmistakably as "pressed"; a later pixel-accurate pass can tighten it.
- **`getText() == ""` on a default-constructed SJToggleButton** — R_vaadin_first lossy direction, same as SJButton (Button's textNode is `""` when unset).
- **Two-layer event fan-out** — a listener on both `:emulators.JToggleButton` and its peer fires twice; register on one layer (unchanged convention).

**Validation.** `SJToggleButtonTest` rewritten for the Button peer: peer is a Button not a Checkbox, ToggleButtonModel default, setSelected (Item + Change, no Action) reflected onto `aria-pressed`, doClick full Item+Change+Action pulse, browser click via `_click` drives the same sequence, setModel push-wire swap, setText via Button text + null→"", layout binds (LEFT→LEADING readback, no WARN) with R_match_swing_errors IAE on bad axis, `setIcon` ImageIcon installs a Vaadin Image with no WARN, happy-path zero-WARN. `SJCheckBoxTest` updated — asserts SJCheckBox is-a Checkbox and no-longer-a SJToggleButton; the Checkbox-peer toggle wiring + borderPaintedFlat drop-and-WARN carry over. Emulator `JCheckBoxTest` / `JRadioButtonTest` green (browser-click bridge casts widened to `AbstractButtonMixin`). Sampler `ButtonsWarnInventoryTest` green — the "Bold" toggle is now located by `Button.class` + text and driven by click.

<a id="SD_sjeditorpane_rte"></a>
## SD_sjeditorpane_rte. Editable `JEditorPane` via `RichTextEditor` — `SJEditorPane`, Shape S (always-RTE, same module)

**Status: implemented.** `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJEditorPane` is the single `JEditorPane` peer; the `Div`-backed `SJEditorPane` (SD_sjeditorpane_div) is deleted. This entry is the full record — the decision, the four-scenario license experiment, and the rejected alternatives. Supersedes SD_sjeditorpane_div's "editable is a future slice if a real target needs it" — the target is the alpha's real-Swing-app coverage, and Vaadin's HTML-default `RichTextEditor` (v24+) makes it cheap.

**Decision.** A new `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SJEditorPane extends com.vaadin.flow.component.richtexteditor.RichTextEditor implements JComponentMixin` lands in `:surrogates` (**same module** — Shape S, not an opt-in module) and becomes `JEditorPane`'s **single, unconditional** peer. HTML round-trip via RTE's `setValue`/`getValue` (HTML is the Flow default — no `asDelta()`); `setReadOnly` ← `!editable`. The `JEditorPane` emulator's ctor peers over `SJEditorPane` unconditionally (the D_peer_ctor_injection protected-ctor seam D_jeditorpane already keeps); RTE is read-only-capable via `setReadOnly(true)`, so the one peer covers both `editable=true` and `editable=false` — including a form field that **toggles view↔edit at runtime** (`setReadOnly` flips freely, no peer change) — and the `editable`-is-mutable-post-ctor wrinkle (SD_sjeditorpane_div / D_jeditorpane) dissolves. `:surrogates` gains a hard dependency on the commercial `com.vaadin:vaadin-rich-text-editor-flow` artifact.

**The read-only `SJEditorPane` (Div peer, SD_sjeditorpane_div) is deleted.** With RTE as the single peer, the Div-backed read-only surrogate has no caller. It is removed outright (not renamed to `SJEditorPaneReadOnly` — dead code with a new name is still dead code; git preserves it, and it is re-derivable from scratch if a faithful static-HTML display peer is ever wanted). SD_sjeditorpane_div is superseded by this entry.

**Why same-module is safe (the license experiment).** RTE is a commercial Vaadin component; the fear was that shipping it in `:surrogates` would tax every migrator. Disassembly + real networked builds (keys renamed away) settled it:

- **The RTE Java ctor is license-free** — no `LicenseChecker` in the jar (disassembled `vaadin-rich-text-editor-flow-25.2.1.jar`); `new RichTextEditor()` never throws on a missing key. (This is *why* the tempting "catch the ctor, fall back to read-only" strategy is impossible — there's no runtime signal.)
- **Classpath presence alone triggers no license check at app *startup*.** RTE registers no `META-INF/services` `VaadinServiceInitListener`, so nothing runs a license check when the app boots. Flow's `BaseLicenseCheckerServiceInitListener` fires only for products that ship their own registered subclass; RTE 25.2.1 does not. The check is build/render-time and usage-gated (keyed off RTE's `@NpmPackage("@vaadin/rich-text-editor")`), never classpath-gated.
- **Production licensing is usage-gated, not classpath-gated.** Production bundles only components actually referenced in code. An *unused* RTE is excluded from the bundle even in a forced from-scratch `npm install` + Vite build (`forceProductionBuild=true`) — verified `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, no license, no commercial banner, RTE absent from the generated flow-imports. So a migrator who never renders an editable editor needs **no license in production**.
- **The only same-module cost is the dev-bundle tax**: dev mode's full scanner wants every classpath `@NpmPackage` in the dev bundle, so RTE forces a one-time `npm install` + dev-bundle rebuild on first dev run for everyone (no license — dev handles it; a rendered RTE without a license degrades to a `Component: vaadin-rich-text-editor 25.2.0` placeholder, no crash).

**Rejected alternatives.**

- **Keep both peers, dispatch Div (read-only) vs. RTE (editable) by editable-intent.** Tempting because the Div renders *any* HTML faithfully (tables, arbitrary CSS) while RTE only renders its Quill subset — so read-only display would keep full fidelity and stay free-tier. Rejected on four grounds: **(1) `editable` is mutable, and no swap is viable** — peer choice is fixed once the Vaadin component is live (a runtime swap would have to backport every applied setting — text, contentType, listeners, border, size — *and* re-splice the element into its parent in the DOM; fragile, silent-failure-prone). **(2) A locked peer breaks the view↔edit form toggle** — a field that starts read-only (Div) can never become editable. **(3) Deferring the choice to first peer-access (lazy-materialize-once) creates an order-dependent surprise** — `setEditable(false); add()` yields a Div but `add(); setEditable(false)` yields RTE: near-identical code, vastly different fidelity/behavior, no visible cause. **(4) The Div's faithful `innerHTML` is also an XSS vector** for user-supplied HTML (SD_sjeditorpane_div punts sanitization to the caller); RTE's subset *is* a sanitizer. Always-RTE trades peak read-only fidelity for a single, predictable, immediately-visible limitation and a working edit toggle — the honest choice for a migration bridge (R_best_effort_behaviour/R_layouts_close_enough). Editing has no alternative anyway (RTE is the only editable-HTML component in Vaadin), so splitting peers optimizes one branch at the cost of coherence across both.

  **Re-examined against measured prevalence and re-rejected.** A field survey of 27 open-source Swing apps ([D_html_read_only_first](../emulators/decisions.md#D_html_read_only_first), 2026-08-17) found the read-only display case this alternative would optimize is **~91% of real `javax.swing.text.html` usage** (13 of 18 apps author nothing), and that the corpus's two most-used APIs are `StyleSheet.addRule` (49 files) and `addHyperlinkListener` (34) — the first structurally impossible on RTE, the second drop-and-WARN here. That is a materially stronger case than the one weighed above, and it still loses: a migrating customer's reason to move to the web includes *editing* rich text, which a `Div` cannot do at all, so a viewer peer would optimize the majority of *files* while failing the actual requirement. Objections (1)–(3) are unaffected by prevalence — they are about peer *selection*, not fidelity — and routing around them via a framework-level selector (the R_leaf_peer_lockdown / [D_frame_strategy](../emulators/decisions.md#D_frame_strategy) carve-out) buys a second peer, a second sanitizer policy, and a `setEditable(true)`-on-a-viewer failure mode for a limitation migrators can see immediately. **Both most-used APIs are reachable on RTE without a second peer** via per-instance shadow-root style injection (`:host([theme~=…]) .ql-editor h2 {…}`) and delegated anchor clicks on the same seam — both now implemented on that seam ([SD_add_css_rule](#SD_add_css_rule) / [SD_hyperlink_listener](#SD_hyperlink_listener)), which removes the last reason to reconsider a viewer peer for them. **Revisit trigger:** a customer making a compelling case for faithful static-HTML display (tables/arbitrary CSS) that is *not* satisfiable by a stage-3 drop to a Vaadin `Html`/`Div` in that one view. Absent that, the `Div` peer stays deleted.
- **Shape M — opt-in `:emulators-richtext` module + `ServiceLoader` `EditableEditorProvider` SPI** (the [D_printing](../emulators/decisions.md#D_printing) precedent). Keeps RTE off non-adopters' classpath entirely → no dev-bundle tax, no commercial artifact in the core tree. Rejected as not worth the module + SPI plumbing once the experiment showed the only avoided cost is the (minor, one-time, cached) dev-bundle rebuild — the production-license concern that would have justified it doesn't exist.
- **Ctor try/catch fallback to read-only.** Impossible — the ctor doesn't throw on a missing license (see above).
- **A second `JEditorPane` class in the opt-in module.** Breaks the single import-swap target — there can be only one `vaadinx.swing.JEditorPane`.

**Accepted costs / limitations.**

- **Dev-bundle tax on all migrators** (one-time `npm install` on first dev run) and **a commercial `vaadin-rich-text-editor-flow` dependency in the core tree** (license-free to download and to run unless an editor is rendered). Both accepted per the "free emulators; pay for RTE editing" framing.
- **Lossy HTML subset (R_vaadin_first) — now in *display* too, not just editing.** RTE accepts only `p, br, h1–h6, strong/em/u/s, a, ol/ul/li` (no nesting), `blockquote, pre, img` (base64 data-URLs only — no web-URL images); `<b>`→`<strong>`, unknown tags→`<p>`. **No tables, no arbitrary CSS/colored spans.** Because RTE is now the only peer, this hits read-only *display* as well: a `JEditorPane` showing a rich HTML report loses its tables — a genuine regression from the deleted Div peer, accepted as consistent + immediately visible (R_best_effort_behaviour/R_layouts_close_enough). A migrator needing faithful static HTML display drops to a Vaadin `Html`/`Div` in that one view (stage-3). This is the main cost weighed-and-accepted against the *keep-both-with-dispatch* alternative above — and the 2026-08-17 prevalence survey identifies it as the **most-hit** limitation of the whole component (report/help viewers are the dominant real-world use), so it warrants migrator-facing prominence, not just a WARN at the call site.
- **Security upside (not a cost).** RTE's subset normalization *is* a sanitizer — safer for user-supplied HTML than the deleted Div peer, which wrote raw `innerHTML` (an XSS vector; SD_sjeditorpane_div punted sanitization to the caller).
- ~~**`HyperlinkListener` drops-and-WARNs**~~ — **no longer a limitation; implemented per [SD_hyperlink_listener](#SD_hyperlink_listener) below.** The original reasoning ("in an editor a link click positions the caret rather than navigating") turned out to be an argument *for* the feature rather than against it, since that is Swing's own behaviour and not a divergence to work around.
- **A `readOnly` RTE reads as a document viewer as-is — no CSS shim is owed** (measured in Firefox on a `setEditable(false)` Sampler pane, 2026-08-17). `setReadOnly(true)` already hides the toolbar (`[part~=toolbar]` → `display: none`, height 0, vs. 34px when editable) and nothing inflates the host to editor size (`min-height: 0`; the host sizes to content). The residue is stock RTE appearance and squarely R_layouts_close_enough cosmetic: a 1px `rgba(0,0,0,.2)` host border (a Swing about-box in a `JScrollPane` has one anyway) and Quill's tight list indent (`.ql-editor li` is `list-style-type: none` + `padding-left: 6px`, so bullets are a `::before` glyph with no hanging indent). Recorded because the fear it retires — "every migrated about-box ships with editor chrome" — is the first objection the always-RTE decision attracts; if a migrator ever does complain about the list indent, [SD_add_css_rule](#SD_add_css_rule)'s injection seam fixes it in one rule.
- **`StyledDocument` / `AttributeSet` surface stays OOS** — this is HTML editing, not styled-run editing; the `JTextPane` follow-on (below) is where that surface lands as R_match_swing_errors(c) WARN.

**Implementation notes.** RTE is a `HasValue<String>` of HTML but **not** a `TextFieldBase`, so `JTextComponent`'s built-in Document↔peer sync (which narrows to `TextField`/`PasswordField`/`TextArea`) skips it — the `JEditorPane` emulator wires both directions itself: `setText` pushes markup into `htmlValue` under `preventPeerEvents`; the peer's `addValueChangeListener` (EAGER, per R_swing_is_truth's "tracks typing") mirrors browser edits back into the Document under the same guard, wrapped in `EHelper.callSwing` (R_callswing_envelope), so `getText()` reflects edits and user DocumentListeners fire. `setEditable` is likewise forwarded from the emulator to the surrogate's `setReadOnly` explicitly (the base only propagates to `TextFieldBase` peers). The `text` field is **not** shadowed on the surrogate (R_vaadin_first Vaadin-first): `getText()` reads `getValue()` back — lossy per the HTML subset — while the emulator's own Document keeps the faithful copy. Validation: `SJEditorPaneTest` (16) + `JEditorPaneTest` (18) + `EditorPanesWarnInventoryTest` (Sampler user-path + API driver).

<a id="SD_hyperlink_listener"></a>
### SD_hyperlink_listener. `HyperlinkListener` — `ACTIVATED` via a shadow-root delegated click, read-only panes only

**Status: implemented.** Supersedes SD_sjeditorpane_rte's accepted-cost bullet above, which had `addHyperlinkListener` drop-and-WARN on both layers. Reopened by the 2026-08-17 prevalence survey ([D_html_read_only_first](../emulators/decisions.md#D_html_read_only_first)), which ranks `addHyperlinkListener` the corpus's **#3 API at 34 of 127 files** — the read-only majority's second-most-used call, and the cheapest of its gaps to close (no new classes, and it *removes* a WARN rather than adding surface).

**Decision.** A delegated `click` listener is installed **into the RTE's shadow root** on first `addHyperlinkListener`. It finds the anchor via `composedPath()`, `preventDefault`s, and dispatches a `emul-hyperlink` CustomEvent carrying the raw `href`; the surrogate's Flow DOM listener (`addEventData("event.detail.href")`) fans out `HyperlinkEvent(ACTIVATED)` through `SHelper.callSwing`. The emulator bridges that into its own `listenerList` with **`source` rebound to the emulator** (the `JComboBox` bridge shape), so JDK-shaped user code casting `e.getSource()` to `JEditorPane` works. `fireHyperlinkUpdate` is public on the emulator, matching the JDK, where it doubles as a user-callable synthesizer.

**The original objection dissolves rather than being worked around.** SD_sjeditorpane_rte declined the feature partly because "in an editor a link click positions the caret rather than navigating." That is *also* what Swing does: `HTMLEditorKit.LinkController` activates a link only when the pane is non-editable. So delivery is gated on `!isEditable()` — client-side to decide whether to suppress the browser's navigation, and again server-side to decide whether the Swing event is owed. What looked like a blocker was the specification.

**Three mechanism facts that shaped the implementation** (all verified in a live browser; none reachable from Karibu):

1. **The listener cannot sit on the host element.** A click on an anchor inside the RTE's shadow DOM *retargets*: a host-level listener sees `e.target` as `<vaadin-rich-text-editor>` and `closest('a[href]')` returns null — the obvious spelling is a silent no-op. `SJTabbedPane`'s tab-header click guard is **not** a transferable precedent: its targets are slotted light-DOM children, which never retarget.
2. **`preventDefault` is mandatory, not hygiene.** Quill renders anchors as `target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`, so without it a link both opens a browser tab *and* fires the Swing event — double-acting where Swing would only fire.
3. **The hook is re-installed on every attach, not once.** Vaadin re-renders a `@PreserveOnRefresh` tree after F5 but does not replay `executeJs` (the same property that motivates `VaadinUtils.injectStyleOnce`), so a once-only install leaves links dead after a reload.

**Installation is lazy** — on the first `addHyperlinkListener`, on both layers. The hook suppresses native navigation, so a pane nobody listens to must keep the browser's own behaviour; eager wiring on the emulator side would have defeated the surrogate's laziness. Residue, accepted: once a listener has been added, links stop navigating natively even if every listener is later removed. That direction matches Swing (a link click never navigates on its own), so the hook is left installed.

**Event contents match what Swing's `LinkController` builds.** `getDescription()` is the raw `href` as authored; `getURL()` is that href resolved — relative links resolve against `getPage()`, the nearest thing available to `HTMLDocument.getBase()` (which is itself an unported gap, 11 corpus files). An href that forms no URL (a bare fragment, a relative path with no page loaded) yields a `null` URL rather than dropping the event, as in Swing. The source `Element` is always `null` — there is no `HTMLDocument` element model to point at.

**Scope.** `ACTIVATED` only. `ENTERED` / `EXITED` hover events are not wired: the JDK guarantees no delivery cadence for them and nothing in the surveyed corpus reads them.

**Hard cap discovered in the browser: only `http` / `https` / `mailto` links are clickable at all.** Flow sanitizes the pushed value with a jsoup `Safelist` whose protocol allowlist for `a[href]` is ftp/http/https/mailto. Every other href — **relative** (`topics.html`), root-relative (`/help/x.html`), `file:`, `jar:`, or a bare fragment — keeps its `<a>` element but **loses the href attribute**, and an hrefless anchor is no longer a link to the editor, so it renders as plain text and can never be clicked. Consequences worth being blunt about:

- A classpath or filesystem help set that links **relatively** — the most natural way to write one, and the shape a migrated help viewer arrives with — loses its navigation entirely. Not degraded: gone, and silently, since nothing WARNs.
- Absolutizing relative hrefs against the page at load time (the obvious fix, and one this project nearly built) **would not help**: the resulting `file:`/`jar:` URL is stripped by the same allowlist. Verified before implementing.
- The workaround, which Sampler's `HtmlViewerPanel` demonstrates because it is what real migrated code must do, is to link by `http(s)` URL and map those URLs onto local resources inside the listener. That is not a hack so much as Swing's own model — the listener has always been what decides a link's meaning.
- SD_hyperlink_listener's relative-href resolution against `getPage()` therefore matters less in practice than it appears: it is correct, and it is exercised by tests, but a browser can only deliver hrefs that survived the allowlist.

**How this was found is the point.** The Karibu gate passed while the feature was broken in the browser: the test fires the `emul-hyperlink` DOM event directly, so it never depended on an anchor existing. Only clicking a real link in a real browser revealed that the anchors were gone. Pinned now by a test asserting exactly which protocols survive `setText`, so a Flow change to the safelist surfaces as a test failure rather than as a migrator's dead help viewer.

**Validation.** `SJEditorPaneTest` + `JEditorPaneTest` drive the `emul-hyperlink` DOM event directly (everything server-side of the browser hook: fan-out, the editable gate, href resolution, listener teardown, lazy installation, `source` rebinding). `EditorPanesWarnInventoryTest` fires the same seam on the Sampler route and asserts the demo's status label took the event — a WARN gate alone would pass if the listener silently never fired. The client half is browser-verified: a real click on Sampler's Demo 1 link fired `ACTIVATED` with the expected description and URL, updated the label by push, and opened no tab.

<a id="SD_add_css_rule"></a>
### SD_add_css_rule. `addCssRule` — author CSS into the RTE shadow root, selectors rewritten onto the content element

**Status: implemented.** The peer half of [D_htmleditorkit](../emulators/decisions.md#D_htmleditorkit) (`HTMLEditorKit`'s `StyleSheet.addRule`, the corpus's most-used HTML API at 49 of 127 files). Also retires SD_sjeditorpane_rte's "no arbitrary CSS" claim *for appearance* — see the boundary below, which is narrower than it first looks.

**Decision.** `SJEditorPane.addCssRule(String)` accepts author CSS — one rule or a whole sheet — and injects it as a `<style data-emul="sjeditorpane-css">` into **this instance's own shadow root**, rewriting each selector to reach RTE's content. `getCssRules()` returns what was applied. Selector rewriting is `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.internal.RteCssRules`, kept as pure static logic precisely because the injection around it is untestable under Karibu — the translation is where the behaviour is pinned down.

**Why `::part()` cannot do this, and why the idea doc's `theme` scoping turned out unnecessary.** RTE renders content as real `<h2>`/`<p>`/`<a>` elements inside its shadow root, and `::part(content) h2` does not reach them — `::part()` styles the part element, never its shadow descendants. Hence injection. The idea doc's technique note then proposed scoping each instance with a `theme` attribute and a `:host([theme~="emul-pane-N"])` prefix; **that is not needed for runtime injection** (and was dropped): a stylesheet inside an instance's own shadow root is already confined to that instance — verified in-browser, with a sibling pane provably untouched. The `theme` prefix earns its keep only in the static `themeFor` delivery shape, where one stylesheet is shared across instances. Not adding it also avoids clobbering Vaadin's own `theme` attribute.

**Two rewrites, both forced by where the content lives.**
- Every selector is confined under `.ql-editor`, RTE's content element — otherwise a rule like `button { … }` would style the editor toolbar as well as the document.
- `body` / `html` map onto the content element **itself**, not to a descendant. This matters more than it sounds: `body { font-family: … }` is the single most common rule in the surveyed corpus, and `.ql-editor body` would match nothing. A `body`-prefixed compound (`body p`, `body > p`) absorbs the prefix and keeps the rest.

**At-rules are skipped whole and WARN, with the rest of the sheet still applied.** Two parsing details are load-bearing, and both were found by tests rather than reasoning: brace-depth tracking, or `@media screen { p { … } }` would be split at its inner `}` and inject a broken fragment; and statement at-rules (`@import …;`) must be consumed at their semicolon, since scanning ahead to the next `{` swallows the *following* rule's selector and silently loses a rule we could have applied.

**Re-pushed on attach, not injected once** — same reasoning as [SD_hyperlink_listener](#SD_hyperlink_listener)'s hook, and the same reason `VaadinUtils.injectStyleOnce` prefers a tree-attached `<style>`: Vaadin re-renders a `@PreserveOnRefresh` tree after F5 but does not replay `executeJs`. The script rewrites the `<style>` element's `textContent` wholesale rather than appending per rule, so one code path serves an incremental `addCssRule` and a full re-push after re-attach. **Verified in-browser: the stylesheet survives an F5.**

**Where the SD_sjeditorpane_rte "no arbitrary CSS" limitation still stands.** Appearance is now reachable; **structure is not, and no stylesheet can change that.** Non-whitelisted tags — tables above all — are dropped as the value enters Quill's model, before any rule could apply. So the accepted-costs bullet above is unchanged in the part that actually hurts (a report loses its tables); what it no longer covers is styling the elements that *do* survive.

**Validation.** `RteCssRulesTest` (17 cases: tag/class/id selectors, `body`/`html` mapping incl. compounds and case, selector lists, multi-rule input, at-rule skipping in both block and statement forms, malformed and empty input) + `SJEditorPaneTest` (accumulation, translation, at-rule WARN, pre-attach hold, defensive copy). Browser-verified end-to-end via Sampler Demo 1: `h2` colour, `body`→content font and `li` colour all took effect, a sibling pane stayed untouched, and the sheet survived a page reload.

<a id="SD_setpage_audit"></a>
### SD_setpage_audit. `setPage` audit — charset from `Content-Type`, `MalformedURLException` on a bad string, always-refetch; `setBase` stays out

**Status: implemented.** Slice 3 of the read-only-first HTML plan, scoped as an audit of the existing `setPage` path (12 corpus files) rather than a build. It found two real defects, both silent, and settled one gap.

**Defect 1 — the charset was read from the wrong header.** The code took its charset from `URLConnection.getContentEncoding()`, which returns the **`Content-Encoding`** header — the *compression* (`gzip`, `deflate`), unrelated to the charset, and **null on an ordinary response even when `Content-Type` declares one**. Probe-confirmed against a live `HttpServer`: a page served as `text/html; charset=ISO-8859-1` had `getContentEncoding() == null`, fell through to the UTF-8 default, and rendered mangled (`café` → `caf?`). Every non-UTF-8 page was silently corrupted. Now `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.internal.HtmlCharset` resolves it browser-style: the `Content-Type` charset parameter, then a `<meta charset>` / `<meta http-equiv>` declaration in the first 4 KB, then UTF-8. The sniff decodes as ISO-8859-1 because it is byte-preserving and cannot throw on arbitrary bytes; an unrecognised charset name falls through to the next source instead of failing.

**Defect 2 — `setPage(String)` threw the wrong exception type (R_match_swing_errors).** The surrogate parsed via `URI.create(url).toURL()`, which raises an **unchecked `IllegalArgumentException`** for malformed input, where the JDK goes through `new URL(String)` and throws **`MalformedURLException`** — an `IOException`, which is exactly what the method signature invites migrated code to catch. So a migrator's `catch (IOException)` sailed past our exception and the app died on a bad URL. Now wrapped in `MalformedURLException` with the original as cause.

**Two honest WARNs added.** A non-HTML media type (the pane renders it as HTML regardless) and a URL fragment (`#section`), since there is no counterpart to the JDK's `scrollToReference` — the page loads, just not scrolled.

**Deliberate divergence, kept: every `setPage` re-fetches.** The JDK skips the load when the URL equals the one already displayed, and the documented way to force a reload is clearing the *document's* stream-description property — a document-model hook that does not exist here (see the base question below). Copying the skip without the escape hatch would leave a "reload this page" action permanently dead, so re-fetching is the better best-effort reading (R_best_effort_behaviour): the ordinary case and the reload case both work. Faithfulness to a JDK behaviour is not worth reproducing when the accompanying escape hatch cannot be. This stands for stage-3 code only: the emulator owns the page on its document and runs the JDK's skip with its escape hatch ([D_setpage_fetch](../emulators/decisions.md#D_setpage_fetch)).

**`HTMLDocument.setBase` stays out of scope, and the reason is worth stating precisely** (11 corpus files, so this is the plan's remaining named gap). **Superseded in part by [D_htmldocument](../emulators/decisions.md#D_htmldocument):** the pane now installs a real `HTMLDocument` for `text/html`, so the cast succeeds and `setBase` / `getBase` round-trip. Finding 3 below was the blocker and it dissolved — the element model is a *projection* of the markup, with `getText()` overridden to keep returning markup, so nothing about `getText` changes. Findings 1 and 2 stand unchanged: an explicit base still redirects nothing, because links resolve against `getPage()` and relative images cannot load at any base. Three findings, taken together:

1. **The dominant case already works without it.** `setPage(url)` establishes the base implicitly, and [SD_hyperlink_listener](#SD_hyperlink_listener) resolves relative hrefs against `getPage()`. So relative links in a fetched page behave correctly today.
2. **For images it is moot.** Relative `<img src>` cannot work at any base, because RTE accepts only base64 data-URL images — a web-URL image is dropped as the value enters Quill, base or no base (SD_sjeditorpane_rte).
3. **The remaining case — an explicit base different from the loaded page — is not reachable, and cannot cheaply be made so.** Migrated code spells it `((HTMLDocument) pane.getDocument()).setBase(url)`, which compiles (the JDK types stay JDK) and throws `ClassCastException` at runtime, since `getDocument()` returns a `PlainDocument`. Making the cast succeed means installing a real `HTMLDocument` as the pane's document — which the plan's P1 rules out for a concrete reason, not caution: `getText()` reads the document, and an `HTMLDocument`'s text content is the *rendered text*, so `getText()` would start returning `Report one` where it returns `<h2>Report</h2><ul><li>one</li></ul>` today. That breaks the pane's primary contract for every migrator who installs a kit, to serve a base that only matters for links that already resolve.

So this is deferred to the element-model slice (where the `HTMLDocument` question is decided on its merits), not papered over: **no non-JDK `setBaseUrl` helper was invented** (R_infra_not_surface — no corpus evidence anything needs one, and adding non-JDK API to the emulator cuts against R_swing_is_truth's Swing-shaped surface). The migration guide documents the shape and what to do instead.

**Validation.** `HtmlCharsetTest` (header parameter incl. quoting/case, `<meta charset>` and `http-equiv` forms, header-beats-meta precedence, UTF-8 fallback, unknown name fall-through, invalid-UTF-8 bytes not throwing, media-type check) + `SJEditorPaneTest` additions driving a real `com.sun.net.httpserver.HttpServer` so charset and media type are checked against an actual `URLConnection`: declared-charset honoured, meta fallback, non-HTML WARN, fragment WARN, `MalformedURLException` on a bad string, and a hit-counting server proving the same-URL re-fetch.

**`JTextPane` follow-on.** `javax.swing.JTextPane extends JEditorPane`, so once `SJEditorPane` lands, `vaadinx.swing.JTextPane` becomes a thin subclass reusing the RTE peer for its `text/html` editing surface, with the StyledDocument API (`getStyledDocument`, `setCharacterAttributes` / `setParagraphAttributes`, `addStyle` / `setLogicalStyle`, `insertComponent`, `insertIcon`) dropping-and-WARNing per R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (c) — no `AttributeSet`/`Style`/inline-component counterpart in RTE-HTML. Reframes the old "land or rule out `JTextPane`" TODO item as "blocked on editable-JEditorPane, then thin subclass."

<a id="SD_caret_selection"></a>
## SD_caret_selection. Caret + selection are implemented, not dropped — dot/mark in `TextStateStore`, `setSelectionRange` pushed to the peer's input element

**Status: implemented.** Narrows [SD_sjtextfield](#SD_sjtextfield), which put "caret/highlighter/keymap" wholesale in the drop-and-WARN bucket. The caret half comes out of that bucket; highlighter/keymap/navigation-filter stay in it.

**Decision.** `JTextComponentMixin` carries Swing's own two-value caret model — `dot` (the caret) and `mark` (the anchored end of a selection) in `TextStateStore` — and every caret/selection method on the mixin is a view onto that pair: `getCaretPosition` / `setCaretPosition` / `moveCaretPosition`, `getSelectionStart` / `End` and their setters, `select` / `selectAll`, `getSelectedText`, `replaceSelection`. Writes push `inputElement.setSelectionRange(start, end, direction)` at the peer element. `getCaret()` returns `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.swing.text.PeerCaret`, a live view whose `getDot` / `getMark` / `setDot` / `moveDot` are the same mechanism. This model is the stage-3 one. The `:emulators` `JTextComponent` keeps its own, the ported `DefaultCaret` ([D_emulator_caret](../emulators/decisions.md#D_emulator_caret)), and uses the mixin only through two members that leave dot/mark alone: `renderSelection(start, end, backward)` pushes a selection render-only, and `addSelectionReportListener(listener, debounceMillis)` reports the browser's selection. That listener gets two reports: every *move* (held for `debounceMillis`, which Flow sends ahead of the next DOM event), and a *pre-edit* report dispatched eagerly on `beforeinput`, which reaches the server ahead of the value change it precedes. The two copies cannot drift, because only one is ever rendered.

**Why this isn't the R_vaadin_first shadow cache the rule forbids.** [R_vaadin_first](../CLAUDE.md#hard-rules)'s ban is on state that exists *only* so a setter's value round-trips through its own getter. Dot/mark drive UI: they move the browser's caret and selection, they decide what `getSelectedText` returns, and they position `replaceSelection`'s edit. That is the UI-functionality bucket, alongside `ButtonModel` and installed `Action`s. The Vaadin counterpart is at the element level rather than the component level — `TextFieldBase` exposes no selection property — which is a reason to reach through `Element.executeJs`, not a reason to call the concept absent. The mixin's own pre-existing comment said as much ("could wire via `Element.executeJs` later").

**Corpus evidence for doing this before the FocusManager work that surfaced it** (GitHub code search, `language:java`, file counts — upper bounds, since JavaFX/SWT share some names): `setCaretPosition` **67,840**, `getCurrentKeyboardFocusManager` 21,440, `getFocusOwner` 18,592, `.transferFocus()` 5,792, `.getCaret().setDot` **1,212**, `FocusManager.getCurrentManager` 866. The caret surface is an order of magnitude more used than the focus-manager surface that led us to it, and the specific `getCaret().setDot(…)` idiom is a real pattern rather than one app's quirk.

**Both directions work. The browser→server half is the `emul-selection` bridge, wired lazily.** Vaadin surfaces no caret or selection property, so the reverse direction is a DOM listener the mixin installs on the peer's inner `inputElement` — `selectionchange` plus `keyup` / `mouseup` / `select` as belt-and-braces for engines that fire it late — which re-dispatches a `emul-selection` custom event on the *host* element, because `selectionchange` does not cross a shadow boundary. Flow's element listener picks that up with `event.detail.start` / `end` / `dir` as event data, writes dot/mark, and fans out to `CaretListener` + `Caret.addChangeListener`.

**It is installed on the first caret-listener registration, not in `installTextComponentBindings`.** Each caret move costs a round trip, and a field nobody listens to should not pay it. Consequence worth knowing: with no listener registered, dot/mark stay a write-only mirror and `getCaretPosition()` can lag a caret the user moved — which is why the read contract below still stands as documented.

**Two dedupes stop the echo, and they are load-bearing.** Our own `setSelectionRange` push makes the browser answer with a `selectionchange` carrying the offsets we just sent. Client-side, the bridge skips a dispatch whose `start:end:direction` matches the last one it sent. Server-side, `fireCaretUpdate` returns early when neither dot nor mark actually moved. Without the second one, a listener that repositions the caret — exactly what the motivating `NumberFormatDocument` does — loops forever.

**Accepted limitation: the read contract.** Consequences of dot/mark being server-side state that only the bridge refreshes:

- Reads **clamp to the Document length**, so a browser edit that shortened the text can never hand out an offset past the end.
- `setText` and the peer-value-change path both leave the caret at the end of the new text. That matches an HTML input on a programmatic value change, and matches Swing's caret riding its Document's insert; for a user typing it is right whenever they type at the end, which is the case the auto-advance idiom cares about, and strictly better than leaving `dot` at a stale 0.
- `getCaretPosition()` is documented as *the last offset written here*, not a reading of the browser.

**What stays drop-and-WARN, with reasons rather than inertia.**

- **`setCaret(Caret)`** — a user-supplied `Caret` exists to *paint* a caret (R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (b)), and the browser draws its input's caret regardless. Honouring the setter would mean `getCaret()` handing back an object whose dot/mark no longer drive anything.
- **Magic caret position, blink rate, caret visibility, `Caret.paint`** — browser-owned presentation. `isVisible()` / `isSelectionVisible()` answer `true` and `getBlinkRate()` answers Swing's own 500ms **without** WARNing: those are accurate answers about a focused HTML input, not deferred work. Only the setters WARN.

**`PeerCaret` with a null target is deliberate, and is the point of the exercise.** A component with no text peer under it — the `Div` fallback ctor, or `JEditorPane`'s RTE — still gets a non-null `Caret` whose methods WARN and read zero. `getCaret()` returning `null` put an NPE one line into every caller (`target.getCaret().setDot(dot)` is the standard idiom), which R_match_swing_errors's warn-and-continue contract forbids: `onUnimplemented` promises a sensible default that never throws, and a `null` that NPEs immediately is not one. This is why the caret slice was done first — it is an R_match_swing_errors correctness fix wearing a feature's clothes.

**Verified in a real browser**, since Karibu records `executeJs` without running it and the client half is therefore untestable headlessly. Sampler's Caret route driven under Playwright (Chromium), reading `inputElement.selectionStart` / `End` / `Direction` directly and the server-rendered readout for the reverse direction:

| driven | server said | browser said |
|---|---|---|
| `select(4, 9)` | caret 9, selection 4..9 | `4 / 9 / forward` |
| `setCaretPosition(19)` | — | `19 / 19` |
| `selectAll()` | — | `0 / 19` |
| real mouse click mid-text | caret at 16 | — |
| `Home`, then 5×`ArrowRight` | caret at 0, then 5 | — |
| 4×`Shift+ArrowRight` | caret 9, selection 5..9 | `5 / 9 / forward` |
| `End` then `Shift+Home` | caret **0**, selection 0..19 | `0 / 19 / backward` |
| `replaceSelection("slow")` over a browser-made selection | caret at 8 | value `The slow brown fox`, `8 / 8` |

The backward row is the one that would have failed a naive implementation: the browser reports the range plus a direction, and the caret belongs at the *front*. The `replaceSelection` row is the full loop — browser selection → server → Document edit → value push → caret push. Zero console errors across the run. The auto-advance demo also confirmed `requestFocus()` moves browser focus, which tier 1 of the focus work will build on.

**Validation.** `SJTextFieldCaretTest` (36 tests: dot/mark defaults, collapse-vs-extend, backwards selections, R_match_swing_errors `IllegalArgumentException` outside the Document, `select`'s clamp-don't-throw contract, `getSelectedText`'s null-when-empty, `replaceSelection` through the Document with DocumentListener fan-out and peer value sync, `PeerCaret` as a live view, caret-follows-text, stale-caret clamping, zero-WARN happy path, plus the listener surface: fan-out on server-side moves, no-op moves firing nothing, removal, `ChangeEvent` sourced at the Caret as Swing's `DefaultCaret` does, `getCaret()` identity stability, a synthesized `emul-selection` event in both directions, the bridge staying unwired until a listener asks, the echo being swallowed, and the two members the emulator uses: a report leaving this component's dot/mark alone, a held registration taking only the moves Flow flushes, removal stopping both reports, and `renderSelection` pushing without a state change or an event) + `JTextComponentCaretTest` in `:emulators`, now over the emulator's own caret ([D_emulator_caret](../emulators/decisions.md#D_emulator_caret)). Sampler's `CaretPanel` route is the browser-facing demo. The three surrogate tests asserting the old `getCaretPosition returns 0 and WARNs` behaviour are updated to assert the implemented contract.

<a id="SD_focus_tracker"></a>
## SD_focus_tracker. `FocusTracker` — the focus-owner pointer is a per-UI browser fact in `:surrogates`, fed by one UI-wide `focusin` listener

**Status: implemented.** The surrogate half of the focus work; the emulator half — `KeyboardFocusManager` / `FocusManager`, traversal, the AWT `FocusEvent` bridge — is [D_focus_managers](../emulators/decisions.md). Sibling of [SD_caret_selection](#SD_caret_selection): same file in the inventory testapp motivated both, and SD_caret_selection's `emul-selection` bridge de-risked the DOM-listener shape this one needed.

**Decision.** `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.FocusTracker` holds one nullable pointer — the Vaadin component that has browser focus — **per UI**, in `ComponentUtil` UI data. Modelled on `BrowserTimeZone`: a per-UI browser fact cached in `:surrogates` so both layers can read it (a stage-3 view has the same "who has focus" question as a stage-2 one). `install()` is called once per UI from `SwingBridgeEmulatorsBootstrap`'s UI-init leg, alongside `BrowserTimeZone.fetch()`.

**Why the pointer lives here and not in `:emulators`.** Module direction is `:emulators → :surrogates`, so a tracker in `:emulators` would be unreachable from surrogate code; and the fact itself is Vaadin-shaped (a `Component`, not a `vaadinx.awt.Component`) — the emulator layer maps it up to an emulator at the point of use, via `EHelper.emulatorFor`. Consistent with the S-prefix convention: the prefix marks the *module*, not surrogate-ness.
