**One UI-wide `focusin`, not a per-peer subscription.** Four options were weighed (per-peer `FocusNotifier` at attach; one `focusin` per UI; `document.activeElement` read on demand through a D_preferences-style VT park; inferring the owner from whichever emulator raised the current `callSwing`). **`focusin` per UI wins on completeness**: a per-peer `FocusNotifier` subscription is blind to every peer sitting on a bare `Div` (`Box`, `JViewport`, the whole `JMenuItem` family), and there are a lot of those. It is also one listener and one round trip per real focus change, and the round trip lands *before* the keystroke that triggers a read — so the pointer is already correct when a `Document` filter asks mid-`insertString`. The activeElement-on-demand variant is exact but only inside a `callSwing` VT context; the infer-from-`callSwing` variant is free but means `getFocusOwner()` reads "last component that talked to the server", which goes wrong after a pure Tab.

**Element identity was the open question, and Flow answers it — but not via `mapEventTargetElement()`.** The idea doc expected to stamp a `data-emul-*` attribute on every peer element and resolve it back through a server-side map. Neither is needed: `DomListenerRegistration.addEventDataElement(expr)` evaluates a JS expression and maps the element it returns to the closest server-controlled `Element`. The expression is

```js
event.composedPath().find(n => n.nodeType === 1 && n.getRootNode() === document)
```

— the first element on the composed path that lives in the document, i.e. the outermost shadow host, which for the `<input>` inside a peer's shadow root is the peer element itself. `FocusTracker` then walks up to the nearest `Element` carrying a `Component`, since a server element can still be a bare `Element` inside a composite peer. No attribute, no map, nothing to leak.

**The obvious spelling is wrong, and fails in the worst way.** `mapEventTargetElement()` reads like the exact API for this, and it is what this entry first claimed. In a real browser it resolves `event.target` to **`<body>`** for a listener on the UI's own element — so the pointer reads back as "nothing focused" *and* clobbers the correct value the peer's own focus event had just stored a moment earlier. Symptom, from the Sampler `Focus` route: the readout names the field you clicked, then the very next keystroke's `getFocusOwner()` answers `null` and the auto-advance idiom silently does nothing. Karibu cannot catch it — it registers DOM listeners without ever dispatching them — so `FocusTrackerTest`'s "focusin bridge identifies the peer by composed path" asserts the registered event-data expression instead, and is the only thing standing between this regression and a silently dead bridge.

**`focusout` with a null `relatedTarget` is what clears the pointer.** Clicking empty space blurs the focused element without focusing anything, and fires no `focusin` at all — so the losing side has to report it. The listener carries `setFilter("!event.relatedTarget")` so it costs a round trip only in that one case; when `relatedTarget` is non-null the `focusin` about to arrive overwrites the pointer anyway. This is the echo-guard analogue SD_caret_selection predicted would be needed, and it turned out to be a filter rather than a two-sided dedupe.

**Both listeners `allowInert()`, because a modal dialog makes the UI element inert.** Flow drops an inert element's DOM events at the RPC handler unless the listener opts out, and a modal child makes `<body>` inert — the very element these listeners sit on. Without the opt-out the pointer froze on whatever had focus when the modal opened, so every focus question asked inside a modal (`getFocusOwner()`, SD_enter_claims' "does the focused field take Enter") answered about the main window. Measured in crud's edit dialog, 2026-09-25: the pointer still named the main window's *Edit…* button while the date spinner had focus. Karibu's `_fireDomEvent` skips the inert check, so `FocusTrackerTest` dispatches through Flow's `EventRpcHandler` instead, after resolving inertness the way a response write does.

**Per UI, deliberately against D_session_scoped_pools's grain.** [D_session_scoped_pools](../emulators/decisions.md#D_session_scoped_pools) moved `Timer` / `SwingWorker` to *session* scope so they survive an F5 `@PreserveOnRefresh` teleport. Focus goes the other way on purpose: a running timer is app state worth preserving across a refresh, focus is browser state the refresh legitimately destroys — a new UI has its own DOM and its own `activeElement`. A session-scoped focus pointer would answer another tab's question.

**Two write paths besides the bridge, both additive.**
- **`setFocusOwner` — the optimistic move.** Every server-side focus request (`Component.requestFocus`, and traversal through it) moves the pointer immediately rather than waiting for the browser's `focusin`. Without it, a `requestFocus` followed by a same-round-trip `getFocusOwner()` — the auto-advance idiom, exactly — would read the *old* owner. The browser's own `focusin` confirms the same value a round trip later, or corrects us if it refused the focus.
- **`noteFocusGained` / `noteFocusLost` — the peer's own focus/blur.** When the emulator's AWT `FocusEvent` bridge (D_focus_managers) is subscribed to a peer, it feeds the pointer too: free on a round trip the peer was already making. `noteFocusLost` clears only if the loser still holds the pointer, since `focusout` on the old element precedes `focusin` on the new one and an unconditional clear would wipe a pointer the newer event has already moved on.

<a id="SD_focus_owner_listener"></a>
**A transition seam, because the layer above needs the *move* and not just the value.** `addFocusOwnerListener(FocusOwnerListener)` hands out a per-UI `Registration` and reports `(oldOwner, newOwner)` on every pointer change; all four write paths — the `focusin` bridge, the `focusout` clear, `setFocusOwner`, `noteFocusGained` / `noteFocusLost` — funnel through one identity-guarded `moveOwner`, so no writer can move the pointer without reporting it. Its one consumer is [D_focus_property_registry](../emulators/decisions.md#D_focus_property_registry): `vaadinx.awt.KeyboardFocusManager`'s bound properties fire from *here*, because the JDK fires them from `setGlobalFocusOwner` and SB-Emulators' equivalent is this pointer's move.

Not a `PropertyChangeSupport` on this class, and not the property names themselves: the seam reports a browser fact in Vaadin terms, and the layer that knows what a `focusOwner` property is does the naming and the JDK-shaped fan-out. That keeps the module direction intact — the emulator layer subscribes, `FocusTracker` emits blind, and a stage-3 view that wants the same fact gets a callback rather than a `java.beans` event it has no use for. This is not the dead seam [SD_sscrollpane](#SD_sscrollpane) rejected: there was nothing on the far end there, and here the far end is a migrator's `PropertyChangeListener`.

**Never throws.** Unlike `BrowserTimeZone.get()`, which fails loud on an unpopulated cache (SD_browser_timezone), an empty focus pointer means "nothing focused" — a legitimate state, not a wiring mistake. No current UI answers `null` as well — including `addFocusOwnerListener`, which hands back a no-op registration rather than refusing.

**Accepted limitations.**
- **An app that skips `SwingBridgeEmulatorsBootstrap` gets no browser-driven focus tracking** — only the optimistic server-side path. Same class of failure as skipping `BrowserTimeZone.fetch()`, and R_no_spi_selfregister already makes wiring the listener a hard requirement.
- **A detached component can linger in the pointer** until the next focus change. One reference, replaced on the next `focusin`; a detach hook would cost more plumbing than the leak is worth.
- **A raw `Focusable.focus()` call bypasses the optimistic write.** `ComponentMixin.requestFocus()` — what stage-3 migrated code calls — does move the pointer, and `ComponentMixin.hasFocus()` / `isFocusOwner()` now read it instead of WARNing and answering `false` (closing the cheap half of [SD_api_three_buckets](#SD_api_three_buckets)'s bucket C). But `focus()` is a Vaadin default method the mixins don't override, so Vaadin-idiomatic code that calls it directly is visible only once the browser reports back. Surrogate-side *traversal* stays a stub for a structural reason, not a cheapness one: it needs a component ring to step through, and the emulator layer owns the Swing-shaped tree that defines the order.

**Validation.** `FocusTrackerTest` (7: default-empty, read-back, clear, `install` idempotence, no leak across UIs, no-current-UI answered rather than thrown, and the composed-path expression above), plus `FocusOwnerListenerTest` (6, Java) for the transition seam: every move reported, an unchanged owner silent, unsubscribe honoured, a listener unsubscribing from its own callback (the reason `moveOwner` walks a copy), per-UI scoping, and the no-current-UI no-op. Karibu records DOM listener registrations without ever dispatching events, so every server-side test drives focus through the optimistic write instead.

**Browser-verified, both engines** (2026-08-20, Sampler `Focus` route). Chromium: clicking a field updates the readout to that field's Swing name, `getFocusedWindow()` answers `SamplerFrame` (the `@MainWindow` frame found by the plain ancestor walk — the D_focus_managers deviation confirmed live), and typing `415` / `555` / `0123` hops focus through all three phone parts. The server-driven focus round-trips too — after each transfer the browser's own `focusin` arrives naming the newly focused peer, so the pointer is re-confirmed rather than assumed. Tab-key moves update the readout (maintainer-confirmed). Firefox: identification and both **clearing** paths — a click into empty space reads back "nothing focused", and `clearFocusOwner()` moves `document.activeElement` to `<body>` with the server agreeing, checked both through the listener-driven readout and by asking again explicitly.

Two caveats on what that proves. The empty-space clear is confirmed *behaviourally* but does not isolate the UI-wide `focusout` listener, since the peer's own blur reaches `noteFocusLost` on the same gesture — both paths are correct, so the observation cannot separate them. And this run is what surfaced the `mapEventTargetElement` defect above, plus the `columns`-width chrome bug (`CssConvert.columnsToCssWidth`) that made the demo's 3-digit fields render one digit wide: two real defects from one pass over a mechanism whose 2829 server-side tests were all green.

---

<a id="SD_sbutton"></a>
## SD_sbutton. `SButton` surrogate — `java.awt.Button` on `ComponentMixin`, deliberately *not* on `AbstractButtonMixin`

`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SButton extends com.vaadin.flow.component.button.Button implements ComponentMixin` — the surrogate for **`java.awt.Button`**, the AWT 1.0 push button, distinct from `SJButton` per [SD_naming](#SD_naming)'s naming rule. First surrogate in the AWT-widget lane; the lane and why Button goes first: [D_awt_lane](../emulators/decisions.md#D_awt_lane).

**Decision.**
- **`ComponentMixin`, not `AbstractButtonMixin`.** Reusing the Swing button mixin would hand us the click→`ActionEvent` fan-out and the actionCommand fallback for free, but would also graft `getModel()` / `setAction()` / `setIcon()` / `isSelected()` / mnemonic onto a surrogate whose JDK original has **no model, no `Action`, no icon, no mnemonic and no selected state** — a `DefaultButtonModel` state machine backing a component that never had one. The overlap is ~40 lines of listener fan-out; duplicating it is the cheaper mistake than a base class built on shallow commonality. `ComponentMixin` is AWT-level by construction (SJTable / SJList / SJTree already implement it standalone), so it drops straight on.
- **One Vaadin subscription drives the fan-out.** The ctor wires `ClickNotifier` → `SHelper.callSwing` (R_callswing_envelope) → `processActionEvent`, which walks an `EventListenerList`. This is R_vaadin_first's shared-subscription wiring shape, the same one SJButton uses one layer up — not an accumulated listener list with nothing behind it.
- **`processActionEvent` is the single dispatch funnel.** Both the peer click and any hand-dispatched event route through it, because overriding it and calling super is AWT's documented interception idiom and a subclass that does so must see browser-originated actions too.
- **`actionCommand` is stored; `label` is not.** A single field, no `Store` class: it is the `ActionEvent` payload — UI functionality, the same justification `ButtonStateStore.actionCommand` carries — and its null means "fall back to the label", so it cannot be reconstructed from the peer. `label` routes to the Vaadin `Button`'s own `getText`/`setText`, accepting the R_vaadin_first loss that `setLabel(null)` reads back as `""`.
- **`getListeners(Class<T>)` is dropped — erasure clash.** Vaadin `Component` already declares `getListeners(Class<? extends ComponentEvent>)`; same erasure, unrelated return type, so the JVM forbids both on one class. Exactly the clash [SD_sjbutton](#SD_sjbutton)'s `AbstractButtonMixin` documents for `getIcon()` / `getUI()`. `getActionListeners()` covers the only listener type this class owns, and the emulator inherits a working `getListeners` from `vaadinx.awt.Component` — which is where migrated code calls it.

**Rationale.** The AWT lane's value is that its expensive half is already built: `ComponentMixin` was written AWT-level, not Swing-level, so a plain `java.awt.Component` leaf costs almost nothing. `SButton` is the proof, and the shape every subsequent AWT widget surrogate copies.

**Accepted limitations.** `setLabel(null)` → `""` round-trip (R_vaadin_first). `getAccessibleContext` WARNs and returns null (as everywhere). No `getListeners(Class<T>)` on the surrogate (above) — the emulator has it.

**Validation.** `SButtonTest` (16 — ctors and the null-label loss, actionCommand round-trip and both fallback directions, peer-click fan-out with `source == SButton`, `processActionEvent` as the subclass-visible funnel, null-listener no-ops, the inherited `ComponentMixin` surface on a non-JComponent host, `paramString`, the `getAccessibleContext` WARN, and a zero-WARN happy path). Emulator side: [D_awt_button](../emulators/decisions.md#D_awt_button). Sampler `AwtButtonPanel` + `AwtButtonWarnInventoryTest` (2).

---

<a id="SD_slabel"></a>
## SD_slabel. `SLabel` surrogate — `java.awt.Label` on a `Span`, alignment as CSS `text-align`

`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SLabel extends com.vaadin.flow.component.html.Span implements ComponentMixin` — the surrogate for **`java.awt.Label`**, AWT 1.0 static text, distinct from `SJLabel` per [SD_naming](#SD_naming). Second surrogate in the AWT-widget lane after [SD_sbutton](#SD_sbutton); the lane: [D_awt_lane](../emulators/decisions.md#D_awt_lane).

**Decision.**
- **A new class, not `SJLabel` re-skinned and not `SJLabel` reused as the emulator's peer.** `SJLabel` implements `JComponentMixin` and carries borders with their CSS round-trip, client properties, the opaque flag, tooltip, an icon child, an inner text `Span` plus the HTML-mode markup shadow, `labelFor`, the mnemonic pair and `updateUI` / `getUIClassID` — **every one of them absent from `java.awt.Label`**, whose whole surface is text plus a three-value alignment. What the two genuinely share is a text passthrough and a three-arm switch. Same call SD_sbutton made against `AbstractButtonMixin`, for the same reason: duplicating ~10 lines beats a base built on shallow commonality. *Rejected:* peering the emulator on `SJLabel`, the way `JTextPane` reuses `SJEditorPane` ([D_jtextpane](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jtextpane)). That precedent earns its keep when the reused surrogate holds large machinery the emulator actually needs — there, the RTE HTML codec; here it would put a `JComponent`-level peer under an AWT-level emulator, and any AWT-specific behaviour would have to be added *into* `SJLabel` guarded by "not for JLabel".
- **Host element is `Span`, not `NativeLabel`.** `SJLabel` hosts on `<label>` for exactly one reason — JLabel's `labelFor` *is* the `<label for="…">` wiring, which `NativeLabel.setFor` hits directly ([SD_sjlabel](#SD_sjlabel)). `java.awt.Label` has no `labelFor`, so the deciding factor inverts: a `<label>` carrying no `for` attribute and wrapping no control is a semantically wrong accessibility node, while `<span>` is the neutral static-text element.
- **Alignment maps to CSS `text-align` on a `display: block` host** — deliberately *not* `SJLabel`'s `inline-flex` + `justify-content`, which exists there to place an icon beside the text. For a text-only element `text-align` **is** AWT's semantics: it aligns the rendered lines, surviving a wrap where `justify-content` would align the box instead, and needs no flex container. `display: block` is seeded in the ctor so the label fills the region its `LayoutManager` handed it (a CSS-Grid child stretches by default) — which is what makes alignment observable at all, and what AWT does, where a `Label` fills its `BorderLayout` region.
- **Nothing is stored — the alignment round-trip is *lossless*.** AWT's three values and the three CSS keywords are in bijection, so unlike `SJLabel`'s LEFT-and-LEADING-both-read-back-as-LEADING collapse ([SD_border_css_lossy](#SD_border_css_lossy)'s shape) the getter reconstructs exactly what was set. That removes the only argument for a shadow field, so R_vaadin_first is satisfied outright rather than by exception. `text` lives in the element's text content.
- **No `PropertyChangeEvent`s.** JDK `Label.setText` and `Label.setAlignment` fire none — PCE-per-property is a Swing-era addition, and the `Component`-level setters that *do* fire (font, colours, enabled) are `ComponentMixin`'s. `SJLabel` fires `"text"` / `"horizontalAlignment"` only because *JLabel* does. Same call SD_sbutton made for `setLabel`.
- **R_match_swing_errors fidelity on the validator.** `setAlignment` throws `IllegalArgumentException("improper alignment: " + alignment)` — the JDK's own message — from the same switch that maps to CSS, so the throw necessarily precedes any write and a bad value leaves the label untouched.
- **No `setEnabled` redirect, unlike `SButton`.** Vaadin's `Button` *class* declares a concrete `setEnabled` that shadows the mixin default, which is why SD_sbutton's surrogate needs an explicit redirect; `Span` declares none, and `ComponentMixin extends HasEnabled` makes the mixin's default the more specific one.

**Rationale.** The AWT lane's second widget exists to show the lane's shape is repeatable and to close a real stage-2 blocker: a `java.awt.Label` left in a corner of an otherwise-Swing view is a hard compile error with no workaround short of rewriting the view. It also isolates the one trap in the family — **`java.awt.Label.LEFT/CENTER/RIGHT` are 0/1/2, colliding numerically with `SwingConstants`' 2/0/4**, so a value borrowed from the Swing set means something else here or throws. That is why the alignment mapping is its own switch rather than a route through `SJLabel`'s Swing-keyed helpers.

**Accepted limitations.** `setText(null)` → `""` round-trip (R_vaadin_first; the emulator owns both fields, so its getter returns null verbatim). `getAccessibleContext` WARNs and returns null. Alignment is invisible in a shrink-wrap context — a `FlowLayout` row gives the label no width to align within — which is also what AWT does there, since `FlowLayout` sizes to preferred.

**Validation.** `SLabelTest` (16 — the three ctors and JDK defaults, ctor-time IAE, the constant-collision trap, text round-trip and the null loss, text reaching the rendered span, alignment landing in CSS, the lossless round-trip over all three values, the throw-before-write guard, the `display: block` seeding, the inherited `ComponentMixin` surface on a non-JComponent host, `paramString`, the `getAccessibleContext` WARN, and a zero-WARN happy path). Emulator side: [D_awt_label](../emulators/decisions.md#D_awt_label). Sampler `AwtLabelPanel` + `AwtLabelWarnInventoryTest` (2).

**Browser-verified** (2026-08-21, Chromium, Sampler `AWT › Label`). The three fixed labels each render as a 1203px-wide block whose text sits flush-left (gaps 0 / 1071), dead-centre (526 / 526) and flush-right (1063 / 0) — the check the server-side tests cannot make, since a lossless CSS round-trip would pass just as well with the alignment invisible on an inline host. Clicking *Cycle alignment* moves the live label to `text-align: center`, re-centres its text (556 / 556) and updates the text in the same push; the `setBackground` tint from `ComponentMixin` renders. No `onUnimplemented` WARN in the server log across the route.

---

<a id="SD_schoice"></a>
## SD_schoice. `SChoice` surrogate — `java.awt.Choice` on `Select<Integer>`; index identity, and a rebuild that clears the value

`SChoice extends Select<Integer> implements ComponentMixin, ItemSelectable` — the AWT 1.0 dropdown, **not** `JComboBox`. Third widget in the AWT lane after [SD_sbutton](#SD_sbutton) / [SD_slabel](#SD_slabel); emulator side: [D_awt_choice](../emulators/decisions.md#D_awt_choice).

**Decision — a new surrogate, not a re-skin of `SJComboBox`.** The sizing table called this "~1 session, re-skin of `SJComboBox`", and that was wrong in an interesting way. `java.awt.Choice` is a `Vector<String>` plus an `int`: no `ComboBoxModel`, no `ListDataListener`, no editable mode, no `ComboBoxEditor`, no `PopupMenuListener`, no `maximumRowCount`, no `ListCellRenderer`, no `ActionListener`. It is `JComboBox` with the entire MVC half deleted. Reusing `SJComboBox` would graft that layer onto a class that has none of it, and would silently inherit the divergence that matters most — `SJComboBox` fires `ItemEvent`s on programmatic selection because `JComboBox` does, and `Choice` fires only on user interaction.

**Decision — Vaadin `Select`, not `ComboBox`.** SD_sjcombobox chose `ComboBox` for `SJComboBox` *because* `JComboBox` needs what `Select` lacks: `allowCustomValue` for editable mode, a filter UI, `pageSize` for `maximumRowCount`. `Choice` needs none of them, and `ComboBox` would hand the migrator a text-input affordance the JDK class does not have — a user could type into a `Choice`. SD_slabel's rule applied a third time: pick the host from what the AWT class *has*, not from what its Swing cousin's host happened to be. A raw `<select>` element was also rejected: its `selectedIndex` is literally the AWT model, but a native dropdown renders unthemed beside every other Vaadin field, and the repo reserves raw-element peering for structural shells with no behaviour (`Box`, `JViewport`, `JScrollBar`).

**Decision — index identity, [SD_sjlist](#SD_sjlist)'s rule applied a second time.** The type parameter is `Integer`: the peer's items are the indices `0..n-1`, with `setItemLabelGenerator` supplying the rendered text from a `List<String>` on the surrogate. `Choice` permits duplicate item Strings, and `Select` keys items through a `KeyMapper` (`Select.java:149`, keyed per item in `createItem`) backed by a `HashMap` — two equal Strings (usually the same interned instance) would collapse to one key, which no `IdentifierProvider` could separate. Three consequences, all simplifications: duplicates just work, with no divergence to document; `selectedIndex` needs **no storage** because the Vaadin value *is* the AWT concept; and the selection-adjust rules operate on the list plus a whole-list rebuild.

**Decision — the R_swing_is_truth guard wraps the items push, not only the selection push.** `Select.reset()` — which every `setItems` funnels through — is `keyMapper.removeAll(); listBox.removeAll(); clear(); …`, so **each item mutation nulls the Vaadin value and fires a ValueChangeEvent**. Guarding only the selection write would let every `addItem` surface as a spurious `ItemEvent`, and quirk 4's auto-select-0 would make it a plausible-looking one. Two corollaries: every mutator re-pushes the selection **after** the rebuild (`modelToPresentation` answers null for an item the `KeyMapper` has not seen yet), and the guard is **save-and-restore, not a flat assignment** — the pushes nest, since `reset()`'s `clear()` re-enters the surrogate's own `setValue`, and a flat `finally = false` would drop the outer guard halfway through the rebuild.

**Decision — the item-mutation family is renamed wholesale.** `addItem` / `insertItemAt` / `removeItem` / `removeItemAt` / `removeAllItems` / `getItemAt`, matching `SJComboBox` exactly. `Choice.removeAll()` and `Select.removeAll()` are an exact signature collision with **opposite** meanings (Vaadin's removes the non-item slotted children — its javadoc says so explicitly), so at least one name had to move; renaming only that one was rejected because with index identity `getItem(int)` on a `Select<Integer>` reads as lying about the item type, and because SD_sbutton's keep-AWT-names precedent rests on the AWT name being *free* on the host, which `setLabel`/`getLabel` are not here (`Select.setLabel` is the floating field caption). AWT's own names live on the emulator, which does not extend `Select`. One method is here for the emulator's sake: `setItemsAndSelection(List<String>, int)` replaces both in one rebuild and applies none of the selection rules, because the emulator keeps AWT's item vector itself and flushes it after each change (D_awt_choice). `getListeners(Class<T>)` is likewise dropped — same erasure as Vaadin's `Component.getListeners`, the SD_sbutton precedent — and `validate()` is widened to public over `Select`'s protected one, the SJComboBox precedent.

**Accepted limitations.** `getAccessibleContext` WARNs and returns null. `setEmptySelectionAllowed` is left alone: it is `false` by default and governs only whether an extra null-valued item renders — it does **not** enforce AWT's "a non-empty Choice always has a selection", which falls out of the mutators' own `select(0)` calls. Every mutation is an O(n) whole-list `setItems` rebuild (`ListDataView` offers only item-identity mutation, no index-based insert) — the same trade `SJComboBox.pushItemsToPeer` makes, free for a dropdown.

**Validation.** `SChoiceTest` (36 — `setItemsAndSelection` replacing both silently and refusing bad input, the four load-bearing quirks as named tests, `getSelectedObjects()` null vs `SJComboBox`'s `Object[0]`, duplicate items independently selectable, the rendered options tracking the list through every mutation, the rebuild-churn regression, guard restoration after a mutation, AWT FIFO listener order, the R_match_swing_errors exception table row by row, the inherited `ComponentMixin` surface, `paramString`, the `getAccessibleContext` WARN, and a zero-WARN happy path). Sampler `AwtChoicePanel` + `AwtChoiceWarnInventoryTest` (2).

**Browser-verified** (2026-08-21, Firefox, Sampler `AWT › Choice`). The four items render as four `vaadin-select-item`s in the list box, the two equal `"imperial"` Strings carrying *distinct* KeyMapper keys — clicking the second one reports `getSelectedIndex() = 2`, not 1, which is index identity doing the job the server-side tests can only assert indirectly. `select(2)` moves the selection and pushes it to the field while the `ItemEvent` readout still shows the *earlier* browser pick (`nautical`) — `select()` is silent end-to-end, not merely under Karibu. `insert("New", 0)` re-renders all five options and re-selects index 0 with no ItemEvent fired, which is the rebuild-churn guard working against a real `Select.reset()`. After `removeAll()` the overlay is empty, the field renders blank, and the readout is `getSelectedIndex() = -1, getSelectedItem() = null, getSelectedObjects() = null`. No `onUnimplemented` WARN in the server log across the route.

<a id="SD_scheckbox"></a>
## SD_scheckbox. `SCheckbox` surrogate — `java.awt.Checkbox` on Vaadin `Checkbox`; only the browser posts events, and the radio glyph is a theme name

`SCheckbox extends com.vaadin.flow.component.checkbox.Checkbox implements ComponentMixin, ItemSelectable` — the AWT 1.0 checkbox, **not** `JCheckBox`. Fourth widget in the AWT lane after [SD_sbutton](#SD_sbutton) / [SD_slabel](#SD_slabel) / [SD_schoice](#SD_schoice); emulator side: [D_awt_checkbox](../emulators/decisions.md#D_awt_checkbox).

**Decision — `ComponentMixin`, not `AbstractButtonMixin`, and the argument is sharper than SD_sbutton's.** The mixin already owns `addItemListener` / `removeItemListener` / `getItemListeners`, `fireItemStateChanged`, `getSelectedObjects`, `isSelected` / `setSelected` and a `ButtonStateStore` to hang them on, so the overlap here is genuinely larger than it was for `java.awt.Button` — this is the one AWT widget where reuse looked close to free. It loses anyway, because **the mixin's `ItemEvent` fan-out is model-driven and that is exactly the behaviour AWT does not have**: `AbstractButtonMixin.setModel` installs an `ItemListener` on the `ButtonModel` that re-fires as a component-level `ItemEvent`, so a `setSelected(true)` — i.e. an AWT `setState(true)` — would deliver an event where AWT delivers nothing. Not surface bloat; a wrong-by-construction event stream on the most-used method of the class, in the direction that silently double-fires migrated code. The rest of the graft is bigger than `Button`'s, not smaller (`getModel` / `setModel`, `setAction` / `getAction`, the icon family, mnemonic, `doClick`, `ActionListener`, `ChangeListener`, `getActionCommand`, plus the layout-key drop-and-WARN block), and `AbstractButtonMixin` sits on `JComponentMixin`, which would bring borders, client properties, the opaque flag and `updateUI` to a class whose JDK base is `java.awt.Component` — SD_slabel's objection to `SJLabel`. Duplicating the ~30-line fan-out is the cheaper mistake for the third time.

**Decision — `isFromClient()` is the whole feedback-loop defence; no `preventPeerEvents` flag.** A real simplification over `SJCheckBox` / `SJRadioButton` / `SChoice`, and it exists *because* of the AWT rule: Swing wants server-side pushes to fire events, so those surrogates need a flag to tell their own echo apart from a user action; AWT wants server-side pushes to fire **nothing**, which is precisely what `isFromClient() == false` already means. R_swing_is_truth's guard-flag guidance is about preventing loops, and here there is no loop to prevent — a server push produces an event the listener discards by contract. Group cascades are server pushes too, so AWT's "the deselected sibling gets no event" falls out with no extra code.

**Decision — the radio glyph is a theme name plus CSS custom properties, and it is appearance only.** A grouped `java.awt.Checkbox` paints as a radio button — in the JDK a live peer property read off `group != null` (`XCheckboxPeer.paint`), not a construction-time type choice, and `XCheckboxPeer.setCheckboxGroup` repaints on change. `setRadioLook(boolean)` / `isRadioLook()` write and read the `emul-radio` theme name on the host element: DOM-backed, so the round-trip is lossless and **no shadow field is needed** — R_vaadin_first satisfied outright, the same way SD_slabel's alignment is. `emul/scheckbox.css` (via `@StyleSheet`, not `@CssImport`, so shipping it never forces a Vite dev-bundle rebuild) turns that theme name into `--vaadin-checkbox-border-radius: 50%` + `--vaadin-checkbox-checkmark-char: "●"`. **Custom properties over `::part` surgery**: Lumo authors its own rules to read `--vaadin-checkbox-*`, so setting them crosses the shadow boundary by inheritance with no specificity fight against the theme's internals; a `::part(checkbox)::after { border-radius: 50% }` rule follows as the Aura fallback, which supports the border-radius knob but has no checkmark-char equivalent.

**Decision — no group awareness, and therefore no deselect veto here.** The rejected alternative is worth naming because it is tempting and the JDK appears to endorse it: `XCheckboxPeer` reads *both* the glyph and the cannot-be-unchecked veto off the same `checkBoxGroup != null`, so one `radioLook` flag could drive both. Rejected — our surrogate is not AWT's peer. AWT's peer sits *below* the group; a Vaadin surrogate sits *beside* it, and [D_buttongroup_browser_click](../emulators/decisions.md#D_buttongroup_browser_click) already ruled that group mutex is an emulator-layer concern. Driving behaviour off a property named for appearance would put the veto in the one module that must not know what a `CheckboxGroup` is. So a standalone `SCheckbox` in radio look un-checks like the checkbox it is — which is also what a standalone `vaadin-radio-button` does — and the emulator vetoes.

**Rejected hosts.** A second peer type per instance (the repo's `VaadinRadioButton`) buys **only the glyph** — its own javadoc records that a standalone `vaadin-radio-button` carries no mutual exclusion — while costing a construction-time peer choice that `setCheckboxGroup` can then contradict. A composite `Div` holding one of each kills the is-a contract that makes a surrogate a surrogate: no longer a `HasValue<Boolean>`, no `Focusable`, no label slot, and two focusable inner controls to keep in sync. Reusing `SJCheckBox` as the peer (D_jtextpane's `JTextPane`-over-`SJEditorPane` move) is rejected for the fourth time in this lane, for SD_sbutton's reason sharpened by the first decision above: the machinery it would bring is precisely the machinery that must not run.

**Clashes against the Vaadin host** (verified at 25.2.3). `getLabel` / `setLabel` are declared by Vaadin with identical signatures and a *semantically aligned* meaning — the text beside the box — so `setLabel` is overridden only to keep the JDK's changed-only guard (never mix with `setLabelComponent`, which Vaadin's `setLabel` evicts). `validate()` is `protected` on Vaadin `Checkbox` against `ComponentMixin`'s public default, so it takes the visibility-widening override SD_toggle_checkbox_first_cut / SD_sjtextfield / SD_sjpasswordfield all carry. `getListeners(Class<T>)` is dropped — same erasure as Vaadin `Component`'s, the SD_sbutton precedent, third occurrence. `setEnabled` needs **no** override for correctness (Vaadin `Checkbox` declares none, so `ComponentMixin`'s default wins on specificity) but keeps the explicit redirect that `SJCheckBox` carries, to pin the choice in source. `java.awt.Checkbox` declares **no constants**, so SD_slabel's `SwingConstants` collision trap does not recur.

**Accepted limitations.** `getAccessibleContext` WARNs and returns null. `setLabel(null)` reads back `""`, so the surrogate's `getSelectedObjects()` yields `Object[]{""}` where AWT yields `Object[]{null}` — the emulator's field shadow is where the null survives (R_swing_is_truth). A smaller inversion worth knowing when writing surrogate tests: an *untouched* Vaadin `getLabel()` returns **null** while `setLabel(null)` yields `""`, the opposite way round from AWT.

**Validation.** `SCheckboxTest` (21 — the quirk-1 pair as the headline (`_setValue` fires exactly one `ItemEvent`; `setState` / `setValue` fire none), both label-null directions, state reaching the rendered element, `getSelectedObjects()` null-vs-single, the theme-name round-trip, radio look proven appearance-only, AWT FIFO listener order, `processItemEvent` as the subclass funnel, the inherited `ComponentMixin` surface and widened `validate()`, `paramString`, the `getAccessibleContext` WARN, and a zero-WARN happy path). Sampler `AwtCheckboxPanel` + `AwtCheckboxWarnInventoryTest` (2).

**Browser-verified** (2026-08-24, Firefox, Sampler `AWT › Checkbox`). The three grouped boxes render as round radios beside a square ungrouped one, and the checked radio shows a filled dot. Two measurements corrected the plan while doing it: `--vaadin-checkbox-border-radius` **is** honoured (`::part(checkbox)` computes `border-radius: 50%`), but `--vaadin-checkbox-checkmark-char` is **not** at 25.2.3 — the part's `::after` resolves to a hard-coded `"✓"`, which is the same fall-through the Sampler's own Swing-look sheet already documents — so the glyph rides a `::part(checkbox)::after { content }` rule instead. That rule then needed `[checked]` added: an app's Swing-look sheet carries an equal-specificity `vaadin-checkbox[checked]::part(checkbox)::after` of its own and is linked *after* the surrogates jar's stylesheet, so the unqualified selector lost the cascade in the app that matters most. No `onUnimplemented` WARN in the server log across the route.

<a id="SD_no_spanel"></a>
## SD_no_spanel. No `SPanel` — `java.awt.Panel` is emulator-only, on a bare `Div`

**Decision.** The `java.awt.Panel` slice lands in `:emulators` only ([D_awt_panel](../emulators/decisions.md#D_awt_panel)); `:surrogates` does not get an `SPanel`. The emulator peers on the plain Vaadin `Div` that `vaadinx.awt.Container`'s no-arg ctor already creates. Second "no surrogate, by design" outcome under [D_surrogate_first](../emulators/decisions.md#D_surrogate_first)'s surrogate-first ordering, after [SD_no_sjoptionpane](#SD_no_sjoptionpane) — but for the opposite reason: SD_no_sjoptionpane's surrogate would have been *hollow because blocking is emulator-only*, this one would be hollow because **there is no state left to hold**.

**Rationale.**

- **Apply SD_sbutton/SD_slabel's test, then its follow-up.** *What state does the Swing class carry that the AWT one does not?* `SJPanel extends Div implements JComponentMixin` carries borders (with [SD_border_css_lossy](#SD_border_css_lossy)'s lossy CSS round-trip), the opaque flag, client properties, tooltip, the `PropertyChangeEvent`-per-property surface, and `getUIClassID` / `updateUI` / `setUI`. `java.awt.Panel` has **none** of them — no `setBorder`, no `setOpaque`, no client properties, no L&F. That much matches `SLabel`'s answer. The follow-up is what differs: *once the Swing half is subtracted, what is left?* For `SLabel` it was text plus a three-value alignment — small but real, and expressible only as CSS on a chosen host element. For `Panel` it is nothing. The **child list** belongs to Vaadin's `HasComponents` (and `ContainerMixin` explicitly *excludes* `add` / `remove` / `getComponent(int)` / `getComponents()` / `getComponentCount()` / `isAncestorOf` over the signature clash); the **`LayoutManager`** belongs to `LayoutStore` with `LayoutCss` dispatch; **`ContainerListener`** is `onNoop` per [SD_listeners_without_analog](#SD_listeners_without_analog). `java.awt.Panel` is the purest member of CLAUDE.md's **group 2** — a structural shell on a bare Vaadin element — in the whole surface: unlike `Box` (which at least owns strut/glue factories) it adds nothing to its base class but a default layout manager. The group-2 carve-out exists for exactly this shape and is cited explicitly rather than left implicit.
- **Two `setLayout` engines, one element, no owner.** `ContainerMixin.setLayout` takes `java.awt.LayoutManager`; `vaadinx.awt.Container.setLayout` takes the *ported* `vaadinx.awt.LayoutManager` — a distinct interface, because its methods reference `vaadinx.awt.Component`. The emulator therefore **cannot delegate** to the mixin: both would run, each writing container CSS to the same `Div` through a different path. That already happens on the Swing side (`SJPanel`'s ctor calls the mixin's `setLayout(new java.awt.FlowLayout())`, then `vaadinx.swing.JPanel`'s ctor writes `vaadinx.awt.FlowLayout` CSS over it) and they agree only because both funnel into `LayoutCss.flowLayoutCss` and both default to `CENTER`. Reproducing that coincidence in a new class with no state buys nothing.
- **`ContainerMixin` has no direct implementor.** Only `JComponentMixin extends ContainerMixin`; `SJPanel` and `SJToolBar` both reach it that way. SD_sbutton could lean on "`ComponentMixin` is AWT-level by construction — `SJTable` / `SJList` / `SJTree` already implement it standalone"; the container-level equivalent of that claim is **unproven**. An `SPanel` would be doing double duty: shipping a class nobody needs *and* serving as the first standalone `ContainerMixin` test. If that proof is worth having it deserves its own slice with its own motivation, not a rider on a four-line emulator.
- **The stage-3 story is already covered.** A migrator rewriting a view onto `:surrogates` who meets an AWT `Panel` reaches for `SJPanel` or a plain `Div`; there is no AWT-flavoured container behaviour they would be missing. The import-rewrite at the stage-2→3 boundary is where they change layers anyway.

**Alternatives considered.**

- **`SJPanel` as the emulator's peer** (the `JTextPane`-over-`SJEditorPane` shape). Rejected for the reason [SD_slabel](#SD_slabel) rejected it for `Label` — it puts a `JComponent`-level peer under an AWT-level emulator and the reused surrogate holds no machinery the emulator needs — and worse here, since `SJPanel`'s ctor eagerly writes `FlowLayout` CSS that the emulator's own `FlowLayout` then overwrites.
- **A new `SPanel extends Div implements ContainerMixin`.** Rejected per the four points above. **Recorded as the deferred option**, honestly rather than built: *if* someone later wants an AWT-shaped stage-3 container — `setBackground(Color)` / `setFont` / `addMouseListener` on a Div, which stock Vaadin does not give you — that is a legitimate but **independently motivated** slice, and it must resolve the two-engines problem before being adopted as `vaadinx.awt.Panel`'s peer. Its shape would be: ctors `SPanel()` / `SPanel(java.awt.LayoutManager)` mirroring the JDK pair, `_installSwingClass()` in the ctor, and **no stored state at all** — no `Store` carve-out is warranted and none should be invented to make a getter round-trip (R_vaadin_first).

**Accepted limitations.**

- **`addContainerListener` on a pure-surrogate container stays `onNoop`** (R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (a), blocked upstream — Vaadin 25 has no server-side child-list mutation event, SD_listeners_without_analog). This is the one place the *emulator* layer is strictly more capable: `vaadinx.awt.Panel` fires `componentAdded` / `componentRemoved` for real, browser-verified in D_awt_panel. A future `SPanel` could close it by overriding `HasComponents.add` / `remove` and firing itself — per-surrogate work, as SD_listeners_without_analog already notes.
- **No AWT-shaped container API in `:surrogates`.** `getComponent(int)` / `getComponents()` / `isAncestorOf` have no compatible Vaadin equivalent (`getChildren()` returns a `Stream`, not an array) and are simply absent from a pure-surrogate container — R_vaadin_first-sanctioned, since the surrogate's API is Vaadin-shaped. `removeAll()` and `getComponentCount()` need no work at all: `HasComponentsOfType`'s versions have identical signatures and compatible semantics. Worth naming one **semantic trap** rather than a signature clash: Vaadin's `getComponentAt(int)` means "child at index" while AWT's `getComponentAt(int,int)` means "hit-test at x,y" — different arity so both compile, but `panel.getComponentAt(0)` returns a child on a surrogate and a WARN + null on an emulator.

**Precedent.** Cite this entry for the "nothing left to hold" triage — a class whose entire delta over its own base is a default configuration value. Distinct from SD_no_sjoptionpane's "the UI functionality *is* the thing we can't do at this layer". Note also what is **not** a factor here: `java.awt.Panel` declares no public fields, so SD_slabel's numeric-constant collision trap has no analogue.

<a id="SD_sscrollbar"></a>
## SD_sscrollbar. `SScrollbar` surrogate — `java.awt.Scrollbar` on `RangeInput`; vertical is the default, and the fan-out cannot live here

`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SScrollbar extends com.vaadin.flow.component.html.RangeInput implements ComponentMixin, HasTooltip, ClickNotifier<SScrollbar>, KeyNotifier` — the AWT 1.0 scrollbar, backing [D_awt_scrollbar](../emulators/decisions.md#D_awt_scrollbar). Fourth surrogate in the AWT lane after [SD_sbutton](#SD_sbutton) / [SD_slabel](#SD_slabel) / [SD_schoice](#SD_schoice) / [SD_scheckbox](#SD_scheckbox), and the first to carry a value model. The design pass it was built from was `ideas/awt-scrollbar.md`, graduated into this entry and D_awt_scrollbar.

**Decision — a real surrogate, not `JScrollBar`'s inert `Div`.** The tempting move is to copy `vaadinx.swing.JScrollBar`: emulator-only, `Div` peer, full bookkeeping, listeners stored and never fired ([D_viewport_scrollbar_shadows](../emulators/decisions.md#D_viewport_scrollbar_shadows)'s "shadow JDK component"). D_viewport_scrollbar_shadows's justification is that *"the JDK JScrollBar instances don't reach the DOM"* because Vaadin `Scroller` owns scrollbar rendering — true for `javax.swing.JScrollBar`, and **false for `java.awt.Scrollbar` on a verifiable point**: AWT's scroll pane hands out `java.awt.ScrollPaneAdjustable` (a different `final` class that does not extend `Scrollbar`, package-private ctor), so no AWT API anywhere returns a `Scrollbar` the migrator did not construct. AWT 1.0 also had no slider, so a `Scrollbar` *was* the zoom / volume / colour knob. A control the migrator added to a layout that renders as an empty gap and never fires its listener is not a cosmetic gap — it is silent, un-WARNable, and past what R_layouts_close_enough licenses.

**Decision — the peer is `RangeInput`, and the deciding property is orientation.** `new Scrollbar()` is **vertical**, unlike every Swing slider, so vertical rendering is the common path rather than a corner case. `RangeInput` has a first-class two-value orientation API; `IntegerSlider` (the `SJSlider` host) is better on three counts — `Integer` value, `KeyNotifier` + `HasTooltip` for free, no `setEnabled` shadow — and loses on the only one that matters: it has **no orientation API at all**, so `SJSlider` renders vertical as `transform: rotate(-90deg)`.

**Decision — `writing-mode`, not `transform: rotate`, and the two are not interchangeable.** Measured 2026-08-24 on a static `<input type=range>` page in **Firefox 153** and **headless Chromium 151**, each control in a flex row whose EAST cell is sized by the control's own box:

| mechanism | visual box | **layout box** | EAST cell reserved | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `writing-mode: vertical-lr` | 16×129 / 20×160 | **16×129 / 20×160** | **20px / 24px** | both engines |
| `writing-mode: vertical-rl` + `direction: rtl` | same | same | 20px / 24px | both engines |
| `transform: rotate(-90deg)` | 16×129 / 20×160 | **129×16 / 160×20** | **133px / 164px** | broken, both engines |
| `setOrientation(VERTICAL)` alone | **129×129 square** / 20×160 | 129×129 / 20×160 | 133px / 24px | broken on Chromium |

(Chromium / Firefox figures.) `transform` rotates the visual and leaves the layout box at its pre-rotation dimensions, so it reserves a 133px cell for a control that renders 16px wide — the failure `SJProgressBar`'s javadoc records (*"collapses to zero width"*), [SD_sjslider](#SD_sjslider) carries as an accepted limitation and a latent bug. `writing-mode` changes intrinsic sizing, so the layout box genuinely becomes tall-and-narrow and the surrounding layout reflows. **This is why the `RangeInput` choice is not a re-run of the rotate gamble.**

**Decision — Vaadin's own `setOrientation` is stale, so the surrogate adds the standardized half.** Read verbatim from `flow-html-components` 25.2.4/25.2.6 sources, `setOrientation(VERTICAL)` sets only the pre-standardization MDN recipe: the non-standard `orient` attribute (Firefox), `appearance: slider-vertical` (**removed in Chromium 132**), and `writing-mode: bt-lr` (an IE-era value, never standardized). `writing-mode: vertical-lr` is absent, which is why the call alone renders a 129×129 square blob on Chromium 151 — and on Firefox it works *despite* the CSS, not because of it (Gecko computes `appearance: auto` and `writing-mode: horizontal-tb`, rejecting both declarations, and renders off the attribute). So the surrogate calls `setOrientation` **and** writes `writing-mode: vertical-lr` itself; Vaadin's stale `appearance` declaration is measured inert once a valid writing-mode is in force, so it is left in place rather than stripped. Filable upstream (R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (a) in origin), but two lines to work around, so nothing is deferred or WARNed.

**Bonus, and it is free:** `vertical-lr` puts the **minimum at the top**, value increasing downward — which is AWT `Scrollbar`'s own convention (verified visually at `value=0` in both engines: thumb at top). `direction: rtl` flips it to min-at-bottom, i.e. `JSlider`'s convention, without `scaleX(-1)`. One mechanism serves both lanes; the Swing-side application of that is parked in [#28](https://github.com/vaadin/swingbridge-emulators/issues/28) because both hosts there are web components whose shadow DOM owns the track layout, which this measurement says nothing about.

**Decision — `visibleAmount` is stored, the peer's `max` carries `maximum - visibleAmount`.** AWT's reachable band is `[minimum, maximum - visibleAmount]`, never `[min, max]`, and `<input type=range>`'s `max` *is* the largest legal value — so `visibleAmount` genuinely drives the peer rather than shadowing it, and `getMaximum()` reconstructs as `peer.max + visibleAmount`. Storing `maximum` and deriving `visibleAmount` is symmetric; this entry records which way it went so a reader of `getMaximum()` need not wonder. `setValues` — the JDK's single clamping funnel, all five steps verbatim — lives **here**, next to the peer writes, so the DOM can never hold an out-of-band value. The emulator runs the same funnel over its own fields (D_awt_scrollbar) and pushes the result, which passes through this one unchanged, since clamping is idempotent.

**Decision — `step` stays 1, and the reason is measured.** `java.awt.Scrollbar` has no `step`; the question was only whether `setUnitIncrement(n)` should write `n` into the peer's HTML `step`. It should not. AWT's `unitIncrement` is only an arrow delta over a continuous value space; HTML's `step` is a **value-space quantizer** enforced on drags and programmatic writes alike. Measured in Chromium 151 (`min=0 max=90 step=16`): `value=37` reads back **32**, `value=90` — the band's own top — reads back **80**, `value=5` reads back **0**. The decisive cost is not coarse arrows but **phantom events**: the surrogate holds the value server-side, so `setValue(37)` leaves the server on 37 and the browser on 32, and under EAGER the user's next touch posts back a rounding of the migrator's own earlier write as an event the user never made. So `unitIncrement` is stored and inert — R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (c), on a property the JDK documents as ignorable by the underlying control — and the migrators who set a non-default `unitIncrement` are largely the hand-rolled-scroller idiom that is already out of scope via `Canvas`.

**Decision — no listener list here; the fan-out lives on the emulator.** `SButton` owns its `ActionListener` list because `ActionEvent`'s source parameter is `Object`. `AdjustmentEvent`'s is typed `java.awt.Adjustable`, and `SScrollbar` **cannot implement `Adjustable`**: `int getValue()` and `int getOrientation()` have the same erasure as `RangeInput`'s `Double getValue()` / `Orientation getOrientation()` with incompatible return types — hard compile errors, not style choices. So the surrogate could not construct an event naming itself, and an inner `Adjustable` adapter as the source would make `(Scrollbar) e.getAdjustable()` a `ClassCastException` in migrated code. The surrogate therefore exposes only Vaadin's `addValueChangeListener` (its stage-3 API, and the R_vaadin_first-correct answer anyway) and D_awt_scrollbar bridges from it. This is the one structural departure from the SD_sbutton/SD_slabel shape. The int-typed `getValue` / `getOrientation` live on the surrogate as `getIntValue` / `getAwtOrientation` — deliberately *not* JDK spellings, since the JDK spellings are taken.

**Decision — `valueIsAdjusting` is browser-driven, which is more than `SJSlider` manages.** EAGER value-change mode makes `input` events arrive during a drag; a raw DOM `change` listener marks drag-end. So the flag is true for exactly the span AWT reports it for, rather than being the round-trip field `SJSlider` settled for. Cost, stated plainly: a keyboard arrow press emits *both* events, so one unit gesture produces two `AdjustmentEvent`s (one adjusting, one not) where AWT produces one. Suppressing the second would break the "commit when `isAdjusting` goes false" idiom that is the property's whole point, so the duplicate is the lesser evil; the gesture-length heuristic (exactly one `input` before `change` ⇒ keyboard) is left unwritten.

**Decision — `setEnabled` overrides but does *not* use SD_sbutton's redirect.** `RangeInput` declares `setEnabled` concretely and keeps its own `enabled` field, reconciling it with `readOnly` in a private method. `ComponentMixin.super.setEnabled(...)` would route past that and leave the field stale so `isEnabled()` disagrees with the DOM. Correct shape here: fire the `"enabled"` PCE, then call `super.setEnabled(...)`. **This diverges from SD_sbutton and is easy to get wrong by copying it.**

**Accepted limitations.** `getAccessibleContext` WARNs and returns null (R_match_swing_errors (b)). R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (c), all four with no upstream ticket to file because the ceiling is the HTML control rather than a Vaadin gap: `visibleAmount` as thumb *length* (no HTML control has a resizable thumb — the property is functional but invisible); `blockIncrement` (PageUp/PageDown move by a browser-chosen, unsettable amount); every browser-sourced `AdjustmentEvent` reporting `TRACK` (nothing distinguishes an end-arrow click from a track click from a keypress, and a wrong type is worse than a consistently honest one); and `setValueIsAdjusting` from user code being advisory — that last one *would* become blocked-upstream (a) if Vaadin exposed a pointer-down/up signal on `RangeInput`. **It renders as a slider, not a scrollbar** — no end arrows, fixed thumb — which reads correctly for the value-knob idiom that survives and wrongly for the hand-rolled scroller that is already out of scope. One more, from the probe: **arrow keys read backwards** on a min-at-top vertical bar (`ArrowDown` decreases, i.e. moves the thumb up) — the generic `<input type=range>` default rather than a writing-mode artefact, accepted as documented rather than remapped.

**Validation.** `SScrollbarTest` (24 — ctor defaults including AWT's VERTICAL, the `setValues` clamping table across every extreme int, `getMaximum` reconstruction with the peer's `max` asserted directly, the orientation bijection with its CSS and IAE message, `step` staying 1 with the whole band still reachable, increment clamp-to-1, `valueIsAdjusting` driven from the browser edge and cleared by a synthetic DOM `change`, the inherited `ComponentMixin` surface on a non-`JComponent` host with tooltip / key / mouse listeners WARN-free, `paramString`'s exact tail, and a WARN-free happy path).

**Decision — a zero value must be written to the DOM explicitly.** `setValues` writes the element's `value` property directly after `setValue(...)`, and this is not belt-and-braces. A value equal to `RangeInput`'s own initial `0.0` makes Vaadin's `setValue` write nothing, and an `<input type=range>` carrying no value attribute renders its thumb at the **midpoint of its range** — so a fresh `new Scrollbar()` (value 0, AWT's default) rendered at 45 on a 0–90 band while the server correctly reported 0. Browser-caught during D_awt_scrollbar's verification pass; Karibu reads the server-side value and sees nothing wrong. **Generalizes:** any surrogate whose peer has a meaningful "no value set" rendering needs its initial value forced onto the element, because the server-side round-trip passes either way.

**Precedent.** Cite this entry for the **"the peer cannot be the event source"** shape: when a Swing/AWT event type declares its source as a *typed interface* the Vaadin host cannot implement, the listener fan-out belongs on the emulator and the surrogate exposes only its Vaadin event. `java.awt.List`'s `ItemSelectable` is the next candidate to check against it. Cite it also for `writing-mode`-over-`transform` whenever a vertical control comes up.

<a id="SD_slist"></a>
## SD_slist. `SList` surrogate — `java.awt.List` on a native `<select multiple size=N>`, not on `Grid`; the DOM is the item store and silence is the platform's

`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SList extends com.vaadin.flow.component.HtmlComponent implements ComponentMixin, Focusable<SList>, java.awt.ItemSelectable`, `@Tag("select")` — the AWT 1.0 scrolling list box, backing [D_awt_list](../emulators/decisions.md#D_awt_list). Fifth surrogate in the AWT lane after [SD_sbutton](#SD_sbutton) / [SD_slabel](#SD_slabel) / [SD_schoice](#SD_schoice) / [SD_scheckbox](#SD_scheckbox) / [SD_sscrollbar](#SD_sscrollbar), and the first with items, a selection and two event types at once. The design pass it was built from was `ideas/awt-list.md`, graduated into this entry and D_awt_list and deleted.

**Decision — the peer is a native `<select>`, and the reason is that it is the same widget.** `java.awt.List` and an HTML `<select size=N>` are both 1995-era scrolling selection boxes, so most of what a peer would otherwise *simulate*, the element simply **is**. `size` is AWT's `rows`; `multiple` is the runtime `setMultipleMode` flip; the `<option>` children are the item store; and writing `option.selected` fires no `change`. Vaadin ships no `<select>` wrapper (checked `flow-html-components` 25.2.6: `Input`, `NativeButton`, `NativeLabel`, `NativeDetails`, `NativeTable*`, `RangeInput`, no `select`), so the host is a bare `HtmlComponent` — SD_sscrollbar's shape, one lane precedent later.

**Decision — `size` is emitted as `max(2, rows)`, and the floor is load-bearing.** A `<select>` renders as a **drop-down** at `size=1` without `multiple`, and as a list box otherwise. Measured 2026-08-25 in **Firefox 153**, reading each element's height and its first `<option>`'s own client rect (an option in a drop-down has a zero-size box, because it is not in the page's layout at all):

| markup | element height | `options[0]` box | renders as |
|---|---|---|---|
| `<select size=4>` | 114px | 26px, visible | list box, ~4 rows |
| `<select multiple>` (no `size`) | 114px | 26px, visible | list box — the browser default is 4 rows, which is AWT's own `DEFAULT_VISIBLE_ROWS` |
| `<select>` | 32px | 0px, not laid out | **drop-down** |
| `<select multiple size=1>` | 39px | 30px, visible | one-row list box; `multiple` forces list-box mode even at `size=1` |

AWT's `rows` defaults to 4 but `new List(1)` is legal, so the one reachable drop-down case is floored away. This is a **rendering** floor only: `getRows()` returns the JDK value verbatim, negatives included, because `rows` never round-trips through the attribute — hence D_awt_list's shadow.

**Rejected peers, with the specifics that killed each.** `Grid<Integer>` was the design pass's own recommendation for two sessions and is the closest runner-up: it handles the runtime mode flip via `setSelectionMode`, and `SJList` proves `ComponentMixin` sits on it cleanly. It loses on four counts the native element wins outright — `rows` has no answer (SD_sjlist dropped `visibleRowCount` with a WARN, which a *constructor* cannot do without failing the exit gate), the silent-mutation contract rests on `SelectionEvent.isFromClient()` holding across ctrl-click / shift-range / checkbox paths, MULTI renders a checkbox column where AWT paints highlighting, and three name clashes need working around (`getSelectedItems(): Set<T>` is a hard error, and `select(int)`/`deselect(int)` bind to *our* method rather than `Grid.select(T)` — a silent overload trap `SJList.pushSelectionToPeer` walks into). It remains the abort path if the theming cost is ever judged unacceptable. `VirtualList` is **not** the lighter option it looks like: `javap` against 25.2.6 shows `setRenderer`, `scrollToIndex`, data-provider plumbing and **no selection model at all** — not even `ClickNotifier` — so selection, highlight CSS and ctrl/shift range logic would all be hand-rolled, with the selection as a pure server-side field. More work than Grid, and R_vaadin_first-worse. `ListBox` / `MultiSelectListBox` are two classes for one runtime flip, and carry two traps verified by compiling against `vaadin-list-box-flow-25.2.3`: `ListBoxBase.rebuild()`'s first statement is `removeAll()`, which an AWT `removeAll()` would silently override (javac accepts it), so every internal rebuild would clear the *items*; and `MultiSelectListBox.presentationToModel` collects selected items into a `Set`, so selecting both duplicate rows yields one element while `modelToPresentation` lights up every index equal to a selected item. `<input list=…>` + `<datalist>` is a single-value autocomplete over a text input — `Choice`/`JComboBox` territory, not an always-open scrolling box (and there is no `<input type="list">`).

**Decision — no item collection; the `<option>` children are the model.** `getItemCount()` is `getChildCount()`, `getItemAt(i)` reads the child's text, `addItem` inserts an `Element`. R_vaadin_first in its strongest form: there is nothing to keep in sync, and **duplicate item Strings are free** — positional `<option>`s need none of [SD_sjlist](#SD_sjlist)'s index identity, no `KeyMapper` collapses two equal Strings onto one key, and the "select one Apple, both light up" failure a `Grid<String>` or `MultiSelectListBox` peer would have had cannot arise.

**Decision — the selection mirror is an R_swing_is_truth write-through, and it needs no `preventPeerEvents` guard.** The browser owns a native `<select>`'s selection and the server cannot read it synchronously, so `SList` keeps a `SortedSet<Integer>` written by the `change` bridge and written *through* by every programmatic `select` / `deselect`. That is not an R_vaadin_first shadow cache — it is the only server-readable copy. The guard is unnecessary for a reason worth stating: our own write fires no `change`, so the feedback loop the guard exists to break **cannot form**. Contrast every value-carrying surrogate in the tree, all of which need one.

**Decision — silence is structural, not enforced.** AWT documents `select`/`deselect` as firing no `ItemEvent` ("the only way to trigger an ItemEvent is by user interaction"), and *every* `java.awt.List` mutator is likewise silent. On a `Grid` peer that contract would rest on `SelectionEvent.isFromClient()` holding across ctrl-click, shift-range and checkbox paths — an assumption needing its own verification. Here a DOM `change` exists **only** because a user committed a selection. Measured: four programmatic `option.selected` writes plus a `selectedIndex` assignment produced zero events.

**Decision — the selection comes back as a joined String, not an array.** The `change` listener carries `addEventData("[...event.target.selectedOptions].map(o=>o.index).join(',')")`. Joined on purpose: the payload stays a plain scalar, so nothing depends on how an array-valued expression marshals. **Verified end to end** against a running Sampler in Firefox 153 — picking index 2 delivered `getItem() = 2 (Integer)`, and a two-row selection delivered both. The ordering question is settled with it: with indices 2, 0, 3 selected *in that order* the expression returns `"0,2,3"`, i.e. `selectedOptions` is in **document order**, matching the X11 peer (AWT's own peerless `selected` array is in selection order, and is the thing the peer overrides). An unparseable payload is dropped rather than propagated, so a malformed event cannot take the bridge down.

**Decision — two render-only setters, for a caller that keeps the model itself.** `setItemsAndSelection(List<String>, int[])` and `setSelectedIndexes(int[])` show exactly what they are given and apply none of the insert / remove / mode rules above, because the emulator keeps AWT's items and selection itself and flushes them here after each change ([D_awt_list](../emulators/decisions.md#D_awt_list)). The emulator's selection can hold what the mirror cannot, since the JDK's own array can — out-of-range, duplicate or unsorted entries, and more than one entry in single mode — so the setter renders what a `<select>` can show (in-range rows; the first one in single mode) and skips the rest. The item diff keeps the common prefix and suffix and touches only the rows between, so a caller flushing after every `add` costs one `<option>` per call rather than a rebuild. When the wanted selection differs from the mirror — the browser's view — it is also set with one `executeJs`, since the server-side `selected` properties lag the browser's own changes and Vaadin drops a property write whose server value is unchanged.

**Decision — the fan-out lives here, unlike SD_sscrollbar's.** That entry flagged `java.awt.List`'s `ItemSelectable` as the next candidate for the "peer cannot be the event source" shape. It is **not** one: `ItemSelectable` declares `getSelectedObjects` / `addItemListener` / `removeItemListener`, none of which clash with anything on `HtmlComponent`, so `SList` implements it and constructs `ItemEvent`s naming itself. Both listener lists therefore sit on the surrogate, SD_sbutton's shape. The one clash that survives on *any* Vaadin host is `getListeners(Class)` — Vaadin `Component` declares `protected Collection<?> getListeners(Class<? extends ComponentEvent>)`, the same erasure with an incompatible return type — so it is dropped here and inherited from `vaadinx.awt.Component` on the emulator.

**Decision — `ItemEvent` carries the `Integer` index.** `XListPeer` posts `Integer.valueOf(index)` at all nine of its post sites. This differs from `Checkbox` (posts the label String) and `Choice` (posts the item String) — per class, every time, and worth re-reading rather than inferring.

**Decision — flipping to single mode keeps the first selected row.** Measured: with indices 0, 2, 3 selected, setting `multiple = false` left `selectedIndex == 0` and one selected option, and fired no `change`. AWT's documented rule is "the item with the location cursor stays, else all are deselected"; there is no location cursor in a browser, so the mirror is trimmed to match the browser. A third outcome, inside the same R_best_effort_behaviour latitude and closer to AWT than dropping the selection wholesale.

**Accepted limitations.** **No Aura theming** — a native `<select multiple>` renders as the browser's own list box. SD_sscrollbar's bargain, and here arguably fidelity rather than a gap; it is a *decision*, not a deferred item. **No virtualization** — every item is an `<option>` in the DOM, which matches an eagerly-populated JDK class whose X11 peer also paints every row. R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (c): Enter's `ActionEvent` reports the *selected* row where AWT reports the *focused* one (a native `<select>` exposes no focus index; the two differ only in MULTI mode), the single-mode re-click that AWT re-fires as `SELECTED` (an `XListPeer` backward-compatibility bug, explicitly commented as such — not a goal), and `getAccessibleContext` WARNing to null (R_match_swing_errors (b)). Not a gap and recorded so nobody "fixes" it: single-mode selection fires **no** `DESELECTED` for the implicitly replaced row, because AWT does not either.

**Validation.** Browser-verified against a running Sampler (Firefox 153) — rendering, the read-back wire, `dblclick`, Enter, `scrollIntoView`, the mode flip and duplicate-row selection all confirmed; details in D_awt_list. Browserlessly, `SListTest` (30 — the `size` floor across four row counts, `<option>` children including a duplicate pair independently selectable, both `addItem` coercions, `removeItemRange`'s downward loop in both its throwing shapes, the selection mirror through single/multiple and through index shifting on insert and remove, every programmatic path asserted silent, the `change` delta producing one `ItemEvent` per moved index with an `Integer` payload, double-click and Enter `ActionEvent`s, a malformed payload surviving, AWT listener order, and a WARN-free happy path).

**Decision — the DOM-event tests must satisfy the server-side filter.** Flow re-checks a `setFilter` expression **server-side**, using the expression itself as the event-data key, so a synthetic `keydown` must carry `"event.key === 'Enter'"` → `true` or the listener never runs. Found by a test that failed for the right reason; recorded because the client-side reading of `setFilter` is the intuitive one and would send the next author hunting in the wrong layer.

**The two defects the browser caught, and no test could.** Both are initial-render disagreements between the server and the DOM, and both were invisible to 1300+ green tests.

1. **The browser auto-selected row 0 on every fresh render** while `getSelectedIndexes()` correctly answered empty. Two causes, needing two fixes. HTML's *selectedness setting algorithm* requires a **single**-select `<select>` to keep exactly one option selected, so the browser picks index 0 the instant the first `<option>` arrives — and a fresh `java.awt.List` has no selection. Worse, the *multi*-select panes had it too, because `multiple` was written as a **property**: Vaadin's property updates land after the children, so at the moment the options arrived the element was still single-select, and flipping `multiple` afterwards does not clear what was already selected. Fix: write `multiple` as an **attribute** (so it is in the initial markup — and therefore `isMultipleMode()` reads `hasAttribute`, since Vaadin's attribute and property maps are separate), plus a `clearBrowserSelection()` that pushes `selectedIndex = -1` whenever the mirror is empty, from the ctor and after every mutation. It uses `executeJs` rather than `setProperty` deliberately: Vaadin suppresses a property write whose server-side value is unchanged, so after a user selection the server still holds the `-1` it last wrote and precisely the update that matters would be dropped.

2. **A Sampler readout wired to only some of its update paths.** Demo 3's list had no `ItemListener`, so its state readout refreshed on a button click but not on a browser selection — displaying stale text. The exit gate drives that pane through buttons, which refresh it, so every server-side check passed.

**Generalizes, and this is the reusable part.** [SD_sscrollbar](#SD_sscrollbar) already recorded that *any surrogate whose peer has a meaningful "no value set" rendering needs its initial value forced onto the element*. Two more, now that the shape has recurred: **a peer property the browser acts on while children are being inserted must be an attribute, not a property** — the property update is a separate, later message; and **a Sampler readout with both a browser-driven and a button-driven path to the same state must be asserted after the browser one**, because the gate exercises the wired path and sees nothing wrong.

**Precedent.** Cite this entry for **"prefer the native element when the JDK widget predates the web platform's divergence"**: `Choice`→`Select` was a Vaadin-component answer because a `Choice` *is* a dropdown, but where the AWT widget and an HTML primitive are the same 1995 control, the primitive carries semantics a component would have to re-implement — and it carries them for free. Weigh it against the theming cost, which is the only thing on the other side.

<a id="SD_sscrollpane"></a>
## SD_sscrollpane. `SScrollPane` surrogate — `java.awt.ScrollPane` on Vaadin `Scroller`; the scroll channel is server→browser only, and the class holds no state at all

`com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.SScrollPane extends com.vaadin.flow.component.orderedlayout.Scroller implements ContainerMixin` — AWT's scrolling container, backing [D_awt_scrollpane](../emulators/decisions.md#D_awt_scrollpane). Sixth surrogate in the AWT lane after [SD_sbutton](#SD_sbutton) / [SD_slabel](#SD_slabel) / [SD_schoice](#SD_schoice) / [SD_scheckbox](#SD_scheckbox) / [SD_sscrollbar](#SD_sscrollbar) / [SD_slist](#SD_slist), and the only one whose whole design question was a *channel* rather than a widget.

**Decision — the peer is `Scroller`, because two of its four behavioural methods map onto AWT almost without loss.** `setContent(Component)` *is* AWT's single-child rule, enforced the same replace-don't-append way; `ScrollDirection` covers the scrollbar display policy at the fidelity [D_scrollbar_policy_mapping](../emulators/decisions.md) already accepted. The mapping is a **one-dimensional three-arm switch**, not D_scrollbar_policy_mapping's 3×3 cross-product, because AWT has one policy for both axes: `SCROLLBARS_NEVER` → `NONE`, `SCROLLBARS_AS_NEEDED` → `BOTH`, and `SCROLLBARS_ALWAYS` → `BOTH` + WARN (R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (a), blocked-upstream — same web component and same gap as D_scrollbar_policy_mapping, so the same classification rather than a second stance).

**Rejected — reusing `SJScrollPane` as the peer**, the [D_jtextpane](../emulators/decisions.md#D_jtextpane) `JTextPane`-over-`SJEditorPane` shape. SD_sbutton and SD_slabel both ran this test and both came out the same way. `SJScrollPane implements JComponentMixin`, which brings border CSS with its lossy round-trip, client properties, the opaque flag, ancestor listeners, tooltip, and `getUIClassID`/`updateUI`/`setUI(ScrollPaneUI)` — **none of which `java.awt.ScrollPane` has**, being a `Container` rather than a `JComponent`. D_jtextpane's precedent earns its keep only when the reused surrogate holds *large machinery the emulator needs*; there it was the RTE HTML codec, here it would be one `setContent` override under a JComponent tail we must not inherit.

**Rejected — a raw `Div` + `overflow: auto`**, which is what the lane's first sizing assumed for this row ("mostly `Div`-peered like `Box`/`JViewport`"). Wrong for D_jscrollpane's own stated reason: `Box` / `JViewport` / `JScrollBar` peer on a bare `Div` precisely because they have *no behaviour left*, being subsumed by a real scroll container. `ScrollPane` **is** the scroll container — it is the `SJScrollPane` of its lane, not the `JViewport`.

**Decision — the scroll channel is server→browser only, and the read direction is not built.** `scrollTo(x, y)` runs `executeJs("this.scrollTop=$0;this.scrollLeft=$1")`, the [SD_caret_selection](#SD_caret_selection) shape (server value is the source of truth, fire-and-forget). There is **no `scroll` DOM listener**, no span model, and no callback seam. The four-line `addEventData` + `throttle(100)` stack that would build the read direction is verified-available and recorded verbatim in the design doc; it is descoped under R_infra_not_surface because no migration target has asked, not because of traffic — a throttled `scroll` listener emits only while a gesture is in flight, roughly what a Vaadin `Grid` already does natively.

**Decision — `SScrollPane` declares no fields.** The `ScrollDirection` lives on the `Scroller`, and the JDK-shaped display-policy int lives on the emulator (D_scrollbar_policy_mapping's split). The scroll offset would have been this class's one legitimate holding — a browser fact, the [SD_focus_tracker](#SD_focus_tracker) shape — but with nothing reporting in it is no longer a browser fact, merely the last value the emulator pushed, and the emulator's two `ScrollPaneAdjustable`s already own it. Keeping a second copy here would be the shadow-cache anti-pattern R_vaadin_first exists to forbid. Likewise **no `Registrations` holder and no listener seam**: R_vaadin_first's shared-subscription shape presupposes a Vaadin subscription, and there is none. A callback seam with nothing on the far end is the listener analogue of the dead hook R_no_vaadin_in_api limb 2 rejects.

**Decision — `setLayout` is overridden to throw on the surrogate too, not only on the emulator.** `ContainerMixin`'s default stores into `LayoutStore` and writes layout CSS — right for a panel, wrong here, since `java.awt.ScrollPane.setLayout` is `public final` and throws `AWTError("ScrollPane controls layout")` even for `null`. A stage-3 pure-surrogate user reaching for it deserves the same answer as a stage-2 one. `getLayout()` answers null directly rather than via the store, so the contract does not depend on the throw holding.

**Decision — the `isAutoScrollingType` guard is duplicated from `SJScrollPane`, not lifted into a shared util.** The design pass proposed `com.vaadin.swingbridge.surrogates.util.ScrollPosition` on R_infra_not_surface grounds, to serve D_viewport_scrollbar_shadows's parked `JViewport.addChangeListener` as well. The descope moots it: what survives is a two-line `executeJs`, and building the shared helper against a read direction we are not building is the "don't expand surface area preemptively" half of the same rule. The guard is ~15 lines and lifting it means touching a Swing-lane class for an AWT-lane slice. Both javadocs name the twin so a future extraction finds both ends. **Net: this slice touched no existing source file in either module.**

**Browser-probed 2026-08-25** (Playwright against the Vaadin 25 docs' own Scroller demo), which retired the design's highest-risk unknown — whether `Scroller` puts the scrollable overflow on the host or an inner shadow part. The shadow root is `<!----><slot></slot>`: **no inner wrapper exists**, `overflow: auto` is on the host, and the host's `scrollHeight`/`clientHeight` were 859/300 over the demo content. `host.scrollTop = 200` was accepted and read back immediately; `= 99999` clamped *synchronously* to 559. So `this.scrollTop` addresses the real scrolling box and needs no `shadowRoot` selector — and the synchronous clamp is what makes D_awt_scrollpane's no-clamp divergence safe. Two findings beyond that: `scroll` is `bubbles: false, composed: false` (an ancestor listener and a `document` capture listener both saw nothing), so a delegated UI-wide listener of the `FocusTracker` shape is **not** an option if the read direction is revived; and the host carries `tabindex="0"`, consistent with `Scroller implements Focusable`.

**Accepted limitations.** `SCROLLBARS_ALWAYS` renders as AS_NEEDED (R_match_swing_errors (a), one WARN at ctor time). **User scrolls are invisible to the server** — the descope, and the substantive one: see D_awt_scrollpane for the four migrated idioms it breaks. Nothing renders or populates wrongly as a result; the browser owns overflow, and a `Grid` or `VirtualList` inside the pane lazy-loads over its own data-communicator channel without consulting the enclosing offset.

**Validation.** `SScrollPaneTest` (12 — the three-arm policy switch with the `ALWAYS` WARN asserted and the IAE message verbatim, `ScrollPaneConstants` values throwing as the safe direction, `setContent` replacing rather than appending, the auto-scroll guard over `Grid` / `TextArea` / nested `Scroller` plus the ordinary-content and null-content paths, `setLayout` throwing for `null` too with `getLayout()` staying null, and a WARN-free happy path). The `scrollTo` push is deliberately **not** asserted — `executeJs` leaves no server-side trace Karibu can read, the stance `SListTest` and `SJEditorPaneTest` already take for their own shadow-DOM seams, and the probe above is the verification instead. The descope is pinned by its own test: `ElementListenerMap.getExpressions("scroll")` must be empty, so reviving the read direction means deleting a failing test rather than slipping past unnoticed.

**Browser-verified 2026-08-25** (Chromium via Playwright against a running Sampler), and it caught one defect no browserless test could.
