All three panes render 260px tall over 678px of content (max scroll 418), Demo 1's pair sits side by side at equal `y`, the scrolled child is a `display: grid` div of 40 `<span>` labels, and the page never scrolls sideways. A **real trusted wheel** (`page.mouse.wheel`, not a synthetic `WheelEvent` — an untrusted one runs no default action and silently proves nothing) scrolled the AS_NEEDED pane to 233 and left the NEVER pane at 0, so the policy mapping is confirmed at the level that matters. `Scroll to 200` drove `scrollTop` to exactly 200 with the readout showing `AdjustmentEvent value=200 — scrollPosition = (0,200)`; `Scroll to top` returned both to 0. **The descope behaves as documented**: after a user wheel to 333 the server readout still said `(0,200)`, stale and silent. The browser clamp is confirmed live — `scrollTop = 99999` became 418 synchronously. Zero `Unimplemented` / `onUnsupportedPeerShape` / exceptions in the server log across the whole route, and zero browser console errors.

**The defect the browser caught, and the tests could not.** Every pane initially had `scrollHeight == clientHeight`: **a `Scroller` with no height constraint grows to fit its content and therefore never scrolls at all**, so the panes rendered but were inert and the AS_NEEDED-vs-NEVER contrast was invisible. Both facts are browser-only — `scrollHeight` and `clientHeight` do not exist browserlessly, so no Karibu test could have failed. Fixed in the Sampler pane by bounding the peer's height (the Swing lane's `ScrollPanesPanel` gets the same bound from the D_inline_route_sizing route-sizing chain via a flex-fill wrap; a fixed height is used here because this pane puts two panes side by side and the exit gate wants the overflow deterministic). **Generalizes**: any future scroll-container slice must assert overflow in a browser, because a container that renders correctly and scrolls nothing looks identical to a working one in every server-side assertion.

<a id="SD_no_invented_events"></a>
## SD_no_invented_events. R_vaadin_first does not license inventing Swing events: `SWindow`'s `"visible"` property change removed

[D_window_fanout](../emulators/decisions.md#D_window_fanout) deleted the invented `"visible"` bound property from the emulator layer after a sweep of `java.desktop` established that **`JPopupMenu` is the only class in `java.awt` + `javax.swing` that fires one**. `SWindow` was firing it from three sites — the peer-originated open/close mirror, `setVisible`, and `dispose()` — one layer down, and D_window_fanout's sweep did not reach the surrogate module.

**Decision — remove all three.** AWT signals visibility with a `ComponentEvent` and nothing else; all three paths already fired `COMPONENT_SHOWN` / `COMPONENT_HIDDEN` alongside, so the property event was pure addition. A stage-3 migrator who keeps a `PropertyChangeListener("visible")` was getting callbacks the desktop never delivered, with no way to know SB-Emulators invented them.

**The reason this needed stating rather than just fixing: `:surrogates` had a plausible-looking excuse and it does not hold.** [R_vaadin_first](../CLAUDE.md#hard-rules) makes this layer Vaadin-first and explicitly *narrows* the Swing API surrogates reproduce, and [R_decline_effect_only](../CLAUDE.md#hard-rules)'s keep-the-state stance is emulator-scoped by construction — so one could argue the surrogate layer is free here. It is not, and the asymmetry runs only one way: R_vaadin_first licenses **dropping** Swing API a surrogate cannot back with a Vaadin counterpart; it says nothing about **adding** Swing API the JDK does not have. `addPropertyChangeListener` is a legitimate Swing-flavoured helper on a surrogate (R_vaadin_first's own listener rule), but what it delivers must be events real Swing delivers. Inventing an event is not a narrower surface, it is a *wrong* one — and unlike a drop, which surfaces as a missing feature the migrator can see, an invented event is silent in exactly the way [R_no_vaadin_in_api](../CLAUDE.md#hard-rules) limb 2 describes.

**Validation.** One test in `SFrameTest` pinning the absence across all three paths — programmatic show, peer-originated close, dispose-while-visible — while asserting the four `ComponentEvent`s AWT *does* send still fire. Pinned rather than merely deleted, so the absence is as deliberate as the presence was.

**Found, not fixed here — the rest of the surrogate fan-out is unaudited.** `:surrogates` fires ~60 distinct bound properties, and a spot-check of four suspicious ones against JDK 25 found three more invented: `columns` (6 sites — neither `JTextField.setColumns` nor `JTextArea.setColumns` fires anything, both just `invalidate()`), `focusLostBehavior` (5 sites — `JFormattedTextField.setFocusLostBehavior` assigns the field and returns), and `text` on the text-component path (`JTextComponent.setText` fires nothing, though `AbstractButton.setText` and `JLabel.setText` genuinely do, so this one is per-site). That is a front of its own, swept as [SD_reverse_fanout_rows](#SD_reverse_fanout_rows) rather than smuggled into this entry.

<a id="SD_property_fanout_audit"></a>
## SD_property_fanout_audit. Surrogate property fan-out audited against JDK 25: 21 invented events removed, and the rule that decides the other direction

The audit [SD_no_invented_events](#SD_no_invented_events) called for. `:surrogates` fired **118** `firePropertyChange` sites across 34 files; every `(class, property)` pair was checked against the JDK 25 setter's **body**. **21 sites were firing events real Swing does not fire**, across 11 property names.

**Method — and it is the point, because the obvious sweep is the wrong one.** [D_window_fanout](../emulators/decisions.md#D_window_fanout) missed `JDialog.setJMenuBar` by grepping SB-Emulators for `firePropertyChange`, which answers *"where does SB-Emulators fire?"* — and tells you nothing about where SB-Emulators fires **and should not**. So this pass built the JDK side first: every `firePropertyChange` in `java.awt` + `javax.swing` (1128 classes), with `String FOO_PROPERTY = "foo"` constants resolved so `TEXT_CHANGED_PROPERTY` compares as `"text"`, then a per-class ancestor walk. Only 28 identifiers failed to resolve, all of them accessibility constants or the generic `firePropertyChange(String, …)` overloads' parameters — none load-bearing. Every candidate the diff produced was then confirmed by reading the JDK method body, because the scanner can only prove absence within the ancestry it computed.

**What was invented, and why each is a trap rather than a slip.** The pattern is a *cover method*: a Swing setter that delegates to a model or to a layout hint, where firing looks natural and the JDK deliberately does not.

| property | sites | the JDK setter |
|---|---|---|
| `columns` | 6 | `JTextField.setColumns` / `JTextArea.setColumns` assign and call `invalidate()`. A layout hint, not a bound property |
| `focusLostBehavior` | 5 | `JFormattedTextField.setFocusLostBehavior` validates the argument, assigns, returns |
| `enabled` | 2 | see below — the interesting one |
| `selectionMode` | 2 | `JList` / `JTable` delegate to the selection model; the notification is its `ListSelectionEvent` |
| `minimum`, `maximum`, `model` | 3 | `JProgressBar` only — see below |
| `actionCommand` | 1 | `AbstractButton.setActionCommand` is one line: `getModel().setActionCommand(…)` |
| `contentType` | 1 | `JEditorPane.setContentType` fires nothing; a kit swap reaches `setEditorKit`, which fires **`"editorKit"`** — a different property, and one SB-Emulators has no editor-kit concept to carry |
| `echoChar` | 1 | `JPasswordField.setEchoChar` assigns, then `repaint()` + `revalidate()` |

**`JProgressBar` is the sharp case, and it is why the unit of audit is the pair and not the name.** `AbstractButton`, `JSlider`, `JTable`, `JList`, `JTree`, `JComboBox` and `JSpinner` all genuinely fire `"model"`; `JProgressBar.setModel` fires only `AccessibleContext.ACCESSIBLE_VALUE_PROPERTY` — on the *accessible context's own* listener list, which `addPropertyChangeListener` on the component never reaches. Likewise `JSlider.setMinimum` / `setMaximum` fire and `JProgressBar`'s delegate silently to the model. Reasoning by family would have got both backwards.

**`enabled` moved rather than being deleted, because the JDK puts the fire one level down.** `java.awt.Component.setEnabled` delegates to the deprecated `enable()`, which fires only the accessibility property; the bound `"enabled"` is **`JComponent`'s override**. SB-Emulators had it on `ComponentMixin`, so every AWT-level surrogate fired it wrongly. The fire now lives on `JComponentMixin` — mirroring the JDK's own placement — which leaves the eight AWT-level surrogates (`SButton`, `SLabel`, `SChoice`, `SCheckbox`, `SList`, `SScrollbar`, `SScrollPane`, `SWindow`) correctly silent, and `SScrollbar`'s own duplicate override removed.

That relocation exposed a **structural residue worth naming**: `SWindow` takes `JComponentMixin` for its *helper* surface (border, tooltip, name), not because a window is a `JComponent` — and `java.awt.Window`, `Frame`, `JFrame`, `JDialog`, `JWindow` are none of them `JComponent`s. So `SWindow` overrides `setEnabled` to suppress the mixin's JComponent-level fan-out, one override covering all five by inheritance. A mixin whose name tracks a JDK class it is not always standing in for will do this again; the fix is local, the shape is general.

**One gap in the opposite direction, fixed here: `SFrame.setResizable`.** `java.awt.Frame.setResizable` fires `"resizable"` (`Frame.java:646`) and `SFrame`'s override **fully honours** the flag through Vaadin's `Dialog` — so unlike a drop-and-WARN property there was no R_vaadin_first exemption to hide behind, and the notification was simply owed.

**The rule that settles the rest of that direction, which is deliberately not swept here.** The JDK fires ~119 property names SB-Emulators' surrogates do not, and the count is misleading, because three classes of them are *correct* to omit:

1. **Painting / print / popup internals** — `graphicsContextScaleTransform`, `paintingForPrint`, `componentPopupMenu`, `inheritsPopupMenu`. Out of scope (R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (b)).
2. **Properties the surrogate does not have at all** — `setRolloverIcon`, `JTree.setEditable`, `JPopupMenu.setVisible`. Nothing to fire from.
3. **R_vaadin_first drop-and-WARN setters** — `SJTabbedPane.setModel`, `SJTable.setRowHeight`, … Here R_vaadin_first drops the **whole property**, not merely its effect, so there is no state whose change an event could describe. **This is the R_vaadin_first/[R_decline_effect_only](../CLAUDE.md#hard-rules) asymmetry doing its job, and it is the boundary SD_no_invented_events left implicit:** R_vaadin_first licenses declining a property outright; what it never licenses is *inventing* one. A declined property firing nothing is consistent; a declined property firing an event would be the worst of both.

So the residue that actually needs auditing is the fourth class — **a property the surrogate really implements, whose JDK setter fires, and which stays silent** — the shape `SFrame.setResizable` was. Enumerating those needs per-site reading rather than the ancestor diff (whose mixin resolution has known noise). **Swept as [SD_reverse_fanout_rows](#SD_reverse_fanout_rows):** 161 rows, 15 owed and fixed, 146 correct — and this entry's "~119 property names" estimate was low for the same reason D_missing_constants found `:emulators`' reverse list undercounted. One row above is corrected there: `componentPopupMenu` is *not* an out-of-scope popup internal, it is implemented.

**The `:emulators` half was swept the same day, and confirms this was not a one-module accident:** [D_property_fanout_audit](../emulators/decisions.md#D_property_fanout_audit) found **21 disagreements** there too — six of the *same* property names, and the *same* AWT-level-to-`JComponent` relocation of `"enabled"`, arrived at independently. It also found the flaw in this entry's method: a body grep is the wrong predicate, because `JComponent.setToolTipText` and `JToolBar.setRollover` fire only by delegating one hop to `putClientProperty`. Neither is a surrogate-side problem (`JComponentMixin.setToolTipText` fires directly and correctly — a surrogate has no client-property table to route through, that would be a shadow `Store`), but the lesson applies to any future pass.

**Validation.** `surrogates` 1324 green. **Seven tests asserted the invented events and were inverted, not deleted** — `focusLostBehavior` (×2), `columns` (×2), `echoChar`, `contentType`, and `ComponentMixinTest`'s `setEnabled auto-fires enabled PCE`, which was asserting at the AWT level where the JDK is silent. Three added: the `"enabled"` fire at `JComponentMixin` level (so the relocation is pinned from both sides), its suppression on a window surrogate, and `SFrame.setResizable`'s restored event. The other 14 invented sites had **no test at all**, which is its own finding — nothing was holding them in place, and nothing would have caught them.

<a id="SD_sjrootpane_structural"></a>
## SD_sjrootpane_structural. `SJRootPane`'s holder stance inverts — it owns the pane chain, lazily but structurally

**Decision.** `SJRootPane` **is** the containment, not a reference holder beside it. It owns the JDK's chain — `{glassPane, layeredPane}` as its own children, with the *layered* pane holding `{menuBar, contentPane}` — and gains `setJMenuBar` / `getJMenuBar`, which is where `javax.swing.JRootPane` puts them. Emulator-side counterpart and the full JDK-source citation: [D_rootpane_containment](../emulators/decisions.md#D_rootpane_containment).

This reverses [SD_sjframe](#SD_sjframe)'s "deliberately non-structural" stance and, with it, **retires SD_sjframe's pure-surrogate-rendering limitation**: a bare `new SJRootPane()` added to any Vaadin container now renders a working root pane. The only half a standalone pane cannot supply is `emul-has-contentpane`, which belongs to whoever owns the host.

`RootPaneScaffold` keeps exactly **one** structural step — planting the root pane in the host — plus the host-side class and the checking-flag routing. Content pane, menu bar and glass pane all delegate into the root pane. `SJFrame`'s content-pane mirror is gone: the two are the same object by construction rather than by an invariant something has to maintain.

**RootLayout as CSS.** The JDK's `RootLayout` stacks the bar and the content pane in y while reaching through the layered pane; SB-Emulators spells that as root pane `display: flex; flex-direction: column; position: relative`, layered pane `display: contents`, bar `flex: 0 0 auto`, content pane `flex: 1 1 auto; min-height: 0`. Measured a layout no-op versus the pre-root-pane DOM. `flex-basis` stays `auto` for the reason the pre-root-pane pass measured: with `1 1 0` an unsized window has no content height to size itself from and collapses.

**Both halves of that shape were measured against a live browser, and each rules out the obvious simplification** — worth stating because a refactor can drop either with nothing going red (Karibu sees no CSS):

- **Two *bare* `Div`s in these slots break the height chain, and cannot be repaired from the content pane.** Measured: the inline `@MainWindow` shell loses 152px, and adding `height: 100%` to the content pane fixes nothing, because `height: 100%` against a `height: auto` parent resolves to `auto`. `display: contents` measured *exactly* the unwrapped baseline, in both the Dialog and inline regimes — and it is Vaadin's own technique, `vaadin-dialog-content` sitting in this very slot with it. A fully-styled wrapper (`flex` column + `height: 100%` + `min-height: 0`) also works, so there are two viable shapes; the bare `Div` is the one that does not.
- **The root pane must stay a real box precisely *because* the layered pane is `display: contents`.** A `display: contents` box has no geometry, so an `inset: 0` glass pane inside one escapes to the nearest positioned ancestor — measured at the whole overlay including the header chrome, curtaining the title bar. Root pane a real flex box (`position: relative`) + layered pane `display: contents` was the only combination measured that gets the height chain *and* the glass pane's containing block right.

**`emul-has-glasspane` is deleted.** The glass pane insets against the root pane's `position: relative`, an ordinary light-DOM box, so the same declaration serves the Dialog and the inline `@MainWindow` paths where the old `::part(content)` reach only worked for one. The `z-index: 1000` stays and is what reconciles a genuine inversion: Swing paints the *lower* child index on top (hence glass pane at index 0), CSS paints later siblings on top.

### Two constraints that are load-bearing, not tidiness

- **`plant()` skips the DOM move when the element is already parented correctly.** The emulator plants its own AWT-typed panes as children of its `JRootPane`, which nests their peers through `Container.addImpl`, and *only then* pushes the references down here. Re-inserting would be a remove-then-add that leaves `Container`'s child list and the DOM disagreeing about the count. This is the seam that lets both layers compute placement and still agree.
- **`display: contents` goes on the instance, never on `JLayeredPane` as a type.** `JDesktopPane` extends `JLayeredPane`, and a universal rule would strip every user-instantiated one of its containing block. Hence the `emul-layeredpane` class, applied by `SHelper.markLayeredPane` to the pane a root pane owns.

### Lazy, and why that is still faithful

Panes are created on first read rather than in the constructor, where the JDK builds all three eagerly. The JDK's call *order* stays observable anyway, because the emulator layer sets all three — in the JDK's order, through the JDK's factory hooks — before any getter runs. Self-populating in the constructor was rejected: the emulator would then double-populate, leaving two layered panes with only one routed to.

### Accepted limitations

- **No z-ordering.** Layer constants round-trip and reorder nothing; Vaadin renders in DOM order and `JInternalFrame` renders as a Dialog overlay ([D_internal_frames](../emulators/decisions.md#D_internal_frames)). R_vaadin_first drop.
- **`setLayeredPane` does not migrate children**, faithfully — the JDK's swaps only its own child, orphaning the bar and content pane onto the outgoing pane. Which is why nothing but a look-and-feel calls it.
- **Glass-pane keyboard focus** is unchanged from D_glasspane_structural/SD_glasspane_structural: it swallows mouse input, tab still reaches behind it, exactly as real Swing's does.

### Validation

`SJRootPaneTest` gains the containment shape (root pane holds 2, layered pane holds `[bar, contentPane]` in order), the four CSS classes, the adopt-not-mint path when `getRootPane().getContentPane()` is read first, and `setJMenuBar(null)` giving the class back. Two assertions inverted, both stating the old stance as deliberate: the hosted glass pane no longer tags the host, and the standalone one is now structural rather than a holder. Full reactor green; browser measurements in D_rootpane_containment.

<a id="SD_reverse_fanout_rows"></a>
## SD_reverse_fanout_rows. The reverse direction SD_property_fanout_audit scoped: 161 rows, 15 owed fires, and four ways a scanner misreads a surrogate

The fourth class [SD_property_fanout_audit](#SD_property_fanout_audit) named and deliberately left — *"a property the surrogate really implements, whose JDK setter fires, and which stays silent"*, the shape `SFrame.setResizable` was. Swept 2026-08-27. **161 `(class, method, property)` rows where JDK 25 fires and `:surrogates` does not; 15 were owed and are fixed; 146 are correct and each class of them is named below.**

With this the fan-out audit is closed in both modules and in both directions.

**SD_property_fanout_audit's triage rule held exactly, and the numbers show why the R_vaadin_first/R_decline_effect_only asymmetry is real rather than rhetorical.** Of the 146 correct omissions, **55 are R_vaadin_first drop-and-WARN** setters — the property is declined whole, so there is no state whose change an event could describe — and **12 more** are `AbstractButtonMixin`'s icon and visual-flag setters routing through its private `warnDrop`, R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (c). A further **77 rows are methods the surrogate does not have at all**, which is the missing-method front, not this one. Run the same diff against `:emulators` and almost none of that survives, because [R_decline_effect_only](../CLAUDE.md#hard-rules) lets the emulator decline only the *effect*. Same JDK, same query, opposite answers — by design.

### What was owed: 15 fires across 6 files

| site | property | why declining was not available |
|---|---|---|
| `ComponentMixin.setPreferredSize` / `setMinimumSize` / `setMaximumSize` | `preferredSize`, `minimumSize`, `maximumSize` | all three write the Vaadin size for real |
| `JComponentMixin.setComponentPopupMenu` | `componentPopupMenu` | installs the menu on the Vaadin target |
| `SJInternalFrame.setClosable` / `setIconifiable` / `setMaximizable` | `closable`, **`iconable`**, `maximizable` | each shows or hides a real header button |
| `SJInternalFrame.setContentPane` / `setLayeredPane` / `setGlassPane` | `contentPane`, `layeredPane`, `glassPane` | real pane swaps through the scaffold / root pane |
| `SJTable.setRowSorter` | `sorter` | already fired `rowSorter`; the JDK fires **both** names |
| `SJTabbedPane.setTitleAt` / `setTabComponentAt` / `insertTab` | `indexForTitle`, `indexForTabComponent`, `indexForNullComponent` | titles and tab components are stored and rendered |

Three value shapes were carried over verbatim because none is guessable:

- **`iconable`, not `iconifiable`.** The JDK fires under the *field* name, not the setter's — [D_property_fanout_audit](../emulators/decisions.md#D_property_fanout_audit) hit the same trap on the emulator side, independently.
- **The `indexFor*` events report *which* tab changed, not what to**: a `-1` sentinel old value and the index as the new, so a listener has to re-read the pane. `setTitleAt`'s guard is `!=` — *reference* inequality — so two equal-but-distinct Strings fire where two interned-equal ones do not; and `insertTab` fires only on the null-component branch, carrying the argument index.
- **`setRowSorter` fires two names for one change.** Dropping the legacy `"sorter"` is a silent break for code registered under it.

**The size trio has one detail worth more than its three lines.** AWT keeps a `prefSizeSet` / `minSizeSet` / `maxSizeSet` flag purely so the *first* event's old value is `null` rather than a computed size. R_vaadin_first forbids a shadow flag, and none is needed: an unset CSS dimension already reads back `null`, so **Vaadin's own property absence is that flag**. `getSize()` cannot express it, because `parsePxOr1` floors it at 1 — so the old value comes from `getWidth() == null && getHeight() == null`, not from the getter. And because all three JDK properties collapse onto the one Vaadin size, `setPreferredSize` also changes what `getMinimumSize()` answers — yet fires only `"preferredSize"`, because firing the other two would be inventing events, which is precisely what [SD_no_invented_events](#SD_no_invented_events) forbids.

`setComponentPopupMenu` is a **correction to SD_property_fanout_audit**, which filed `componentPopupMenu` under "painting / print / popup internals — out of scope (R_match_swing_errors (b))". That was a misread of this body, which really does install the menu; the property is implemented and the event was owed.

### Four ways a scanner misreads `:surrogates`, all four found by being wrong first

The emulator-side diffs map `vaadinx.awt.Foo` onto `java.awt.Foo` by package prefix and are done. Nothing that simple works here, and each shortcut produced a confidently wrong count before it was caught:

1. **The join key is the JDK's class name, not the surrogate's.** `scan.py` records `SJLabel` and `JComponentMixin`; joining those against the JDK's `JLabel` and `JComponent` matches nothing, so *every fire that exists* reports as missing. The first run claimed **240 rows** including `SJLabel.setText`, which fires on line 230. Same family as [D_missing_constants](../emulators/decisions.md#D_missing_constants)'s `diff3.py` bug: the enumeration was right and the key was wrong.
2. **Inherited fan-out lives in a *mixin*, not in a surrogate.** `javax.swing.JComponent` is ported as `JComponentMixin`, `java.awt.Component` as `ComponentMixin`, `AbstractButton` as `AbstractButtonMixin`. Per-`S*`-class enumeration reports every `JComponent` property as missing from all thirty-odd surrogates at once. Mapping `XxxMixin` → `Xxx` restores a 1:1 diff — the mixin *is* the port.
3. **A declining setter may reach its WARN one hop away.** `AbstractButtonMixin` routes twelve R_vaadin_first drop-and-WARN setters through a private `warnDrop(method, arg)`, so a direct token scan calls all twelve real implementations that forgot to fire. This is D_property_fanout_audit's `putClientProperty` lesson recurring on the *classifier* side — and the one-hop walk then over-corrected, suppressing `ComponentMixin.setPreferredSize` because the `setSize(Dimension)` it delegates to has an `onNoop` in its **null guard**. A hop to a WARN is evidence, not a verdict, so it is now its own tag for a human to read rather than a silent suppression.
4. **A deliberate silent no-op reads as a real implementation.** `SWindow.setIconImages` has an empty body and a comment — no WARN token to find — because a browser tab has no window icon to set. Correctly silent, and indistinguishable from a gap by grep.

One smaller find in passing: **`SJRootPane.setDefaultButton` was already firing, through `PceSupport.of(this).fire(...)` rather than the mixin's `firePropertyChange`** — the module's only fire the scanner could not see, so a correct site was reported as missing. Rerouted through the inherited method: same listeners, and now visible to the audit.

### The 2 remaining `METHOD-REAL` rows

- `AbstractButton.getDisabledIcon` → `disabledIcon`. The JDK's fire sits on the *lazy L&F derivation* path (`UIManager.getLookAndFeel().getDisabledIcon`), R_match_swing_errors sub-bucket (b). Same verdict as [D_owed_events](../emulators/decisions.md#D_owed_events) reached for the emulator.
- `Window.setIconImages` → `iconImage`. The silent no-op above.

### Validation

Full reactor `clean install` green; `:surrogates` 1331 (7 new tests in a new `PropertyFanoutTest`), `:emulators` 1904. `diff6.py` re-runs to 146 rows: 77 no-method, 55 WARN-declined, 12 hop-declined, 2 named above.

**Two of the seven pins failed first and SB-Emulators was right both times**, for the same reason `:emulators`' D_event_value_audit pins did: `PropertyChangeSupport` filters equal values before dispatch, so `setMaximizable(false)` and `setIconifiable(false)` on a fresh frame deliver nothing — those buttons start hidden, matching the JDK's own defaults. The *direction* of a toggle in a fan-out test is load-bearing, not cosmetic.

---

<a id="SD_surrogate_theme_css"></a>
## SD_surrogate_theme_css. The surrogate half of the colour doctrine: the CSS rules apply, the colour table does not

**Status: implemented.** The emulator-side doctrine and its full rationale are
[D_theme_is_lookandfeel](../emulators/decisions.md#D_theme_is_lookandfeel); this entry records only
what `:surrogates` takes from it and what it declines.

**Decision.** `:surrogates` inherits the doctrine's **CSS mechanism rules** and **none** of its
Java-side machinery. The split is not a compromise — the two halves answer different questions, and
only one of them is about API fidelity.

### Declined: the colour table, the `UIResource` marker, and the three-way rule

[R_vaadin_first](../CLAUDE.md#hard-rules) rejects a `Store` shadow cache whose only job is
round-tripping a setter through its own getter, and a per-class table of Metal `ColorUIResource`
defaults is exactly that on this side. The table exists to serve two contracts that are
emulator-only:

- `getBackground()` never returning `null`, so migrated *Swing* code doing
  `getBackground().darker()` does not NPE; and
- `isBackgroundSet()` answering what real Swing answers.

A stage-3 migrator holds a Vaadin component and expects Vaadin semantics. They do not write
`getBackground().darker()` expecting Metal grey, so neither contract has a customer here. Unchanged,
deliberately: `ComponentMixin.getBackground()` / `getForeground()` keep reconstructing from CSS,
`JComponentMixin.isOpaque()` keeps reading `background-color` back with no boolean field, and
`SJPanel` keeps reporting `isOpaque() == false` on a fresh instance
([SD_vaadin_first_binding](#SD_vaadin_first_binding)'s lossy directions stand).

**A consequence worth stating, because it reads as an omission rather than a choice.** Without the
table this layer has no way to tell an L&F default from app intent — unset CSS is simply unset. So
the doctrine's three-way rule collapses here to two rows:

| condition | CSS emitted |
|---|---|
| `background-color` set | someone set it → the literal colour |
| unset | nothing |

There is no theme-default row **because there is no default to detect**, which means an `SJPanel`
never receives an opaque fill the way a stage-2 `JPanel` does. That is correct rather than missing:
the case that motivated the fill — an opaque glass pane failing to curtain the content beneath it —
is a stage-2 problem, and a stage-3 migrator composing `SJPanel`s is writing their own CSS anyway.

### Inherited: both CSS mechanism rules

*Which* CSS a surrogate emits is not an API-fidelity question at all. It decides whether the rendered
page is themable, and that is identical for both layers — a stage-3 view has exactly as much right to
be themable as a stage-2 one. So both rules carry over verbatim:

1. **A colour SB-Emulators supplies goes to a class bound in a shipped stylesheet, never inline.** An inline
   style outranks every stylesheet rule short of `!important`, so an inline SB-Emulators default is one the
   migrator cannot override by standard means. A colour the *app* set stays inline, because there it
   is intent that must beat the theme.
2. **Theme tokens are the `--vaadin-*` base styles, never `--lumo-*`.** Base style properties are
   defined in every Vaadin 25 theme — Aura (the default), Lumo, and bare base styles — so SB-Emulators never
   picks a theme and the migrator's own theme binds the value.

This is where most of the work lands: the `--lumo-*` sites are predominantly in this module —
`SJToolBar` (the `addSeparator()` tint), `SJSeparator`, `BorderCss`'s `TitledBorder` `::before` rule,
and `sjtogglebutton.css`. `BorderCss`'s `var(--lumo-base-color, white)` is the module's existing
precedent for asking "what colour is the surface behind me"; it becomes `--vaadin-background-color`.

### Why this is not an inconsistency

The asymmetry is [D_emulator_surrogate_split](../emulators/decisions.md#D_emulator_surrogate_split)'s,
reproduced one level down. Per-view code never sees both layers, so a stage-2 view gets Swing-shaped
colour semantics and a stage-3 view gets Vaadin-shaped ones — while both render into the same
themable page. It is the same shape as `JComponentMixin.getTopLevelAncestor()` walking the Vaadin
parent chain where the emulator walks the emulator hierarchy: same concept, layer-appropriate answer.

### Validation

`:surrogates` 1341 green. Two existing tests pin behaviour this entry **preserves**, and their staying
green through the emulator-side implementation *is* the check that the split held rather than being
flattened: `SJPanelTest.java:129` ("fresh SJPanel reports isOpaque false per R_vaadin_first lossy
direction") and `JComponentMixinTest.kt:166` ("setOpaque true without background reads back false").
If either goes red, the colour table leaked across the module boundary.

The CSS half landed as a token sweep: `SJToolBar`, `SJSeparator`, `BorderCss`'s `TitledBorder`
`::before` rule and `sjtogglebutton.css` now name `--vaadin-*` base tokens. Three tests pinned the
literal token strings and moved with them.

**One thing this split decided on its own**, which is the sign it was worth writing down separately:
when `JTabbedPane`'s per-tab colours needed a home, "no shadow stores here" answered the question
without further argument — the state went to the emulator, the declined effect stayed here.

<a id="SD_background_model_hop"></a>
## SD_background_model_hop. A Swing model may be mutated off the UI thread; the surrogate hops its fan-out onto the UI thread, synchronously

**Status: implemented, with an attached/detached test pair per component, for `SJComboBox`,
`SJProgressBar`, `SJTable` (table and selection models), `SJList`, `SJTree` (tree and selection
models) and `JTextComponentMixin` (the `Document` push, tested through `SJTextArea`).** The hop is
also in `SJTable`'s column model, `SJSlider`, `SJSpinner`, `SJCheckBox` / `SJRadioButton` /
`SJToggleButton` (`ButtonModel`), `SJTabbedPane` and `SJColorChooser`, which landed with
[D_attach_aware_hop](../emulators/decisions.md#D_attach_aware_hop) and have no test pair. They are
left out on purpose: a worker flipping a checkbox or switching a tab is setter-shaped, not
model-shaped, and `JSlider` / `JSpinner` subscribe to their model the way the tested `JProgressBar`
does. `SJTabbedPane`'s and `SJColorChooser`'s hops are not reached from stage 2 at all: `JTabbedPane` and
`JColorChooser` do not share their models with them (D_jtabbedpane, D_jcolorchooser), and
`JTabbedPaneBackgroundModelTest` covers the former's emulator side.
`runOnOwnerUI` hops through the session the surrogate captured in its private `hopSession` field,
and reads the node's owning state tree only under that session's lock: an unlocked `getUI()` or state
node read races the UI thread's attach (D_attach_aware_hop).

**Only in a model callback, never in a setter.** The hop exists because a model mutated directly
reaches the surrogate without passing any emulator method; a surrogate *setter* is always called
by someone, and when that is an emulator the emulator hops (`withPeer`). So a surrogate that both
mutates its model and pushes to its peer in one setter moves the push into its model callback:
`SJSlider` / `SJProgressBar` push their range from the `BoundedRangeModel` `ChangeListener`, and
`setMinimum` / `setMaximum` no longer write the peer. That also makes a range change made on the
model directly reach the peer, which it did not. The caret setters, which have no model to call
back from, hop emulator-side. Each call site carries
`// Allowed by R_tolerate_off_ui_thread because callback from model: <the listener>` on the line
above it, and `ModelCallbackHopMarkerTest` gates it.

**Decision.** Surrogates are UI-thread-confined by default ([R_tolerate_off_ui_thread](../CLAUDE.md#hard-rules),
surrogate limb). The exception is a surrogate meant to do background work, or holding a model a
background thread is expected to mutate: it may hop onto its own UI thread to apply the UI changes.
`SJComboBox`'s `ListDataListener` runs `onModelEvent` through `SHelper.runOnOwnerUI`, so a worker
filling a `DefaultComboBoxModel` gets the peer write *and* the item / action / list-data fan-out on
the UI thread, before `addElement` returns.

**Why the model.** Filling a model from a worker (a database load) is the ordinary Swing pattern, and
the model is the one Swing-typed thing a stage-3 migrator still hands the surrogate. Hopping in the
emulator instead — `withPeer` around `JComboBox.addItem` — misses `model.addElement` called directly.

**Synchronous**, because `DefaultComboBoxModel.addElement` fires into `JComboBox.contentsChanged` /
`intervalAdded`, which fire the item and action events before it returns (JDK 25 `src.zip`); a
deferred body reorders them against the caller's next line, as
[D_sync_ui_hop](../emulators/decisions.md#D_sync_ui_hop) rejects.

**The whole fan-out hops, not just the peer write.** Firing the events on the caller's thread would
hand a stage-3 listener a background thread, whose first `label.setText(...)` is an unlocked write;
"a Vaadin component's events arrive on the UI thread" is what a stage-3 user expects. Accepted costs:

- `accessSynchronously` runs the body on the *calling* thread with the lock held and the UI current:
  the listener still runs on the worker, but `isEventDispatchThread()` says `true`, and a slow
  listener freezes the tab rather than the worker.
- An emulator listener opening a modal parks `callSwing`'s virtual thread while the worker carries
  on; on the desktop the worker would block. Niche, not designed for. An emulator that subscribes
  to the model itself does not pay this; see *Emulator side*.
- Each model event rebuilds the peer's item list, so n `addElement`s cost n lock acquisitions at O(n)
  each. Coalescing would fix it but is asynchronous; left until someone hits it. The Grid-backed
  surrogates do not pay this: `SJTable` / `SJList` / `SJTree` only mark the data provider dirty
  (`refreshAll` / `refreshItem`), and the Grid fetches lazily. Measured 2026-09-29: 50,000
  `addRow`s from a worker into an attached `SJTable` took 74 ms, the same as on the UI thread.

**No UI to hop to: inline.** A detached combo (built on a worker before it joins a window — the
`testapps/inventory` shape) or one whose UI has left its session runs the fan-out on the calling
thread; no request thread can reach that tree. The UI comes from the component (`getUI()`), so
`:surrogates` needs neither `EmulatorContext` nor the blocking-dialog machinery.

**Emulator side.** In the inline case an emulator bridge that re-fires the surrogate's fan-out
(`JTree`, the buttons) is reached with no UI current, where `EHelper.callSwing`
throws. Those model-driven bridges use `EHelper.relayModelEvent` — `callSwing` when a UI is current,
inline otherwise — since a browser event always has a UI and the JDK fires model events on the
mutating thread. Peer listeners keep `callSwing` and its throw.

**An emulator that owns its state subscribes to the model, not to the surrogate.** `JSlider`,
`JProgressBar` and `JSpinner` put their `ChangeListener` on the shared model, as the JDK's
`setModel` does, `JComboBox` registers itself as its model's `ListDataListener` and runs
the JDK's item / action engine there, `JList` puts the JDK's `ListSelectionHandler` on its
shared selection model, lazily at the first `addListSelectionListener`, `JTabbedPane` keeps
the JDK's `ModelListener` on a selection model it does not share with the surrogate at all, and so does
`JColorChooser` with the L&F's model listener, a browser pick coming back as the chooser panel's model write. Their getters read the model rather than the peer
([D_emulator_owned_state](../emulators/decisions.md#D_emulator_owned_state)). Their listeners run on the mutating
thread, outside the hop and without the lock, which is where the JDK runs them. A modal opened
there takes [D_modal_from_background](../emulators/decisions.md#D_modal_from_background)'s
blocking path, so the worker blocks until the user answers, as on the desktop. The surrogate's hop
still covers the peer write. The relay stays at the subscription site: a browser-originated change
arrives with a UI current and gets `callSwing`'s virtual thread, and a migrator's override of
`createChangeListener` gets it too. `JComboBox` registers itself rather than a listener object, so
its relay sits inside the `ListDataListener` bodies instead. Leaving it out is silent until a modal
opens: a browser pick reaches the model through the surrogate's inline `SHelper.callSwing`, so an
`ActionListener` opening a `JOptionPane` has no fiber to park in (`JComboBoxTest.browserPickRunsInAUiFiber`).

**A bridge that runs inside the hop needs the relay.** Inside the hop the UI is current, so a direct
bridge runs the emulator's listeners on the worker holding the lock. A listener there that opens a
modal ("import finished" at 100%) takes `Dialog`'s UI-thread park path and throws
`IllegalStateException: This can only be called from a virtual thread`. Measured 2026-09-21 on a
`JProgressBar` bridged through its surrogate with the relay removed. The relay puts that listener on
`callSwing`'s virtual thread instead.

**The selection shift that follows a model change runs on the mutating thread.** `JTable` and
`JTree` are their model's listeners, as the JDK's are, so the selection shift that follows a row
insert or a node removal runs on the worker. `JList` is not, since in the JDK `BasicListUI` shifts
the selection; the emulator does that UI's job from its own `ListDataListener`, on the worker too,
and `SJList.setSelectionAdjustedByOwner` keeps the surrogate from shifting it again — a stage-3
`SJList` with no owner still shifts its own, inside its hop. Recorded in D_jlist and pinned by
`JListBackgroundModelTest`.

Tests: `SJComboBoxBackgroundModelTest`, `JComboBoxBackgroundModelTest`,
`SJProgressBarBackgroundModelTest`, `JProgressBarBackgroundModelTest`, `SJTableBackgroundModelTest`,
`JTableBackgroundModelTest`, `SJListBackgroundModelTest`, `JListBackgroundModelTest`,
`SJTreeBackgroundModelTest`, `JTreeBackgroundModelTest`, `SJTextAreaBackgroundModelTest`,
`JTextAreaBackgroundModelTest`, `JTabbedPaneBackgroundModelTest`.

<a id="SD_detach_after_closed"></a>
## SD_detach_after_closed. `SWindow.dispose()` detaches after the client's `closed` event, so the overlay can hand focus back

**Status: implemented.**

**Decision.** When the browser is animating a window's close, `dispose()` leaves the element
attached and removes it on the element's `closed` event. Otherwise it detaches at once, as before.
"Animating a close" means the `opened` property went true → false *after* a response had carried
it as true. A window shown and disposed in one request never reached the browser, so no `closed`
event is coming. `WINDOW_CLOSED`, `COMPONENT_HIDDEN` and the `everShown` reset stay at
`dispose()`: only the peer's detach moves.

**Why.** The trigger was `testapps/crud`'s validation idiom: `JOptionPane.showMessageDialog(...)`
then `nameField.requestFocusInWindow()`. Detaching in the same response as the close removes the
alert while its `OK` button has focus, which leaves focus on `<body>`. The overlay's
`_resetFocus()` then restores focus to its opener. That is the edit dialog's `OK`, via
`FocusRestorationController`'s deferred `setTimeout` branch, which fires *after* the app's
`focus()` and does not re-check. With the element still attached, the overlay restores
synchronously when `opened=false` lands, before any `executeJs` in the response. The app's
`focus()` then comes last and wins, as the JDK's does on the desktop. Measured in Chromium on
Vaadin 25.3.0, 2026-09-25. When the app moves no focus, Vaadin's restore still returns focus to
the opener (`Cancel` → `Add…`).

**This is Flow's own shape.** `OverlayAutoAddController` removes an auto-added `Dialog` on
`closed` rather than on close. The listener copies its `allowInert()` and
`DisabledUpdateMode.ALWAYS`, so a window that is inert behind another modal, or disabled, still
gets its detach.

**Fallbacks.**
- **The `closed` event never arrives** (the tab is gone, or something detached the element
  first). The peer then stays attached and closed. That is the state `setVisible(false)` already
  leaves a window in, so it is not a new state to support. Nothing that decides behaviour reads the
  peer's attachment: displayability is a field
  ([D_window_displayable](../emulators/decisions.md#D_window_displayable)), `EHelper.getWindows()`
  and `AutoShutdown` read the `WindowRegistry`, the modal latch is released by `hide()` /
  `dispose()`, and `vaadinx.awt.Window.onPeerDetached` is a no-op. Flow releases modality at
  `setOpened(false)`, so a lingering peer blocks nothing. The stage-3 `SWindow.getWindows()` walks
  the attached tree and lists the window until it goes, which the JDK's `getWindows()` does for
  disposed windows too.
- **Karibu** sends no `closed` event, but `clientRoundtrip()`'s `cleanupDialogs` removes every
  closed `Dialog` itself. Browserless tests therefore see the detach at the next lookup.
- **Re-shown before `closed` arrives**: opening clears the pending detach, and the handler checks
  `!isOpened()`, so a late `closed` from the earlier close leaves the window up.

**Accepted.** A surrogate-level `SHierarchyListener` / `SAncestorListener` on the window's content
is driven by Vaadin detach, so it fires one round-trip later than it used to. The emulator's
listeners are fired from the emulator and don't move.

Tests: `SWindowDetachAfterClosedTest`.

<a id="SD_enter_claims"></a>
## SD_enter_claims. The default button's Enter yields to a focused component that claims Enter itself

**Status: implemented.** Found by the `guide-migrateapp crud` run of 2026-09-25: Enter in the edit dialog's edited date spinner committed the date *and* closed the dialog.

**The JDK rule.** `JRootPane` binds the default button `WHEN_IN_FOCUSED_WINDOW`, and key dispatch offers a key to the focused component's `WHEN_FOCUSED` bindings (then its ancestors') first; a binding consumes the key only when its action's `isEnabled()` says so. For Enter that is, read from the JDK 25 `src.zip`:

| focused component | its Enter action | enabled when |
|---|---|---|
| `JTextField`, `JPasswordField` | `notify-field-accept` (`JTextField.NotifyAction`) | `hasActionListener()` — an `Action` counts, since `setAction` installs it as one |
| `JFormattedTextField`, hence `JSpinner`'s editor | `CommitAction` | `isEdited()` — and **only** that; unedited, it does not fall back to the listener rule |
| `JTextArea`, `JEditorPane` / `JTextPane` | `insert-break` | editable |

`SJRootPane`'s default button was a UI-wide Vaadin `Shortcuts` Enter that never looked at focus, and the fields' own Enter listeners do not stop it — so every enabled case above also fired the button. Measured in the browser: Enter in the edited date spinner, in the edited `IntegerField`-backed level spinner, and in an editable `JTextArea` each closed crud's dialog (the text area never got its newline).

**Decision.** The shortcut asks `EnterClaims.claimed(focusOwner, rootPane, layerClaims)` before clicking: the `FocusTracker` focus owner and each ancestor below the root pane, as the JDK walks focused-then-ancestors. A component claims by implementing `EnterClaims.Claimant`. The walk is what lets a spinner's inner `DatePicker`, which is where focus actually lands, find the spinner. A focus owner outside the root pane claims nothing.

**"Edited" is "the browser committed this value in the current round trip".** The server cannot see an edit in progress: the browser sends typed text only on commit. But Vaadin's fields commit in their own Enter keydown handler, before the event bubbles to the shortcut, so the value sync and the shortcut's `keydown` arrive in **one request, value first** — measured on `DatePicker` and `IntegerField`. `EnterClaims.noteClientCommit` marks the field from its value listener when `isFromClient()`, and a `beforeClientResponse` clears the mark, so a second Enter on the now-unedited field reaches the button, as in Swing. Accepted gap: retyping the current value sends no value change, so that Enter reaches the button where Swing's `isEdited()` would consume it.

**A text area's claim needs a browser half.** The shortcut `preventDefault()`s every Enter it matches, client-side, before the server can decline it — so declining kept crud's dialog open but still cost the text area its newline (measured). `SJTextArea` therefore also stops an editable area's Enter from propagating (`keydown` filtered on `event.key === 'Enter' && !element.readonly`), so the shortcut never sees it; the server-side claim stays as the rule of record. The field claims need no such half: their Enter has no browser default worth keeping, and they commit in their own handler first. The editor pane's rich-text editor consumes Enter in its own handler too; not browser-verified, since crud has no editor pane.

**The focus pointer had to work inside modals first.** The claim is asked of `FocusTracker`'s focus owner, which froze while a modal was open until its listeners were made `allowInert()` ([SD_focus_tracker](#SD_focus_tracker)) — without that fix, every rule here was consulted against the main window.

**Layering.** The surrogates carry every rule they can see (text area, editor pane, `SJTextField` / `SJPasswordField` listeners, the formatted family, `SJSpinner`), so a stage-3 surrogate app gets the JDK behaviour too. The one rule only the emulator can see — its own `JTextField` `ActionListener`s, which live on the emulator rather than on the peer — enters through `SJRootPane.setLayerEnterClaims`, installed by the emulator `JRootPane`'s constructor; it exempts `JFormattedTextField` as the JDK does.

**Not done.** `JComboBox` (Enter with the popup open) and `JTable` editing claim Enter in the JDK too; neither is wired, per R_infra_not_surface, and each is one more `Claimant`.

**Testing.** Karibu's `fireShortcut` runs a client round trip before dispatching, which ends the round trip a commit belongs to — so `EnterClaimsTest` asserts the commit rule on `claimsEnter()` directly and the other rules through the shortcut; `DefaultButtonEnterTest` covers the emulator rule. The one-request ordering and the newline are browser facts, verified in crud's edit dialog: an edited date or level spinner keeps the dialog open on the first Enter and closes it on the second, the bio text area takes a newline, and a plain name field's Enter still clicks OK.
